# Discovery digest — 10 read-only dimension audits at HEAD 5af69e0c (2026-08-16)

Source: evidence/10-discovery-raw.json. Adversarial verdicts included where the verifier ran.


====================================================================================================

## DIMENSION: topology

### Narrative summary
At 5af69e0c the repository contains 10 app roots (7 under apps/, 3 under apps/mobile/), 11 service roots, 39 top-level entries under packages/ (43 real packages once packages/typescript/* and packages/dart/* are expanded), 2 tools/ trees, and 8 infrastructure/ subtrees. The seed observation reconciles exactly and is not a contradiction: 8 = the ARCH-R1 constitutional core runtimes (AGENTS.md:70-78); 9 = production CI deploy targets = 5 Vercel Next apps + 4 Cloudflare Workers (scripts/validators/deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:18-31,140 and .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:331-427); 10 = canonicalRuntimeRoots (6 app + 4 service, scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:113-116) — the 9 deploy targets plus apps/mobile/merchant-app, which deploys on a separate workflow (.github/workflows/mobile-merchant-release.yml); 11 = production-topology.json entries with status "active", i.e. the 10 code roots plus the non-code root database/supabase; 21 = all production-topology.json runtime entries (11 active + 6 reserved + 4 frozen). deployment-topology.json carries only 17 nodes because it silently omits the 4 frozen apps while still declaring generatedFrom "runtime-manifests" — the frozen apps all have runtime.manifest.json files, so that provenance claim is literally false. The single most consequential finding is services/lcis-api: a real, built, tested Hono service (@letbuyy/lcis-api, 1,527 lines of src across 4 files, 2,205 lines of tests, 52 HTTP routes, 7 workspace deps) that has NO runtime.manifest.json, appears in NO topology file, is in neither canonicalServiceRoots nor reservedServiceRoots, has no auth middleware at all, and exposes live provider probes (/db/status, /github/status, /cloudflare/status, /vercel/status). It is launched by a first-class root script (package.json:7) and is inside the root build/typecheck/test graph. It is not dead code and it is not deployed — it is an undeclared ninth service root, which is exactly the shape ARCH-R2 forbids. The repository already knows this: config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json classifies it HUMAN_DECISION_REQUIRED with a ratchet probe, so the existence is GOVERNED-as-open, not undetected. The six reserved services and the two reserved mobile roots are genuinely clean — each contains exactly two files (AGENTS.md + runtime.manifest.json), zero source, zero tests, zero package.json, zero deploy config; ART-19/ARCH-R4/ARCH-R6 fully conform. The four frozen apps are genuinely de-deployed: no vercel.json, absent from config/vercel/projects.json, absent from both production and staging deploy matrices, excluded from root build/lint/typecheck, and their manifests declare frozen-not-deployed. However "frozen" holds only at the app-root level: the app-marketplace backend is mounted on the always-deployed monolith at /app-store and /app (services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:756-757) over 11 route modules, and a 623-line WebAssembly app-functions executor sits inside the deployed gateway source tree with zero importers. All seven ARCH-R7 rejected packages are absent with 0 importers. ARCH-R11/R12/R13 are clean at both the import and package.json dependency level; ARCH-R14 is clean. LCIS/intelligence tooling now outweighs commerce: 19 lcis-* packages carry 119,107 TypeScript lines versus 81,100 across the 21 commerce packages, and all 19 have build/test scripts inside the root turbo graph. Separately, the platform-owned checkout.letbuyy.com origin has a provisioned DNS record but is missing from every reserved-subdomain list, making it claimable by a merchant as a storefront subdomain.


### Findings (19)


**[S1] services/lcis-api is an undeclared ninth service root — real built runtime in no topology document, no manifest, no auth**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/lcis-api is a complete workspace package (@letbuyy/lcis-api, private, type:module) with 4 source files totalling 1,527 lines, 2 test files totalling 2,205 lines, build/typecheck/test/start scripts, and 7 workspace dependencies (@letbuyy/lcis-core, -live, -execution, -governance, -operations, -engineering, -orchestration) plus hono ^4.7.0 and zod ^4.3.6. src/app.ts registers 52 routes (10 GET, 42 POST) including live provider probes /db/status, /github/status, /cloudflare/status, /vercel/status and /live/health. src/middleware.ts implements request-id, security headers, a per-IP token bucket, Zod body validation and audit logging, but NO authentication or authorization of any kind. src/server.ts binds an HTTP listener on LCIS_API_PORT (default 7100). It is launched by a first-class root script and, because the root build/typecheck filters exclude only the 4 frozen apps, it is inside `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck` and `turbo test`. It has NO runtime.manifest.json (the only apps/* or services/* root with a package.json that lacks one), appears in neither config/runtime/production-topology.json nor config/runtime/deployment-topology.json, and is in neither canonicalServiceRoots nor reservedServiceRoots. It is absent from every deploy workflow. config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json already records it as HUMAN_DECISION_REQUIRED with a ratchet probe and forbids reclassifying it WEB_V1_REQUIRED or WEB_V1_DEFERRED.
- EXPECTED: ARCH-R2 (AGENTS.md:81): MUST NOT add, deploy, or wire any runtime not in ARCH-R1; adding a new runtime root requires a Constitution amendment + ARB approval + DOC 03/04 updates BEFORE source is created. AGENTS.md:388 states 'Topology is the 8 runtimes. Eight, not nine.' Every runtime root must carry a runtime.manifest.json and appear in the topology files.
- EVIDENCE: services/lcis-api/package.json:1-32; services/lcis-api/src/server.ts:10-20; services/lcis-api/src/middleware.ts:1-177 (no auth middleware defined or exported); services/lcis-api/src/app.ts:437,478,520,562 (provider status routes); package.json:7 ("lcis:api": "tsx services/lcis-api/src/server.ts"); package.json:16,124 (build/typecheck exclude only the 4 frozen apps, so lcis-api is in the graph); scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:97-111 (canonicalServiceRoots and reservedServiceRoots both omit services/lcis-api); config/runtime/production-topology.json:28-228 (21 runtimes, none is services/lcis-api); config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:449-459; AGENTS.md:81
- RISK: An HTTP service with zero authentication and 52 routes — including adapters that read Supabase, GitHub, Cloudflare and Vercel credentials from process.env — exists as first-class, buildable, launchable source with no owner, no deployment authority, no environment authority, and no secret-store declaration. Nothing structurally prevents someone from adding it to a deploy matrix or running it beside a production credential set. It also drags 7 LCIS packages into the commerce build/typecheck/test critical path, so LCIS tooling breakage blocks commerce merges. Because it is outside the runtime-manifest and topology validators, none of the manifest, environment, secret-topology or deployment-safety gates apply to it.
- RECOMMENDATION: ARB must issue one of three explicit rulings and record it as a Constitution/DOC-03 amendment: (a) DELETE — remove services/lcis-api and route LCIS access through packages/lcis-cli / packages/lcis-mcp only; (b) RELOCATE — move it under tools/ alongside tools/graph-server and tools/central-intelligence, out of the services/* workspace glob, and drop the root `lcis:api` script; or (c) ADOPT — amend ARCH-R1 to a 9-runtime topology, add services/lcis-api/runtime.manifest.json, register it in canonicalServiceRoots and both topology files, and add authentication before any deploy. Until then, add a negative assertion to runtime-topology-validator.mjs that every services/* directory containing a package.json must appear in canonicalServiceRoots or reservedServiceRoots — the validator today only asserts the forward direction (canonical root implies present), which is precisely why this root is invisible.
- CAPACITY: Adds 7 LCIS workspace packages (~46k lines) to the root build/typecheck/test graph that every commerce PR must pass; CI merge-safety runs a full-repo `pnpm turbo typecheck` (.github/workflows/ci.yml:156), so LCIS compile time is on the merge critical path for all 100->10,000-merchant work.

**[S1] checkout.letbuyy.com is a provisioned platform origin but is not a reserved subdomain — a merchant can claim it**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91-98 provisions a CNAME for `checkout` to cname.vercel-dns.com commented 'Vercel — checkout.letbuyy.com (hosted checkout origin)'. The string `checkout` appears in NEITHER packages/tenant-core RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS (16 labels) NOR RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS (~55 labels). Consequently resolveStorefrontHost('checkout.letbuyy.com','letbuyy.com') returns {kind:'subdomain', subdomain:'checkout'} rather than {kind:'reserved'}, and SubdomainSchema's .refine(!isReservedSubdomain) accepts 'checkout' at store creation (services/api-gateway/src/routes/stores.ts:647 POST '/' validates with CreateStoreSchema, whose subdomain field is SubdomainSchema). The database-side public.validate_subdomain SECURITY DEFINER function also omits 'checkout' from its reserved_words array. `checkout` is the only DNS record provisioned in dns.tf (@, www, admin, accounts, console, checkout, api, webhooks, *, connect) that is absent from RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS. Separately, no Vercel project in config/vercel/projects.json is bound to checkout.letbuyy.com, and apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts has no host branch for it — checkout is implemented only at apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/checkout/page.tsx, i.e. per-tenant subdomain, not on a distinct origin.
- EXPECTED: The Constitution requires a hosted checkout.letbuyy.com origin with cart-bound signed handoff. A platform-owned origin must be in the reserved label set so no tenant can claim it, and it must be bound to a named deploy target.
- EVIDENCE: infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91-98; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:27-44 (RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS — no 'checkout'); packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:46-57 (RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS — no 'checkout'); packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:135 (resolveStorefrontHost returns 'reserved' only via isReservedStorefrontLabel); packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:155-163 (SubdomainSchema refine); services/api-gateway/src/routes/stores.ts:647; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8074-8084; config/vercel/projects.json:1-78 (5 projects, none for checkout.letbuyy.com); apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/checkout/page.tsx
- RISK: A merchant who registers the store subdomain 'checkout' takes ownership of the hostname the Constitution designates as the platform's hosted checkout origin. That is a phishing-grade takeover of the exact origin buyers are taught to trust for payment, and it silently blocks activating the constitutionally-required hosted-checkout origin later. The three divergent reserved-word lists (TS RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS, TS RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS, SQL reserved_words) mean no single source of truth governs which hostnames are platform-owned.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add 'checkout' to RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS in packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts (which flows into RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS) and to the reserved_words array in the SQL validator, and extend scripts/validators/reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs to assert that every hostname label provisioned in infrastructure/dns/dns.tf is present in RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS — that assertion would have caught this. Then decide and record which deploy target serves checkout.letbuyy.com, or remove the DNS record until the hosted-checkout origin is real.

**[S1] Frozen apps are de-deployed at the root, but the app-marketplace backend is live on the always-deployed monolith**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: apps/app-marketplace, apps/developer-portal, apps/theme-marketplace and apps/platform-operations-portal are correctly frozen at the root level: no vercel.json, absent from config/vercel/projects.json, absent from both .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml and the two staging workflows, excluded from root build/lint/typecheck, and each runtime.manifest.json declares runtimeCategory 'frozen-next-app', deploymentAuthority.platform 'frozen-not-deployed', config [] and secretStore 'none - frozen app is not deployed'. But the marketplace/developer BACKEND is not frozen: services/api-gateway/src/routes/app-store/ contains 11 route modules (app-lifecycle-routes, billing-oauth-routes, compliance-routes, developer-routes, developer-tooling-routes, extension-routes, growth-routes, install-lifecycle-routes, parity-data, schema-drift, search-score) and services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:756-757 mounts them at /app-store and /app on the always-deployed production Worker. config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json already flags this signal with expectedRemoval:true.
- EXPECTED: ARCH-R10 / the frozen-app policy: frozen roots are source-kept and de-deployed. A frozen capability should not have its third-party OAuth, billing and revenue-share surface mounted on a production deploy target.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:756-757; services/api-gateway/src/routes/app-store/ (11 route modules); apps/app-marketplace/runtime.manifest.json (deploymentAuthority.platform = frozen-not-deployed); scripts/validators/deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:83-113; config/vercel/projects.json:1-78; config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:125 (app_marketplace_backend::gateway_app_store_routes, expectedRemoval true); apps/merchant-control-plane/src/components/apps/EmbeddedAppBridgeClient.tsx
- RISK: The deploy-matrix validator gives a green 'frozen apps are not deployed' result while the frozen capability's attack surface — third-party app OAuth, install lifecycle, billing and revenue share — is reachable on the production gateway. A postMessage app-bridge host also ships inside the deployed merchant-control-plane. 'Frozen' as currently validated is a statement about app roots, not about capabilities, and readers of the topology files will draw the wrong conclusion.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either remove the /app-store and /app router mounts from services/api-gateway/src/index.ts and the EmbeddedAppBridgeClient from merchant-control-plane, or amend the frozen-app policy to state explicitly that freezing applies to app roots only and enumerate which frozen capabilities remain mounted on production runtimes. Extend deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs to assert that no frozen capability has a mounted gateway route, so the green result means what it appears to mean.

**[S2] A 623-line WebAssembly app-functions executor sits inside the deployed gateway source tree with zero importers**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts is 623 lines implementing WebAssembly module compilation, caching and instantiation. Grepping services/api-gateway/src for 'app-functions-runtime', 'AppFunctionsRuntime' and 'CheckoutExtensionHost' returns no importer — the only references anywhere are string literals in apps/developer-portal/src/lib/reference.ts:19 and packages/shared-types/src/app-platform.ts:1239,1256. It is present and type-checked inside a production deploy root but not mounted on any request path.
- EXPECTED: ARCH-R9 (AGENTS.md:94): MUST NOT add the Wasm checkout-functions runtime / CheckoutExtensionHost / app-functions executor to any live path; checkout extensibility is a documented seam only. ARCH-R8 forbids a Wasm extension runtime in this envelope outright.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts:81-95 (WebAssembly.Exports, WebAssembly.Memory, compile(), wasmModuleCache); services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts (623 lines total); rg 'app-functions-runtime|AppFunctionsRuntime|CheckoutExtensionHost' services/api-gateway/src/ -> no importer; apps/developer-portal/src/lib/reference.ts:19; packages/shared-types/src/app-platform.ts:1239,1256; AGENTS.md:94
- RISK: A constitutionally-forbidden third-party code-execution runtime is one import statement away from being live inside the money-handling monolith. The current unmounted state is enforced only by the absence of an import, not by any validator; a single PR adding `api.route('/extensions', ...)` would activate arbitrary Wasm execution on the production payment path with no gate firing.
- RECOMMENDATION: Delete services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts, or move it out of the deployed root and behind an explicit ARB-gated activation. **[S2] The entire tools/ tree (46k lines, plus a Qdrant/ClickHouse/SigNoz docker-compose) is outside every governance perimeter**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: tools/ contains two trees: tools/graph-server (81 .mjs/.js/.ts files, 7,287 lines) and tools/central-intelligence (121 files, 38,877 lines, plus a docker-compose.yml declaring qdrant, signoz-clickhouse and related containers). tools/central-intelligence has its own package.json named @letbuyy/central-intelligence. Neither tree is in pnpm-workspace.yaml (globs are apps/*, apps/mobile/*, the enumerated packages/*, packages/typescript/*, services/*), neither is in turbo.json, and activeRootPrefixes in scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:163-170 lists apps/, services/, packages/, database/, infrastructure/, scripts/ — tools/ is absent. tools/central-intelligence therefore installs dependencies outside the frozen lockfile.
- EXPECTED: Every directory containing executable source and a package manifest should be inside a declared governance perimeter with an owner, an import boundary and a supply-chain gate — or be explicitly declared out-of-scope internal tooling with that exemption recorded.
- EVIDENCE: pnpm-workspace.yaml:1-42 (no tools/ glob); turbo.json (no tools reference); scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:163-170 (activeRootPrefixes omits tools/); tools/central-intelligence/package.json (name @letbuyy/central-intelligence, private); tools/central-intelligence/docker-compose.yml:13-40 (qdrant, signoz-clickhouse services); tools/graph-server/ (81 files, 7,287 lines)
- RISK: 46,164 lines of executable code — including HTTP servers and container definitions that mount volumes and expose ports 6333/6334 — are exempt from the supply-chain validator, the undeclared-import validator, the provider-isolation validator, the secret-topology validator and the frozen lockfile. Dependencies added there are never audited. Because it is outside pnpm-workspace, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` in CI does not pin what tools/central-intelligence actually runs.
- RECOMMENDATION: Record an explicit classification for tools/ (internal-tooling, non-deployable, never on a production credential set) in the Constitution or DOC-03, and either add tools/ to activeRootPrefixes so the supply-chain and provider-isolation validators cover it, or add a validator asserting that nothing under tools/ is referenced by any apps/*, services/* or packages/* source and that no deploy workflow touches it.
- CAPACITY: tools/central-intelligence's docker-compose provisions Qdrant + ClickHouse locally; if any of it were ever run beside production these are unbudgeted stateful services outside the declared Cloudflare + Vercel + Supabase envelope.

**[S2] LCIS/intelligence packages are 59% of the packages/ TypeScript footprint and sit on the commerce CI critical path**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: packages/ holds 40 pnpm workspace packages (21 commerce/runtime, 19 LCIS) plus 4 Dart packages. The 19 lcis-* packages contain 542 TypeScript files / 119,107 lines; the 21 commerce packages contain 228 files / 81,100 lines. Every one of the 19 LCIS packages declares both a build and a test script, so all are in the root turbo graph (root build/typecheck filters exclude only the 4 frozen apps). .github/workflows/ci.yml:156 runs a full-repo `pnpm turbo typecheck` in the merge-safety gate and ci.yml:126 runs `pnpm turbo test`. No app or service imports any @letbuyy/lcis-* package (verified by ripgrep across apps/ and services/ excluding services/lcis-api), so the LCIS cluster is genuinely non-load-bearing for commerce at runtime.
- EXPECTED: Internal intelligence tooling should not gate commerce delivery. The Constitution's envelope is a commerce platform; tooling weight should be isolated from the build/typecheck/test path that every merchant-facing PR must pass.
- EVIDENCE: Computed over packages/*/package.json and packages/typescript/*/package.json: LCIS 19 pkgs / 542 files / 119,107 lines; commerce 21 pkgs / 228 files / 81,100 lines; package.json:16 (build filters exclude only the 4 frozen apps); package.json:124 (typecheck, same filters); package.json:123 (test:workspaces = turbo test); .github/workflows/ci.yml:156 (full-repo typecheck in merge-safety gate); rg '@letbuyy/lcis-' apps/ services/ --glob '!services/lcis-api/**' -> no hits; pnpm-workspace.yaml:21-39 (all 19 lcis packages enumerated)
- RISK: Every commerce PR pays LCIS build and typecheck time, and any LCIS compile break blocks merchant-facing merges even though no runtime imports LCIS. As the platform scales toward 1,000 and 10,000 merchants this inverts the delivery ratio: more than half the shared-package surface a contributor must keep green is observability tooling, not commerce.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add --filter exclusions for @letbuyy/lcis-* to the root build/typecheck/test scripts, or move the LCIS package cluster under tools/ alongside graph-server and central-intelligence so it leaves the commerce workspace entirely. Record the intended relationship (tooling never gates commerce CI) as an ADR so the boundary is defensible rather than incidental.
- CAPACITY: Directly constrains engineering throughput: 119k lines of tooling on the merge critical path is a fixed tax on every commerce change, and it grows the CI wall-clock for the 100->1,000 merchant delivery cadence.

**[S2] deployment-topology.json claims generatedFrom "runtime-manifests" but silently omits the 4 frozen apps' manifests**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: config/runtime/deployment-topology.json declares generatedFrom:"runtime-manifests" and carries 17 nodes: 1 database + 4 workers + 5 Next apps + 1 mobile app + 6 reserved services. config/runtime/production-topology.json carries 21 runtime entries covering the same 11 active plus 6 reserved plus the 4 frozen apps. All four frozen apps DO have runtime.manifest.json files (apps/app-marketplace, apps/developer-portal, apps/theme-marketplace, apps/platform-operations-portal), so a genuine generation from runtime manifests would produce 21 nodes, not 17. scripts/validators/runtime-topology-validator.mjs:16 asserts only that the generatedFrom string equals "runtime-manifests" — it never verifies that the node set matches the manifest set.
- EXPECTED: ARCH-R5 states production-topology.json is regenerated from the real deploy matrix. A file asserting its own provenance must have that provenance verified, and the two topology files must be reconcilable without a silent 4-entry delta.
- EVIDENCE: config/runtime/deployment-topology.json:2-5 (generatedFrom "runtime-manifests"), 17 nodes enumerated; config/runtime/production-topology.json:28-228 (21 runtime entries); scripts/validators/runtime-topology-validator.mjs:16; apps/app-marketplace/runtime.manifest.json, apps/developer-portal/runtime.manifest.json, apps/theme-marketplace/runtime.manifest.json, apps/platform-operations-portal/runtime.manifest.json (all exist)
- RISK: Two governance files that both claim to describe the topology disagree by 4 entries with no recorded reason, and the validator that guards them checks a literal string rather than the derivation. Any downstream consumer — LCIS, an auditor, an AI agent — reading deployment-topology.json alone will conclude the frozen apps do not exist as runtime roots at all. The 21-vs-17-vs-11-vs-10-vs-9-vs-8 spread is exactly the kind of ambiguity that produced this audit's seed question.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either add the 4 frozen apps to deployment-topology.json with deploymentKind 'frozen-not-deployed' so 21 = 21, or change generatedFrom to an honest value such as 'deployable-runtime-manifests' and document the exclusion rule in the schema. Strengthen runtime-topology-validator.mjs to assert node-set equality against the on-disk runtime.manifest.json set rather than checking a provenance string.

**[S2] Production Durable Object namespace and D1 database are provisioned in the gateway deploy manifest but reachable from no request path**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml declares, for both staging and production, a Durable Object binding RUNTIME_COORDINATOR (class LetbuyyRuntimeDurableObject, new_sqlite_classes migration) and a D1 binding RUNTIME_DB (letbuyy-production-d1 / letbuyy-staging-d1, migrations_dir ../../database/cloudflare/d1/migrations), plus a Hyperdrive binding. The DO class at services/api-gateway/src/durable/runtime-coordinator.ts is 46 lines exposing only /health and /checkpoint. Across the whole gateway source, RUNTIME_COORDINATOR and RUNTIME_DB appear only as an Env type declaration (index.ts:206,208) and a Boolean presence check in a diagnostics response (routes/internal-console.ts:808,810). No route obtains a DO stub, and no route queries D1.
- EXPECTED: The Constitution declares ONE PostgreSQL source of truth. Provisioned production infrastructure should be reachable from a request path or removed; a second datastore in the production deploy manifest needs an explicit, narrow, recorded purpose.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:322-341 (production hyperdrive, d1_databases RUNTIME_DB letbuyy-production-d1, durable_objects RUNTIME_COORDINATOR); services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:29 (new_sqlite_classes = ["LetbuyyRuntimeDurableObject"]); services/api-gateway/src/durable/runtime-coordinator.ts:1-46; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:206,208 (Env declarations only); services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-console.ts:808,810 (Boolean presence checks only); scripts/validators/d1-governance-validator.mjs:49-57; scripts/validators/durable-object-validator.mjs:14-31
- RISK: Two billable Cloudflare stateful primitives (SQLite-backed Durable Object namespace, D1 database) are provisioned in every production deploy with no traffic, and both the D1 and DO validators pass because they assert manifest/terraform/class-export shape rather than request-path reachability. D1 additionally introduces a second data plane holding provider_execution_evidence, d1_migration_lineage, outbox_restoration_lineage and rollback_lineage — recovery-critical lineage stored outside the declared single source of truth, with its own migration directory and no reconciliation against Postgres.
- RECOMMENDATION: Record explicitly whether D1 is an accepted secondary evidence store (and if so, amend the 'one source of truth' language to scope it to commerce data and state the recovery contract when D1 and Postgres disagree), or remove the D1 and DO bindings from the production manifest until a request path uses them. Extend d1-governance-validator/durable-object-validator with a reachability assertion so 'governed' implies 'used'.
- CAPACITY: Two provisioned-but-idle stateful primitives per environment carry fixed cost at every scale tier and add two more recovery surfaces to the runbook without serving traffic.

**[S2] The SQL validate_subdomain reserved list diverges from tenant-core by 8 labels and is enforced by no single-source validator**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8070-8084 defines SECURITY DEFINER public.validate_subdomain with a hardcoded reserved_words array. It omits eight labels present in packages/tenant-core RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS: apps, themes, developer, dcv, connect, status, docs, dev. Two of these are provisioned platform infrastructure — `connect` has a DNS CNAME at infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:132 and `dcv` is the domain-control-validation label used for custom-domain issuance. The function has no caller in apps/ or services/ (ripgrep for validate_subdomain across both trees returns nothing), so the live path today is the TypeScript SubdomainSchema. scripts/validators/reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs reads only tenant-core, the gateway schema facade, gateway auth/onboarding and storefront seo — it never reads any .sql file.
- EXPECTED: Reserved subdomain labels are a single-source contract owned by packages/tenant-core (asserted by reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs:38-45). A third, divergent copy inside a SECURITY DEFINER database function violates that contract.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8070-8084; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8125-8131; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:27-57; infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:132 (connect CNAME); scripts/validators/reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs:8-50 (reads no SQL); rg 'validate_subdomain' services/ apps/ -> no callers

### Adversarial verdicts (3)


**OVERSTATED** — services/lcis-api is an undeclared ninth service root — a real built runtime present in no topology document, carrying no runtime.manifest.json and no authentication — rated S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: services/lcis-api is an undeclared ninth service root: a real, buildable, launchable Hono HTTP service (@letbuyy/lcis-api, 1,527 src lines, 52 routes — 10 GET / 42 POST) that is absent from canonicalServiceRoots and reservedServiceRoots (scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:97-111), absent from config/runtime/production-topology.json and deployment-topology.json (zero `lcis` hits), and is the ONLY apps/* or services/* root with a package.json and no runtime.manifest.json. Its middleware (services/lcis-api/src/middleware.ts:1-177) implements request-id, security headers, per-IP rate limiting, Zod validation and audit logging but NO authentication or authorization. The enforcement gap is structural: scripts/validators/runtime-validator.mjs readdirs apps/ (:21,33-48) and rejects unexpected app roots, but for services it only iterates the canonical list (:72) and never enumerates the services/ directory, so an undeclared service root cannot be detected — a direct ARCH-R2 (AGENTS.md:81) breach with no ARB amendment. Because root build/typecheck (package.json:16,124) filter only the 4 frozen apps and turbo.json carries no task filters, it sits in `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck` and `turbo test` (VERIFIED-CONFIG). Mitigations the original claim missed: it IS code-owned (.github/CODEOWNERS:6,27), it DOES carry a hand-authored services/lcis-api/AGENTS.md that forbids adding production deploy targets, it is registered HUMAN_DECISION_REQUIRED in config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:450-460, and that registry is enforced as a gate-registered growth ratchet (scripts/validate-governance.mjs:180) whose un-baselined probes would FAIL if it were added to .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml or to the api-gateway dependency graph. It is not deployed (no wrangler/vercel/Docker config, absent from every deploy workflow, and no canonical runtime depends on any @letbuyy/lcis-* package), and its provider routes return only status/latency/missing-env-var names, never credential values. The 19 packages/lcis-* workspaces are in the build graph independently of this root, so the "drags 7 packages into the critical path" causation does not hold. Correct severity: S2 — a tracked, undeployed architecture/governance conformance breach with a genuine validator blind spot; final classification (canonical / reserved / governed tooling tier / move under tools/ / delete) is HUMAN-REQUIRED.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The structural core is fully CONFIRMED and I found evidence stronger than the claim supplied; the risk narrative and S1 rating are not earned.

CONFIRMED by direct reading at 5af69e0c:
- services/lcis-api/package.json:2,11-30 — @letbuyy/lcis-api, private, type:module, build/typecheck/test/start scripts, 7 workspace LCIS deps + hono ^4.7.0 + @hono/node-server ^1.13.0 + zod ^4.3.6. Line counts exact: app.ts 1324 + index.ts 5 + middleware.ts 177 + server.ts 21 = 1,527; app.test.ts 2026 + execution.test.ts 179 = 2,205.
- Route counts exact: grep -cE '^\s*app\.get\(' = 10, app.post = 42, total 52. src/server.ts:10-20 binds LCIS_API_PORT default 7100.
- No auth: src/app.ts:94-100 registers only requestId/securityHeaders/rateLimiter/auditLog; src/middleware.ts exports 6 factories, none authenticating; grep for auth|token|bearer|api-key|jwt in app.ts returns no guard.
- scripts/validators/governance-common.mjs:97-111 — canonicalServiceRoots (4) and reservedServiceRoots (6) both omit it. `rg -c lcis config/runtime/*.json` returns zero hits (absent from production-topology.json and deployment-topology.json). No deploy workflow references it; production-deploy.yml:400-408 lists 5 web apps, none lcis-api. No wrangler.toml / Dockerfile / vercel.json in the root. GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md contains no LCIS or tooling-root carve-out.
- My own enumeration (`for d in apps/* apps/mobile/* services/*`) confirms it is the ONLY root with a package.json and no runtime.manifest.json — 22 manifests exist, none under services/lcis-api.
- STRONGER than claimed: scripts/validators/runtime-validator.mjs:21,33-48 readdirs apps/ and asserts every directory is an allowed root, but :72 only iterates canonicalServiceRoots — services/ is never enumerated. An undeclared service root is structurally undetectable, which is the real mechanism behind the ARCH-R2 escape (AGENTS.md:81 itself claims enforcement via runtime-manifest-validator).
- Build-graph inclusion is VERIFIED-CONFIG (not executed): package.json:16,124 filter only the 4 frozen apps and turbo.json has no task filters, so a workspace with a build script is in the graph.

REFUTED / OVERSTATED elements:
1. "no owner" is wrong. .github/CODEOWNERS:6 (`* @saai-sidd`) and :27 (`/services/ @saai-sidd`) assign an owner, and services/lcis-api/AGENTS.md is tracked (git ls-files) and hand-authored, subordinating the boundary to root governance and stating "do not add production deploy targets". Governance docs asserting it has no AGENTS.md (docs/theme-system-current-state/02-runtime-and-route-map.md:75, 17-open-decisions-and-arb-gates.md:611) are HISTORICAL/stale.
2. "Nothing structurally prevents someone from adding it to a deploy matrix" is largely refuted. scripts/validators/web-v1-scope-perimeter-validator.mjs is registered in the gate at scripts/validate-governance.mjs:180 and enforces config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json as a growth ratchet. Only `lcis_api_service::source_root` is baselined (registry:120); the `web_deploy_matrix_entry` probe (registry:458, watching .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml for `lcis-api`) and `gateway_runtime_dependency` probe (registry:459, watching services/api-gateway/package.json for `lcis`) are NOT baselined, so those exact escalations fail the gate today. The residual gap is narrower: a new/other workflow (staging-deploy.yml, production-rollout.yml) or a manual ops launch is not probed.
3. "drags 7 LCIS packages into the commerce build/typecheck/test critical path" misattributes causation. packages/lcis-core/package.json:13-15 and packages/lcis-governance/package.json:11-13 show those packages carry their own build/typecheck/test scripts and are enumerated by pnpm-workspace.yaml independently; deleting services/lcis-api would not remove them from turbo.
4. The credential/security framing is bounded, not live. packages/lcis-adapters/src/supabase/SupabaseAdapter.ts:85,97,100-101 does read SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY into apikey/Bearer headers, but packages/lcis-live/src/factory.ts:33-38 wires GET-only host-allowlisted FetchHttpClients, and src/app.ts:452-472,478-512 return only adapter status/latencyMs/missingEnvVars names/findings — no credential values. No canonical runtime depends on any @letbuyy/lcis-* package (rg over apps/*/package.json and services/*/package.json), so it is not in any deployed bundle. The auth gap is a local-tooling exposure on an undeployed server, not a production credential path at this SHA.

Net: a genuine, well-evidenced ARCH-R2 conformance breach with a real validator blind spot, but it is undeployed, code-owned, boundary-documented, already registered HUMAN_DECISION_REQUIRED, and partially gate-ratcheted against the precise escalation the claim says is unprevented. That is S2, not S1. Evidence state VERIFIED-SOURCE stands for the corrected claim; the classification decision itself is HUMAN-REQUIRED.

**OVERSTATED** — checkout.letbuyy.com is a provisioned platform origin but is not a reserved subdomain — a merchant can claim it (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `checkout` is absent from every enforced reserved-name list, so any merchant can permanently claim the hostname the Constitution designates as the hosted checkout origin. VERIFIED-SOURCE: RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:27-44) and RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS (:46-57) omit 'checkout'; SubdomainSchema's refine (:155-163) therefore accepts it, and POST /stores validates with CreateStoreSchema (services/api-gateway/src/routes/stores.ts:647, mounted index.ts:793) with no other name gate; sites has only a UNIQUE index on subdomain (000_baseline.sql:15756-15760) and no later migration adds a reserved-name CHECK or trigger. Because resolveStorefrontHost returns kind:'subdomain' for that host (index.ts:134-136), store-context.ts:206-216 would map checkout.letbuyy.com to the squatter's site and proxy.ts:321-322/:404 would serve it — the same host the gateway mints handoffs to (wrangler.toml:286; cart-routes.ts:567). The 16-label list is deepEqual-pinned by tests/contracts/reserved-subdomain.test.ts:19-46 and re-asserted by scripts/validators/reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs:16-31, so the omission is gate-locked, not accidental drift. Two corrections to the original framing: (a) the origin is DECLARED in Terraform (infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91-98), not proven provisioned — live apply and Vercel host attach are open human items (SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:14,:23; EXECUTABLE_HUMAN_REQUIRED.md:16; tests/executable/human-required-live-paths.test.mjs:9); (b) no buyer is routed to that host today — storefront-runtime has no root /checkout route, no Vercel project binds it, and handoff minting 503s without CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET (cart-routes.ts:540-544). The realized impact today is a pre-emptible, irreversible squat that blocks the cutover; it becomes a live payment-page hijack at the moment DNS + host attach land. The SQL reserved_words array (000_baseline.sql:8074-8084) also omits 'checkout' but has zero call sites, so it is list divergence, not a bypassed guard.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The mechanism is fully confirmed and I found corroboration the auditor missed; only the severity and the word "provisioned" are unearned.

CONFIRMED by direct read: infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91-98 declares the `checkout` CNAME (dns.tf is now the only zone file; letbuyy_com.tf is gone). `rg "checkout" packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts` returns ZERO hits — absent from RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS (index.ts:27-44, 16 labels) and RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS (:46-57). resolveStorefrontHost (:134-136) returns 'reserved' only via isReservedStorefrontLabel, so 'checkout.letbuyy.com' resolves as {kind:'subdomain', subdomain:'checkout'}. SubdomainSchema (:155-163) refines only on isReservedSubdomain, CreateStoreSchema (:201-203) uses it, and services/api-gateway/src/routes/stores.ts:647 validates POST /stores with it (mounted at index.ts:793); the only other gate in the handler is uniqueness (stores.ts:673-700). No DB backstop: sites carries only sites_subdomain_key UNIQUE (000_baseline.sql:15756-15760) — no CHECK/trigger on subdomain in ANY later migration (checked all of 073-171+). `checkout` is indeed the only named dns.tf record missing from RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS.

EVIDENCE THE AUDITOR MISSED, which strengthens the finding: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:286 sets production CHECKOUT_ORIGIN="https://checkout.letbuyy.com" and cart-routes.ts:567 mints `new URL('/checkout', checkoutOrigin(c))`; store-context.ts:206-216 resolves that host to sites.subdomain='checkout'; proxy.ts:321-322 + :404 rewriteToStore would serve the squatter's store at the handoff target. The gap is locked in by the governance gate: tests/contracts/reserved-subdomain.test.ts:19-46 deepEqual-pins the 16-label list without 'checkout', and scripts/validators/reserved-subdomain-single-source-validator.mjs:16-31 repeats it.

WEAKENING FACTS the claim understates. (1) "provisioned" is Terraform source, never applied: SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:14 (SKIP-D5-DNS-TERRAFORM-APPLY) and :23 (SKIP-D6 — "attach the host to apps/storefront-runtime", provision CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET), EXECUTABLE_HUMAN_REQUIRED.md:16 (HR-DNS-CHECKOUT), tests/executable/human-required-live-paths.test.mjs:9 (test.skip). Configuration is not deployment. (2) The present-tense "phishing-grade takeover of the exact origin buyers are taught to trust" does not hold at this SHA: `ls apps/storefront-runtime/src/app` shows no root /checkout route (only store/[subdomain]/checkout), no Vercel project binds the host (config/vercel/projects.json — storefront prod is store.letbuyy.com), and handoff minting fails closed with 503 when CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET is absent (cart-routes.ts:540-544). No live path sends a buyer to that host today. (3) The SQL reserved_words list (000_baseline.sql:8074-8084) is real but `rg validate_subdomain` shows ZERO call sites — it is defined-but-never-called, so it evidences list divergence, not a bypassed live guard.

Net: a genuine, durable, self-serve squat on the platform's designated payment hostname that permanently blocks the constitutionally-required cutover — but its buyer-facing payment-hijack consequence is contingent on a DNS/host-attach step the repo itself records as not performed. S2 now, escalating to S1 the moment the host is attached.

**REFUTED** — Frozen apps are de-deployed at the root, but the app-marketplace backend is live on the always-deployed monolith: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:756-757 mounts 11 app-store route modules at /app-store and /app on the production Worker, exposing third-party app OAuth, install lifecycle, billing and revenue share, while the deploy-matrix validator reports a green "frozen apps are not deployed" result. (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The app-marketplace/developer backend source is retained and compiled into the always-deployed api-gateway Worker (11 route modules under services/api-gateway/src/routes/app-store/, mounted at index.ts:756-757), but it is NOT reachable. A dedicated fail-closed kill switch registered at index.ts:729-732 (and :740-743 for the operator plane) returns 404 APP_PLATFORM_DISABLED for every /app-store/**, /app/**, /operations/marketplace-reviews/** and /operations/developers/** request unless FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM is explicitly positive; that variable is declared in no wrangler.toml var block and no deploy workflow. A CI-wired behavioral test (services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/app-platform-killswitch.test.ts) builds the real gateway, enumerates all routes from the live routers, and proves 404 under verbatim production and staging vars with a positive control. The merchant-control-plane and platform-console UI entry points, and the one non-prefix-reachable helper (compliance-routes.ts:129), are independently gated. The residual, correctly INFO-grade, is that 'frozen' here is enforced by a runtime flag plus dead-but-shipped code rather than by code removal (config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:114 expectedRemoval true, pending ARB), that services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:68-69 still allowlists the frozen prefixes for proxying, and that deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:83-113 asserts only app-root properties and never capability reachability — so readers of that validator alone should not infer capability-level freeze.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: INFO / VERIFIED-TEST
- REASONING: The auditor cited the mount (index.ts:756-757) but did not read 27 lines above it. services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:729-732 registers `appPlatformKillSwitch` on '/app-store', '/app-store/*', '/app', '/app/*' BEFORE the mounts; Hono composes in registration order, so the gate runs first. services/api-gateway/src/middleware/appPlatformKillSwitch.ts:24-27 is explicitly fail-CLOSED (`return value ? POSITIVE.has(value) : false`) and its docblock states it deliberately does not reuse the fail-OPEN `featureGate`. index.ts:740-743 gates the operator plane (/operations/marketplace-reviews, /operations/developers) identically. Verified in the COMMITTED tree at 5af69e0c via `git show 5af69e0c:services/api-gateway/src/index.ts`; `git status --porcelain services/api-gateway/ tests/app-platform/` returns empty, so this is not a dirty-working-tree artifact.

The flag is undeclared everywhere: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml declares only FEATURE_GRAPHQL/EXTENSIONS/LIQUID/AI_CREDIT_PURCHASE at :39-42, :130-133, :246-249; a repo-wide grep for FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM across *.toml/*.yml/*.json returns hits only in tests, docs and artifacts — never in wrangler.toml or any deploy workflow.

This is VERIFIED-TEST, not merely VERIFIED-CONFIG. I read services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/app-platform-killswitch.test.ts: it imports the real handler from ../index.ts (:135-137), enumerates every route from the live appStoreRouter/appApiRouter rather than hard-coding them (:155-165, asserting >=60 routes), and asserts status 404 AND error.code === 'APP_PLATFORM_DISABLED' for every enumerated route across nine env shapes including verbatim [env.production.vars] and [env.staging.vars] (:216-249). A positive control (:285+) asserts an explicit opt-in re-opens /v1/app-store/categories, proving the 404s are the gate and not a broken mount. A named-family assertion (:253-283) covers exactly the families the claim calls an attack surface: app OAuth2, bridge/fetch, embed-session, install lifecycle, billing/revenue share, developer platform. Both this test (.github/workflows/ci.yml:159 -> test:unit:gateway -> package.json services/api-gateway:10 glob src/__tests__/*.test.ts) and the UI test (ci.yml:121,164 -> test:behavioral -> package.json:120 glob tests/*/*.test.mjs) run in CI.

The app-bridge sub-claim also fails: every host page 404s first — apps/merchant-control-plane/src/app/[store]/apps/[installationId]/page.tsx:16, apps/merchant-control-plane/src/app/[store]/apps/page.tsx:23, apps/merchant-control-plane/src/app/[store]/settings/apps/page.tsx:13, apps/platform-console/src/app/(dashboard)/marketplace-governance/page.tsx:39, apps/platform-console/src/app/(dashboard)/developers/page.tsx:246, all via the mirrored fail-closed flag in apps/merchant-control-plane/src/lib/app-platform-flag.ts:11-13.

I searched for the bypass the auditor should have found. The only app-store code reachable outside the gated prefixes is `dispatchStoreAppComplianceWebhook`, imported into the non-gated services/api-gateway/src/routes/customers.ts:37 and called at :1313 — the prefix middleware cannot reach it. It carries its own inline gate at services/api-gateway/src/routes/app-store/compliance-routes.ts:129, which suppresses all third-party egress while still writing the GDPR data-subject record. No other mount of appStoreRouter/appApiRouter exists (grep across services/api-gateway/src). The admin GraphQL restPaths '/app-store/apps' and '/app-store/installations' (graphql/admin.ts:375-376) resolve through `restDelegate: (c, request) => app.fetch(request, c.env)` (index.ts:786-788), a full re-entry that re-runs the gate.

Two evidence-discipline errors: the cited registry line is config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:114, not :125; and that entry's own note (:322) reads "DO NOT EDIT under this workflow. Owned by the separate urgent task on codex/emergency-appbridge-killswitch. Recorded here only" — a deliberately stale governance record predating the kill switch, treated by the auditor as source evidence of reachability.

What survives is narrow and INFO-grade: scripts/validators/deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:83-113 does only assert app-root properties (Vercel registry, vercel.json, manifest fields, root script exclusion) and never capability reachability — the auditor's meta-observation about validator scope is correct, but the alarm is unfounded because a different, tested control covers the capability. Residual drift: services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:68-69 still allowlists /v1/app-store and /v1/app in ALLOWED_PREFIXES (inert while the gateway 404s), and the ~340KB of frozen source still compiles into the production gateway bundle pending Wave 1 / ARB removal.

Honest boundary: I have no authorized live provider access, so I cannot prove no operator set FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM as an out-of-band Cloudflare secret. Every committed artifact at this SHA is off.

### Unknowns
- Whether services/lcis-api has ever been deployed or run against production provider credentials. The repository shows no deploy manifest and no workflow reference, but I have no authorized live provider access; a Cloudflare/Vercel/Render account listing or a host process inventory would settle it. HUMAN-REQUIRED / provider-action.
- Whether services/lcis-api should be deleted, relocated to tools/, or adopted as a ninth runtime. config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json explicitly classifies it HUMAN_DECISION_REQUIRED and forbids reclassification without an ARB amendment; only an ARB ruling recorded as a Constitution/DOC-03 amendment can settle it.
- Which Vercel project (if any) actually serves checkout.letbuyy.com. The DNS CNAME exists (dns.tf:91-98) but no project in config/vercel/projects.json is bound to it and proxy.ts has no host branch. Settled by a `vercel domains inspect checkout.letbuyy.com` / project-domain listing, or by an ARB decision to remove the record.
- Whether any site row already exists with subdomain='checkout'. Settled by `SELECT id, subdomain FROM sites WHERE subdomain IN ('checkout','dcv','connect','apps','themes','developer','status','docs','dev')` against production — the eight labels that the SQL validate_subdomain reserved_words array omits relative to tenant-core.
- Whether the production Cloudflare D1 database letbuyy-production-d1 and the RUNTIME_COORDINATOR Durable Object namespace have any data or request volume. wrangler.toml declares them and terraform provisions them, but no request path reads them at this SHA. Settled by `wrangler d1 info letbuyy-production-d1` and Cloudflare Analytics for the DO namespace — provider access required.
- Whether the /app-store and /app router surfaces on the deployed gateway are reachable in production or gated by a runtime kill switch. services/api-gateway/src/routes/app-store/app-platform-killswitch.test.ts exists, suggesting a switch, but I did not read the flag's production default or the environment variable that sets it. Settled by reading the killswitch middleware plus the production env matrix.
- Whether AGENTS.md:92's statement that the seven rejected packages are 'kept temporarily until their asserting validators are retargeted' is now simply stale text, or whether some validator retargeting is still outstanding. The roots are deleted and the validator asserts non-existence, so the text appears stale, but the referenced LEG-001..008 and ADR-002/023 items were not read.
- Whether the frozen apps' 231 retained source files (95+26+74+36) contain any code that reads production secrets or would build if unfrozen. They are excluded from build/lint/typecheck so nothing verifies they still compile; establishing this needs a one-off `pnpm --filter @letbuyy/app-marketplace build` which is a mutating/generating command outside this audit's read-only scope.
- Whether tools/central-intelligence has ever been run with production credentials or its docker-compose stack started against production data. The compose file provisions Qdrant and ClickHouse with volume mounts; only a host/container inventory would settle it.
- The actual production behavior of apps/mobile/merchant-app releases. mobile-merchant-release.yml builds and uploads a signed AAB artifact but I did not trace whether any step publishes to Google Play; the workflow's trigger conditions (line 3 'on:') were not fully read.
- Whether database/supabase counting as an 'active runtime' in production-topology.json is an intentional modeling choice or an artifact. It is the difference between the 10 and 11 counts; a DOC-03 statement of what production-topology.json's runtimes[] is meant to enumerate would settle it.
- Whether services/mobile-bff's single 516-line src/index.ts genuinely implements the composite /v1/dashboard fan-in that ARCH-R1 row 3 describes as 'a real composite/fan-in BFF'. I established the file size and that it has one test file, but did not read the route implementations.

====================================================================================================

## DIMENSION: data

### Narrative summary
Migration truth: `database/supabase/migrations` holds exactly 126 tracked .sql files with numeric prefixes {000} ∪ {073..197}; the ONLY gap is 001–072 (collapsed into `000_baseline.sql`) and there are ZERO duplicate prefixes — `ls database/supabase/migrations/ | sed 's/_.*//' | sort -n | uniq -d` returns empty and `129_mobile_customer_segments.sql` appears exactly once. Root `AGENTS.md:156` (MIG-R2) is therefore wrong on three counts at this SHA: it declares "current max (161)" (real max 197), it orders agents to "fix the existing duplicate prefix (129)" (no duplicate exists), and it cites an enforcing validator named `migration-history-validator` that does not exist anywhere in the repo. The real gate is `scripts/validators/sequential-migration-validator.mjs`, which passes legitimately because it hard-codes a baseline exemption `KNOWN_GAP_END = 72` (line 7) and only checks contiguity above 72 — 073..197 is contiguous. So the 161-vs-197 discrepancy is (a) stale governance prose, not a sequencing defect. Rollback pairing is a different and real defect: the convention is `<forward-basename>.rollback.sql` under `database/supabase/rollback/` (all 48 files name-match a forward migration exactly), giving 48 paired / 78 unpaired of 126. Twelve migrations at or above the MIG-R3 threshold are unpaired — 138, 140, 157, 170, 175, 179, 182, 183, 184, 186, 187, 194 — whereas `AGENTS.md:157` names only 138/140/157, so nine new violations accumulated after the rule was written (AGENTS.md last committed 2026-06-11; migration 170 landed 2026-06-13, 197 on 2026-08-15). The promised "rollback-pairing validator gating any migration ≥ 138" does not exist: `rollback-integrity-validator.mjs` governs Cloudflare Worker version rollback, not SQL, and only three validators read the SQL rollback directory, each reading one hard-coded file. So the 161/197 story is BOTH a doc-drift defect and, on the MIG-R3 leg, a real un-gated governance failure. Schema layer: 423 distinct tables are created across the migration log; every one receives `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` somewhere, and `164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:63-64` runs an information_schema-driven loop that ENABLEs+FORCEs RLS and installs an `app_tenant` GUC policy on every table carrying `site_id`/`store_id` (259 of 423). The critical failure is not FORCE coverage but permissive-policy leakage: 110 policies survive to final replay state with `USING (true)`/`WITH CHECK (true)`, and about 20 of those have NO `TO` clause (PostgreSQL default = PUBLIC) or target anon/authenticated. Migration `163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:1-4` documents this exact defect class in its own header and remediates precisely 12 tables — leaving `auth_password_set_intents` (a password-reset authority table), `gift_cards`, `collections`, `collection_products`, `pages`, `onboarding_attempts`, `store_surveys`, `audit_logs`, `analytics_events`, `leads`, `contact_submissions`, `plans`, `employee_roles`, `ai_usage`, four `app_*` discovery tables and `employee_permissions` still carrying live PUBLIC true-predicate policies. Because PERMISSIVE policies OR together, these defeat the app_tenant policies 164 later added to the same tables. Tenancy at runtime is the second major gap: `services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:29` is the only `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` path, and its wrapper `withResolvedTenantTx` (`tenant-context.ts:67`) has ZERO production callers — the only references are its own definition and a string-matching governance test. All real gateway traffic uses `getSupabaseAdmin` (999 call sites across 115 files, 96 of them route files) which returns a service_role PostgREST client wrapped in an application-layer proxy (`tenant/tenant-scoped.ts`) that merely requires an `.eq('site_id', …)` predicate. The `app_tenant` role, the `app.site_id` GUC and every GUC policy from migrations 139/163/164 are installed but carry no traffic. Money schema conforms on the canonical ledger (8 account codes, `writer_module = 'settlement'` CHECK, append-only trigger AND `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE`, bigint `amount_minor`, derived `ledger_account_balance_minor`) but NOT on the operational path: `orders` money is `numeric(12,2)` rupees by deliberate design (`109_orders_money_width.sql:3,11-17`), `payment_transactions.amount` is `numeric(10,2)`, gift-card balances are numeric, and roughly 30 `*_minor` columns are 32-bit `integer` (overflow at ₹2.14 crore). Second data authority: `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml` binds D1 `RUNTIME_DB`, DO `RUNTIME_COORDINATOR`, KV `CACHE`, R2 `R2_BUCKET` and Hyperdrive in both staging and production. D1 holds only provider/deploy/rollback lineage tables and has ZERO readers or writers in the entire repo — pure config drift, not a second authority. The Durable Object stores exactly two strings and is never invoked. KV holds rate-limit counters and a storefront cache-version epoch — non-authoritative, but the counters are non-atomic get-then-put on an eventually-consistent store, so limits are effectively per-colo. R2 holds derived artifacts. Partitioning is the highest-stakes call: the monthly workflow really does execute DDL against production, but only `CREATE TABLE … PARTITION OF <shadow>` and `ALTER TABLE <shadow> DETACH PARTITION` on the `*_partitioned` shadow parents — no live table is partitioned or renamed. However `131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-140` installs five ENABLED row-level AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE triggers that mirror every write on `worker_request_logs`, `runtime_telemetry_metrics`, `outbox_events`, `platform_queue_jobs` and `webhook_deliveries` into the shadows, and nothing ever calls the provided `set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false)` kill switch. Constitution line 296 requires "the partition-shadow seam (kept, mirror disabled)". Migration `179`'s own header is proof the mirror runs against live traffic: a shadow column mismatch raised SQLSTATE 42601 and "broke EVERY outbox write". Worse, `migration-safety-validator.mjs:132-141` asserts that the scheduled production partition-maintenance workflow must exist — a validator enforcing a capability the Constitution rejects, exactly the failure mode Constitution line 98 forbids.


### Findings (24)


**[S0] auth_password_set_intents carries a live PUBLIC USING(true) WITH CHECK(true) policy - schema-level account-takeover primitive**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: database/supabase/migrations/078_password_set_intents.sql:52-55 creates CREATE POLICY "Service role manages auth password set intents" ON public.auth_password_set_intents USING (true) WITH CHECK (true) with NO TO clause. In PostgreSQL a policy without TO applies to PUBLIC. RLS is enabled on the table (078:41) but this PERMISSIVE policy passes for every role. No later migration drops or replaces it (grep across 073-197). No REVOKE exists for this table (163's REVOKE list at 163:202-215 covers 11 other tables only). The table's own header (078:1-3) states it is the authority that binds identity password creation to a freshly verified OTP.
- EXPECTED: Constitution R-3/SEC-R6: no public USING(true); tenant/identity tables must fail closed with an explicit TO clause and a real predicate. Migration 163's own header (163:1-4) documents this exact defect class as a P2 emergency.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/078_password_set_intents.sql:41; database/supabase/migrations/078_password_set_intents.sql:52; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:1; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:202
- RISK: An unauthenticated caller holding only the public Supabase anon key can INSERT a password-set intent row for an arbitrary user_id/email and then drive the identity-platform password-set flow to take over any merchant or customer account. Exploitability depends on anon/authenticated holding the table INSERT grant, which Supabase provisions by default and which no migration revokes for this table.
- RECOMMENDATION: Ship a migration that drops the policy and recreates it FOR ALL TO service_role USING (true) WITH CHECK (true), plus REVOKE ALL ON TABLE public.auth_password_set_intents FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, with a paired rollback. Verify live grants first with information_schema.role_table_grants.

**[S0] 18 further PUBLIC/anon true-predicate policies survive to final replay state, including gift_cards, collections, pages and audit_logs**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Replay-order analysis of all 126 migrations (CREATE POLICY minus later DROP POLICY IF EXISTS) yields 680 final-state policies, 110 with USING(true)/WITH CHECK(true). Beyond the ~90 correctly scoped TO service_role, these remain PUBLIC-effective and were never dropped or replaced: collection_products (000_baseline:26645), collections (:26652), gift_cards (:26666), onboarding_attempts (:26694), pages (:26708), store_surveys (:26743), ai_usage (:23960), plans (:24277), employee_roles (:24284), audit_logs (:24380), contact_submissions (:25808), leads (:25815), analytics_events (:25822), app_collections/app_collection_items/app_featured_placements/app_discovery_events (100:133,145,152,155), employee_permissions (107:121, FOR SELECT USING(true)); plus explicit anon/authenticated true policies on skill_endorsements (:24270, :25865) and smart_collections (:25924). Migration 163 remediated exactly 12 tables and no others. Because PERMISSIVE policies OR together, these defeat the app_tenant tenant-GUC policies that 164:63-73 later added to the same tables.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: RLS ENABLE+FORCE with per-transaction tenant GUC on all tenant tables; no blanket public predicate (R-3/SEC-R6).
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:26666; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:26652; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:26708; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:24380; database/supabase/migrations/100_app_discovery_foundation.sql:133; database/supabase/migrations/107_security_rls_hardening_and_payment_uniqueness.sql:121; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:1; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:67
- RISK: Cross-tenant read AND write of store pages, collections, onboarding attempts, surveys and gift cards, and INSERT into audit_logs (audit forgery), by any holder of the public anon key. Merchant A can read and modify Merchant B's catalog content, and any party can pollute the audit trail relied on for money reconciliation.
- RECOMMENDATION: Author a 198-series migration mirroring 163's pattern for every table listed here: DROP the untargeted policy, recreate FOR ALL TO service_role, add a tenant-GUC policy for authenticated where the table has site_id/store_id, and REVOKE writes from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated. Ship with a paired rollback and extend the RT red-team suite to cover each table.

**[S0] The app_tenant / tenant-GUC RLS mechanism has zero runtime callers - all gateway traffic runs on service_role**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Migrations 139/163/164 install the whole non-BYPASSRLS tenancy stack: current_site_id() reading app.site_id (139:8-14), CREATE ROLE app_tenant NOLOGIN NOBYPASSRLS (164:11), and ENABLE+FORCE+app_tenant policies over every site_id/store_id table (164:63-73). The only code path that activates it is withTenantTx (SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant, services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:29) via wrapper withResolvedTenantTx (lib/tenant-context.ts:67). grep -rn withResolvedTenantTx services apps packages --include='*.ts' returns exactly two hits: the definition and a string-matching governance test. Meanwhile getSupabaseAdmin - which returns createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient(createSupabaseServiceClient(env)) (lib/supabase.ts:13-15, doc-comment on :11 says 'bypasses RLS') - has 999 call sites across 115 non-test files, 96 of them route files. Tenant isolation in production therefore rests entirely on the JS Proxy in tenant/tenant-scoped.ts, which only requires that an .eq('site_id', ...)-shaped predicate was called before then.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: RLS ENABLE+FORCE with a per-transaction tenant GUC, gateway OFF blanket service_role.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:29; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13; services/api-gateway/src/tenant/tenant-scoped.ts:95; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:11
- RISK: Database-enforced tenant isolation is entirely bypassed at runtime. Any route that forgets a predicate, or any bug in the Proxy's heuristic scope detection (it accepts eq/in/filter/match/or/insert/upsert on a scope column and treats sites specially, accepting id/subdomain/store_name/custom_domain as scope), yields a cross-tenant query with full service_role authority. There is no second line of defence.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either migrate read/write paths to withResolvedTenantTx over Hyperdrive-pooled SQL so app_tenant + GUC becomes load-bearing, or take this to the ARB as an explicit documented constitutional deviation with a compensating control. Do not leave the gap silently while migrations 163/164 imply it is closed.
- CAPACITY: Pooled SQL under app_tenant caps concurrent connections (withTenantTx requests maxConnections 3 per isolate); switching 999 call sites to transactions changes the concurrency profile from stateless PostgREST to pooled connections and must be sized before the 1k-merchant step.

**[S1] Partition shadow mirror triggers are ENABLED on five live hot tables - Constitution requires the mirror disabled**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: 131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-140 creates five AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE ... FOR EACH ROW triggers on the LIVE tables worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics, outbox_events, platform_queue_jobs and webhook_deliveries, each executing mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow() (131:9-56), which per row selects from retention_policies and runs dynamic DELETE/INSERT INTO <shadow> SELECT ($1).*. The file's header (131:2-5) states the shadows 'must not sit inert'. A kill switch set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(boolean) exists (131:58) but a repo-wide grep finds NO caller in any migration, script or workflow. Migration 179's header (179:1-11) is direct evidence the mirror executes against live traffic: a missing shadow column raised SQLSTATE 42601 and 'broke EVERY outbox write - including theme release publishing via publish_theme_release()'.
- EXPECTED: LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:296 - 'the partition-shadow seam (kept, mirror disabled)'; :316 lists sharding/partitioning-now as rejected with 'the partition helper seam exists, disabled'.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:9; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:58; database/supabase/migrations/179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql:3; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:296
- RISK: Every write to the transactional outbox and the queue-job table is doubled and pays a per-row catalog lookup plus dynamic-SQL EXECUTE inside the caller's transaction. Any future column drift between a live table and its shadow aborts the live write - which has already happened once and broke all outbox writes, i.e. checkout side-effects and theme publishing. This is an availability-critical coupling on the money path.
- RECOMMENDATION: Ship a migration that calls set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false) (or drops the five triggers) with a paired rollback, restoring the constitutionally required disabled-seam state. If the mirror is genuinely wanted, it requires a section-14 constitutional amendment plus a column-drift guard so shadow mismatch can never abort a live write.
- CAPACITY: Doubles write volume on outbox_events, platform_queue_jobs, webhook_deliveries, worker_request_logs and runtime_telemetry_metrics, and adds a per-row SELECT plus dynamic EXECUTE to each. At 30k orders/month this is tolerable; it becomes a hard throughput ceiling on the outbox/queue hot path as merchant count scales toward 1k-10k.

**[S1] migration-safety-validator mandates the scheduled production partition-maintenance workflow - a validator enforcing a constitutionally rejected capability**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: scripts/validators/migration-safety-validator.mjs:132-141 asserts that .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml contains cron: "15 2 1 * *", production-migrations, PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING and --months-ahead=3, and :120-130 asserts the driver contains the ensure/detach calls and psql spawn. The workflow's schedule branch sets EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE=true (workflow :32) and targets the production DB secret (:33), so the gate makes the scheduled production DDL job mandatory to keep green.
- EXPECTED: LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:98 - 'Validators must enforce this Constitution, never the rejected end-state... It may not certify capabilities the Constitution forbids.' Combined with :296/:316 (partitioning-now rejected, seam disabled).
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/migration-safety-validator.mjs:132; scripts/validators/migration-safety-validator.mjs:120; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:3; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:32; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:98
- RISK: The 107-validator gate structurally prevents anyone from disabling or removing the partitioning machinery without first editing the validator - governance actively defending the rejected end-state. A green gate does not mean 'conforms to the Constitution' for this dimension.
- RECOMMENDATION: Retarget migration-safety-validator.mjs to assert the partition seam is DISABLED (mirror triggers absent/disabled, workflow not scheduled) rather than asserting the scheduled execution exists, under AGENT-R4 which explicitly permits retargeting a validator to enforce a Constitution rule.

**[S1] No rollback-pairing validator exists; 78 of 126 migrations are unpaired and 9 new violations landed after MIG-R3 was written**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Rollback convention is <forward-basename>.rollback.sql in database/supabase/rollback/; all 48 files map 1:1 by exact basename, zero orphans. Real pairing audit: 48 paired / 78 unpaired of 126. Unpaired at or above the MIG-R3 threshold: 138, 140, 157, 170, 175, 179, 182, 183, 184, 186, 187, 194 (12). AGENTS.md:157 names only 138/140/157 and promises 'a rollback-pairing validator gates merge for any migration >= 138'. No such validator exists: rollback-integrity-validator.mjs (14-57) governs Cloudflare Worker wrangler rollback --version-id lineage, not SQL, and only three validators touch the SQL rollback directory, each reading one hard-coded file (commission-bps-zero:52, session-security:101, provider-mode-badge:89). AGENTS.md was last committed 2026-06-11; migration 170 landed 2026-06-13 and 197 on 2026-08-15, so 9 new unpaired migrations accumulated with no gate.
- EXPECTED: AGENTS.md:157 MIG-R3 - every migration MUST ship a forward AND a paired rollback; a rollback-pairing validator gates merge for any migration >= 138.
- EVIDENCE: AGENTS.md:157; scripts/validators/rollback-integrity-validator.mjs:23; scripts/validators/commission-bps-zero-validator.mjs:52; scripts/validators/session-security-validator.mjs:101; scripts/validators/provider-mode-badge-validator.mjs:89
- RISK: A bad schema change at or after 138 (including 179, which itself fixed a production-breaking outbox failure) has no rehearsed DOWN path. Recovery falls back to PITR restore, which for a single-Postgres, single-region topology means whole-database rollback and data loss for every tenant, not a targeted revert.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a sequential-migration-style validator that, for every migration with prefix >= 138, requires database/supabase/rollback/<basename>.rollback.sql, with an explicit dated ARB-approved waiver list for forward-fix-only migrations. Author the 12 missing DOWNs or waive them individually.

**[S1] AGENTS.md MIG-R2 is stale and factually wrong on all three of its claims, and cites a validator that does not exist**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: AGENTS.md:156 states 'New migrations MUST be numbered sequentially from the current max (161) - i.e. the next migration is 162... MUST fix the existing duplicate prefix (129)... (migration-history-validator; [validator-gap] dup-prefix check)'. The same prose was already identified (AGENTS.md:7) as having previously encoded drift as law.

Consider a validator that reconciles the AGENTS.md max-migration claim against the filesystem so this cannot drift again.

**[S1] no-public-using-true-validator is blind to policies with no TO clause - the exact defect class migration 163 was written to fix**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: scripts/validators/no-public-using-true-validator.mjs:23-26 flags an offender only when hasTruePolicy && publicRole, where publicRole requires a literal TO PUBLIC|anon|authenticated in the policy text. A policy such as CREATE POLICY x ON public.gift_cards USING (true) WITH CHECK (true); has no TO clause, so publicRole is false and it is not flagged - even though PostgreSQL applies it to PUBLIC. Migration 163's header (163:1-4) states this exact fact: 'PostgreSQL policies without an explicit TO clause apply to PUBLIC, so the baseline service_role_* names were not actually service-role-only.' The validator also skips 000_baseline.sql entirely (:13-15, :19) and its final-state replay analysis (:79-124) is scoped to a hard-coded set of 12 sensitiveTables (:34-47).

- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/no-public-using-true-validator.mjs:23; scripts/validators/no-public-using-true-validator.mjs:13; scripts/validators/no-public-using-true-validator.mjs:34; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:1
- RISK: Every finding in the two S0 RLS items above is invisible to this gate. The gate is green while 19 PUBLIC-effective true-predicate policies are live, and any new migration can add more of the same shape without failing CI.
- RECOMMENDATION: Treat 'no TO clause' as PUBLIC in policyRoles/appliesBeyondServiceRole, drop the blanket 000_baseline exemption in favour of an explicit per-policy legacy allowlist that must shrink, and extend the final-state replay analysis from 12 tables to all tables.

**[S1] The RT-1..RT-9 tenant red-team suite - the only behavioral RLS proof - is not executed by any CI workflow**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: tests/executable/tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs implements real behavioral cases (RT-2 orders through RT-8 billing_history) by driving SET ROLE app_tenant + set_config('app.site_id') against a docker Postgres and comparing raw-REST authenticated counts to app_tenant counts (:52-65). It is invoked only by package.json:113 test:foundation:wave2. A grep for 'wave0|wave1|wave2|wave4|redteam|ephemeral-postgres' across .github/workflows/ returns nothing; ci.yml runs typecheck, lint, test:behavioral, test:unit:gateway, test:contracts, validate:governance and turbo build only. rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:25-32 asserts merely that the test file CONTAINS certain strings.
