- EVIDENCE: tests/executable/tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs:52; package.json:113; scripts/validators/rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:25; .github/workflows/ci.yml:121
- RISK: There is no VERIFIED-TEST evidence anywhere that tenant isolation actually holds against a real database. The gate's only tenancy signal is string matching, which is exactly the 'file presence as coverage' anti-pattern TEST-R10 forbids. The three S0 findings above would all have been caught by running this suite against the full 000..197 replay.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a CI job that boots the ephemeral Postgres, replays 000..197, and runs test:foundation:wave0/1/2/4 as build-failing. Extend RT cases to cover the unremediated PUBLIC-policy tables (auth_password_set_intents, gift_cards, collections, pages, audit_logs).

**[S1] Operational money columns are numeric rupees, not bigint paise, on orders, payment_transactions, order_items and gift cards**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The canonical ledger conforms (ledger_entries.amount_minor bigint, 139:132). The operational path does not: 109_orders_money_width.sql:11-17 widens orders.subtotal/shipping_cost/tax/discount/total/coupon_discount to numeric(12,2) and its header (:3) states 'Storefront order money remains numeric(_,2)'; payment_transactions.amount is numeric(10,2) (000_baseline:10311); order_items price/total/unit_price/discount_amount/tax_amount/taxable_amount/cgst_amount/sgst_amount/igst_amount are numeric (000_baseline:10022-10042); gift card initial_value/balance/balance_after are numeric (000_baseline:9371-9372, 9400-9401, 9448-9449) and the v1 gift-card RPCs declare numeric locals (173:30,62,79,208,263; 174:31-32); carts totals numeric (000_baseline:8697-8702).
- EXPECTED: Constitution: one canonical append-only double-entry ledger, bigint paise. AGENTS.md:159 MIG-R5 explicitly schedules the bigint cutover as Wave 5, after Wave-1 correctness wiring.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/109_orders_money_width.sql:3; database/supabase/migrations/109_orders_money_width.sql:11; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:10311; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:9371; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:132
- RISK: Two money representations coexist: numeric rupees on the order/payment/gift-card path and bigint paise in the ledger. Every reconciliation between them is a rounding boundary; numeric(10,2) on payment_transactions also caps a single transaction at Rs 99,999,999.99. This is the mutable-balance / dual-representation class that ANTI-R4 names a root-cause P0 pattern.
- RECOMMENDATION: Confirm with the ARB that this is the deliberate Wave-5 deferral rather than drift, and pin a dated cutover plan following MIG-R4 expand-backfill-verify-contract with a per-row variance=0 parity proof between the numeric columns and the ledger before contract.

**[S1] Roughly 30 *_minor money columns are 32-bit integer, overflowing at Rs 2.14 crore**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Columns named *_minor (paise) declared as integer rather than bigint include: payout_statements gross/platform_fee/tax/net_amount_minor, wallet_ledger_entries.amount_minor and commission_reconciliation_entries gross/commission/gst/net_payable_minor (119_mobile_money_ledgers.sql:16-19,52,107-112); cod_reconciliation_entries expected/collected/deposited/difference_amount_minor (140:26-29); developer payout reconciliation expected/ledger/delta_minor (134:23-25,70); app billing amount_minor/usage_unit_amount_minor (093:14,20; 095:15-16,73-74); spend controls monthly_cap_minor/alert_threshold_minor (096:7-8); discount_amount_minor (106:63); domain_orders amount_minor (090:165); risk detection amount_minor (124:218); and baseline partner payout ledger amount/partner_amount/platform_fee/partner_gst_minor (000_baseline:8265-8271, 10245-10249) plus 8469-8472, 12104, 12423.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: bigint paise for all money. AGENTS.md MIG-R6/PAY-R6 treat money tables as first-class.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/119_mobile_money_ledgers.sql:16; database/supabase/migrations/119_mobile_money_ledgers.sql:107; database/supabase/migrations/140_mobile_cod_reconciliation.sql:26; database/supabase/migrations/134_internal_os_developer_360_payout_reconciliation.sql:23; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8265
- RISK: A single payout statement, COD reconciliation batch or commission settlement above Rs 2,14,74,836.47 raises an integer-overflow error, aborting the transaction. At the 10,000-merchant envelope, monthly aggregate settlement rows will reach this ceiling.
- RECOMMENDATION: Widen all *_minor columns to bigint in one additive migration with a paired rollback (integer to bigint is a widening ALTER; verify lock/rewrite behaviour on the target PostgreSQL version first). Add a validator that rejects any new `*_minor integer` declaration.
- CAPACITY: Directly caps per-row settlement/payout magnitude; binds before the 10k-merchant step and possibly before 1k depending on merchant GMV distribution.

**[S1] KV-backed rate limiting is a non-atomic read-modify-write on an eventually-consistent store, so limits are per-colo not global**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:320-324 (fixed window) and :618-623 (query-cost leaky bucket) both do await cache.get(key), compute, then await cache.put(key, ..., { expirationTtl }) with no compare-and-set. Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent across colos. Production enables this path explicitly: wrangler.toml env.production.vars sets RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK = "on" with an in-file founder note dated 2026-08-01 stating Upstash Redis is the intended backend, that no UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN is provisioned, and that 'Cloudflare KV has a daily write quota and each limited request costs one KV write, so sustained high volume can exhaust it. REPLACE WITH UPSTASH before scale.' The fixed-window limiter additionally auto-enables KV in production regardless of the flag (rateLimit.ts:860 isProduction || kvRateLimitFallbackEnabled(env)).
- EXPECTED: Rate limits that gate /v1 data routes and registration/login should be globally accurate and durable under concurrency.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:320; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:618; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:860; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:281
- RISK: Effective request budget is multiplied by the number of Cloudflare colos serving the tenant and by concurrent-request interleaving within a colo, so a documented limit of N is not enforced as N. Separately, one KV write per limited request means the KV daily write quota becomes a hard availability ceiling; on exhaustion the fixed-window limiter returns unavailable (fail-closed, :330-341) and /v1 data routes return 503.
- RECOMMENDATION: Provision the intended Upstash Redis backend (the atomic Lua path is already implemented at rateLimit.ts:641+) before increasing merchant count, or move the limiter to a Durable Object - the RUNTIME_COORDINATOR DO is already bound and unused, and DO single-threaded storage gives exact counting without the KV write quota.
- CAPACITY: Primary throughput/availability constraint on all /v1 data routes: one KV write per limited request against a daily quota, plus per-colo rather than global limits. Binds well before the 10k-merchant step.

**[S2] D1 RUNTIME_DB is bound in staging and production with a lineage schema and has zero readers or writers in the repo**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml binds RUNTIME_DB to letbuyy-staging-d1 (d1e49c22-...) and letbuyy-production-d1 (76d197a5-...), both with migrations_dir ../../database/cloudflare/d1/migrations. The single D1 migration (0001_provider_execution_foundation.sql) creates provider_execution_evidence, d1_migration_lineage, outbox_restoration_lineage and rollback_lineage - deployment/provider evidence and rollback lineage, plus an outbox restoration cursor/checksum table. A repo-wide grep for those four table names returns hits ONLY in scripts/validators/d1-governance-validator.mjs:18-21. The only runtime reference to the binding is `runtime_db: Boolean(c.env.RUNTIME_DB)` in a diagnostics response (routes/internal-console.ts:808). No env.RUNTIME_DB.prepare/exec/batch call exists.
- EXPECTED: Constitution R-15: ONE PostgreSQL source of truth. Any additional datastore must be non-authoritative operational state with a live owner.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:196; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:325; database/cloudflare/d1/migrations/0001_provider_execution_foundation.sql:1; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-console.ts:808; scripts/validators/d1-governance-validator.mjs:16
- RISK: Not a second data authority today - nothing writes it, so it cannot diverge from Postgres. It is however a provisioned, billed, unmonitored SQL database whose declared purpose (outbox_restoration_lineage carries outbox_cursor, source_checksum, restored_checksum, divergence_detected) is precisely the shape that WOULD become a second authority over outbox state if wired. The d1-governance-validator certifies binding hygiene, which reads as endorsement of a live capability that does not exist.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either delete the D1 bindings, the migration and the validator as config drift, or take a deliberate ARB decision to wire the lineage writers and constrain outbox_restoration_lineage to write-once audit rows that can never be read back as authority. Do not leave it half-provisioned.

**[S2] Durable Object RUNTIME_COORDINATOR is bound in both environments, stores two strings, and is never invoked**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: wrangler.toml declares [[migrations]] tag = "v1-runtime-coordinator" new_sqlite_classes = ["LetbuyyRuntimeDurableObject"] and binds RUNTIME_COORDINATOR in env.staging and env.production. services/api-gateway/src/durable/runtime-coordinator.ts persists exactly two keys: 'initialized' (:14) and 'checkpoint' (:31), exposed via GET /health and POST /checkpoint (:22,:26). The class is exported (index.ts:99) and typed (index.ts:208), and the only other reference is `runtime_coordinator: Boolean(c.env.RUNTIME_COORDINATOR)` in diagnostics (internal-console.ts:810). No idFromName/get/fetch dispatch exists. config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json declares durableObjects.checkpointPolicy = 'snapshot-and-outbox-cursor-required-before-restoration' - the code stores no outbox cursor and no snapshot.
- EXPECTED: Constitution R-15 one source of truth; topology manifests must not claim capabilities the code lacks (R-23 anti-fabrication).
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/durable/runtime-coordinator.ts:14; services/api-gateway/src/durable/runtime-coordinator.ts:31; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:99; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-console.ts:810; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:1
- RISK: Not a second data authority - it holds no business state. It is unused coordination scaffolding with a SQLite storage backend declared, and the topology manifest overstates its checkpoint semantics, so anyone reading the manifest during an incident would believe a restoration checkpoint exists when none is written.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either delete the DO binding, class and migration tag, or repurpose it as the atomic rate-limit counter (which would simultaneously fix the KV rate-limiter accuracy and write-quota findings). Correct cloudflare-topology.json's checkpointPolicy to match what the code actually persists.
- CAPACITY: An unused single-threaded DO is available capacity headroom: repurposing it for rate limiting would remove the KV write-quota ceiling on /v1 routes.

**[S2] 164 grants app_tenant DML on all FUTURE tables via ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES without installing a FORCE/policy for them**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: 164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:28-31 runs ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLES TO app_tenant (and sequence usage). The ENABLE+FORCE+policy installation, however, is a one-shot catalog loop over tables existing at replay time of 164 (:39-74). Any table created after 164 therefore inherits app_tenant DML grants by default but gets no app_tenant policy unless its own migration adds one. Of the 13 tables created in migrations 165+, four (metric_rollups, mrr_arr_rollups, cohort_snapshots, funnel_steps - 169:153-156) have ENABLE but no FORCE.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: RLS ENABLE+FORCE on every tenant table, and grants must not outrun policies.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:28; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:39; database/supabase/migrations/169_internal_os_executive_analytics_rollups.sql:153
- RISK: If a future table is created with app_tenant grants and RLS is not enabled on it, app_tenant reads and writes every row unfiltered. If RLS is enabled but no app_tenant policy is added, app_tenant is denied entirely - a silent runtime failure. Either way the outcome is determined by what a future migration author remembers, not by a gate (and the FORCE ratchet that should catch it is dead code, see the rls-force-coverage finding).
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a post-164 migration that re-runs the catalog loop, and make it a repeatable maintenance function invoked at the end of every migration that creates tables; or narrow the ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES so grants are only issued alongside a policy. Add FORCE to the four 169 rollup tables.

**[S2] no-blanket-service-role-validator exempts services/ and packages/, and proves the app_tenant helper only by string match**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:33 restricts the service-role offender scan to entry.path.startsWith('apps/'), so services/api-gateway (999 getSupabaseAdmin call sites) and packages/ are never scanned. Lines 16-21 assert only that services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts CONTAINS the strings 'SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant', 'tenantGucSetLocalStatement' and the refusal message - it never checks that anything calls the helper. Line 13 accepts the tenant-guard Proxy wrapper as the sanctioned control for service-role clients.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: gateway OFF blanket service_role. A validator named for that rule should measure gateway service-role usage.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:33; scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:16; scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:13; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13
- RISK: The validator's green result is compatible with the exact state the Constitution forbids: 100% of gateway data access on service_role and the app_tenant path with zero callers. Governance reports conformance on a dimension it does not measure.
- RECOMMENDATION: Extend the scan to services/ with an explicit shrinking allowlist, and add an assertion that withResolvedTenantTx has at least N production callers (ratcheting upward) so the app_tenant path is proven load-bearing rather than merely present.

**[S2] Storefront cache-version KV keys are written with no TTL and read failure silently degrades to version '0'**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:89 writes cache.put(storefrontCacheVersionKey(handle), version) with no expirationTtl, so storefront-cache-version:<handle> keys persist indefinitely, one per store handle ever seen. storefrontCacheVersion (:74-81) catches any read error and returns '0'. The version string is embedded in the HTTP cache key (:100), and response bodies live in caches.default with max-age 60 / stale-while-revalidate 300 (:12-13,:24-26).
- EXPECTED: Cache state is non-authoritative operational state; invalidation must be correct and the key space bounded.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:89; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:75; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:100
- RISK: On a transient KV read failure the gateway falls back to version '0', which is the key generation used before the store's first invalidation - so a KV blip serves the oldest cached generation rather than bypassing the cache. Key space grows monotonically with store count (bounded near 10k, so growth itself is minor). Correctly tenant-scoped by store handle; not a data-authority risk.
- RECOMMENDATION: On KV read failure, bypass the cache (return a unique sentinel version) rather than falling back to '0'. Optionally set a long expirationTtl on version keys with a write-through refresh.
- CAPACITY: Cache-version reads add a KV read to storefront request paths; the '0' fallback converts a KV degradation into stale-content serving rather than into origin load - safer for capacity, wrong for correctness.

**[S2] R2 object keys are not tenant-prefixed; shipping-label objects are keyed only by label id**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Uploads use ${folder}/${auth.user.id}/${Date.now()}-${uuid}.${ext} (routes/upload.ts:168) - owner-scoped, with store_id present only in customMetadata (:196-199). Shipping labels use a flat shipping-labels/${labelId}.pdf (routes/shipping-labels.ts:224). Retrieval fetches the DB row by id and only then calls resolveTenantContextForOwnedSite(c, row.site_id) before reading the object (:379-384), so tenancy is enforced by the application check, not by the key space. Media objects are additionally served publicly via ASSET_PUBLIC_BASE_URL (cdn.letbuyy.com), relying on the 122-bit UUID for unguessability (:164-168 comment). No R2 lifecycle or expiry configuration exists in the repo.
- EXPECTED: Tenant-scoped key spaces so a bucket-level mistake cannot cross tenants, plus a defined retention/lifecycle policy.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/upload.ts:168; services/api-gateway/src/routes/shipping-labels.ts:224; services/api-gateway/src/routes/shipping-labels.ts:379
- RISK: R2 holds derived artifacts (labels, exports, media), not business truth, so this is not a second data authority. But a flat key space means any future code path that lists or serves by key without the DB ownership check leaks cross-tenant PDFs containing customer names and addresses. Absent lifecycle rules, objects orphaned by row deletion persist indefinitely, which is both a cost and a data-retention/DPDP exposure.
- RECOMMENDATION: Prefix all R2 keys with site_id (e.g. {site_id}/shipping-labels/{labelId}.pdf) so the key space itself is tenant-partitioned, and define an R2 lifecycle policy plus a reconciliation job for orphaned objects.

**[S2] Default (no --env) wrangler config lacks KV, D1, DO and Hyperdrive bindings that both named environments have**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml's top-level section declares only an R2 bucket (letbuyy-media-staging), the queue producers, observability and the [[migrations]] DO class tag - no kv_namespaces, no d1_databases, no durable_objects.bindings, no hyperdrive. Both [env.staging] and [env.production] declare all four. config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json sets wrangler.mustUseExplicitEnv = true.
- EXPECTED: Deploy configuration should be unambiguous; a deploy without --env should be impossible or equivalent to a named environment.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:1; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:88; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:190; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:1
- RISK: A wrangler deploy without --env produces a worker named letbuyy-api with no CACHE binding - the fixed-window rate limiter's Boolean(env.CACHE) guard (rateLimit.ts:860) then falls to the in-memory store, so limits reset per isolate, and the query-cost limiter has no backend at all. The mustUseExplicitEnv contract is a manifest assertion, not a mechanism.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either remove the top-level bindings entirely so an env-less deploy fails loudly, or mirror the staging bindings there. Verify wrangler-env-validator.mjs actually blocks env-less deploy paths in the release workflows.
- CAPACITY: An env-less deploy silently degrades rate limiting to per-isolate memory, removing the platform's only request-budget control.

**[S3] Four analytics rollup tables created after migration 164 have ENABLE but not FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: 169_internal_os_executive_analytics_rollups.sql:153-156 enables RLS on metric_rollups, mrr_arr_rollups, cohort_snapshots and funnel_steps and adds service_role-all plus super_admin-read policies (:158-193), but issues no FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY. These tables carry no site_id/store_id column, so 164's catalog loop would not have covered them even had they existed at that point, and they were created after 164 in any case. All other post-164 tables (176, 184, 185, 194, 195) do declare FORCE.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: RLS ENABLE+FORCE. Without FORCE, the table owner role bypasses RLS entirely.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/169_internal_os_executive_analytics_rollups.sql:153; database/supabase/migrations/169_internal_os_executive_analytics_rollups.sql:158; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:63
- RISK: Low direct exposure - these are platform-global executive metrics with service_role and super-admin-only policies, and they hold no per-tenant rows. The defect is that they demonstrate the FORCE ratchet is not operating (see the rls-force-coverage finding), so the next table to slip through may be tenant-scoped.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY for the four tables in the next migration with a paired rollback, and fix the ratchet so this class cannot recur.

- CURRENT: Prefixes present are {000} plus {073..197}, contiguous above 072, with no duplicates anywhere (ls database/supabase/migrations/ | sed 's/_.*//' | sort -n | uniq -d returns empty). 000_baseline.sql (the pg_dump-shaped baseline, about 27k lines) subsumes what were migrations 001-072. sequential-migration-validator.mjs encodes exactly this: KNOWN_GAP_END = 72 with the comment 'migrations 001-072 are absent by design' (:5-7), a duplicate check over all prefixes (:44-49), a contiguity check restricted to nums > 72 (:52-61), and a fail on untracked migration files (:17-26). The validator passes for the right reason. AGENTS.md's claimed duplicate 129 does not exist and its claimed enforcing validator does not exist.

- RISK: None from the sequence itself. Recording this explicitly so the ARB does not spend a cycle chasing the phantom duplicate-129 defect that current governance prose asserts.

**[INFO] Canonical ledger append-only enforcement fully conforms: trigger AND REVOKE, writer_module CHECK, 8 accounts, derived balance**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: ledger_accounts.account_code CHECK enumerates exactly eight codes - pa_clearing, merchant_payable, platform_revenue, gst_payable, cod_receivable, reserve, vendor_payable, refund_clearing (139:79-88). ledger_transactions.writer_module has NOT NULL DEFAULT 'settlement' CHECK (writer_module = 'settlement') (139:113), enforcing the single-writer rule at the schema level. Append-only is enforced BOTH ways as PAY-R6/MIG-R6 require: BEFORE UPDATE and BEFORE DELETE reject triggers on ledger_entries and ledger_transactions (139:358-376) AND REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON public.ledger_transactions/ledger_entries FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role, app_tenant (165:463-464). ledger_entries.amount_minor is bigint with CHECK > 0 (139:132); FKs to transactions and accounts are ON DELETE RESTRICT (139:127,129); a DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED constraint trigger enforces debit=credit per transaction (139:378-382, 336-338); reversal_of self-FK supports correction by reversing entry (139:114). Balance is derived by function ledger_account_balance_minor (160:173), used at 160:224 and 160:398, with ledger_balance_breaks() as a reconciliation probe (139:344-356). The five ledger-family tables get ENABLE+FORCE+site_id GUC policy (139:384-406).
- EXPECTED: Constitution: one canonical append-only double-entry ledger, bigint paise, derived balances, one writer.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:79; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:113; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:358; database/supabase/migrations/165_d5_canonical_money_paths.sql:463; database/supabase/migrations/160_foundation_money_ledger_authority.sql:173
- RISK: None identified at the schema layer for the canonical ledger itself. Recording as a positive control so the ARB can distinguish this conforming core from the non-conforming operational money path (numeric rupees on orders/payments/gift cards) reported separately.
- RECOMMENDATION: Preserve as-is. When the Wave-5 bigint cutover lands, hold this schema as the reference shape.

**[INFO] Scheduled partition maintenance executes DDL against production monthly, but only on shadow parents - no live table is partitioned**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml runs cron: "15 2 1 * *" (:3-4) in the production-migrations environment (:30) with EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE forced true on the schedule branch (:32) and SUPABASE_DB_URL bound to PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING (:33), invoking the driver with --execute --months-ahead=3 (:46-53). The driver runs ensure_next_hot_table_partitions(3) and detach_expired_hot_table_partitions() via psql (:29-40, :80-90). Those functions operate exclusively on retention_policies.shadow_table: partition creation is CREATE TABLE public.<shadow>_YYYY_MM PARTITION OF public.<shadow> (079:178-184) and detachment is ALTER TABLE public.<shadow> DETACH PARTITION (079:293-297). No migration performs the live rename-swap; 079's header states it 'is intentionally not performed in a replayable migration' (079:2) and the shadow table comment repeats that live cutover requires an approved maintenance-window swap (079:529). backfill_hot_table_partition_shadow (079:319-372) has no caller.
- EXPECTED: LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:296/:316 - partition-shadow seam kept, disabled; no partitioning-now on live tables.
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:3; scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:29; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:2; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:178; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:293
- RISK: The DDL leg of the partitioning question is NOT a constitutional breach: live hot tables remain unpartitioned and unrenamed, and the workflow only maintains shadow parents. The breach is the separate mirror-trigger finding, which is what actually feeds those shadows. Recorded separately so the ARB does not conflate the two and disable the wrong thing.
- RECOMMENDATION: When disabling the mirror, also idle this workflow's schedule (workflow_dispatch only) so the seam is fully dormant as section 296 requires; retarget migration-safety-validator.mjs:132-141 accordingly.
- CAPACITY: A monthly job creating up to 4 empty partitions per policy across 5 policies is negligible load; the capacity cost of this seam is entirely in the per-row mirror trigger, not here.

### Unknowns
- Whether the production/staging Postgres actually has the anon and authenticated roles holding SELECT/INSERT grants on auth_password_set_intents, gift_cards, collections, pages, onboarding_attempts, store_surveys and the app_* discovery tables. Supabase's platform bootstrap grants these by default and no migration REVOKEs them for these tables (163 only REVOKEd for 11 other tables), but the grant itself is not in the repo. Settle with: SELECT grantee, privilege_type FROM information_schema.role_table_grants WHERE table_name IN (...) AND grantee IN ('anon','authenticated');
- Whether the deployed database state actually equals the replay of migrations 000..197. There is no schema-diff artifact, no `supabase db diff` output, and no applied-migrations ledger in the repo. Settle with a read-only SELECT * FROM supabase_migrations.schema_migrations ORDER BY version; plus pg_dump --schema-only diffed against a clean replay.
- Whether the partition shadow mirror is currently enabled in the production database. The repo state says ENABLED (131:112-140, no disable caller), but an out-of-band operator could have run set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false). Settle with SELECT tgname, tgenabled FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname LIKE 'mirror_%_partition_shadow';
- Whether the monthly partition-maintenance workflow has ever actually run against production and how many shadow partitions exist. Settle with the GitHub Actions run history for hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml plus SELECT * FROM public.partition_lifecycle_partitions;
- Actual row volume and write rate on outbox_events / platform_queue_jobs / webhook_deliveries, needed to size the mirror trigger's write-amplification cost against the 30k orders/month envelope. Requires live pg_stat_user_tables or Analytics Engine access.
- Whether the D1 databases letbuyy-staging-d1 (d1e49c22-...) and letbuyy-production-d1 (76d197a5-...) actually exist, have had 0001_provider_execution_foundation.sql applied, and contain any rows. No code writes them, but an out-of-band script or manual wrangler d1 execute could have. Requires an operator-run read-only wrangler d1 query.
- Whether the Durable Object namespace has any persisted state in production. The class is bound and declared with new_sqlite_classes but never invoked from code; an external caller via a route not present in this SHA cannot be excluded from repo evidence alone.
- Whether the KV namespace ids 90551a8f... and ee82af47... correspond to the topology-declared names letbuyy-cache-staging / letbuyy-cache-production. wrangler.toml binds by id only. Settle with an operator-run `wrangler kv namespace list`.
- The real-world accuracy of the KV-backed rate limiter under concurrency, and whether the Cloudflare KV daily write quota is being approached. The non-atomic get-then-put and eventual consistency are proven from source; the observed error rate is not. Requires Cloudflare Analytics/KV metrics.
- Whether the 9 newly-unpaired migrations (170,175,179,182,183,184,186,187,194) were consciously exempted by a human or simply slipped past an absent validator. No ADR, waiver file, or exemption list referencing them was found. Requires founder/ARB confirmation.
- Whether the 12 unpaired migrations >=138 are individually reversible in principle (some, e.g. 179's ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS, are trivially reversible; others such as 175's RPC search_path fix may be forward-fix-only by design). Deciding which need a DOWN versus a documented forward-fix-only waiver is an ARB judgement.
- Whether `gift_cards` (baseline, numeric balance, PUBLIC true policy) is still a live serving table or dead legacy superseded by gift_card_accounts/gift_card_ledger_entries. Migration 173's header remarks the redemption path never reads public.gift_cards, which suggests legacy, but no drop or deprecation marker exists. Settle with pg_stat_user_tables seq/idx scan counts.
- Whether any of the roughly 30 int32 *_minor columns are on a path that can legitimately exceed Rs 2.14 crore in a single row (e.g. payout_statements.gross_amount_minor for a large merchant's monthly settlement). Needs a business volume ceiling decision from the founder.
- Whether `withResolvedTenantTx` is intended to become the primary data path (making the current 999 service_role call sites a migration backlog) or whether the application-layer tenant guard proxy is the accepted permanent control. This is a constitutional interpretation question for the ARB.
- Whether pooled SQL (Hyperdrive) is actually reachable from the Worker in production - withTenantTx throws TenantTransactionUnavailableError when getPooledSql returns null, so even if routes were switched over, the app_tenant path may fail closed. Requires a live connectivity probe.
- Whether the ephemeral-Postgres foundation suites (package.json test:foundation:wave0/1/2/4) are executed by any CI system outside .github/workflows (e.g. a self-hosted runner or a pre-merge hook). Repo evidence shows no workflow reference.
- The complete final-state policy picture for tables whose policies are created inside dynamic DO/EXECUTE blocks with computed policy names (e.g. 164's md5-suffixed app_tenant_guc_* names). Static analysis of the migration text cannot fully resolve these; a live SELECT * FROM pg_policies WHERE schemaname='public'; is required for an authoritative census.
- Whether R2 objects orphaned by row deletion (shipping labels, exports, uploads) are ever reclaimed. No lifecycle rule, no delete-on-cascade hook, and no reconciliation job was found; the storage-cost and data-retention consequence needs an operator/legal decision.

### Narrative summary
All 16 workflows in .github/workflows/ were read at 5af69e0c. Exactly one workflow deploys production on push (production-deploy.yml, push:main + dispatch), gated by branch-lag-guard -> governance-gate -> release-preflight -> migration-preflight -> staging-e2e -> migrate-production -> deploy-workers-production -> deploy-apps-production -> release-required-jobs-succeeded -> smoke-test-production. Its deploy matrix is exactly 9 targets: 4 Cloudflare Workers (api-gateway, webhook-service, queue-workers, mobile-bff) and 5 Vercel Next apps (marketing-site, identity-platform, merchant-control-plane, storefront-runtime, platform-console) = the constitutional 8 canonical runtimes plus platform-console. No frozen app appears in production-deploy.yml, deploy-staging.yml, or staging-deploy.yml, and deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs enforces that. HOWEVER, the second production deploy path — production-rollout.yml (workflow_dispatch, environment=production, execute=true) -> infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs — deploys apps/app-marketplace, apps/theme-marketplace and apps/platform-operations-portal with `vercel deploy --prod` (lines 125,126,129) and omits services/mobile-bff from its worker loop (lines 89-93). That path is not covered by deploy-matrix-topology-validator, which reads only three workflow files. All four wrangler.toml files declare a top-level `name` identical to their `[env.production].name`, so a bare `wrangler deploy` overwrites the production Worker with the staging-flavoured default block; for api-gateway that block sets FEATURE_GRAPHQL/FEATURE_EXTENSIONS/FEATURE_LIQUID="true", ENVIRONMENT="staging", staging queues/R2, and binds no KV, D1, Hyperdrive, Durable Object, service binding or route. docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:24-29 records that this actually happened in production on 2026-08-01 (api.letbuyy.com NXDOMAIN). I assumed Cloudflare's documented non-inheritable-key semantics for [vars] (per-env vars REPLACE, never merge with top-level); the repo encodes the same assumption at cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs:126 ("must explicitly declare non-inheritable vars") and cloudflare-topology.json:16 ("mustUseExplicitEnv": true). The effective production flag set on api-gateway disables GraphQL, extensions and Liquid and enables RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK="on"; staging enables all three feature flags and has no RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK. The rate-limit implementation (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts) selects Upstash Redis first, then Cloudflare KV, then in-memory; the query-cost limiter (mounted globally) has no `isProduction ||` clause so it fails CLOSED with 503 unless KV is explicitly enabled. The "Upstash not provisioned" claim is supported only by a wrangler.toml comment and a runbook — and is directly contradicted by production-deploy.yml:76-77,111-112,327-328, which makes PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN hard release-blocking preconditions and then pushes them as Worker secrets; either the runbook is stale or every push-to-main release fails at release-preflight. Production Razorpay secrets are never provisioned by any production deploy path and deployment-safety-validator.mjs:140-155 actively FORBIDS provisioning them there, while config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:135-137 lists them as required Worker secrets and services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:900,1062 fails closed without them. The DEPLOY-R1 branch-lag guard is genuinely enforced (`git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/stepup/main` == 0, scripts/validators/branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:84-88) and at this SHA main, stepup/main, origin/main and origin/stepup/main are all 5af69e0c, so lag is 0. No committed secret values were found. Three staging-named workflows exist with genuinely divergent, partly overlapping responsibilities and no machine gate collapsing them, so the ART-03/DEPLOY-R2 "collapse the duplicate staging workflow to one" obligation is OPEN. Hot-table partitioning (migrations 079-083), a live partition shadow-mirror trigger (migration 131) and a monthly self-executing partition maintenance workflow are all present despite the constitutional prohibition on partitioning-now.


### Findings (19)


**[S1] Default wrangler block shares the production Worker name and re-enables forbidden capability flags**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: All four wrangler.toml files set a top-level `name` identical to `[env.production].name` (api-gateway L1 vs L219 both "letbuyy-api"; queue-workers L1/L224; webhook-service L1/L69; mobile-bff L1/L87). For api-gateway the default block sets FEATURE_GRAPHQL/FEATURE_EXTENSIONS/FEATURE_LIQUID="true" (L39-41), ENVIRONMENT="staging" (L32), staging queues and cdn-staging, and declares NO kv_namespaces, NO d1_databases, NO hyperdrive, NO durable_objects binding, NO services binding, NO analytics dataset and NO routes. Because [vars] is a non-inheritable key, `wrangler deploy` with no --env replaces the live production Worker's vars with that set. No validator asserts the default name differs from production; cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs only checks the [env.*] blocks (L125). infrastructure/scripts/cloudflare/wrangler-exec.mjs:34 guards this, but every GitHub Actions step calls `pnpm exec wrangler deploy` directly and bypasses the wrapper.
- EXPECTED: The default/top-level block must not be able to overwrite the production Worker. Either the top-level name must be distinct (or absent), or the wrangler config must not carry a staging-flavoured default at all. Constitution forbids a Wasm/extension runtime; FEATURE_EXTENSIONS must never be "true" on the letbuyy-api Worker.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:1; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:219; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:39; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:40; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:32; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:1; services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:1; services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml:1; docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:24; infrastructure/scripts/cloudflare/wrangler-exec.mjs:34
- RISK: One `wrangler deploy` without --env from services/api-gateway silently replaces api.letbuyy.com's Worker: routes vanish (documented NXDOMAIN incident), KV/D1/Hyperdrive/Durable Object bindings are dropped, ENVIRONMENT flips to "staging" so isProductionRuntime() returns false and rate limiting degrades to a per-isolate in-memory counter, and the extensions/GraphQL surfaces flip on. docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:24-29 records this exact failure occurring in production on 2026-08-01.
- RECOMMENDATION: Rename the top-level block to a non-production name (e.g. "letbuyy-api-default") or delete the default [vars]/bindings entirely, and add a validator assertion that top-level `name` != `[env.production].name` for every services/*/wrangler.toml. Also route CI worker deploys through infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs instead of bare `pnpm exec wrangler deploy`.
- CAPACITY: A default-env deploy removes the CACHE KV binding, which is the only production rate-limit backend the config comment claims is active; the gateway then either fails closed on every /v1 data route or limits per-isolate, both of which destroy throughput predictability.

All three have a package.json (the existsSync guard at L133 does not skip them). This script is invoked with --execute by production-rollout.yml:174 for target=all|apps. scripts/validators/deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs reads only .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml, deploy-staging.yml and staging-deploy.yml (L11-15) so this path is not covered; config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:326 likewise scopes its frozen_app_deploy_target rule to production-deploy.yml alone.

**[S1] Production Razorpay secrets are provisioned by no deploy path and are explicitly forbidden in the one that could**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:135-141 lists RAZORPAY_KEY_ID, RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET, RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET and RAZORPAY_ROUTE_* under workers.requiredSecrets. production-deploy.yml sets 19 api-gateway secrets (L312-330) and none of them is a Razorpay key. scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:140-155 actively asserts that production-deploy.yml must NOT contain `wrangler secret put RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET --env production` or the corresponding preflight checks ("A4 production go-live atom must not require or provision provider-money secret"). staging-deploy.yml:96-98 does set them for staging. services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:900 and :1062 return early when c.env.RAZORPAY_KEY_ID or c.env.RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET is absent.
- EXPECTED: Either the secret matrix should not list platform Razorpay keys as required Worker secrets, or a governed production path must provision them. The two artifacts currently state opposite requirements and the runtime depends on the values.
- EVIDENCE: config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:135; config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:136; config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:137; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:312; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:96; scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:140; scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:149; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:900; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:1062
- RISK: If the production Worker's Razorpay secrets were ever set only by an out-of-band manual `wrangler secret put`, nothing in the repository records, verifies or rotates them; if they were never set, every Razorpay order-creation route on api.letbuyy.com fails closed and only COD works. Neither state is distinguishable from the repository, and a Worker redeploy cannot restore them.
- RECOMMENDATION: Decide, at ARB level, whether platform-level Razorpay credentials are in scope for the go-live atom. Then make the secret matrix and deployment-safety-validator agree, and record the human-owned provisioning step in the release runbook with an explicit verification command.

**[S1] The "Upstash not provisioned" premise is contradicted by the production deploy workflow's own hard preconditions**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=UNKNOWN  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:250-258 states that no UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN is provisioned and that RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK="on" is a temporary founder-approved workaround; docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:81-82 states "Neither production nor staging currently has these secrets, and no Upstash credential exists in the 27 configured GitHub Actions secrets." But .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:76-77 and :111-112 make PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL and PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN hard release-blocking preconditions (release-preflight exits 1 if either is empty), and :327-328 then `wrangler secret put`s both onto the production Worker. rateLimit.ts:850 and :912 short-circuit to Redis whenever both are present, which makes RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK a no-op.
- EXPECTED: One consistent account of the production rate-limit backend. Either Upstash is provisioned (in which case the KV fallback flag and its comment are dead and must be removed per the runbook's own instruction at line 79) or it is not (in which case production-deploy.yml can never complete release-preflight and no push-to-main release has ever run).
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:250; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:259; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:76; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:111; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:327; docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:81; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:850; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:912
- RISK: Nobody can tell from the repository which rate-limit backend production actually runs. If the runbook is current, every push to main dies at release-preflight and production is deployed only by manual wrangler invocations outside the gated pipeline — which is exactly the condition that produced the 2026-08-01 routeless-Worker incident. If the runbook is stale, production is running on Redis with a misleading comment that steers operators to the wrong diagnosis.
- RECOMMENDATION: Have a human read the GitHub Actions secret list and the production Worker's secret list, then reconcile: delete or correct the wrangler.toml comment and the runbook, and remove RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK if Redis is live. Record the answer as a dated evidence line, not a comment.
- CAPACITY: Which backend is live determines whether the gateway can sustain >1 limited request/second on a hot key. KV cannot; Redis can. This is the binding constraint on /v1 throughput at 100 merchants and above.

**[S1] Cloudflare KV rate-limit fallback is non-atomic, write-quota bound, and collapses server-side traffic onto one key**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: checkKvRateLimit (rateLimit.ts:320-325) and checkKvQueryCostLimit (:618-623) both perform a read-then-write (cache.get followed by cache.put) with no atomic primitive. Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent and rate-limits writes per key, so concurrent requests read the same counter and one write wins. On a KV error the query-cost path sets queryCostKvUnavailableUntil (:627, 60s or 10min for quota errors) and returns unavailable, which the middleware turns into 503 in production (:1078-1088) rather than 429. The query-cost limiter is mounted globally on /v1 (index.ts:681-688) and keys unauthenticated traffic by CF-Connecting-IP (:1029), so server-side calls from Vercel collapse to a single egress IP and therefore a single KV key. The production KV binding is the shared CACHE namespace (wrangler.toml:313-316), not the dedicated letbuyy-rate-limit-production namespace declared in config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:63-66.
- EXPECTED: A rate-limit backend with atomic increment and no per-key write ceiling (the Upstash Redis path already implemented at rateLimit.ts:170-187 and :644-680 uses EVAL with INCR/SET).
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:320; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:618; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:627; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:1078; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:1029; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:681; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:313; config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:63; docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:56
- RISK: Under concurrency the limiter under-counts (two simultaneous requests both read N and both write N+1), and under sustained load the hot key trips the write ceiling and returns 503 to legitimate traffic. The runbook records measured production behaviour on 2026-08-01: a 120-request single-IP burst produced 100x200 and 20x503, and 10 requests one second apart produced 200 503 503 503 503 200 503 200 200 200.
- RECOMMENDATION: Provision Upstash Redis and set the two Worker secrets, then remove RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK per the runbook. Independently, stop server-side callers (marketing-site /api/founding-pricing) from sharing one rate-limit key — forward the visitor IP or use an authenticated service identity so Vercel egress does not become a single hot key.
- CAPACITY: This is the hardest throughput ceiling in the stack: one KV write per limited request against a per-key write-rate limit means the /v1 surface cannot exceed roughly one request per second per rate-limit key regardless of Worker or Postgres capacity.

**[S1] Hot-table partitioning is implemented, shadow-mirrored live, and auto-maintained on a monthly schedule**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=constitutional-amendment
- CURRENT: database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql, 080_partition_runtime_telemetry_metrics.sql, 081_partition_outbox_events.sql, 082_partition_platform_queue_jobs.sql and 083_partition_webhook_deliveries.sql create partitioned parents. 131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:9-14 installs a SECURITY DEFINER trigger function mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow that copies live INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE traffic into the partitioned shadow tables before any rename-swap ("the hot-table partition shadows must not sit inert", L2-5). 179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql extends it. .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml runs on cron "15 2 1 * *" (L5) and sets EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE = `github.event_name == 'schedule' || inputs.execute == true` (L35), so the monthly schedule executes ensure_next_hot_table_partitions + detach_expired_hot_table_partitions against PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING (L36) with no human approval.
- EXPECTED: The Constitution declares partitioning-now FORBIDDEN in the 100 -> 10,000 merchant / ~30k orders-per-month envelope. A partitioning programme with live shadow mirroring and an unattended monthly production DDL job is the opposite of that.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql; database/supabase/migrations/081_partition_outbox_events.sql; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:2; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:9; database/supabase/migrations/179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:5; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:35; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:36; scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:29
- RISK: Every write to the five hot tables (including outbox_events, the transactional outbox the Constitution makes load-bearing) pays a synchronous SECURITY DEFINER trigger that writes a second copy. A monthly unattended job performs DDL (partition create/detach) on the production database with no plan-then-approve step; a failure detaches or fails to create a partition and writes to the parent start erroring.
- RECOMMENDATION: Take this to the ARB: either amend the Constitution to permit partitioning in this envelope, or disable the shadow-mirror triggers and convert hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml to plan-only on schedule (require inputs.execute for any DDL). At minimum, remove `github.event_name == 'schedule'` from EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE.
- CAPACITY: The shadow-mirror trigger doubles write amplification on the outbox and queue tables, which are the throughput-critical tables at 30k orders/month; the partitioning itself is a scale technique the envelope does not need.

**[S2] ART-03 / DEPLOY-R2 obligation to collapse the duplicate staging workflow to one is OPEN**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Three staging-named workflows exist and two of them deploy the same runtimes with different semantics. deploy-staging.yml (push stepup/main) deploys 4 Workers with `--env staging` and 5 Vercel apps as PREVIEW deployments (`vercel pull --environment=preview` + `build` + `deploy --prebuilt`, L162-164), sets no secrets and runs no DB migration. staging-deploy.yml (workflow_dispatch only, push disabled by comment at L4-7) migrates the staging DB (L46), provisions ~20 staging Worker secrets (L80-100), deploys the same 4 Workers, and deploys the same 5 Vercel apps as PRODUCTION-alias deployments (`vercel deploy --prod`, L212) with 22 injected env vars. staging-runtime-deployment.yml is an infrastructure lane (Terraform / Cloudflare primitives). None is dead: workflow-trigger-coverage-validator.mjs:414-423 lists all three as live deploy lanes. No validator asserts they be merged.
- EXPECTED: AGENTS.md:331 DEPLOY-R2: "Collapse the duplicate staging workflow to one."
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml:5; .github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml:162; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:4; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:46; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:212; .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:11; scripts/validators/workflow-trigger-coverage-validator.mjs:414; AGENTS.md:331
- RISK: The two lanes produce different Vercel deployment types against the same STAGING_*_PROJECT_ID, so whichever ran last determines whether the staging alias points at a preview or a production-aliased build, and whether the app's env came from the Vercel project or from GitHub secrets. Staging therefore has no single reproducible definition, which undermines production-deploy.yml's staging-e2e gate (L172-223).
- RECOMMENDATION: Verdict: OPEN (not satisfied, not partially satisfied — both app-deploying lanes are live and functionally divergent). Merge deploy-staging.yml and staging-deploy.yml into one workflow with a dispatch input for the secret-provisioning and DB-migration steps, keep staging-runtime-deployment.yml as the separate infrastructure lane, and add a validator assertion that exactly one workflow deploys staging app code.

**[S2] The automatic staging lane deploys code without migrating the staging database**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: deploy-staging.yml is the only staging workflow with an automatic trigger (push to stepup/main, L5). Its jobs are governance-gate, deploy-workers, deploy-apps and smoke-test-staging; there is no `supabase db push` step. The staging migration step exists only in staging-deploy.yml:44-52, which is workflow_dispatch-only. production-deploy.yml:172-223 then gates the entire production release on a full Playwright E2E run against that same staging environment.

- RECOMMENDATION: Add the staging `supabase db push --include-all` step (with the untracked-migration guard already present at deploy-staging.yml:46-53) as a job that deploy-workers depends on, mirroring the production ordering that deployment-safety-validator.mjs:113-115 already enforces.

**[S2] The two production deploy paths use different wrangler var semantics (--keep-vars vs replace)**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs:57 always appends `--keep-vars` to `wrangler deploy`, and deployment-safety-validator.mjs:17 pins that fragment. That path is reached from production-rollout.yml via production-deploy.mjs:105. The push-to-main lane, production-deploy.yml:337/352/368/384, calls `pnpm exec wrangler deploy --env production` with no --keep-vars, which replaces the Worker's vars with exactly the [env.production.vars] block.
- EXPECTED: One var semantic for production. Either both paths replace (so the repository is the sole source of truth for vars) or both preserve, but not one of each.
- EVIDENCE: infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs:57; scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:17; infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs:105; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:337; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:368; .github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml:88
- RISK: A var set on a Worker by an earlier default-env or dashboard action (e.g. FEATURE_EXTENSIONS="true") survives every --keep-vars rollout deploy and is invisible in the config, so reading wrangler.toml does not tell you the deployed flag state. The two paths can leave the same Worker in different effective configurations from the same commit.
- RECOMMENDATION: Drop --keep-vars from deploy-worker-runtime.mjs (and its validator pin) so both paths replace vars, or route production-deploy.yml through the wrapper so both preserve. Whichever is chosen, add a post-deploy step that dumps and asserts the Worker's effective var set.

**[S2] Production app URL topology contradicts the Vercel project registry and the production smoke test**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: config/vercel/projects.json declares production URLs store.letbuyy.com for storefront-runtime (L44) and www.letbuyy.com for marketing-site (L59), matching config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:141 ("storefront": "store.letbuyy.com"). But production-deploy.yml:433 sets NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=https://letbuyy.com for ALL five apps in the matrix, and smoke-test-production sets both LETBUYY_E2E_MARKETING_URL and LETBUYY_E2E_STOREFRONT_URL to https://letbuyy.com (L522,525). staging-deploy.yml:182-194 by contrast gives each app its own per-app app_url.
- EXPECTED: Each app receives its own canonical production origin, and the smoke test asserts against distinct marketing and storefront origins.
- EVIDENCE: config/vercel/projects.json:44; config/vercel/projects.json:59; config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:141; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:433; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:522; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:525; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:182
- RISK: Every production app is built with NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=https://letbuyy.com, so self-referential links, canonical tags, OAuth redirects and cookie-scoped absolute URLs in the storefront and console point at the apex rather than their own origin. The production smoke test cannot detect a broken storefront origin because it never requests store.letbuyy.com.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL into the matrix as a per-app value sourced from config/vercel/projects.json (as staging-deploy.yml already does), and split the smoke test's marketing and storefront URLs.

**[S2] Two different definitions of "production runtime" make staging structurally unable to prove production rate-limit behaviour**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: rateLimit.ts:58-67 defines isProductionRuntime as: true if ENVIRONMENT or APP_ENV is "production"; if either is set to anything else, false; only if both are unset does it consult NODE_ENV. featureFlags.ts:13-17 defines it as: true if ANY of ENVIRONMENT, APP_ENV, NODE_ENV equals "production". Every wrangler env block sets NODE_ENV="production" including [env.staging.vars] (api-gateway L125). So on the staging Worker the feature-flag helper reports production (fail-closed default) while the rate-limit helper reports non-production (in-memory limiter, no fail-closed 503).
- EXPECTED: One shared runtime-environment predicate used by every gate, so that staging exercises the same code paths as production.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:58; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/featureFlags.ts:13; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:125; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:241; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:887; docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:36; services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/rate-limit-query-cost-backend.test.ts:81
- RISK: The staging E2E suite that gates every production release (production-deploy.yml:172-223) runs against an in-memory per-isolate rate limiter, so it cannot reproduce the 503 RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE class of failure that took production /v1 down on 2026-08-01. Staging green is systematically not evidence for production, and the repository's own runbook says so at line 38.
- RECOMMENDATION: Extract a single exported runtimeEnvironment() helper and use it in both middlewares. Add a staging-only configuration that exercises the real backend selection (e.g. set RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK in [env.staging.vars]) so staging traverses the KV/Redis path.
- CAPACITY: Without a staging path that exercises the real limiter, no load test can establish the /v1 request ceiling before production traffic finds it.

**[S2] Production Worker binds D1, Durable Objects and Hyperdrive alongside the single Postgres source of truth**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml [env.production] binds a D1 database letbuyy-production-d1 with its own migrations_dir database/cloudflare/d1/migrations (L326-330), a Durable Object RUNTIME_COORDINATOR backed by SQLite (L27-29, L332-334), a Hyperdrive pool (L322-324) and a KV CACHE namespace (L313-316). config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:114-126 formalises a D1 lineage/rollback/backup policy and a Durable Object checkpoint policy. config/runtime/production-topology.json lists Supabase Database as the only database runtime (L30-38).
- EXPECTED: Constitution: ONE PostgreSQL source of truth. D1 and Durable Object SQLite storage are additional stateful stores whose relationship to that invariant is not declared in production-topology.json, which lists no D1 or DO runtime.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:326; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:332; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:322; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:27; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:114; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:120; config/runtime/production-topology.json:30; config/runtime/production-topology.json:230
- RISK: Two additional durable stores exist in production with their own migration lineage and no representation in the production topology manifest or the outbox/ledger model. If any tenant-scoped or money-adjacent state lands in D1 or a Durable Object it escapes RLS, the tenant GUC and the canonical ledger; nothing in the deploy path or the topology manifests would surface that.
- RECOMMENDATION: Have the ARB rule on whether D1 and Durable Object storage are permitted in this envelope and for what data classes. Then either represent them explicitly in production-topology.json with a stated data-class restriction, or remove the bindings. Add a validator that fails if any tenant or money table name appears in database/cloudflare/d1/migrations.
- CAPACITY: Hyperdrive is the connection-pool ceiling for the gateway against Supabase; D1 and DO add independent per-object write ceilings that no capacity model in the repo accounts for.

**[S2] Push notifications are permanently dry-run in production**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml [env.production.vars] sets PUSH_DISPATCH_MODE="dry-run" (L256) and FCM_PROJECT_ID / FCM_CLIENT_EMAIL / APNS_KEY_ID / APNS_TEAM_ID / APNS_BUNDLE_ID to "SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_..." placeholders (L257-261). The same values appear in the default block (L36-41) and [env.staging.vars] (L142-147); only APNS_ENVIRONMENT differs (production at L262).
