The placeholder values are also not credential-shaped so committed-secret-placeholder-validator does not flag them, and production-deploy.yml provisions no FCM/APNS secrets for queue-workers (it sets only INTERNAL_API_SECRET and QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET at L360-361).
- RECOMMENDATION: Record push dispatch as an explicit Class-A skip in config/runtime/production-safety-preflight.json alongside the existing SKIP-W3-SENTRY-DSN entries, or provision FCM_PRIVATE_KEY / APNS_PRIVATE_KEY as Worker secrets in production-deploy.yml and flip PUSH_DISPATCH_MODE.

**[S3] FEATURE_LIQUID is configured in all three environments but read by no runtime code**  ·  domain=theme  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: FEATURE_LIQUID is set to "true" in the default block (wrangler.toml:41) and [env.staging.vars] (:132) and "false" in [env.production.vars] (:248), and is declared in the Env interface (index.ts:260) and the GatewayFeatureFlag union (featureFlags.ts:4). No featureGate('FEATURE_LIQUID', ...) is ever constructed — index.ts:693-702 creates gates only for FEATURE_GRAPHQL and FEATURE_EXTENSIONS — and no call site of isGatewayFeatureEnabled passes it. The actual Liquid renderer default is a compile-time constant, packages/theme-engine/src/theme-governance.ts:14 THEME_LIQUID_RENDERING_DEFAULT_ENABLED = false.
- EXPECTED: Either the flag gates the Liquid renderer, or it is removed. A configured production value of "false" for the constitutionally canonical renderer, that has no effect, is misleading.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:41; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:132; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:248; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:260; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:693; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/featureFlags.ts:4; packages/theme-engine/src/theme-governance.ts:14
- RISK: An operator reading the production wrangler config concludes Liquid is deliberately disabled in production and either flips it (no effect) or plans a rollout around it (no effect). The real renderer switch is a source constant requiring a code change and deploy.
- RECOMMENDATION: Delete FEATURE_LIQUID from all three [vars] blocks and from the GatewayFeatureFlag union, or wire it to the theme-engine default. Document that THEME_LIQUID_RENDERING_DEFAULT_ENABLED is the real switch.

**[S3] DEPLOY-R1 branch-lag guard verified enforcing; BRANCH_STRATEGY.md still asserts the resolved lag as a live gap**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: scripts/validators/branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:84 executes `git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/stepup/main` and asserts the count is 0 (:85-88), after asserting GITHUB_REF_NAME is main (:77-80) and force-fetching both refs (:66-72). It is wired into production-deploy.yml:24-37 (fetch-depth 0, LETBUYY_ENFORCE_BRANCH_LAG=1) as the first job that release-preflight depends on, and into branch-lag-monitor.yml hourly. Measured at this SHA: main, stepup/main, origin/main and origin/stepup/main all resolve to 5af69e0c and `git rev-list --count main..stepup/main` returns 0. But .github/BRANCH_STRATEGY.md:53-57 still states under "Known reality gaps" that main is 106 commits behind and origin/main 291 behind, measured 2026-08-09 at ee371182.
- EXPECTED: DEPLOY-R1 requires `git rev-list --count main..stepup/main` == 0 and forbids silent re-accumulation. The guard's HEAD..origin/stepup/main form is equivalent when run from a main checkout and is correctly divergence-safe (it measures ancestry, not tree equality). The doc should reflect the resolved state.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:84; scripts/validators/branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:77; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:24; .github/workflows/branch-lag-monitor.yml:5; .github/BRANCH_STRATEGY.md:53; AGENTS.md:330
- RISK: The guard measures ancestry only, so a merge that preserves history without importing content (as commit 7dfe3131 deliberately did for main's 1384cb5d) satisfies it while main's tree is discarded. That is correct here and was documented in the merge message, but the guard alone cannot detect a case where it is not. Meanwhile the stale doc tells any reader the deploy meta-blocker is still open.
- RECOMMENDATION: Update BRANCH_STRATEGY.md:51-61 to record that the lag was closed at 5af69e0c and by what merge. Keep the guard as-is; optionally add a companion assertion that main's tree is reachable from the integration branch when a divergence-preserving merge is used.

**[S3] A workflow named "Staging Runtime Deployment" triggers on pushes to the production branch**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:11-24 declares push on branches [main, "release/**"] with a path filter covering services/**, infrastructure/**, config/runtime/** and scripts/validators/**. On a push event `inputs.execute` is empty, so the `!inputs.execute` condition at L73 selects the plan-only step and the mutating bootstrap at L77-87 does not run. Every push to main therefore starts both production-deploy.yml and this staging plan concurrently (different concurrency groups: production-release-production vs staging-runtime-deployment).
- EXPECTED: A staging-named lane should be reachable from the staging integration branch, not from the production branch. The plan-only behaviour is correct but incidental — it depends on `inputs.execute` being falsy for push events rather than on an explicit event guard.
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:11; .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:73; .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:77; .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:37; .github/BRANCH_STRATEGY.md:42; scripts/validators/workflow-trigger-coverage-validator.mjs:420
- RISK: The staging-mutating bootstrap step is one `if:` expression away from running on every production-branch push, and the job already declares `environment: staging` with CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN and LETBUYY_PROVIDER_MUTATION_APPROVED available. The workflow-trigger-coverage validator checks only that this lane is unreachable from codex branches (L436-445), not that it is unreachable from main.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add an explicit `if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'` to the bootstrap-execute step, and either retarget the push trigger to stepup/main or fold this lane into the single staging workflow required by DEPLOY-R2.

**[S3] Frozen-app and reserved-service deploy identifiers persist in environment templates and the Cloudflare resource registry**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: config/env/production.env.template:32-36 declares PRODUCTION_APP_MARKETPLACE_PROJECT_ID, PRODUCTION_DEVELOPER_PORTAL_PROJECT_ID, PRODUCTION_THEME_MARKETPLACE_PROJECT_ID and PRODUCTION_PLATFORM_OPERATIONS_PORTAL_PROJECT_ID; staging.env.template:32-36 mirrors them. production.env.template:136-141 declares live public URLs themes./apps./developer.letbuyy.com. config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:23-46 declares production Worker names for all six constitutionally-empty reserved services (letbuyy-checkout, letbuyy-notifications, letbuyy-search, letbuyy-media-service, letbuyy-analytics, letbuyy-event-bus). deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs correctly keeps all of these out of config/vercel/projects.json and the three checked workflows.
- EXPECTED: Frozen apps have no deploy identity and reserved services have no provisioned resource identity. Names that exist only to be unused invite reconstitution.
- EVIDENCE: config/env/production.env.template:32; config/env/production.env.template:136; config/env/staging.env.template:32; config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:23; config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:43; config/runtime/production-topology.json:198; scripts/validators/deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:83
- RISK: The templates are the operator-facing instruction for populating the secret store, so an operator following them creates Vercel project ids for four frozen apps and public DNS expectations for three frozen origins. Combined with the frozen roots already present in production-deploy.mjs:125-129, that is the full set of inputs a rollout needs to publish them.
- RECOMMENDATION: Strip the four frozen-app project-id lines and the three frozen-app public URLs from both env templates, and either delete the six reserved-service worker name entries from config/cloudflare/resource-names.json or annotate them as reserved-not-provisioned with a validator assertion.

**[INFO] Runtime and topology manifests are self-declared contracts, not deployment evidence**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=GOVERNED  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:4 declares "mode": "governance-contract-not-live-evidence", :129 "liveEvidenceTracked": false, :135 "mustRedactSecrets": true, :137 "failurePolicy": "fail-closed". The same mode string appears in 12 further config/runtime manifests and is asserted by five validators (provider-evidence-validator.mjs:21, cloudflare-topology-validator.mjs:22, rollback-integrity-validator.mjs:17, manifest-lineage-validator.mjs:20, cloudflare-binding-validator.mjs:54). config/runtime/production-topology.json:6 carries the same mode while listing 11 runtimes with status "active".
- EXPECTED: These manifests are load-bearing for governance and non-load-bearing for deployment truth, and the repository already says so.
- EVIDENCE: config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:4; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:129; config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:137; config/runtime/production-topology.json:6; scripts/validators/provider-evidence-validator.mjs:21; scripts/validators/cloudflare-topology-validator.mjs:22; scripts/validators/manifest-lineage-validator.mjs:20
- RISK: The risk is misreading, not malfunction. These manifests CAN establish: the intended resource names and bindings, which runtimes are declared active/reserved/frozen, the required env-var and secret surface, and a fail-closed policy the validators enforce against wrangler.toml. They CANNOT establish: that any Worker is deployed, that any KV namespace, D1 database, Hyperdrive pool or queue exists in the Cloudflare account, that any route resolves, that any secret is set, or that any runtime value is what the manifest says. `status: "active"` in production-topology.json is a self-declaration; even the KV and D1 ids hardcoded in wrangler.toml (32-hex and UUID-shaped literals) prove only that a config references an id, not that the resource exists or is bound.
- RECOMMENDATION: Keep the mode strings and the five validators that pin them. When any launch document cites a manifest, require it to state which of the two categories above the claim falls into. Provider-side existence remains a human/ops verification with account access.

### Adversarial verdicts (5)


**OVERSTATED** — Default wrangler block shares the production Worker name and re-enables forbidden capability flags (S1, VERIFIED-CONFIG): one `wrangler deploy` without --env silently replaces api.letbuyy.com's Worker, dropping routes and all bindings, degrading rate limiting, and flipping extensions/GraphQL on; CI bypasses the wrangler-exec guard.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: All four Worker configs set a top-level `name` identical to their `[env.production].name` (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:1 vs :219; queue-workers :1 vs :224; webhook-service :1 vs :69; mobile-bff :1 vs :87), and for api-gateway the top-level block is staging-flavoured (ENVIRONMENT/APP_ENV="staging" L32-33, FEATURE_GRAPHQL/FEATURE_EXTENSIONS/FEATURE_LIQUID="true" L39-41, staging queues L73-91, staging R2 L93-96) while declaring no routes, KV, D1, Hyperdrive, Durable Object, service, or analytics bindings. Because wrangler replaces plain-text vars and the binding set on deploy, a manual `wrangler deploy` run from services/api-gateway without `--env` would overwrite the production `letbuyy-api` script with that shape: FEATURE_GRAPHQL would re-open /storefront/graphql (index.ts:715-716), and rateLimit.ts:58-67 + :861 would degrade the global query-cost limiter to a per-isolate in-memory counter. No validator asserts the top-level name differs from production (cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs checks only [env.*], L120-184; mobile-bff is not covered at all). However the exposure is manual-operator-only: all four CI deploy steps pass --env explicitly (production-deploy.yml:337,352,368,384; staging-deploy.yml:108,127; deploy-staging.yml:88,96,104,112), all four package `deploy` scripts hard-require --env via deploy-worker-runtime.mjs:24 (and add --keep-vars at :57), and wrangler 4.100.0 emits an explicit "no target environment was specified" warning. Contrary to the original claim, the extension surfaces do NOT re-open — appPlatformKillSwitch (index.ts:722,729-733; appPlatformKillSwitch.ts:25-28) is registered first and fails closed on FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM, which is declared nowhere — and the Constitution's Wasm/extension prohibition (ART-16, R-12) targets executing extension code in the live path, not the FEATURE_EXTENSIONS flag, which is "true" in staging by design. Secrets survive the deploy (runbook:28-29), so impact is availability-only and reversible. The documented 2026-08-01 occurrence (docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:24-29) is REFERENCE-ONLY evidence that this operator error has materialised once.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-CONFIG
- REASONING: The configuration facts are all CONFIRMED by direct reading. services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:1 (`name = "letbuyy-api"`) is identical to :219 under [env.production]; same pattern in services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:1 vs :224, services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:1 vs :69, services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml:1 vs :87. The top-level api-gateway block (L1-100) declares staging vars (L32-71, incl. FEATURE_GRAPHQL/EXTENSIONS/LIQUID="true" at L39-41), staging queues (L73-91), the staging R2 bucket (L93-96), and NO kv_namespaces, d1_databases, hyperdrive, durable_objects.bindings, services, analytics_engine_datasets, or routes. scripts/validators/cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs only asserts names inside [env.*] (L125); its sole top-level checks are L186-191 (production queue/R2 leakage), so the top-level `name` is genuinely unguarded, and services/mobile-bff is absent from workerConfigs (L10-38) entirely. The rate-limit degradation is verified in source, not just inferred: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:58-67 returns false when ENVIRONMENT/APP_ENV are explicitly non-production (NODE_ENV is only consulted when both are absent), and L861 then selects the per-isolate memory limiter because neither CACHE nor RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK exists in the top-level block. Four material overstatements. (1) Reachability: the claim's "every GitHub Actions step ... bypasses the wrapper" implies CI exposure, but every deploy step passes --env explicitly (production-deploy.yml:337,352,368,384; staging-deploy.yml:108,127; deploy-staging.yml:88,96,104,112), and all four worker package.json `deploy` scripts route through infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs, which hard-fails without --env at :24 and adds --keep-vars at :57. deployment-safety-validator.mjs:50, cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs:116 and supply-chain-validator.mjs:35 all forbid a raw `wrangler deploy` package script. Residual exposure is manual operator invocation only. (2) "Silently" is false: wrangler 4.100.0 (pinned in all four package.json) warns explicitly when environments are defined and none is specified (logger2.warn "Multiple environments are defined ... no target environment was specified"); it proceeds, so the mechanism holds, but it is not silent. (3) "the extensions ... surfaces flip on" is REFUTED: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:722 and :729-733 register appPlatformKillSwitch BEFORE extensionsFeatureGate on every FEATURE_EXTENSIONS-gated route, and src/middleware/appPlatformKillSwitch.ts:25-28 is fail-closed on FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM, which appears in no wrangler.toml and no workflow. Only FEATURE_GRAPHQL (index.ts:715-716) genuinely re-opens a surface. (4) The constitutional framing is wrong: GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:245-248 and :407 (R-12) forbid EXECUTING third-party/extension/Wasm code in the live checkout/storefront path, not the FEATURE_EXTENSIONS registry flag; [env.staging.vars]:131 sets it "true" and tests/feature-flags/gateway-feature-gates.test.mjs:15 asserts only that production is "false", so "must never be true on the letbuyy-api Worker" is stricter than the law. Finally, impact is availability-only and reversible: secrets are preserved across deploy forms (docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:28-29), so there is no money, data-integrity, or tenant-isolation consequence. The 2026-08-01 precedent at that runbook:24-29 is real first-party documentation but is REFERENCE-ONLY, not source or provider evidence. Net: a genuine, unguarded operator footgun with documented precedent, but not an S1 — every automated path is guarded, wrangler itself warns, the frozen app-platform surface stays closed, and recovery is a redeploy with --env.

**CONFIRMED** — Frozen apps are production deploy targets in the production-rollout script path (ART-22) — infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs lists three frozen apps in appRoots and runs `vercel deploy --prod` on them when invoked with --execute from production-rollout.yml, uncovered by any validator.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs:121-130 hardcodes THREE frozen app roots — apps/app-marketplace (:125), apps/theme-marketplace (:126), apps/platform-operations-portal (:129) — into the `apps` deploy target (apps/developer-portal is NOT in the list). All three have package.json so the existsSync guard at :133 does not skip them, and at :138-140 with --execute and environment=production the script runs `pnpm dlx vercel deploy --cwd <appRoot> --yes --prod`. Two workflows reach this with --execute: production-rollout.yml:174 (target defaults to `all`, :17) and live-production-certification.yml:117 -> phase4-live-certify.mjs:135, the latter passing --skip-validation which bypasses validate:governance. infrastructure/deployment/release-promotion.yaml:19 declares exactly this command as the governed production deploymentCommand, so a routine rollout ships the frozen apps silently. No validator covers it: deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:11-15 reads only the three workflow YAMLs, and deployment-safety-validator.mjs:22-32 / production-readiness-validator.mjs:625-638 read the script but assert only the presence of safety fragments, never the absence of frozen roots; web-v1-scope-registry.json:326 scopes its frozen_app_deploy_target rule to production-deploy.yml alone. This contradicts ART-22 (Constitution:289-293), ART-24, production-topology.json:198-228 and each app's runtime.manifest.json deploymentAuthority.platform="frozen-not-deployed". Impact correction: the script sets no VERCEL_PROJECT_ID and the frozen apps have no vercel.json, no .vercel link and no config/vercel/projects.json entry, so the effect is creation of NEW unlinked Vercel projects published at auto-assigned *.vercel.app production aliases with no environment variables — unconfigured public origins for frozen code that receives no security-review lane, not functioning app-marketplace/ops-console surfaces on canonical letbuyy.com domains.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: I read every cited artifact and could not refute the defect; the mechanism, the reachability, and the validator gap all hold, and I found MORE reachability than claimed.

CODE PATH (VERIFIED-SOURCE): infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs:121-130 hardcodes appRoots including apps/app-marketplace (:125), apps/theme-marketplace (:126), apps/platform-operations-portal (:129). All three have package.json (`ls apps/{app-marketplace,theme-marketplace,platform-operations-portal}/package.json` -> present), so the existsSync guard at :133 does NOT skip them. At :134-136 the non-execute branch only prints a plan; at :138-140 the execute branch builds ["dlx","vercel","deploy","--cwd",appRoot,"--yes"], pushes "--prod" when environment==="production" (:139), and runs it (:140 — the claim cited 141, which is the closing brace; trivial off-by-one). Nothing else in the file filters frozen roots (I read the entire 146-line file).

TRIGGER (VERIFIED-CONFIG): .github/workflows/production-rollout.yml:174 runs the script with --execute; VERCEL_TOKEN/VERCEL_ORG_ID are supplied at :170-171; `environment` accepts production (:13) and `target` defaults to `all` (:17).

Two validators DO read the script — deployment-safety-validator.mjs:22-32 and production-readiness-validator.mjs:625-638 — but both only assert the PRESENCE of safety fragments; neither asserts ABSENCE of frozen roots. config/governance/web-v1-scope-registry.json:326 scopes frozen_app_deploy_target to production-deploy.yml alone. No other validator matched (`grep -rn "frozen" scripts/validators/*.mjs | grep -i "deploy|vercel|rollout"`).

Deploying the app-marketplace or developer-portal as active topology before the named trigger."; ART-24 makes platform-operations-portal the fold-then-delete loser. config/runtime/production-topology.json:198-228 declares all four frozen/"not-deployed"; apps/{app-marketplace,theme-marketplace,platform-operations-portal}/runtime.manifest.json each carry runtimeCategory "frozen-next-app" and deploymentAuthority.platform "frozen-not-deployed".

(2) infrastructure/deployment/release-promotion.yaml:19 declares `--environment=production --target=all --execute` as THE production deploymentCommand (:6 for staging), and infrastructure/cloudflare/workers-topology.md:57 documents the same — so this is the governed promotion procedure, not an exotic flag combination; an operator doing a normal rollout ships the frozen apps silently. (3) Staging is affected identically (same code path minus --prod). (4) The governance record is actively wrong about this: docs/merchant-system-discovery/2026-08-04T225330+0530-ee371182/03-SYSTEM-AND-RUNTIME-MAP.md:24 asserts "No frozen app and no reserved service appears in any deploy matrix. DEPLOY-R2 is HELD", derived solely from production-deploy.yml.

OVERSTATED — two narrative errors that do not change severity: (a) apps/developer-portal is NOT in appRoots (I confirmed the full list), so the "developer-platform surfaces" half of the risk statement is wrong; three of the four frozen roots are affected. (b) The script sets no VERCEL_PROJECT_ID (contrast the canonical path, .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:400-429, which pins per-app VERCEL_PROJECT_ID from secrets); no frozen app has a vercel.json (`ls apps/*/vercel.json` returns only the 5 active apps; deploy-matrix-topology-validator.mjs:85 enforces this), no .vercel link exists, and config/vercel/projects.json has no entry for them. So `vercel deploy --cwd apps/app-marketplace --yes --prod` would CREATE a new unlinked Vercel project under VERCEL_ORG_ID and publish it at an auto-assigned *.vercel.app production alias with NO environment variables — an unconfigured public origin, not a functioning marketplace on a canonical letbuyy.com domain.

SEVERITY: S1 holds. It is the documented production promotion command, reachable from two workflows (one bypassing governance validation), with `all` as the default target, silently violating a constitutional Forbids with zero validator coverage. Mitigations (manual dispatch only; GitHub `production` environment protection is UNKNOWN from the repo; no secrets provisioned so the surfaces would be inert) argue against S0 but not below S1. This is proof the path EXISTS and is uncovered — not proof any such deploy has occurred; I have no live provider access.

**OVERSTATED** — Production Razorpay secrets are provisioned by no deploy path and are explicitly forbidden in the one that could (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The secret matrix mis-classifies deliberately human-deferred Razorpay credentials as mandatory Worker secrets. config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:135-141 places RAZORPAY_KEY_ID/KEY_SECRET/WEBHOOK_SECRET/ROUTE_* in `workers.requiredSecrets` — not in the sibling `optionalProviderSecrets` bucket (:148) — and ciSecrets:249-256 declares PRODUCTION_RAZORPAY_* GitHub secret suffixes, while governance law A4 (scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:139-155, tests/operations/production-deploy-workflow.test.mjs:108-122, tests/launch/lb3-refund-backend.test.mjs:128-160) deliberately bans provisioning them in .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml so the go-live atom stays rollback-clean, with live keys deferred to the recorded human runbook HR-MERCHANT-PSP-LIVE (EXECUTABLE_HUMAN_REQUIRED.md:6). No validator reconciles the two (scripts/validators/environment-validator.mjs:90-124 loads the matrix but never checks requiredSecrets against provisioning). Two secondary gaps are real: RAZORPAY_KEY_ID and RAZORPAY_ROUTE_* have no in-repo production provisioning path at all, whereas RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET and RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET do (infrastructure/scripts/security/rotate-secrets.mjs:20,22,56 via phase4-live-certify.mjs:133 and .github/workflows/live-production-certification.yml:111,113) — so the rotation script cannot restore a working Razorpay configuration by itself. The runtime 503 at payments.ts:900,:1062 and checkout-payment-routes.ts:464 is the intended fail-closed posture for this launch phase, not an outage.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S3 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: All five cited artifact facts reproduce exactly. config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:135-141 lists RAZORPAY_KEY_ID/KEY_SECRET/WEBHOOK_SECRET/ROUTE_* in `workers.requiredSecrets`, with a sibling `optionalProviderSecrets` bucket at :148 holding Stripe/PayPal/Cashfree. .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:312-330 sets 19 api-gateway secrets, none Razorpay. scripts/validators/deployment-safety-validator.mjs:139-155 forbids the provisioning fragments; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:96-98 sets them for staging. services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:900,:1062 and services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:464 return 503 PAYMENT_UNAVAILABLE when the env keys are absent — and I confirmed the public storefront route has NO per-merchant credential fallback, so the runtime dependency on platform env is real.

But three claims are refuted.

(1) "Provisioned by no deploy path" is false for two of the three core keys. infrastructure/scripts/security/rotate-secrets.mjs:20,22 lists RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET and RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET in `requiredSecretNames`, and :56 runs `wrangler secret put <name> --config wrangler.toml --env <environment>` against services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml (:10), webhook-service and queue-workers. It is invoked with `--execute` from infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-live-certify.mjs:133 (non-dev branch), which is run by .github/workflows/live-production-certification.yml with `--environment=production`, and that workflow supplies ROTATE_SECRET_RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET (:111) and ROTATE_SECRET_RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET (:113). This is an in-repo, CI-invocable production provisioning path. Only RAZORPAY_KEY_ID and the four RAZORPAY_ROUTE_* names have no in-repo production path (confirmed absent from infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-deploy.mjs, infrastructure/terraform/, and services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml [env.production.vars] — the only match there is AI_CREDIT_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID at :275, a different variable).

(2) The risk statement "nothing in the repository records, verifies or rotates them" is false on all three verbs. Records: EXECUTABLE_HUMAN_REQUIRED.md:6 registers HR-MERCHANT-PSP-LIVE; LAUNCH_READINESS_2026_06_12/MERCHANT_APP_V1_FEATURE_READINESS_2026_08_09/14_HUMAN_REQUIRED_ACTIONS.md:67 lists H-7 naming all three keys; docs/final-v1-certification/DEPLOYMENT_MATRIX.md:14 and TOP_50_BLOCKERS.md:16 track it. Rotates: rotate-secrets.mjs above. Verifies: services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/payments.test.ts:167,187-188 and tests/executable/human-required-live-paths.test.mjs:6 assert the fail-closed seam.

(3) The framing "the two artifacts state opposite requirements" implies unnoticed drift; it is an adjudicated decision. EXECUTION_LEDGER.md (V1-LAUNCH-B2) records "GOVERNANCE REVERSAL: scope-lock's production-deploy.yml Razorpay-injection patch REJECTED by deployment-safety law A4 ... money secrets stay in the HR-MERCHANT-PSP-LIVE human runbook". FINAL_TRUTH_AUDIT_2026_06_11/G4_HUMAN_EXECUTION_PACKET.md states the A4 atom intentionally excludes money secrets so schema rollback stays clean; REALITY_PROMOTION_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md states those subsystems "stay sandbox/fail-closed ... not launch blockers, as long as nothing claims them live". The 503 is therefore the intended launch-phase behavior, not a defect, which is why S1 (live money-path outage) is not earned.

What genuinely survives is narrower and real: the matrix classifies Razorpay as `requiredSecrets` rather than in the adjacent `optionalProviderSecrets` bucket or any human-deferred bucket (the file contains no human/deferred marker at all), and ciSecrets declares PRODUCTION_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID (:249) and PRODUCTION_RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET/WEBHOOK_SECRET/ROUTE_* (:252-256) as expected GitHub secret suffixes while deployment-safety-validator.mjs:152 explicitly bans `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.PRODUCTION_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID }}` in the production deploy. Nothing cross-checks this: scripts/validators/environment-validator.mjs:90-124 loads the matrix but only asserts frozen-runtime owner exclusion and Vercel suffix rules — it never compares requiredSecrets against any provisioning path. So the inconsistency is real and ungated, but it is a config-classification defect, not a provisioning gap.

**OVERSTATED** — The "Upstash not provisioned" premise (wrangler.toml:250-258, runbook:81-82) is CONTRADICTED by production-deploy.yml's hard release-blocking preconditions on PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN (:76-77, :111-112) and its wrangler secret put step (:327-328); nobody can tell which rate-limit backend production runs. Severity S1, evidence UNKNOWN.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The production rate-limit backend is NOT ambiguous in the repository, and there is no contradiction: two dated, mutually corroborating 2026-08-01 artifacts (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:250-259 and docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:1-82, neither changed since) establish that the production Worker runs the CACHE-KV fallback with no Upstash Redis. The Upstash entries in .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml are a release PRECONDITION added 2026-06-05 (ab327e19), not a state assertion, and the file has not been touched since 2026-06-12.

The defensible finding is a release-integrity gap, not a documentation contradiction: the gated production pipeline is hard-blocked at release-preflight (production-deploy.yml:111-112 -> exit 1 at :131, transitively gating deploy-workers-production at :287-289) on two secrets the runbook says do not exist, while the runbook's own root cause and remediation describe the production Worker being deployed, mitigated and rolled back by hand. The runbook attributes the 2026-08-01 routeless-Worker incident to a plain `wrangler deploy` (line 27), which the pipeline can never emit because all four of its production deploys pass `--env production` (:337, :352, :368, :384) - proving that deploy did not come from the gate. Pushes to main have occurred since (1384cb5d, 2026-08-13; origin/main == HEAD == 5af69e0c), so the workflow triggers but its outcome is externally unverifiable.

Secondary, CONFIRMED and precisely as claimed: rateLimit.ts:850 and :912 short-circuit to Redis before consulting kvRateLimitFallbackEnabled, so RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK becomes dead configuration the moment both Upstash vars are set - i.e. the pipeline's own secret-put step at :327-328 would silently make wrangler.toml:259 inert without removing it, which is exactly what runbook:78-79 instructs an operator to do by hand. That is a real latent drift hazard, but it is conditional and causes no defect at this SHA.

Requires a human/ops decision to close: only an operator with GitHub and Cloudflare access can state whether PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_* exist and whether any production-deploy run has ever reached deploy-workers-production.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-CONFIG
- REASONING: Every cited line is accurate. .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:76-77 and :111-112 do make the two Upstash secrets release-blocking (missing+=(...) then exit 1 at :131), :327-328 does wrangler secret put both, and the block is transitive to deploys (deploy-workers-production:287-289 <- migrate-production:227-228 <- staging-e2e:176 <- migration-preflight:136-137 <- release-preflight:53). services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:850 and :912 do compute useRedis = Boolean(redisUrl && redisToken) and return before kvRateLimitFallbackEnabled is consulted, so RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK is inert when Redis is present; queryCostRateLimit is globally mounted at services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:684.

But the "contradiction" framing does not survive. The workflow expresses a REQUIREMENT, not a state assertion; the wrangler comment and runbook assert a STATE. Both are simultaneously true if the gated pipeline never completes. Three checks resolve the ambiguity the risk statement rests on, all against the claim:

(1) Temporal ordering. `git log -S 'PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL' -- .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml` returns ab327e19 (2026-06-05) and 38094747 (2026-06-02); `git log -- .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml` shows the file last touched 2026-06-12 (86add97d). Both contradicting artifacts are 2026-08-01 (8d9dda77), and `git log -- services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md` shows nothing later. The newer, dated, mutually corroborating pair wins over an older precondition list.

(2) The runbook's own root cause is self-refuting for the pipeline hypothesis. docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:24-28 says the Worker was deployed by a PLAIN `wrangler deploy` (no --env), producing a routeless Worker. `grep -rn "wrangler deploy" .github/workflows/` shows production-deploy.yml is the only workflow deploying production and every invocation is `--env production` (:337, :352, :368, :384). The incident deploy therefore cannot have originated in the pipeline. The remediation (:74-75, :78) and rollback (:104, version cd89c406-...) are likewise manual wrangler invocations. Manual, out-of-pipeline production deployment is the consistent reading, not a contradiction.

(3) The claim's second horn ("no push-to-main release has ever run") is partly falsified as stated: `git rev-parse origin/main` = 5af69e0c = HEAD and `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...HEAD` = 0 0, and 1384cb5d (2026-08-13) merged to main after the runbook. Pushes to main occurred, so the workflow triggered; whether release-preflight passed is external and UNKNOWN. Relatedly, docs/implementation/DEPLOYMENT_GAP_REPORT.md:13 ("branch not pushed; CI/deploy cannot trigger", 2026-06-14) is stale at this SHA.

Severity: S1 is not earned. Rate limiting is enforced on either backend (rateLimit.ts:860-878 fail-closed KV path; :930-936 query-cost KV path; :880-884 and :938-941 fail closed with warnRateLimitFailClosed when no backend exists in production). The KV write-quota limitation is disclosed in-repo (wrangler.toml:257-258) and founder-approved, and is independently tracked as a human action (LAUNCH_READINESS_2026_06_12/MERCHANT_APP_V1_FEATURE_READINESS_2026_08_09/14_HUMAN_REQUIRED_ACTIONS.md:71, H-11). No runtime defect is proven. What remains is a genuine release-integrity/governance gap, S2.

Evidence state: UNKNOWN understates the repo-side facts, which are directly established from configuration and source at this SHA. VERIFIED-CONFIG is correct for the load-bearing facts (workflow gating, secret-put, wrangler var); the external GitHub Actions secret inventory and whether any pipeline run completed remain UNKNOWN with no authorized live access, which is precisely why this cannot be escalated. The runbook's "27 configured GitHub Actions secrets" sentence is REFERENCE-ONLY and must not be treated as source evidence.

Excluded from evidence: the untracked docs/research/2026-08-16-architecture-truth-foundation-and-scale/ tree (audit output, not repository topology).

**OVERSTATED** — Cloudflare KV rate-limit fallback is non-atomic, write-quota bound, and collapses server-side traffic onto one key (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The Cloudflare KV rate-limit fallback performs a non-atomic read-then-write in both checkKvRateLimit (rateLimit.ts:321-325) and checkKvQueryCostLimit (rateLimit.ts:618-623), so concurrent requests under-count, and a KV error trips an isolate-global backoff (rateLimit.ts:42,627, 60s or 10min per :577-583) that makes every non-exempt /v1 route return 503 RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE (rateLimit.ts:1078-1088) instead of 429. The query-cost limiter reaches KV only because RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK="on" is set at wrangler.toml:259 (rateLimit.ts:928 has no isProduction clause; without the flag it fails closed entirely), it is mounted on /v1 with a narrow skip list (index.ts:684-688, 356-371), and it keys unauthenticated traffic by CF-Connecting-IP (rateLimit.ts:1029), so server-side gateway calls such as apps/marketing-site/src/app/api/founding-pricing/route.ts:13-26 concentrate onto a small number of egress IPs and therefore a hot KV key. Production binds the shared CACHE namespace (wrangler.toml:313-316) rather than the letbuyy-rate-limit-production namespace declared at config/cloudflare/resource-names.json:63-66, which no wrangler config in the repo binds. Whether this path is live in production is INFERRED, not verified: it holds only if UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN are unset on the production Worker, which .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:111-112,327-328 would make impossible for any successful CI deploy; the evidence for absence is the runbook (docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:81-82) and the flag never having been removed per its own exit criterion (:79). The 2026-08-01 burst measurements are REFERENCE-ONLY, though the cost arithmetic independently shows those 503s cannot be legitimate enforcement.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / INFERRED
- REASONING: Every cited path:line is accurate; the code defect is real. I confirmed: checkKvRateLimit does cache.get then cache.put with no atomic primitive (rateLimit.ts:321-325); checkKvQueryCostLimit does the same (rateLimit.ts:618-623); the KV catch sets the module-global queryCostKvUnavailableUntil (rateLimit.ts:627) with 60s/600s backoff (rateLimit.ts:577-583); the middleware converts unavailable to 503 in production (rateLimit.ts:1078-1088); unauthenticated keying is CF-Connecting-IP (rateLimit.ts:1029); the limiter is mounted on /v1 (index.ts:684-688); production binds the shared CACHE namespace (wrangler.toml:313-316) while resource-names.json:63-66 declares letbuyy-rate-limit-production, and I verified no wrangler config in the repo binds any KV namespace other than CACHE. The Redis path is genuinely atomic (EVAL INCR at rateLimit.ts:170-187, EVAL GET/SET at :644-680), so the expected state is right. I also independently corroborated the mechanism: /v1/pricing is a GET with no include/expand/limit, so calculateQueryCost returns 1 (rateLimit.ts:509-522); 120 requests x cost 1 = 120 points against capacity 200 leaking 20/s (index.ts:685-686) cannot fill the bucket, so the runbook's 20x503 cannot be legitimate enforcement and must be backend failure. Three things make VERIFIED-SOURCE unearned. (1) The claim omits the precondition: rateLimit.ts:928 has no `isProduction ||` clause, so the query-cost KV path is reachable ONLY because RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK="on" is set at wrangler.toml:259 — a line the claim never cites. Without the flag the limiter fails closed 100%, a different failure mode. (2) Unresolved refutation vector: .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:111-112 hard-fails the release when PRODUCTION_UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN are absent and :327-328 writes them to the Worker. If any CI production deploy ever succeeded, useRedis is true (rateLimit.ts:912,917) and BOTH KV paths are unreachable dead code, making the finding latent. The repo cannot settle live secret state; the only evidence for absence is the runbook's own assertion (docs/runbooks/production-api-rate-limit-backend.md:81-82) plus the fact that the flag was never removed despite the runbook naming removal as the exit criterion (:79). That is a strong inference, not source verification. (3) The measured 2026-08-01 production numbers (:56-62) are REFERENCE-ONLY runbook prose, not verifiable at this SHA. Two lesser imprecisions: "mounted globally on /v1" omits the skip list at index.ts:356-371 (auth/*, graphql, four storefront-customer paths are exempt), and "a single egress IP" is an assumption about Vercel egress — the server-side call is real (apps/marketing-site/src/app/api/founding-pricing/route.ts:13-26, force-dynamic, and it makes two gateway calls per visitor), but Vercel functions egress from a varying pool absent static-IP provisioning, so the defensible statement is drastically reduced key cardinality, not literally one key. Severity S1 survives and the blast radius is if anything understated: queryCostKvUnavailableUntil is a module-level variable (rateLimit.ts:42), so one KV error suppresses the limiter for EVERY key in that isolate, returning 503 on every non-exempt /v1 data route for 60s, or 10 minutes on a quota-shaped error. Separately, the fixed-window KV path IS auto-enabled in production with no flag (rateLimit.ts:860, `isProduction || flag`), so if Upstash is absent the non-atomic counter also governs the login/OTP/password-reset abuse limiters unconditionally.

### Inventory notes
A. WORKFLOW INVENTORY (16 files, .github/workflows/, all read at 5af69e0c)

1. ci.yml (271L) — "CI". on: pull_request[develop,main,stepup/main,codex/web-v1-integration] + push[same 4]. Jobs: typecheck, secret-hygiene, lint, unit-tests, merge-safety-gate, governance, mobile-merchant, build. No provider mutation. No schedule. Gates merge (named required checks in BRANCH_STRATEGY.md:74-75).
2. architecture-governance.yml (189L) — on: pull_request[path-scoped, base-branch agnostic] + push[main, master, codex/**]. 17 continue-on-error gate steps + gate-aggregate (L151-189) re-imposes failure. No mutation. Gates merge. NOTE: no push trigger for stepup/main.
3. production-deploy.yml (540L) — "Production deploy". on: push[main] + workflow_dispatch. MUTATES: Supabase prod schema (`db push --include-all`, L281), 4 Worker deploys `wrangler deploy --env production` (L337,352,368,384), ~19 `wrangler secret put --env production` (L312-330,345,360-361,376-377), 5 `vercel deploy --prod` (L426). Gates deploy. No schedule. concurrency: production-release-production.

MUTATES when execute=true: `terraform apply -auto-approve` (production-deploy.mjs:82), worker deploys, `vercel deploy --prod`. Emits SBOM. Requires LETBUYY_PROVIDER_MUTATION_APPROVED (L173).

9. live-production-certification.yml (137L) — on: workflow_dispatch{environment, mode: auto|plan|execute}. Runs 12 validator gates then phase4-live-certify.mjs; carries ROTATE_SECRET_* inputs (L107-116) so it can mutate provider secrets in execute mode. Attests SBOM.
10. branch-lag-monitor.yml (25L) — on: schedule "17 * * * *" + workflow_dispatch. Read-only; runs branch-lag-guard-validator with LETBUYY_ENFORCE_BRANCH_LAG=1.
11. hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml (59L) — on: schedule "15 2 1 * *" (monthly) + workflow_dispatch{environment, execute}. MUTATES PRODUCTION DB: EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE = `github.event_name == 'schedule' || inputs.execute` (L35), so the monthly schedule runs `--execute` against PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING by default (L32,36).

B. STAGING WORKFLOW RECONCILIATION (what each ACTUALLY does)
- deploy-staging.yml: the only AUTOMATIC staging lane (push stepup/main). Governance gate -> build 4 workers -> `wrangler deploy --env staging` x4 -> Vercel PREVIEW deploys (`pull --environment=preview` + `build` + `deploy --prebuilt`, L162-164) -> curl health checks. NO secret provisioning, NO DB migration, NO Playwright.

E. RATE LIMITING (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts)
  Backend order fixed-window (checkRateLimitKeys L840-896): Upstash Redis if URL+TOKEN present -> KV if `Boolean(env.CACHE) && (isProduction || RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK on)` (L860) -> fail-closed 503 in production (L882-885) -> in-memory otherwise.
  Backend order query-cost (checkQueryCostRateLimitKey L898-945): Redis -> KV ONLY if `Boolean(env.CACHE) && kvRateLimitFallbackEnabled(env)` (L928, NO isProduction clause) -> fail-closed (L939-942) -> in-memory.
  queryCostRateLimit is mounted globally on /v1 (index.ts:681-688).
  KV algorithm: non-atomic get-then-put (checkKvRateLimit L320-325; checkKvQueryCostLimit L618-623). Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent and rate-limits writes per key, so concurrent requests lose updates (under-count) and a hot key trips the queryCostKvUnavailableUntil backoff (L613-614,627) which returns 503 rather than 429.
  Fail mode: CLOSED in production (503 RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE) at rateLimit.ts:982-996, 1078-1088, 458-472, 1158-1168. OPEN-ish in staging: isProductionRuntime() is false there (ENVIRONMENT=staging), so staging silently uses the per-isolate in-memory limiter — staging green is NOT evidence for production.
  Test cover: services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/rate-limit-query-cost-backend.test.ts exercises real backend selection + enforcement with a stub KV (5 assertions read). It does NOT exercise KV eventual consistency or the write-rate ceiling.

G. MANIFEST EVIDENTIARY WEIGHT
config/runtime/cloudflare-topology.json:4 `"mode": "governance-contract-not-live-evidence"`, :129 `"liveEvidenceTracked": false`, :137 `"failurePolicy": "fail-closed"`. Same mode string appears in 12 other config/runtime manifests and is ASSERTED by 5 validators (provider-evidence-validator.mjs:21, cloudflare-topology-validator.mjs:22, rollback-integrity-validator.mjs:17, manifest-lineage-validator.mjs:20, cloudflare-binding-validator.mjs:54). These manifests can carry: the intended contract, the expected resource names/bindings, and a fail-closed policy the validators enforce against the wrangler configs. They CANNOT carry: proof a Worker is deployed, proof a KV/D1/Hyperdrive resource exists in the Cloudflare account, proof a route resolves, proof a secret is set, or proof of any runtime value. Their own `status: "active"` and `mode` strings are self-declarations that the repo explicitly refuses to treat as live evidence.

## DIMENSION: flow-identity

### Narrative summary
The merchant realm is genuinely two-stage: `apps/identity-platform` is the only runtime holding `LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK` (production-deploy.yml:446 scopes it with a matrix conditional) and the only runtime calling `new SignJWT(...).sign(key)` with ES256/EdDSA (identity-issued-session.ts:52-68); the gateway is verify-only via `createLocalJWKSet` over an env-injected JWKS (identity-session-verifier.ts:78-106), pins asymmetric algs, and rejects HS256 — and that verifier has a REAL behavioral test with generated keypairs covering forge/HS256/missing-jti denial paths (identity-issuer-d3.test.ts:93-153). That is where the good news stops. The ES256 JWT is validated ONCE, at `POST /auth/custom/sessions/create` (routes/auth.ts:1130), and is then stored verbatim as the row value in `public.sessions.token` (routes/auth.ts:1142-1149); every subsequent request authenticates against that opaque DB row via `validate_and_rotate_merchant_session`, not against the signature. R-14 is therefore satisfied only for the merchant asymmetric realm. Repo-wide there are at least five other runtimes that MINT credentials: `services/api-gateway` (customer HS256 `issueCustomerSession` at customer-registration.ts:141 and customer-engagement-routes.ts:610/770; app-scoped HS256 access tokens; app authorization codes; storefront preview tokens), `apps/storefront-runtime` (customer session at session.ts:56; storefront gate tokens at proxy.ts:288/426), `apps/platform-console` (HS256 console session + magic link, console-session.ts:47/58), `apps/identity-platform` itself for the HS256 `lb_accounts` cookie (cookies.ts:176-180), and Postgres, which mints a fresh `gen_random_bytes(32)` session bearer on rotation (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:98). The `identity-sole-issuer-validator` passes only because it explicitly allowlists `packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts` and three siblings (identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:11-15) — the violation is waived, not absent. Worse, `production-deploy.yml:317+444` pushes the SAME `PRODUCTION_CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET` to the api-gateway Worker and to all five Vercel Next apps, and line 315+443 does the same for `AUTH_SECRET`: for both symmetric realms the verification key IS the forgery key in six runtimes. Five live `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallbacks remain (checkout.ts:88, cart-routes.ts:307, media.ts:60, export.ts:174, webhook-service/index.ts:584) — SEC-R7 names the webhook one DEAD verbatim; the platform-console one was correctly removed. Because `AUTH_SECRET` also signs the `lb_accounts` cookie, and `lb_accounts` is one of the two guards on `/api/auth/switch`, an actor who obtains `AUTH_SECRET` from any of six runtimes can forge the "proven account" half of the ART-06 fix. The ART-06 /switch fix IS present in source (switch/route.ts:56-81 enforces rate-limit → CSRF → valid session → target-in-signed-accounts-cookie, in that order), but the only coverage is source-text `indexOf`/`assert.match` ordering assertions (auth-switch-guard.test.ts:41-67, whose own doc-comment admits the route contract test "belongs in the route integration suite") plus a `mustContain` substring gate (identity-security-gate.mjs:25-27). No test exercises the 401 or 403 denial path. The claim→tenant-GUC bridge is the largest structural gap: `withTenantTx` does the right thing (`SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant`, `SELECT set_config('app.site_id',$2,true)` with a UUID assertion at tenant-core/index.ts:586-598, refusing to fake it over REST) but has ZERO production call sites — `withResolvedTenantTx` is referenced only by tenant-context.ts:72 and a source-text test, while 116 gateway files use `getSupabaseAdmin` (blanket service_role over PostgREST). The governing validator only substring-checks that the helper file contains the right strings (no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:16-21). Tenant isolation is therefore application-side (`verifySiteOwnership`, auth-user.ts:58-85) plus a proxy that only asserts SOME tenant-column predicate exists, never that its VALUE matches the session (tenant-scoped.ts:52-77). Session rotation is dead code: both `validateMerchantSessionToken` callers pass `{ rotate: false }` (middleware/auth.ts:136, routes/auth.ts:387), so the rotation/reuse-detection RPC branch and `session_token_rotation_events` never fire and the 30-day bearer never rotates. The JTI replay cache is a per-isolate in-memory `Map` (internal-auth.ts:79-100) with a 5-minute TTL — not KV, not D1, not Postgres — so replay protection is best-effort per Worker isolate; the merchant JWT's own `jti` is only length-checked, never stored. No `MOCK_JWKS` literal survives (ART-07 satisfied: `/jwks` returns 404 flag-off or 501 unconfigured, oidc-disabled.ts:126-145), and the anon-writable `super_admin_allowlist` was re-scoped by migration 163 (`FOR ALL TO service_role` + `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated`, 163_d2:185-216). Capacity: token verification needs no network JWKS fetch, but every authenticated merchant request costs two Supabase REST round-trips AND a Postgres `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` + unconditional `UPDATE public.sessions` — a write and a row lock per request; a login storm additionally pays bcryptjs cost-12 (pure JS) inside the Worker plus ~6 cross-service HTTP hops.


### Findings (20)


**[S1] Customer-realm HS256 signing secret is distributed to six runtimes — verification key is the forgery key (R-14 / IDN-R1 / SEC-R7)**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `PRODUCTION_CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET` is pushed to the api-gateway Worker (production-deploy.yml:317) AND injected into all five Vercel Next apps (production-deploy.yml:444, matrix :400-409 = marketing-site, identity-platform, merchant-control-plane, storefront-runtime, platform-console). Two of those runtimes actually mint customer sessions with it: the gateway via `issueCustomerSession` (customer-registration.ts:141; customer-engagement-routes.ts:610,:770) and storefront-runtime via `provider.issueSessionToken` (session.ts:56). The signing algorithm is HS256 (customer-session.ts:52).
- EXPECTED: Constitution R-14 / IDN-R1: identity-platform is the SOLE token issuer; every other runtime is verify-only. AGENTS.md:233 (SEC-R7) forbids signing tokens with a shared symmetric secret across trust domains.
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:317; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:444; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:400; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:409; packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts:52; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-registration.ts:141; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-engagement-routes.ts:610; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/session.ts:56; scripts/validators/identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:11
- RISK: A read-only compromise of ANY of six runtimes (a marketing-site SSRF, a leaked Vercel env, a Worker log spill) yields the ability to mint a valid customer session for any customer of any store. The customer session is the sole gate on /customer/orders, /customer/addresses, /customer/profile and wishlist routes (storefront.ts:70-76), so the blast radius is buyer PII and order history across every tenant. marketing-site and platform-console hold the secret and have no legitimate need for it.
- RECOMMENDATION: (1) Immediately stop injecting CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET into marketing-site, merchant-control-plane and platform-console — those three never call any customer-session function; that is a one-line matrix conditional exactly like line 446 already does for LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK. (2) Record an explicit, time-boxed ARB deviation for the remaining two-runtime symmetric customer realm, or schedule the migration to identity-platform-issued asymmetric customer tokens. (3) Remove `packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts` from the `legacyAllowedUntilD3` allowlist in identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs once (2) lands, so the validator stops waiving the violation.
- CAPACITY: Customer session verification is a pure in-Worker HMAC with zero DB reads (middleware/customer-session.ts:56-68), so the symmetric design is the cheapest verification path in the system — replacing it with an asymmetric or DB-backed scheme has a real throughput cost that must be budgeted.

**[S1] Five live `|| AUTH_SECRET` cross-domain fallbacks remain, and AUTH_SECRET also signs the cookie that is half the /switch account-takeover guard**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `|| AUTH_SECRET` survives at checkout.ts:88 (cart-bound handoff signing), cart-routes.ts:307, media.ts:60, export.ts:174, and webhook-service/index.ts:584 — the last is the exact example SEC-R7 names DEAD verbatim. internal-console.ts:441 uses `c.env.AUTH_SECRET` directly. The SAME AUTH_SECRET is the HS256 key for identity-platform's `lb_accounts` cookie (cookies.ts:13,176-179), and `lb_accounts` membership is guard 2 of 2 on /api/auth/switch (switch/route.ts:69-81). AUTH_SECRET is deployed to the gateway Worker and to all five Next apps (production-deploy.yml:315,443).
- EXPECTED: AGENTS.md:233 (SEC-R7): no shared symmetric secret across trust domains; `AUTH_SECRET` MUST NOT be a fallback for any other secret; the webhook `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallback is DEAD. The platform-console fallback was correctly removed (console-session.ts:24), proving the pattern is known and fixable.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:88; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:307; services/api-gateway/src/routes/media.ts:60; services/api-gateway/src/routes/export.ts:174; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:584; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-console.ts:441; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:13; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/switch/route.ts:69; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:315; AGENTS.md:233
- RISK: AUTH_SECRET is simultaneously the checkout-handoff signing key, the media-upload ticket key, the data-export auth key, the webhook internal-dispatch key, and the lb_accounts key. Disclosure in any one of six runtimes gives an attacker: forged cart-bound checkout handoffs (money path), forged media upload tickets, forged export authorization, forged webhook dispatch — and a forged lb_accounts cookie. Combined with any legitimately-obtained session, the forged lb_accounts cookie satisfies the second /switch guard, restoring the ART-06 account-takeover for any verified merchant account. The ART-06 fix is thus only as strong as the weakest of six AUTH_SECRET holders.
- RECOMMENDATION: Make CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET, MEDIA_UPLOAD_SIGNING_SECRET and INTERNAL_API_SECRET hard-required (fail-closed, copying the `getConsoleSessionSecret` production pattern at console-session.ts:35-37) and delete all five `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallbacks. Give `lb_accounts` its own dedicated key (e.g. ACCOUNTS_COOKIE_SECRET) so the /switch guard stops sharing a key with the money path. Add a CI signer-lint that fails on any new `|| env.AUTH_SECRET`; no such validator exists today.

**[S1] The claim→tenant-GUC bridge has ZERO production call sites; all 116 gateway files authenticate tenancy application-side over blanket service_role**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `withTenantTx` (tenant-transaction.ts:15-35) is correct in isolation: it acquires a pooled SQL connection, runs `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant`, then `SELECT set_config('app.site_id', $2, true)` where the value passes `assertTenantSiteId` UUID validation (tenant-core/index.ts:586-598), and throws rather than faking the transaction over REST. But it is called only by `withResolvedTenantTx` (tenant-context.ts:72), which is called by nothing. Repo-wide search for `withTenantTx|withResolvedTenantTx` outside those two files and tests returns only source-text test assertions (tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts:42). Meanwhile 116 files under services/api-gateway/src use `getSupabaseAdmin` — a service_role PostgREST client that bypasses RLS. The `no-blanket-service-role-validator` only asserts the helper FILE contains the right substrings (:16-21); it never checks that any route calls it.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: ONE PostgreSQL source of truth with RLS ENABLE+FORCE and a per-transaction tenant GUC; gateway OFF blanket service_role. IDN-R5/R-4: the GUC value must derive from a validated token claim.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:29; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:593; scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:16; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13; services/api-gateway/src/tenant/tenant-scoped.ts:52; services/api-gateway/src/lib/auth-user.ts:58; services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts:42; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:10
- RISK: The database-enforced tenant boundary is not load-bearing for any request. Every tenant decision reduces to two application-side controls: `verifySiteOwnership` (auth-user.ts:58-85, a service_role SELECT on sites) and the `createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient` proxy — which only asserts that SOME tenant-column predicate is present, never that its VALUE matches the session (tenant-scoped.ts:52-77, `markScopeFromCall` sets `scoped=true` for `.eq('store_id', <anything>)`). A single forgotten `verifySiteOwnership` call in any of 116 files is a silent cross-tenant read with no DB backstop. The GUC machinery, the app_tenant role and 100+ FORCE-RLS policies in migration 164 all sit unused.
- RECOMMENDATION: To the ARB: either (a) wire at least the highest-value read paths (orders, payments, customers) through `withTenantTx` and prove it with a red-team test that a forged site_id returns zero rows, or (b) formally record that DB-level tenant isolation is deferred and that `verifySiteOwnership` is the sole boundary, and back it with a validator that enumerates every `getSupabaseAdmin` route and asserts an ownership check precedes it. Also strengthen no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs to count actual `withTenantTx` call sites rather than substring-matching the helper.
- CAPACITY: Wiring withTenantTx changes the data path from PostgREST HTTP to a pooled direct-SQL connection with `maxConnections: 3` per isolate (tenant-transaction.ts:20-24). At 10,000 merchants across many Worker isolates that connection budget is the binding constraint and must be sized against Supabase's pooler limits before any rollout.

**[S1] Merchant session rotation and reuse-detection are dead code — both call sites pass rotate:false, so a 30-day bearer never rotates**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `validate_and_rotate_merchant_session` implements rotation, reuse detection and revocation-on-reuse (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:37-117), backed by `session_token_rotation_events` with a UNIQUE old_token_hash (161_foundation_session_security.sql:21-33). Both and only both TypeScript callers pass `{ rotate: false }`: middleware/auth.ts:136 and routes/auth.ts:387. `MERCHANT_SESSION_ROTATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 10*60` (merchant-session.ts:39) is therefore never reached. The `rotated_session_token` handling in identity-platform (api/auth/session/route.ts:25-28) is unreachable. The session cookie TTL is 30 days (cookies.ts:85; AUTH_SESSION_DURATION_MS = 30d at auth-core:127).
- EXPECTED: Migration 161's own header states the intent: 'live merchant session idle timeout, rotation, reuse detection, and revocation'. A 30-day bearer with no rotation and no reuse detection is the opposite of that design.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:136; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:387; services/api-gateway/src/lib/merchant-session.ts:39; database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:95; database/supabase/migrations/161_foundation_session_security.sql:1; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts:25; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:85
- RISK: A stolen merchant session token is valid for up to 30 days with no rotation window that would surface theft. Token-reuse detection — the control that would catch a cloned cookie — never runs, so `session_token_rotation_events` stays empty and the 'reuse_detected' revocation branch is unreachable. Idle timeout (8h) does still fire because the RPC updates `last_active` unconditionally (:119-124), which is the only live session control besides explicit logout.
- RECOMMENDATION: Decide deliberately: either flip the read path to `{ rotate: true }` (and accept that Postgres then becomes a credential minter — see the separate finding) or delete the rotation branch, the rotation-events table and MERCHANT_SESSION_ROTATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS so the codebase stops advertising a control it does not run. Whichever way, shorten the 30-day cookie TTL, which is only defensible with rotation.
- CAPACITY: Enabling rotation adds a second row UPDATE plus an INSERT into session_token_rotation_events every 10 minutes per active session, on top of the per-request UPDATE that already exists — roughly a 10-20% write increase on the sessions path at steady state.

**[S1] The ART-06 /switch fix is present in source but has NO behavioral test — every guard is asserted by source-text ordering only**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: The fix IS in the handler and is correctly ordered: rate-limit (switch/route.ts:35-41) → CSRF (:44-46) → a VALID current session, 401 otherwise (:56-63) → the target email must appear in the signed httpOnly lb_accounts cookie, 403 otherwise (:69-81) → only then destroySession/lookup/createSession (:84-110). But the entire test suite for it is `readFileSync` + `indexOf`/`assert.match` on the route SOURCE TEXT (auth-switch-guard.test.ts:41-67), which asserts only that certain strings appear in a certain order. The file's own doc-comment concedes 'The full route-level contract test (no-session → 401, unproven-target → 403, proven-target → 200) ... belongs in the route integration suite' (:20-22). The governance gate is likewise a `mustContain` substring list (identity-security-gate.mjs:25-27).
- EXPECTED: An S0 account-takeover fix landed in the first wave should have a behavioral regression test that invokes POST and asserts 401 on no session, 403 on an unproven target, and 200 on a proven one. Neither denial path is exercised by any test at this SHA.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:56; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:69; apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts:41; apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts:55; apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts:20; scripts/governance/identity-security-gate.mjs:25
- RISK: Any refactor that preserves the identifier names and their textual order while breaking the logic — e.g. changing `if (!currentToken || !currentSession)` to `if (!currentToken)`, or making `isProvenAccount` default true on a cookie-decrypt failure (decryptAccounts returns `[]` on error, cookies.ts:189-191) — passes both the test and the gate and silently reopens the S0. There is no signal that would catch regression of the highest-severity identity defect on record.
- RECOMMENDATION: Write a route-level integration test that mounts the POST handler with mocked next/headers + fetch and asserts the three outcomes, especially the two denial paths. Model it on services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/identity-issuer-d3.test.ts:93-153, which is the one identity test in this repo that actually generates keys and exercises forgery rejection.

**[S2] JTI replay cache is a per-isolate in-memory Map, not a durable store; the merchant JWT's own jti is never recorded**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Internal-auth replay protection is `const consumedInternalJtis = new Map<string, number>()` at module scope in the Worker (internal-auth.ts:79), swept lazily with a 5-minute maxAge (:90-98). It backs `verifyInternalSessionHeaders` (:123), `verifyInternalAppRequest` (:162) and `verifyInternalIdentityPayload` (:191). No KV, D1 or Postgres is involved. Separately, the merchant ES256 token carries a `jti` (identity-issued-session.ts:67) but the gateway verifier only checks `typeof jti === 'string' && jti.length >= 16` (identity-session-verifier.ts:74-76, :101-103) — the value is never persisted or compared.
- EXPECTED: A replay cache for cross-service signed requests must be shared across all serving instances. Cloudflare Workers run many independent isolates per colo and across colos; module-scope state is per-isolate and evicted at will.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:79; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:91; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:123; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:162; services/api-gateway/src/lib/identity-session-verifier.ts:74; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/identity-issued-session.ts:67
- RISK: A captured internal-auth request (signature + JTI + timestamp) can be replayed against a different isolate within the 5-minute freshness window and will be accepted, because the second isolate's Map is empty. The affected surface includes `/auth/custom/sessions/create` and `/auth/custom/users/*` — i.e. session minting. The merchant JWT's jti gives no replay protection at all; it is a presence check that satisfies a validator, not a control.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move the JTI cache to the CACHE KV namespace (already bound and used by the rate limiter, rateLimit.ts:860) or to a Postgres table with a UNIQUE(jti) and a TTL sweep, keyed by `kind:jti`. Either accept the added latency on internal calls or shorten the freshness window materially. Also state explicitly, in code, that the merchant `jti` is a future-replay hook and not a live control, so it stops reading as one.
- CAPACITY: A durable JTI cache adds one KV or Postgres round-trip to every internal service-to-service call. At the current 2-DB-round-trip-per-request baseline this is a third hop and needs a latency budget; KV (eventually consistent, ~10ms hot) is the cheaper option and is already provisioned.

**[S2] Every authenticated merchant request performs a Postgres row-lock plus an unconditional row UPDATE — a write per read**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `validate_and_rotate_merchant_session` does `SELECT * FROM public.sessions WHERE token = p_token FOR UPDATE` (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:58-62) and then, on every accepted call, `UPDATE public.sessions SET token=..., last_active=v_now, ...` (:119-124) — the UPDATE is outside the rotation `IF` and runs unconditionally. The gateway calls this on every request carrying `X-LetBuyy-Internal-Session-Token` (middleware/auth.ts:136) and follows it with a second query against `users_custom` (:138-142). Both go through `getSupabaseAdmin` — PostgREST over HTTP, not a pooled connection.
- EXPECTED: Constitution envelope: 100 → 1,000 → 10,000 merchants. A session-validation path should be a cached or index-only read, not a write plus an exclusive row lock.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:58; database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:119; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:136; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:138; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13; database/supabase/migrations/161_foundation_session_security.sql:13
- RISK: Throughput is bounded by Postgres write capacity on one table, not by read capacity. Every merchant dashboard page fanning out N parallel API calls takes N exclusive locks on the SAME session row, serializing them and adding lock-wait latency that grows with UI fan-out. The write amplification also drives WAL volume, autovacuum load and index bloat on `sessions` proportional to total authenticated request volume — a growth curve that has nothing to do with merchant count.
- RECOMMENDATION: Split the hot path: make validation a read-only function (no FOR UPDATE, no UPDATE) and move `last_active` maintenance to a coarse-grained update (only when the stored value is older than, say, 60s) or to an async touch. Consider caching accepted (token → userId, email, exp) in the CACHE KV namespace for a short TTL so the common case costs zero DB round-trips, with revocation handled by an explicit cache-bust on logout. Measure before/after; this is the single highest-leverage identity capacity change available.
- CAPACITY: This is the dominant per-request cost of the merchant identity path: 2 Supabase REST round-trips plus 1 exclusive row lock plus 1 row write on every authenticated call. It sets the ceiling for merchant-control-plane concurrency.

**[S2] Postgres mints session bearer tokens with gen_random_bytes on rotation — a second credential minter waiting to be switched on**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: When `p_rotate` is true and the interval has elapsed, the RPC executes `v_new_token := encode(gen_random_bytes(32),'hex')` and writes it back as the live session token (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:98,:120). The credential that replaces the identity-platform-issued ES256 JWT is therefore minted by the database, with no issuer, audience, subject or signature. Today this branch is unreachable because both callers pass rotate:false, but the code path is deployed. The gateway also mints `randomHex(32)` opaque session tokens directly when no identity token is supplied outside production (routes/auth.ts:1142, gated by isProductionGatewayRuntime at :1120-1127).
- EXPECTED: R-14 / IDN-R1: identity-platform is the sole token issuer; every other runtime — and the database — is verify-only.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:98; database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:120; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1142; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1120; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1130; scripts/validators/identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:18
- RISK: Flipping `rotate: true` — a one-word change that looks like a security improvement and would pass every validator, since the sole-issuer validator scans only apps/services/packages and not .sql — silently demotes the merchant realm from identity-issued asymmetric tokens to database-issued opaque tokens after the first 10 minutes of every session. The ES256 signature would then be verified exactly once per login and never again, and no gate would notice.
- RECOMMENDATION: If rotation is wanted, rotation must call back into identity-platform for a freshly signed token rather than generating one in SQL. Extend identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs to scan database/supabase/migrations for token-minting patterns (`gen_random_bytes` written into a credential column), since today it explicitly skips .sql files.

**[S2] The `lb_accounts` cookie is a signed — not encrypted — JWT carrying merchant PII, and it doubles as an authorization credential**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `encryptAccounts` (cookies.ts:175-180) builds `new SignJWT({ accounts }).setProtectedHeader({ alg: 'HS256' }).sign(getSigningKey())` — a signed JWT whose payload is base64url, not encrypted. The payload holds up to 10 entries of `{ email, name, avatar, lastLogin }` (:71-76, :215). TTL is 90 days (:28) and the cookie carries COOKIE_DOMAIN (`.letbuyy.com` per production-deploy.yml:450), so it is sent to every platform subdomain. `decryptAccounts` returns `[]` on any verification failure (:186-191). This same cookie is the second authorization guard on /switch (switch/route.ts:69-81).
- EXPECTED: A cookie named and commented as 'encrypted' should be encrypted (JWE) if it carries PII, and an authorization credential should not double as a PII carrier with a 90-day lifetime across every subdomain.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:173; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:176; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:28; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:189; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/switch/route.ts:69; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:450
- RISK: Anyone who obtains the cookie value — a subdomain XSS, a proxy log, a shared-device browser profile — reads a merchant's full multi-account email list in cleartext (DPDP-relevant personal data), for up to 90 days. The misleading `encrypt`/`decrypt` naming and the 'encrypted accounts cookie' comment mean a future reviewer is likely to assume confidentiality that does not exist. Because the same value is an authorization input to /switch, its confidentiality and its integrity are now coupled to one shared key.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either switch to JWE (jose `EncryptJWT`) with a dedicated key, or reduce the payload to opaque account identifiers and resolve emails server-side. Rename encryptAccounts/decryptAccounts to signAccounts/verifyAccounts so the code stops overstating its guarantee. Separate the /switch proof from the PII carrier.

**[S2] Six-digit OTP is brute-forceable: per-IP throttle only, no per-token or per-email attempt counter, and the token is validated by plain equality**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: The OTP is 6 digits (`randomOtp` at auth.ts:214-218, range 100000-999999). The only throttle is `checkRateLimit('verify_otp', ip)` — 5 attempts per 15 minutes keyed on the x-forwarded-for IP (verify-otp/route.ts:20; rate-limit.ts:43). The gateway's `/custom/tokens/validate` does a plain `.eq('token', token).eq('type', type)` lookup (auth.ts:1304-1311) with no attempt counter on the token row and no per-email throttle; the token is invalidated only on SUCCESS (`update({used:true})` at :1333), never after N failures. That update is also a read-then-write, not a conditional update.
- EXPECTED: A 6-digit email OTP that grants email-verified status (and, via set-password, a full merchant session) needs an attempt counter bound to the token or the identity, not to the caller's IP.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:214; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1304; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1329; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1333; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/verify-otp/route.ts:20; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/rate-limit.ts:43
- RISK: The keyspace is 10^6 and the throttle is per-source-IP. An attacker with 1,000 IPs gets 5,000 guesses per 15 minutes against a live OTP; over a typical token lifetime that is a meaningful fraction of the space against a targeted signup, and the token is never burned by failures. Success yields email-verified state and a password-set intent, i.e. account creation control over the victim's address. The read-then-update also permits two concurrent requests with the same valid token to both succeed.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add an `attempts` column to verification_tokens, increment it on every validate call, and hard-invalidate at 5. Key the identity-platform rate limit on the email as well as the IP. Make the consume atomic: `UPDATE verification_tokens SET used=true WHERE id=$1 AND used=false RETURNING ...` instead of SELECT-then-UPDATE.

**[S2] `sessions.token` stores the raw bearer credential in cleartext, including the full ES256 JWT**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `POST /auth/custom/sessions/create` inserts `token` directly: `db.from('sessions').insert({ user_id, device_id, token, expires_at })` where `token = providedToken || randomHex(32)` and providedToken is the identity-issued ES256 JWT (routes/auth.ts:1141-1149). Validation then matches on the raw value: `WHERE sessions.token = p_token` (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:61). By contrast, the rotation-events table correctly stores only SHA-256 hashes (`old_token_hash`, `new_token_hash`, :111-112), and customer password reset tokens are hashed (customer-engagement-routes.ts:491), showing the hashed pattern is understood elsewhere.
- EXPECTED: A bearer credential should be stored as a hash so that read access to the table does not equal impersonation — exactly the pattern already used two tables over.
