- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1144; database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:61; database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:111; database/supabase/migrations/161_foundation_session_security.sql:13; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-engagement-routes.ts:491
- RISK: Any SELECT on `public.sessions` — a support query, a backup, a logical-replication stream, a read-replica, an over-broad service_role query in one of 116 files — hands over live, directly usable merchant session bearers for up to 30 days. With rotation disabled those tokens do not age out. The partial index `idx_sessions_active_token` on the raw token makes bulk extraction cheap.
- RECOMMENDATION: Store `token_hash = encode(digest(token,'sha256'),'hex')` and match on it in the RPC (the rotation-events table already models this exactly). Migrate the index to the hash column. Never return the raw token from any read endpoint.

**[S2] bcryptjs cost 12 runs inside the api-gateway Worker on the login path with no declared CPU limit**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `MERCHANT_PASSWORD_BCRYPT_ROUNDS = 12` using the pure-JavaScript `bcryptjs` package (merchant-password.ts:1-12), invoked synchronously on the login hot path at routes/auth.ts:527 and on hashing at :499. Customer registration also runs bcryptjs (customer-registration.ts:6). services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml declares no `[limits]` block, so the account-default CPU ceiling applies.
- EXPECTED: Password hashing at cost 12 is correct for security; running it in pure JS inside a CPU-metered edge isolate is a deployment-shape decision that needs an explicit CPU budget and a measured p99.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/merchant-password.ts:3; services/api-gateway/src/lib/merchant-password.ts:11; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:527; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-registration.ts:6; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:3
- RISK: bcryptjs is materially slower than a native implementation; cost 12 is 4,096 iterations. Under a login storm this becomes the CPU bottleneck of the whole gateway, and if the per-invocation CPU ceiling is exceeded the Worker is terminated mid-login, producing failures that look like an outage rather than a rate-limit. Because CPU time is billed on Workers, a credential-stuffing campaign converts directly into cost even when every attempt fails — the 5-per-15-minutes-per-IP limit does not bound a distributed attacker.
- RECOMMENDATION: Measure actual CPU ms per bcrypt.compare on staging via `wrangler tail`, then declare an explicit `[limits] cpu_ms` in wrangler.toml so the failure mode is a clean error rather than an unbounded bill. Evaluate moving password verification behind a Postgres SECURITY DEFINER function (pgcrypto crypt/gen_salt) so hashing runs on database CPU, which is capacity you already provision. Add an early per-email throttle before bcrypt so failed attempts do not pay hashing cost.
- CAPACITY: This is the binding constraint on login throughput. Every login and every registration serializes on one bcryptjs cost-12 computation in the metered isolate; it dominates the ~6 cross-service HTTP hops the login flow already performs.

**[S2] No audit record is written for any merchant authentication event (login, switch, logout, session create) in either identity-platform or the gateway**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: A case-insensitive search for 'audit' across apps/identity-platform/src returns nothing. services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts contains no write to audit_logs or any equivalent for /custom/sessions/create, /sessions/destroy, /sessions/destroy-all or /users/authenticate. The only auth telemetry is a fire-and-forget new-device email (login/route.ts:106-111) and `console.error` on failure. By contrast, platform-console DOES audit its login/MFA/logout (console-auth-server.ts:208-238 → /internal/console/audit-logs), and the customer realm writes activity rows (`insertCustomerActivity(..., 'login', ...)`, customer-engagement-routes.ts:76). identity-platform's own runtime.manifest.json asserts 'auth state changes must be correlated without exposing credentials or tokens'.
- EXPECTED: The realm that suffered a documented S0 account-takeover (ART-06 /switch) should record who switched into which account, from which IP and device, and when — otherwise post-incident scope determination is impossible.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:110; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts:106; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1108; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1176; apps/platform-console/src/lib/console-auth-server.ts:208; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-engagement-routes.ts:76; apps/identity-platform/runtime.manifest.json:60
- RISK: If AUTH_SECRET leaks or the /switch guard regresses, there is no durable record from which to determine which accounts were entered, by whom, or when — the exact forensic question an account-takeover incident asks. The `sessions` and `devices` tables give a weak proxy (device_id, created_at) but carry no actor, no source IP and no event type, and destroy-all overwrites revocation state in bulk.
- RECOMMENDATION: Emit an audit row on session create, switch, logout and logout-everywhere carrying actor user_id, target user_id (for switch), IP, device fingerprint and outcome — reusing the console's existing /internal/console/audit-logs shape and the audit_logs table that migration 000_baseline already defines with a trigger. This is small and is the prerequisite for being able to answer 'who was affected' after any identity incident.

**[S2] The identity 'production runtime' gate excludes staging and preview, so non-production deploys sign PII cookies with a public literal and mint no asymmetric token**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `isProductionIdentityRuntime` returns true only when NODE_ENV/APP_ENV/VERCEL_ENV/NEXT_PUBLIC_ENV equals the literal 'production' (dev-auth-bypass.ts:13-18). On a staging or Vercel-preview deploy it returns false, so: (a) `getSigningKey` falls back to the hard-coded literal `'dev-only-insecure-auth-secret-set-AUTH_SECRET-in-prod'` when AUTH_SECRET is short/unset (cookies.ts:19-23); (b) `createIdentityIssuedMerchantSessionToken` returns null instead of throwing (identity-issued-session.ts:41-46); (c) cookies are set with `secure: false` (cookies.ts:63). The gateway independently permits token-less session creation outside production (routes/auth.ts:1120-1127), so a staging login mints an opaque randomHex(32) session with no signature at all.
- EXPECTED: Staging carries real merchant data patterns and is the pre-production e2e gate (production-deploy.yml requires staging-e2e). Its identity posture should match production, or the difference should be a recorded, deliberate decision.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/lib/dev-auth-bypass.ts:13; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:19; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:22; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:63; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/identity-issued-session.ts:41; services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:1120; .github/workflows/staging-deploy.yml:83
- RISK: On staging/preview the lb_accounts cookie is signed with a key that is published in this repository, so anyone can forge the /switch 'proven account' guard there; cookies travel without Secure; and the asymmetric issuance path — the single most important identity invariant — is never exercised before production. Staging e2e therefore cannot catch a broken ES256 path, which is precisely the failure that would take production login down.
- RECOMMENDATION: Treat staging as production for these gates: add 'staging' and 'preview' to isProductionIdentityRuntime, or invert the check to fail-closed unless an explicit LETBUYY_ALLOW_INSECURE_IDENTITY flag is set for local dev only. The staging workflow already pushes STAGING_AUTH_SECRET (staging-deploy.yml:83), so the secret exists — only the gate is wrong.

**[S2] Magic-link login is a GET with no CSRF and no bot check; console magic links are stateless and replayable for their full 15-minute TTL**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Merchant: `GET /api/auth/verify?token=&type=magic_link` mints a full 30-day session and sets the cookie (verify/route.ts:16,:96-99) with only a per-IP rate limit — no CSRF check and no Turnstile, unlike the password login path (login/route.ts:31,42). The token is at least single-use (validateToken marks `used`, auth.ts:1333). Console: the magic-link token is a stateless HMAC payload with a 15-minute exp (auth-core:997-1008); `readConsoleMagicLinkToken` verifies signature and expiry only (callback/route.ts:60-63) with no single-use store, so the same link can be redeemed repeatedly for 15 minutes.
- EXPECTED: A link that mints a session is a credential. It should be single-use (merchant path is), and consuming it should not be a plain GET that any prefetcher, link scanner or email-security crawler can trigger.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/verify/route.ts:16; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/verify/route.ts:96; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts:42; apps/platform-console/src/app/api/console-auth/callback/route.ts:60; apps/platform-console/src/lib/console-session.ts:63; packages/auth-core/src/index.ts:997; packages/auth-core/src/index.ts:134
- RISK: Merchant: an email-security scanner or browser prefetch that fetches the link burns the token before the user clicks — a reliable self-DoS on magic-link login — and a GET that mints a session is trivially triggerable from any page the victim visits. Console: because the token is stateless, a leaked or logged operator magic link (referrer, proxy log, shared inbox) grants repeated platform-operator session issuance for 15 minutes; only the subsequent TOTP step stands between that and full console access.
- RECOMMENDATION: Merchant: land the session on an interstitial POST/confirm, or at minimum add Turnstile to the consumption step. Console: record consumed magic-link JTIs in a durable store (KV or Postgres) and reject reuse — the operator realm is the highest-privilege realm in the platform and is the one with no single-use enforcement.

**[S3] CSRF is a JS-readable double-submit cookie and a legacy cookie name is still accepted**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `setCsrfCookie` sets `letbuyy_csrf` with `httpOnly: false` so the client can read it (cookies.ts:139-147), and `validateCsrf` accepts either `letbuyy_csrf` or the legacy `lb_csrf` cookie, comparing it to the X-CSRF-Token header (csrf.ts:21-24). The comparison is a plain `===` on values of length >= 16 (:11-14). This is the CSRF layer guarding /switch, /login, /signup, /set-password and /create-store.
- EXPECTED: Double-submit with a JS-readable cookie is a recognized weaker pattern; the /switch route comment itself calls out 'the JS-readable double-submit CSRF cookie' as an aggravating factor in the original ART-06 takeover (switch/route.ts:18-20).
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:143; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/csrf.ts:22; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/csrf.ts:14; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:18
- RISK: Any XSS on an accounts-domain page reads the CSRF token directly and defeats the check. The token is not bound to the session, so a token minted for one session is accepted for another. The still-accepted `lb_csrf` name widens the surface with no stated purpose; earlier governance docs list removing `lb_csrf` as a deletion item.
- RECOMMENDATION: Bind the CSRF token to the session (HMAC of the session id under a dedicated key) so a stolen or foreign token does not validate, and drop the `lb_csrf` fallback. Note that SameSite=strict on the cookie is already carrying most of the real protection here.

**[S3] A hard-coded developer identity is granted a full auth context whenever SUPABASE_URL contains 'localhost'**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `getInternalSessionAuthContext` falls through to `isLocalSupabaseEnvironment(c.env)` and, if true, returns an authenticated context for user id 'dev-local-user-id' with a specific real email address (middleware/auth.ts:158-166). `isLocalSupabaseEnvironment` is a substring test: `url.includes('localhost') || url.includes('127.0.0.1')` (lib/supabase.ts:150-153). The fallback triggers only after the internal HMAC session verification has already been attempted and thrown or returned nothing.
- EXPECTED: Development conveniences that mint an auth context should key off an explicit opt-in flag (the repo already has LETBUYY_ENABLE_DEV_AUTH_BYPASS, used correctly in identity-platform and platform-console), not off a substring of a URL.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:158; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:162; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:150; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/dev-auth-bypass.ts:20
- RISK: The gate is substring-based, so any SUPABASE_URL containing the literal 'localhost' anywhere — a tunnel hostname, a proxy alias, a misconfigured staging value — silently enables an unauthenticated identity on the gateway. It also hard-codes a personal email address into shipped source, which is both a privacy leak and a confusing artifact in a production bundle.
- RECOMMENDATION: Require `LETBUYY_ENABLE_DEV_AUTH_BYPASS === 'true'` in addition to the localhost check (matching the pattern already used at dev-auth-bypass.ts:20-24), and replace the hard-coded email with a placeholder such as dev@localhost.

**[S3] /switch destroys the caller's session before validating that the target user exists and is verified, stranding the caller on failure**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: The handler calls `await destroySession(currentToken)` at switch/route.ts:84, then resolves the target via /auth/custom/users/by-email at :87, then returns 404 if the user is missing or unverified at :102-104 — without restoring or clearing the now-revoked session cookie. A thrown error between :84 and :113 leaves the same state via the catch-all 500 at :138-141.
- EXPECTED: Session destruction should be the last mutation, after every precondition has passed, or the response should clear the stale cookie on the failure path.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:84; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:102; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:138; apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:113
- RISK: A merchant whose target account exists in the lb_accounts cookie but was since deleted or un-verified is logged out of their working account and left holding a cookie pointing at a revoked session, with a 404 body that does not explain it. This is a self-inflicted logout, not a security hole, but it is the kind of confusing state that generates support load during onboarding.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move `destroySession(currentToken)` to after the target-user resolution and verification succeed (i.e. just before createSession at :110), or call clearSessionCookie on every failure path after :84.

**[INFO] MOCK_JWKS placeholder is genuinely gone — OIDC serves 404 flag-off and 501 unconfigured (ART-07 / IDN-R6 satisfied in source)**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: A repo-wide search for MOCK_JWKS across .ts/.tsx/.mjs/.js (excluding node_modules/.next/worktrees/archive) returns exactly one hit — a governance rule string in a test fixture (packages/lcis-tracking/test/timeline.test.ts:45). `/jwks` and `/.well-known/jwks.json` both delegate to `oidcSkeletonResponse('jwks', request)` which returns 404 when FEATURE_IDENTITY_PROVIDER is off (oidc-disabled.ts:71-86,:100) and 501 IDENTITY_JWKS_NOT_CONFIGURED when no real JWKS is configured (:129-145). When a JWKS IS configured, `sanitizePublicJwk` strips any key containing private material (d/p/q/dp/dq/qi/oth/k) and rejects non-ES256/EdDSA algs (:32-46), and there is a real behavioral test proving a private JWK is refused (oidc-jwks.test.ts:63).
- EXPECTED: ART-07 / IDN-R6: OIDC is canonical-but-deferred; until real it serves 404, never a placeholder JWKS.
- EVIDENCE: apps/identity-platform/src/lib/identity-foundation/oidc-disabled.ts:71; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/identity-foundation/oidc-disabled.ts:129; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/identity-foundation/oidc-disabled.ts:40; apps/identity-platform/src/app/jwks/route.ts:3; apps/identity-platform/src/app/.well-known/jwks.json/route.ts:3; apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/oidc-jwks.test.ts:63; packages/lcis-tracking/test/timeline.test.ts:45
- RISK: None at this SHA. Residual exposure is flag-driven: enabling FEATURE_IDENTITY_PROVIDER together with FEATURE_NEW_KEY_SERVICE would publish whatever LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PUBLIC_JWKS contains, with a 300s public cache header (:156) — so both flags must remain production-forbidden until the token cutover completes.
- RECOMMENDATION: No action. Keep FEATURE_IDENTITY_PROVIDER and FEATURE_NEW_KEY_SERVICE production-forbidden and consider a validator asserting both default to false, so this stays true.

**[INFO] The anon-writable super_admin_allowlist is re-scoped in migration source; applied state is unverified**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: The 000_baseline policy was `CREATE POLICY service_role_manage_allowlist ON public.super_admin_allowlist USING (true) WITH CHECK (true)` with no TO clause — which in PostgreSQL applies to PUBLIC, i.e. anon-writable (000_baseline.sql:26680). Migration 163 drops and recreates it as `FOR ALL TO service_role` (163_d2:185-189), re-scopes the two super-admin policies to `TO authenticated USING (public.is_super_admin())` (:191-200), and executes `REVOKE ALL ON TABLE public.super_admin_allowlist FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` (:216). The migration's own header names the root cause correctly: policies without an explicit TO clause apply to PUBLIC (:1-4).
- EXPECTED: ART-06 / SEC-R8: the anon-writable super_admin_allowlist is an S0 that must be closed. `public.is_super_admin()` reads this table (000_baseline.sql:4424-4427), so write access to it is write access to platform super-admin.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:26680; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:185; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:191; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:216; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:4424; database/supabase/migrations/163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql:1
- RISK: The fix is correct in source, but a migration file is not proof of applied state. If migration 163 has not run against production, the baseline PUBLIC policy is still live and any anon key holder can INSERT themselves into super_admin_allowlist and become a platform super-admin. This is the single highest-consequence open question in this dimension and it is answerable only with live DB access, which this audit does not have.
- RECOMMENDATION: Ops must confirm applied state: run `SELECT polname, polroles::regrole[] FROM pg_policy WHERE polrelid = 'public.super_admin_allowlist'::regclass` against production and verify no policy has {public} or {anon} in polroles, plus `\dp public.super_admin_allowlist` showing no anon/authenticated grants. Treat this as a launch-blocking verification, not a code task.

### Adversarial verdicts (5)


**OVERSTATED** — Customer-realm HS256 signing secret is distributed to six runtimes — verification key is the forgery key (R-14 / IDN-R1 / SEC-R7). Claimed S1 / VERIFIED-CONFIG: PRODUCTION_CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET is pushed to the api-gateway Worker (production-deploy.yml:317) AND injected into all five Vercel Next apps (production-deploy.yml:444, matrix :400-409), two of which mint customer sessions with it; a read-only compromise of ANY of six runtimes yields the ability to mint a valid customer session for any customer of any store.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Two runtimes mint customer sessions with the same shared HS256 secret, so the verification key is the forgery key (violates Constitution R-14 / ART-05, AGENTS.md IDN-R1 / SEC-R7). VERIFIED-SOURCE: customer sessions are HS256 HMAC where signing and verification use the identical secret (packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts:52,:86). Two runtimes mint on live, mounted paths — api-gateway via issueCustomerSession (routes/storefront/customer-registration.ts:141; routes/storefront/customer-engagement-routes.ts:610,:770; mounted index.ts:752 -> storefront.ts:1312 -> public-routes.ts:1227) and apps/storefront-runtime via createSession (src/lib/session.ts:34,:56, called from app/api/customer/{login,register,set-password}/route.ts). EXECUTION_LEDGER.md:241 records the storefront local re-mint as deliberate. The api-gateway Worker's copy of the secret is VERIFIED-CONFIG (.github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:317, wrangler secret put); storefront-runtime must hold it or the app throws (session.ts:34). Constitution ART-05:174,:176 and R-14:411 and AGENTS.md:262 (IDN-R1) forbid any runtime other than identity-platform minting tokens for the buyer/customer realm, and AGENTS.md:233 (SEC-R7) forbids a shared symmetric signing secret; SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:53 waives only the merchant-session drain, not this. scripts/validators/identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:11 allowlists customer-session.ts and its regexes cannot match the raw WebCrypto HMAC, so the green gate is not evidence of conformance. Impact: read-only compromise or log spill at EITHER minting runtime yields forgery of a valid customer session for any customer of any store; routes/storefront.ts:70-76 makes that session the sole gate on /customer/orders, /customer/profile, /customer/addresses and wishlist, and the customer_id needed is retrievable via /customer/by-email (customer-engagement-routes.ts:633), gated only by the likewise-shared INTERNAL_API_SECRET (production-deploy.yml:448). CORRECTION to the original claim: the assertion that the secret is "injected into all five Vercel Next apps" at production-deploy.yml:444 is not established — that step runs `vercel deploy --prod` with no --prebuilt, no -e/-b, and no `vercel env add`, so the step-level env: block reaches only the five GitHub Actions runner environments, not the deployed Vercel runtimes. Distribution to marketing-site, merchant-control-plane, identity-platform and platform-console is declared by a class-wide config list (config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:60; config/env/ownership/runtime-env-ownership.json:5-19) that is demonstrably a permissive superset — the same list's LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK:62 IS scoped per-app at :447 — and those four apps contain zero source references to the variable; their actual Vercel project env is out-of-repo and UNKNOWN. So "six runtimes" is INFERRED, not verified; two are proven. Additional unreported instance: services/api-gateway/src/routes/export.ts:174 reuses CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET as a fallback CSV-export signing secret.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The CORE is confirmed and is in fact established from source, not merely config. (1) HS256 symmetric signing where the verification key IS the signing key: packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts:52 sets alg 'HS256' and verifyCustomerSession recomputes the same HMAC with the same secret at :86. (2) TWO runtimes mint on live, mounted paths — not merely define a function. Gateway: issueCustomerSession at routes/storefront/customer-registration.ts:141-143, routes/storefront/customer-engagement-routes.ts:610-615 and :770-775; I traced the mount chain services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:752 -> routes/storefront.ts:1312 -> routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:1227 -> registerStorefrontCustomerEngagementRoutes. Storefront-runtime: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/session.ts:34 reads process.env.CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET and :56 issues via createHostOnlyShopperIdentityProvider, called from app/api/customer/login/route.ts:92, app/api/customer/register/route.ts:91, app/api/customer/set-password/route.ts:73. EXECUTION_LEDGER.md:241 records the storefront proxy "re-mints locally (createSession)" as deliberate design. (3) The governing law squarely covers the buyer realm and is not waived: Constitution ART-05 at :174/:176 ("sole issuer ... for all three realms" incl. buyer/customer; "Forbids ... Any service minting tokens other than identity-platform. A shared symmetric signing secret across services"), R-14 at :411; AGENTS.md:262 (IDN-R1) and :233 (SEC-R7). SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:53 waives only the merchant-session / gateway-mint drain telemetry, NOT the customer realm. (4) The cited validator does not exonerate: scripts/validators/identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:11 explicitly allowlists packages/auth-core/src/customer-session.ts, and its signingPatterns (:17-22 — SignJWT / jwt.sign / setProtectedHeader alg HS*) cannot match the raw WebCrypto HMAC actually used, so the green gate is not evidence of conformance. (5) Blast radius is real: routes/storefront.ts:70-76 gates /customer/orders, /customer/profile, /customer/addresses, /customer/wishlist solely on requireCustomerSession (middleware/customer-session.ts:58), and the customer_id needed to forge a token is obtainable from /customer/by-email (customer-engagement-routes.ts:633), which is gated only by INTERNAL_API_SECRET — itself shared across the same runtimes at production-deploy.yml:448.

WHERE THE CLAIM IS OVERSTATED — the "six runtimes / injected into all five Vercel Next apps" breadth is NOT verified, and the mechanism asserted at production-deploy.yml:444 is wrong. The deploy step at :443 runs `pnpm dlx vercel@latest deploy --prod --token ... --scope ...` with no `--prebuilt`, no `-e`/`-b` flag, and there is no `vercel env add` anywhere in .github/workflows or scripts/. Without --prebuilt, Vercel builds remotely from the project's own stored environment; a GitHub Actions step-level `env:` block is not forwarded into the Vercel build or the deployed runtime. (The staging path, deploy-staging.yml:162-164, uses `vercel pull` + `build` + `deploy --prebuilt` — env still comes from the pulled project config, not the CI shell.) So line :444 verifiably materializes PRODUCTION_CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET into five GitHub Actions RUNNER environments; it does not inject it into five app runtimes. The supporting config is likewise a permissive superset, not a per-app grant: config/env/staging-production-secret-matrix.json:60 lists CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET under a shared nextApps.server block whose owners are all five apps (:29-35), and config/env/ownership/runtime-env-ownership.json:5-19 treats all five as one env class with one schema — but that same shared list contains LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK at :62, which the workflow demonstrably DOES scope to identity-platform only (:447), proving the list is not a faithful per-app grant. The file also shows per-app scoping is expressible (platformConsoleOnly, :78-84). Only apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/session.ts:34 reads the variable in app source; marketing-site, merchant-control-plane, identity-platform and platform-console have zero source references (rg over apps/ services/ packages/). The actual Vercel project environment is out-of-repo, so whether those four hold the secret is UNKNOWN, not VERIFIED. Verified holders are two: api-gateway (VERIFIED-CONFIG, wrangler secret put at :317) and storefront-runtime (strongly inferred — session.ts:34 throws if absent, so it must be provisioned for the app to function).

NET: severity stays S1 — even bounded to the two proven runtimes, a second issuer mints buyer sessions with a shared symmetric secret whose verification key is the forgery key, gating all buyer PII across every tenant, in direct violation of a MUST with no waiver. Evidence state should be raised to VERIFIED-SOURCE for the dual-issuer violation (the claim under-labelled its strongest half as VERIFIED-CONFIG) while the six-runtime distribution is downgraded to INFERRED/UNKNOWN. Aggravating fact the original claim missed: services/api-gateway/src/routes/export.ts:174 reuses CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET as a fallback CSV-export signing secret (`INTERNAL_API_SECRET || CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET || AUTH_SECRET`), cross-purposing the same key across two signing domains — an independent SEC-R7 instance.

**OVERSTATED** — Five live `|| AUTH_SECRET` cross-domain fallbacks remain, and AUTH_SECRET also signs the cookie that is half the /switch account-takeover guard (claimed S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: One symmetric AUTH_SECRET value is deployed to six distinct trust domains — the api-gateway Worker and all five Next apps including marketing-site (.github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:315,443) — violating SEC-R7 (AGENTS.md:233), and that same value is the HS256 signing key for the `lb_accounts` cookie (apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:13,176-180) whose membership check is guard 2 of 2 on POST /api/auth/switch (apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:69-81, guard 1 at :56-63 being merely "hold any valid session"). Disclosure of AUTH_SECRET from any one of those six runtimes, combined with a self-registered account, therefore restores the ART-06 account takeover against any email_verified merchant account (route.ts:110-113 mints the target's session). Five `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallbacks remain in source (checkout.ts:88, cart-routes.ts:307, media.ts:60, export.ts:174, webhook-service/index.ts:584) plus a direct use at internal-console.ts:441, and none is covered by scripts/validators/session-security-validator.mjs (a hand-listed file allowlist at :28,42,53,57,65,75), so the gate stays green. CORRECTIONS to the original claim: the webhook fallback (index.ts:584) is unreachable dead code — production-deploy.yml:345 gives that Worker only INTERNAL_API_SECRET and no wrangler.toml var supplies AUTH_SECRET — so "forged webhook dispatch" is not an available consequence and webhook-service is not an AUTH_SECRET holder; export.ts:174 is never reached because INTERNAL_API_SECRET is deployed in both environments (:322, staging-deploy.yml:89); media.ts:60 IS live-selected in production (MEDIA_UPLOAD_SIGNING_SECRET is in no deploy workflow) but its ticket has no verifier (the /upload router at upload.ts:99-106,146 authorizes by session + site ownership), so "forged media upload tickets" is inert; internal-console.ts:441's `lb_imp_` impersonation token has no verifier anywhere in the repo (only mint site :448), so it too is inert; and the checkout handoff fallback is NOT reached in production (CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET deployed at :320) though it IS reached in staging (staging-deploy.yml sets AUTH_SECRET at :83 and no CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET), and even a forged handoff cannot tamper with amounts because checkout.ts:249-270 recomputes the cart digest from the DB row. The residual defect is key-separation and blast-radius collapse plus a verbatim SEC-R7 violation the gate cannot see — not a directly exploitable vulnerability. Escalates to S1 if the GitHub environment secret PRODUCTION_CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET is unpopulated (making the production checkout-handoff key AUTH_SECRET too) or if any of the six runtimes has a known environment-disclosure path; both are HUMAN-REQUIRED to determine.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-CONFIG
- REASONING: EVERY CODE CITATION IS ACCURATE. I confirmed all five fallbacks verbatim at this SHA: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:88 and services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:307 (`CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET || c.env.AUTH_SECRET || STORE_ACCESS_SIGNING_KEY`), services/api-gateway/src/routes/media.ts:60, services/api-gateway/src/routes/export.ts:174, services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:584. internal-console.ts:441 does use `c.env.AUTH_SECRET` directly. cookies.ts:13 reads AUTH_SECRET and cookies.ts:176-180 signs `lb_accounts` HS256 with it; the /switch guard-2 chain is real (getAccounts() at route.ts:69, isProvenAccount at :70-72, guard 1 = any valid session at :56-63, then createSession(target) at :110). AUTH_SECRET is deployed to the gateway Worker (production-deploy.yml:315) and to all five Next apps (:443, matrix at :400-409). AGENTS.md:233 names the webhook `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallback DEAD verbatim, and apps/platform-console/src/lib/console-session.ts:24 has no fallback. I also found evidence the auditor MISSED that strengthens the finding: scripts/validators/session-security-validator.mjs:28,42,53,57,65,75 is a hand-listed file allowlist (auth-core, internal-auth.ts, internal-secret.ts, app-store/shared.ts, four api/client.ts files) that covers NONE of these six sites — the gate is green while SEC-R7 is violated.

WHAT I REFUTED — four of the five named forgery consequences do not exist. (1) WEBHOOK: production-deploy.yml:345 sets only INTERNAL_API_SECRET on the webhook worker; AUTH_SECRET appears in no webhook deploy step and in no `[vars]` block of services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml. `env.AUTH_SECRET` is undefined in that runtime, so index.ts:584's fallback is unreachable dead code — a real SEC-R7 textual violation with zero exploitability, and the webhook-service is NOT one of the "six AUTH_SECRET holders." (2) EXPORT: INTERNAL_API_SECRET is deployed to the gateway in production (:322) and staging (staging-deploy.yml:89), so AUTH_SECRET is a third-position fallback never selected; "forged export authorization" is not available. (3) MEDIA: the fallback IS live-selected (MEDIA_UPLOAD_SIGNING_SECRET appears in no deploy workflow — only config/env/templates/worker.env.example:57, config/env/schemas/worker.schema.json:385, scripts/validators/environment-validator.mjs:70), so the production media key genuinely IS AUTH_SECRET — but the ticket it mints is never verified: `x-letbuyy-media-upload-token` and `upload_token_hash` occur only at media.ts:317 and :293, and the actual /upload router (mounted index.ts:815) authorizes by session + verifyUploadStoreOwnership (upload.ts:99-106,146). Forging it buys nothing. (4) IMPERSONATION: `lb_imp_` appears ONLY at its mint site internal-console.ts:448 across the entire repo — no verifier exists, so internal-console.ts:441 is inert today.

WHAT I PARTIALLY REFUTED — CHECKOUT. production-deploy.yml:320 deploys CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET, so in production the `|| AUTH_SECRET` fallback is NOT reached (assuming the GH secret is populated — unknowable from the repo). It IS reached in STAGING: staging-deploy.yml:77-100 sets AUTH_SECRET (:83) but no CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET, so in staging the checkout-handoff HMAC key and the lb_accounts cookie key are literally the same value. Separately, even a forged handoff is not a price-tampering primitive: checkout.ts:249-270 requires a matching carts row on id/store_id/session_id and recomputes expectedCartDigest server-side from the DB row.

WHAT SURVIVES, INTACT AND SERIOUS. Independent of any fallback, one symmetric AUTH_SECRET value is deployed to six distinct trust domains (gateway Worker + marketing-site, identity-platform, merchant-control-plane, storefront-runtime, platform-console — :315 and :443), that value is the HS256 key for lb_accounts, and lb_accounts membership is guard 2 of 2 on /switch where guard 1 is only "you hold some valid session." The takeover chain is mechanically confirmed: attacker self-registers (satisfies guard 1 in their own client, where httpOnly at cookies.ts:62 is irrelevant), forges lb_accounts with AUTH_SECRET, POSTs /switch with any email_verified target, and route.ts:110-113 mints and sets that target's session. So AUTH_SECRET disclosure from ANY of six runtimes — including marketing-site, which has no identity role whatsoever — restores ART-06.

CALIBRATION. The "current state" and "expected state" sections are accurate; the "risk claimed" section is where the overstatement lives. S1 is not earned because there is no unauthenticated exploit path — every consequence is gated behind prior AUTH_SECRET disclosure, and the enumerated forgery payoffs are mostly inert. S2 is the defensible level. Evidence state drops from VERIFIED-SOURCE to VERIFIED-CONFIG because the load-bearing part of the corrected claim (which runtimes actually hold AUTH_SECRET, which fallbacks are actually selected) comes from workflow configuration, which is not proof of deployed behavior; the code sites themselves remain VERIFIED-SOURCE. Note also that apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts is a readFileSync+indexOf source-string test (:41-67), not behavioral — the guard is VERIFIED-SOURCE, never VERIFIED-TEST. Minor citation error: the switch route is at apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts, not the cited src/lib/auth/switch/route.ts.

**CONFIRMED** — The claim→tenant-GUC bridge has ZERO production call sites; all 116 gateway files authenticate tenancy application-side over blanket service_role
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The claim→tenant-GUC bridge is dead code at this SHA: `withTenantTx` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15-35) is reached only through `withResolvedTenantTx` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67-73), which has zero callers anywhere in the repository — the only other occurrence is a source-text `assert.match` in services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts:42. No runtime code in services/, apps/ or packages/ sets `app.site_id` or `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` outside that unused helper. Consequently the app_tenant role, the per-transaction GUC and the FORCE-RLS policies created by database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql are not load-bearing for any request; migration 196_theme_rls_tenant_hardening.sql:30-36 itself records that service_role carries BYPASSRLS which overrides FORCE, so the gateway is unaffected by them. All tenant decisions are made application-side over a blanket service_role PostgREST client — 116 files / 993 uses of `getSupabaseAdmin` under services/api-gateway/src — behind two controls: `verifySiteOwnership` (lib/auth-user.ts:58-85, itself a service_role SELECT on sites) and the `createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient` proxy, which asserts only that a tenant COLUMN NAME appears in a predicate and never that its VALUE matches the session (tenant-scoped.ts:52-77). The guard is further incomplete: it wraps only `.from()`, so the 77 `.rpc(` calls bypass it, three sites construct an unguarded service client directly (middleware/auth.ts:133, routes/auth.ts:1342, routes/employee.ts:235), and the registry covers 265 of ~414 tables. `scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:10-21` cannot catch any of this: it substring-checks two helper files and regex-scans only apps/. Minor correction to the original wording: 116 files use the service-role client, but not all of them perform a tenancy check — some public/platform routes perform none, which is the exposure rather than a mitigation.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: Every cited line verified at 5af69e0c. (1) Zero call sites: `rg -n "withTenantTx|withResolvedTenantTx" -g '*.ts' -g '*.tsx' -g '*.mjs' -g '*.js'` (excluding node_modules/.claude/worktrees/graphify-out) yields exactly 5 hits — the definition (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15), the wrapper (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:16,67,72), and one source-text assertion (services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts:42). No route, middleware, worker or other service calls it. 16+ route files DO import resolveTenantContextForOwnedSite/ForOrder from tenant-context.ts (routes/orders.ts:10, routes/payouts.ts:6, routes/settlements.ts:14, etc.) — proving the module is live but the transaction seam specifically is not. (2) No alternate GUC path: `rg "app\.site_id|set_current_site_id|SET LOCAL ROLE" services apps packages --glob '!*.test.*' --glob '!__tests__/**'` returns only tenant-transaction.ts:10,29 and packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:555. (3) Service-role breadth: 116 files, 993 occurrences of getSupabaseAdmin in services/api-gateway/src; getSupabaseAdminFromEnv (lib/supabase.ts:13-14) wraps createSupabaseServiceClient in the guard proxy only. (4) Validator scope: scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:10-21 substring-checks lib/supabase.ts and lib/tenant-transaction.ts, then regex-scans only entries under apps/ (or `entry.extra`) for service_role tokens — it never inspects services/** route call sites, exactly as claimed. (5) Guard proxy weakness confirmed: tenant-scoped.ts:56-58 `if (typeof args[0] === 'string' && columns.includes(args[0])) state.scoped = true` matches the column NAME only; the value is never compared to the session. REFUTATION ATTEMPTS FAILED AND CORROBORATED INSTEAD: (a) No later migration restores the path — and database/supabase/migrations/196_theme_rls_tenant_hardening.sql:30-36 states as an empirically verified fact ("Proven on the local database with a throwaway forced table") that postgres and service_role carry BYPASSRLS which "takes precedence over FORCE", concluding "The gateway (service_role via getSupabaseAdmin) ... [is] therefore unaffected" — i.e. migration 164's FORCE-RLS + app_tenant policies are inert for gateway traffic, by the repo's own later admission. (b) The one production getPooledSql consumer (routes/storefront.ts:647,657,672,691) issues raw sql.unsafe() with no SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant and no GUC, so even the pooled path is not the bridge. (c) tests/executable/*-postgres.test.mjs exercise real Postgres RLS but CREATE OR REPLACE their own current_site_id()/set_current_site_id() in a scratch DB and SET ROLE app_user manually — they prove the policies work when the GUC is set, never that any request sets it. (d) No dynamic re-export or barrel indirection exists. UNDERSTATED ASPECTS (strengthen the claim): three gateway sites bypass even the guard proxy with a raw createSupabaseServiceClient(c.env) — middleware/auth.ts:133, routes/auth.ts:1342, routes/employee.ts:235; 77 `.rpc(` calls bypass the from()-only proxy entirely; the tenant registry covers 265 tables (tenant-tables.registry.ts) against ~414 in the schema. ONLY IMPRECISION: "all 116 gateway files authenticate tenancy" — 116 files USE getSupabaseAdmin; not all perform an ownership check (266 verifySiteOwnership occurrences across the tree, and several public/platform routes perform none), which is the risk rather than a mitigation. Severity S1 holds: this is a total conformance failure of a named Constitutional invariant with two application-side compensating controls still standing (verifySiteOwnership at lib/auth-user.ts:58-85 and the predicate-presence proxy), and no exploitable cross-tenant read is demonstrated within this claim itself — so not S0. Evidence state VERIFIED-SOURCE is earned for the call-site, file-count, validator-scope and proxy-semantics assertions; the downstream consequence that service_role bypasses RLS in the live database rests on migration 196's header (in-repo, empirically stated for a local DB) plus standard Supabase role behaviour, which is INFERRED for production, not live-verified.

**OVERSTATED** — Merchant session rotation and reuse-detection are dead code — both call sites pass rotate:false, so a 30-day bearer never rotates (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Merchant session token rotation and token-reuse detection are implemented in the database but never reachable in production: `validate_and_rotate_merchant_session` gates both behind `p_rotate` (175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:95-117 rotation, :37-56 reuse-detection), and the only two TypeScript callers pass `{ rotate: false }` (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:136, services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:387) — introduced that way by the very commit that shipped the feature (8ceb83d1). `session_token_rotation_events` therefore stays empty, the `reuse_detected` revocation branch and the `rotated_session_token` cookie-refresh path (routes/auth.ts:410 → apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts:25-28) are dead, and `MERCHANT_SESSION_ROTATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 10*60` (merchant-session.ts:39) is never consulted. Merchant sessions are 30-day tokens (auth-core:127, identity-issued-session.ts:5, cookies.ts:85) with no rotation window that would surface a cloned cookie. Two corrections to the original framing: (1) only routes/auth.ts:387 is the actual defect — middleware/auth.ts:136 has no channel to return a new cookie to the browser, so `rotate:false` there is required, not a bug; (2) enabling rotation is not a one-line fix, because three of the four consumers of `GET /auth/custom/session` discard `rotated_session_token` (identity-platform lib/auth/session.ts:45-73, mobile-bff index.ts:248, api-client index.ts:3888) and because the RPC's rotation mints an opaque `gen_random_bytes(32)` token (175:98) that would displace the identity-issued ES256 session token required in production (routes/auth.ts:1120-1139). Severity is S2, not S1: this is a missing detection control behind live compensating controls — the 8h idle timeout does fire (175:84-93; sessions.last_active DEFAULT now() at 000_baseline.sql:11712), logout/logout-everywhere revocation works and is enforced on every request (routes/auth.ts:1189,1219 → 175:69-72), the cookie is httpOnly and HMAC-wrapped in transit to the gateway (auth-core:621-646), and refunds/payouts require step-up (payments.ts:497-506, payouts.ts:576-585). No later migration re-enables rotation; the session-security validator (scripts/validators/session-security-validator.mjs:90-121) is grep-only and the sole rotation test passes `p_rotate=true` by hand against a synthetic fixture (tests/executable/foundation-wave4-session-postgres.test.mjs:98,112), which is why the gap survived a green gate.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: THE CORE FACT IS CONFIRMED. I could not refute it.

1) The RPC really does implement the controls. `database/supabase/migrations/175_fix_session_rpc_search_path.sql:37-56` is the reuse-detection + revoke-on-reuse branch; `:95-117` is the rotation branch (gated on `IF p_rotate AND last_rotated_at <= now - interval`); backing table + `UNIQUE (old_token_hash)` at `161_foundation_session_security.sql:21-33`; header intent at `161:1-2`. I checked for the classic "a later migration fixed it" failure: `rg -l "validate_and_rotate|public.sessions" database/supabase/migrations/` returns only 000/107/159/161/175 — **175 is the final definition at this SHA**, and no later migration re-enables rotation or changes the default.

2) The flag really is off on every live path. `rg -n "validateMerchantSessionToken"` over apps/services/packages returns exactly two call sites, both `{ rotate: false }`: `services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts:136` and `services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts:387`. `merchant-session.ts:69` passes `p_rotate: options.rotate === true`, so both send `false` and the `MERCHANT_SESSION_ROTATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 10*60` constant (`merchant-session.ts:39`) is unreachable. Consequence chain verified: `rotated` is always false → `rotated_session_token` at `routes/auth.ts:410` is always `undefined` → `apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts:25-28` is dead → `session_token_rotation_events` is never written → the `reuse_detected` branch (175:44-56) never fires.

3) `git log -L 130,140:services/api-gateway/src/middleware/auth.ts` shows `{ rotate: false }` was introduced by 8ceb83d1 "feat(foundation): complete wave 4 session security" — the same commit that shipped the RPC. Rotation has never been enabled.

4) 30-day lifetime confirmed independently three ways: DB row `expires_at = now + AUTH_SESSION_DURATION_MS` (`routes/auth.ts:1143`, `packages/auth-core/src/index.ts:127` = 30d); identity-issued JWT `MERCHANT_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS = 30*24*60*60` (`apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/identity-issued-session.ts:5,66`); cookie `maxAge: 30*24*60*60` (`cookies.ts:85`).

5) The "green gate" traps the claim correctly names are real: `scripts/validators/session-security-validator.mjs:90-121` only greps the migration for the strings `reuse_detected`/`idle_timeout` and greps `routes/auth.ts` for `rotated_session_token` — it never asserts a caller passes `rotate:true`. And the only test that exercises rotation, `tests/executable/foundation-wave4-session-postgres.test.mjs:98,112`, passes `true` explicitly against a hand-built fixture schema in Docker. So there is no VERIFIED-TEST covering the live path.

WHERE THE CLAIM IS OVERSTATED.

A) The framing "both call sites pass rotate:false" implies both are defective. One of them is not. `getInternalSessionAuthContext` (`middleware/auth.ts:117-169`) returns an `AuthContext` and has no channel to hand a new cookie back to the browser; flipping it to `rotate:true` would silently replace `sessions.token` and lock the merchant out on the next request. `rotate:false` there is load-bearing and correct. The real defect is one site: `routes/auth.ts:387`.

B) The remediation is materially larger than "flip a flag", which the claim's severity assumes. `GET /auth/custom/session` has four consumers and only one propagates the rotated token: `apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts:25-28` handles it, but `apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/session.ts:45-73` (`validateSession`, used by `/api/auth/switch:57`, `/api/auth/stores:19`, `/api/auth/create-store:39`), the mobile-bff passthrough (`services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:248`) and `packages/typescript/api-client/src/index.ts:3888` all discard it. Enabling rotation today would log those callers out every 10 minutes. Additionally, in production the token must be identity-issued ES256 (`routes/auth.ts:1120-1139`, `IDENTITY_ISSUER_REQUIRED` 503; verifier at `lib/identity-session-verifier.ts:42-108`), while the RPC's rotation mints `gen_random_bytes(32)` hex (`175:98`) — rotation would replace an identity-issued token with a gateway-minted opaque one, conflicting with the sole-asymmetric-issuer invariant. This is an unfinished design, not a forgotten boolean.

C) S1 is not earned. This is a missing defence-in-depth *detection* control, not an auth bypass; it requires prior token compromise. Compensating controls I verified as live: the 8h idle timeout does fire (`175:84-93`, and `sessions.last_active DEFAULT now()` at `000_baseline.sql:11712` plus the unconditional update at `175:119-124`, so the branch is reachable from session creation onward); revocation works and is checked on every request (logout `routes/auth.ts:1189`, logout-everywhere `:1219` set `revoked_at`; RPC checks it at `175:69-72`); the cookie is httpOnly (`cookies.ts:61`) and is never presented directly to the gateway — the app server re-wraps it in an HMAC-signed internal header (`packages/auth-core/src/index.ts:621-646`); and the highest-value actions are separately step-up gated (refund `routes/payments.ts:497-506`, payout `routes/payouts.ts:576-585`). Note the shape-only cookie check at `auth-core:544-570` does NOT verify the JWT, so it neither helps nor hurts here. S2 is the defensible severity.

D) The claim's "Expected state" leans on migration 161's own header comment. That is an in-repo intent comment, not law — I grepped `GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md` for "rotat"/"session": the Constitution keeps the session-security validator (line 152) but nowhere mandates merchant session rotation or reuse detection. So this is a broken-own-design finding, not a Constitutional violation, and evidence_state stays VERIFIED-SOURCE (not GOVERNED).

**OVERSTATED** — The ART-06 /switch fix is present in source but has NO behavioral test — every guard is asserted by source-text ordering only (claimed S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE).
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The ART-06 /switch fix is present and correctly ordered in apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:35-110, but it has no behavioral test: the only coverage is apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts, which asserts source-TEXT presence and ordering via readFileSync + indexOf/assert.match (:24-67), and the file's own doc-comment concedes the 401/403/200 route contract test is unwritten (:20-22) — corroborated by docs/auth/2026-06-09-identity-execution-recovery/TEST_COVERAGE_REPORT.md:15-19, which lists those tests as planned. No test anywhere in the repository invokes a Next route handler (`grep -rln "await POST(" --include="*.test.ts" --include="*.test.mjs" --include="*.spec.ts" tests/ apps/ packages/` → zero hits), so neither denial path is exercised.

Two corrections to the original finding. First, it UNDERSTATED the governance side: scripts/governance/identity-security-gate.mjs is not merely a weak substring gate, it is dead code — `grep -rn "identity-security-gate"` returns no hits in package.json, turbo.json, any .github/workflows file, or scripts/validate-governance.mjs, and its own header at :10 says only "Wire into CI". The same is true of scripts/validators/frozen-path-override-gate.mjs:16, which nominally locks this exact route path behind a FROZEN-OVERRIDE human trailer but is likewise unreferenced, with no .husky directory and no installed .git/hooks. Both contribute zero signal.

Second, it OVERSTATED the risk: a signal does exist for coarse regression. auth-switch-guard.test.ts runs in CI via .github/workflows/ci.yml:126 (`pnpm turbo test --filter="...[HEAD^1]"`, turbo.json:38) whenever identity-platform changes, and a wholesale revert to the pre-fix handler fails its assertions at :48 and :62. The precise, defensible gap is that only a LOGIC-PRESERVING-TEXT subversion escapes — e.g. weakening `if (!currentToken || !currentSession)` to `if (!currentToken)` while leaving the `await validateSession(currentToken)` expression in place passes both the test (which only compares indexOf positions at :49-50) and the gate (which only requires the `validateSession(` substring). Note also that the current decrypt path fails CLOSED, not open: getAccounts/decryptAccounts return [] on verify failure (apps/identity-platform/src/lib/auth/cookies.ts:185-201), and `[].some(...)` yields 403 at route.ts:73-81. Finally, no in-repo client calls this endpoint (the hits in apps/identity-platform/src/app/select/page.tsx:125,136 are JS `switch` statements), so the route remains HTTP-reachable by an attacker but has no product surface driving refactors. Net: a real S2 assurance gap — a correct fix whose only live safeguard is a text-shape test — not an S1 defect and not a total absence of regression signal.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: CORE FACTS CONFIRMED. apps/identity-platform/src/app/api/auth/switch/route.ts:35-110 implements the guards in the claimed order (rate-limit :35-41, CSRF :44-46, valid-session-or-401 :56-63, isProvenAccount-or-403 :69-81, destroySession/lookup/createSession :84-110). apps/identity-platform/src/__tests__/auth-switch-guard.test.ts:1-67 is entirely readFileSync + indexOf/assert.match over source text — including the rate-limit tests at :24-39 which regex rate-limit.ts source. The doc-comment concession is verbatim at :20-22. scripts/governance/identity-security-gate.mjs:26 is a four-element mustContain substring list. `grep -rln "await POST(" --include="*.test.ts" --include="*.test.mjs" --include="*.spec.ts" tests/ apps/ packages/` returns ZERO results repo-wide — no test anywhere invokes a Next route handler, so neither denial path is behaviorally exercised. docs/auth/2026-06-09-identity-execution-recovery/TEST_COVERAGE_REPORT.md:15-19 independently lists the 401/403/200/429 contract tests as planned, not written (REFERENCE-ONLY corroboration).

ONE SUB-ELEMENT IS UNDERSTATED. `grep -rn "identity-security-gate"` and `grep -rn "frozen-path-override-gate"` (excluding node_modules and .claude/worktrees) return zero hits outside the scripts themselves — absent from package.json, turbo.json, all .github/workflows/*, and scripts/validate-governance.mjs. `.husky/` does not exist and `.git/hooks/` contains only samples. So identity-security-gate.mjs never runs, and the file-freeze human-review control that locks this exact path (scripts/validators/frozen-path-override-gate.mjs:16) also never runs. The claim credits the gate as a weak-but-present signal; it is in fact a zero signal.

THREE ELEMENTS ARE OVERSTATED OR WRONG.
(1) "There is no signal that would catch regression of the highest-severity identity defect on record" is refuted. auth-switch-guard.test.ts DOES run in CI: .github/workflows/ci.yml:126 runs `pnpm turbo test --filter="...[HEAD^1]"`, turbo.json:38 defines the `test` task, and apps/identity-platform/package.json `test` runs `tsx --test src/__tests__/*.test.ts`. A wholesale revert to the pre-fix handler fails `assert.ok(currentTokenIndex > -1)` at test :48 and `savedAccountsIndex > -1` at :62. The real gap is narrower: logic-preserving-TEXT subversion, not all regression.
(2) The cookies.ts:189-191 risk framing is imprecise. decryptAccounts returning [] on error currently FAILS CLOSED — `[].some(...)` is false, so route.ts:73-81 returns 403. The cited lines describe existing safe behavior, not a live weakness. Further, the named regression "isProvenAccount default true on cookie-decrypt failure" written naturally as `savedAccounts.length === 0 || savedAccounts.some(...)` would FAIL the test at :57, because the exact string `const isProvenAccount = savedAccounts.some` would be absent (indexOf = -1, so `isProvenAccountIndex > savedAccountsIndex` fails). Only a suffix variant slips through. The claim's OTHER named scenario is valid: changing `if (!currentToken || !currentSession)` to `if (!currentToken)` while keeping the `await validateSession(currentToken)` text passes both test (:49-50 only check textual position) and gate (only checks `validateSession(` substring).
(3) No in-repo caller exists for POST /api/auth/switch — the only non-generated references are the route, the test, and the two dead gates; apps/identity-platform/src/app/select/page.tsx:125,136 are JS `switch` statements, not endpoint calls. This does not remove attacker HTTP reachability, but it means no product surface is driving refactors of this file, lowering regression likelihood.

SEVERITY. The reported defect is an assurance/test-coverage gap on a fix that is present and correct at this SHA. There is no exploitable condition now, and a CI-executing coarse-revert signal exists. S1 is not earned; S2 is the defensible level, sitting at the upper end of S2 because both nominal governance controls for this path are dead code.

EVIDENCE STATE. VERIFIED-SOURCE stands: every element was established from source at 5af69e0c plus exhaustive read-only searches (the absence claims rest on repo-wide greps I ran, not on inference).

### Unknowns
- Whether migrations 161/163/164/175 are actually applied to the production database. All RLS/GUC/session-RPC conclusions are from migration source only; no authorized live DB access. Settled by: `supabase migration list --linked` output or a production `pg_policies`/`pg_proc` dump reviewed by ops.
- Whether LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK and LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PUBLIC_JWKS are actually provisioned in the production Vercel/Cloudflare secret stores. If unset, `createIdentityIssuedMerchantSessionToken` throws in production (identity-issued-session.ts:42-44) and every merchant login fails. Settled by: `wrangler secret list --env production` + Vercel env listing.
- Whether UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/TOKEN are provisioned. Without them auth rate limiting degrades to the CACHE KV namespace and, if that is also absent, fails closed in production (rateLimit.ts:848-885) — i.e. all logins 503. Settled by: provider secret inventory.
- Whether INTERNAL_AUTH_LEGACY_ACCEPT has been set to 'false' anywhere. It defaults ON (internal-auth.ts:40-45), so the shared legacy INTERNAL_API_SECRET is still accepted alongside the per-caller keyring. Settled by: deployed env inspection.
- The actual PBKDF/bcrypt wall-clock cost of bcryptjs at cost 12 inside the deployed Worker, and the configured CPU limit. services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml declares no [limits] block, so the account default applies. Settled by: a load test or `wrangler tail` CPU-time observation on staging.
- Whether `sessions.token` (which stores the raw bearer, including full ES256 JWTs) is excluded from any logical replication, backup export, or read-replica surface. Settled by: DBA review of backup/export scope.
- Whether the `/switch` route-level contract test named in docs/auth/2026-06-09-identity-execution-recovery/TEST_COVERAGE_REPORT.md was ever written elsewhere in the tree — I found no behavioral test invoking the POST handler. Settled by: running the full identity suite and inspecting coverage for switch/route.ts.
- Whether COOKIE_DOMAIN is actually set to `.letbuyy.com` in production for identity-platform (production-deploy.yml:450 sets it for all five apps, including storefront-runtime, whose customer cookie is documented as host-only). Whether storefront-runtime's customer cookie is genuinely host-only at runtime is not provable from source since apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/session.ts:59-65 omits `domain` — but the env var is exported into that build. Settled by: inspecting a live Set-Cookie header on staging.
- Whether the ES256 keypair lives in a real KMS/HSM or is a plain env string. LETBUYY_IDENTITY_PRIVATE_JWK is read via process.env and JSON.parsed (identity-issued-session.ts:19-29), which implies plaintext env custody. Settled by: an ARB/ops statement on key custody and rotation procedure.
- Whether identity-platform emits any structured auth audit event to an external sink. Its runtime.manifest.json claims 'structured redacted identity event logs' and 'auditOwnership: No execution audit ownership', but I found no audit write in apps/identity-platform/src and none in services/api-gateway/src/routes/auth.ts. Settled by: log-sink inspection or a named owner confirming the intended audit path.
