## DIMENSION: flow-commerce

### Narrative summary
Two parallel checkout implementations exist at 5af69e0c and only one is wired to the shipped UI. Path A (LIVE): the storefront React checkout posts to `/api/orders` → `POST /v1/storefront/:subdomain/orders` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:251), which is mounted at services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:752 — twenty-seven lines BEFORE `api.use('/*', authMiddleware)` at index.ts:779 — so it is fully unauthenticated, has no Turnstile/bot gate, and requires NO signed cart handoff. Path B (built, unused by any UI): the ART-14 hosted checkout at `/v1/checkout/*` (index.ts:753). ART-14/R-11 is therefore NOT enforced on the live order-create path. The ART-14 signing/verification code itself is correct: cart-routes.ts:547-566 signs `HMAC-SHA256(secret, {site,cart,token,channel,expires_at,cart_content_digest})` where the digest is SHA-256 over sorted line items with unit_price_minor/line_total_minor plus sorted uppercased coupon codes (packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:510-547); checkout.ts:263-288 RE-COMPUTES the digest from the current DB cart and rejects on mismatch with a timing-safe compare, so a mutated cart genuinely invalidates the handoff — no replay of stale contents. There is no explicit `subtotal` term, only per-line totals. The severe defect is downstream: `POST /v1/checkout/:cartId/confirm` (checkout.ts:638-699) accepts an arbitrary 1-200 char `provider_payment_id` from the shopper, performs NO provider signature check and NO `assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed`, and calls `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger`, which marks `payment_transactions.status='captured'`, sets `orders.payment_status='paid'`, writes `payment_webhook_events(signature_valid=TRUE)` (000_baseline.sql:1479-1508), and posts a canonical ledger transaction crediting `merchant_payable` (168:238) — for money that was never received. The route is publicly reachable in production because `checkoutSigningSecret` falls back to `AUTH_SECRET`/`STORE_ACCESS_SIGNING_KEY` (checkout.ts:88), and outside production it falls back to a hardcoded literal. `checkout.letbuyy.com` has a Terraform CNAME (infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91) but apps/storefront-runtime has no top-level `/checkout` route, and `checkout` is absent from RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS and RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:27,46) — a merchant can register the subdomain that the hosted checkout origin resolves to. Inventory: reserve→commit is collapsed into one transaction (166:134 reserve with TTL 1200s, 166:221 commit immediately), so stock is hard-decremented at order creation BEFORE payment for prepaid orders; `checkout_sessions.inventory_reservation_ids` is never written by any code path, so the confirm-time reservation revalidation at checkout.ts:393-403 is dead code. Concurrency is pessimistic: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on `products` (166:87) AND on `inventory_levels` (141:121), both held for the whole ~30-40-statement order transaction. The stale-reservation sweeper IS wired and scheduled (queue-workers/src/index.ts:634, wrangler.toml crons `17 */6 * * *`) but at a 6-hour cadence against a 20-minute TTL. Ledger: PAY-R2 is resolved for captures and refunds — migration 168 makes `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger` and `complete_order_refund` post balanced entries — but `create_checkout_order` posts ZERO ledger entries and still sets `payment_status='paid'`/`status='confirmed'`/`payment_transactions.status='captured'` when a gift card covers the whole order (170:299-300, 170:472). Money on the primary path is `numeric(10,2)` rupees, not bigint paise (000_baseline.sql:10131-10135, 10311, 10022-10023) — R-8 unmet; only `ledger_entries.amount_minor` is bigint (139:130). R-6 holds at the DB level (one writer function `post_ledger_transaction`, `writer_module CHECK (= 'settlement')` at 139:113, append-only triggers plus REVOKE UPDATE/DELETE), though four gateway TS call sites can author arbitrary line-sets. Step-up is real cryptography — HMAC-SHA256 over a versioned payload with constant-time compare and a 10-minute window (security-controls.ts:193-215), backed by a TOTP-verified stored challenge (security.ts:167-215) — not a string-length check; its only gap is that the `jti`/challenge row is never consumed, so a token is replayable within its window. Webhook ingest is correct: signature verified, then `payment_webhook_events` upserted on `(provider, provider_event_id)` and duplicates short-circuited BEFORE any mutation (webhook-service/src/index.ts:726-775). Constitutionally, `withTenantTx` (the per-transaction `SET LOCAL app.site_id` plus `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant`) is defined but called from zero routes — every gateway query runs on `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`; and forbidden "partitioning-now" is live on five hot tables including `outbox_events`, with a row trigger that doubles every outbox write on the checkout path.


### Findings (25)


**[S0] Hosted checkout confirm captures payment and posts a canonical ledger credit from an unverified client-supplied provider_payment_id**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `POST /v1/checkout/:cartId/confirm` requires only that `provider_payment_id` be a non-empty 1-200 char string (checkout.ts:638, schema packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:116). It performs no provider signature verification, no `assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed`, and no provider-side payment fetch, then calls `PaymentExecutionAdapter.confirmPayment` (checkout.ts:691) which invokes `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger`. That RPC sets orders.payment_status='paid', payment_transactions.status='captured', inserts payment_webhook_events with signature_valid=TRUE, and posts a balanced ledger transaction debiting pa_clearing and crediting merchant_payable. The route only records whether the ledger row appeared (checkout.ts:713) into session metadata; it never asserts on it.
- EXPECTED: Any transition of an order to paid, and any credit to merchant_payable, must be predicated on cryptographically verified provider evidence (HMAC over provider references) and ideally a server-side provider capture confirmation — the same standard the storefront verify route and the SaaS billing route already apply.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:638; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:689; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:691; packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:116; services/api-gateway/src/execution/payment-execution.adapter.ts:94; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:1448; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:1479; database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:238; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:88; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:753
- RISK: A shopper who legitimately obtains a signed handoff for their own cart (available to anyone via GET /v1/storefront/:sub/cart/:token/checkout-url) can drive contact -> delivery -> payment-intent(provider=razorpay, mode=sandbox) -> confirm with provider_payment_id 'x' and receive a real, fully paid order with inventory committed and merchant_payable credited for money never received. Free goods at unlimited scale, plus a poisoned ledger that reconciliation will not catch because the fabricated capture is balanced. The payment_webhook_events row written with signature_valid=TRUE actively defeats forensics. Reachable in production because checkoutSigningSecret falls back to AUTH_SECRET.
- RECOMMENDATION: Fail closed immediately: reject any non-COD hosted-checkout confirm that does not carry a provider signature verified server-side by the corresponding provider adapter, plus assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed, plus a provider-side capture/amount check via fetchRazorpayPayment. Until that is implemented, unmount /v1/checkout (services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:753) — no shipped UI uses it.
- CAPACITY: None directly, but a fabricated-capture abuse loop would commit inventory on every call, converting the defect into an inventory-exhaustion amplifier against every SKU in every store.

**[S0] The live public order-create route bypasses the ART-14 signed cart handoff entirely and is unauthenticated and un-bot-gated**  ·  domain=checkout  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `router.post('/:subdomain/orders', ...)` is registered on the storefront router (checkout-payment-routes.ts:251, registered via public-routes.ts:763) and mounted at index.ts:752, twenty-seven lines before `api.use('/*', authMiddleware)` at index.ts:779. It accepts a store subdomain, customer details and item ids and creates a real order with committed inventory. There is no signed handoff check, no customer session requirement, and no Turnstile (Turnstile is applied only to customer registration/engagement routes). This is the path the shipped storefront UI actually uses (checkout-client.tsx:414 -> apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/api/orders/route.ts:48).
- EXPECTED: ART-14 / R-11 require the public order-create path to be reachable only behind the cart-bound signed handoff from the hosted checkout origin.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:251; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:763; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:752; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:779; apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/checkout/checkout-client.tsx:414; apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/api/orders/route.ts:48; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:684
- RISK: The entire ART-14 investment is inert on the live path. Anonymous callers can create unlimited COD orders (created with status 'confirmed' and stock committed at 166:199 with zero payment), exhausting every merchant's sellable inventory and flooding fulfilment queues. There is no cart binding at all, so the server-authoritative cart is not the source of the order lines — the client posts item ids and quantities directly.
- RECOMMENDATION: Decide the V1 checkout topology at ARB: either make the hosted handoff the sole order-create entry and gate /storefront/:subdomain/orders behind it, or explicitly retire the hosted-checkout path and add a customer-session or Turnstile plus per-IP/per-site rate limit plus a server cart binding to the storefront route. Do not leave both live.
- CAPACITY: An unauthenticated write endpoint that runs ~15 DB round-trips and a 30-40 statement locking transaction per call is the easiest way to saturate the DB connection pool; there is no per-route cost weighting, only the global 20-points/sec bucket.

**[S1] create_checkout_order rejects 'cashfree' although four routes and the payment adapters accept it**  ·  domain=checkout  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Migration 170 DROPs the 14-arg `create_checkout_order` (170:27) — the exact overload that migration 075's DO block had patched to allow cashfree (075:50-62) — and recreates a 16-arg version whose guard is `IF p_payment_method NOT IN ('razorpay','stripe','paypal','cod') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION` (170:81). Cashfree is gone. Meanwhile StorefrontCreateOrderBody.paymentMethod accepts 'cashfree' (checkout-payment-routes.ts:56), the hosted checkout accepts it (checkout.ts:144), and cashfree order/verify routes exist (checkout-payment-routes.ts:701 onward).
- EXPECTED: The set of payment methods accepted by the HTTP layer must equal the set accepted by the canonical order RPC, or the mismatch must be an explicit, tested rejection.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:27; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:81; database/supabase/migrations/075_cashfree_payment_provider.sql:50; database/supabase/migrations/075_cashfree_payment_provider.sql:62; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:56; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:144
- RISK: Any shopper selecting Cashfree gets a 500 from 'Order creation failed: unsupported payment method: cashfree' after passing validation. Silent regression introduced by a migration whose own header comment claims call-compatibility. No test covers it.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either add 'cashfree' back to the guard in a new migration, or remove cashfree from StorefrontCreateOrderSchema, checkout.ts:144 and the cashfree routes. Add a contract test asserting that the route-level payment-method enum is a subset of the RPC guard list.

**[S1] Inventory reserve and commit are collapsed into one transaction — stock is hard-decremented before payment and the TTL reservation lifecycle is never exercised by checkout**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `create_order_with_inventory_reservations` calls `reserve_inventory(..., 1200s TTL ...)` at 166:134 and then `commit_inventory_reservation` at 166:221 inside the SAME transaction, so inventory_levels.available is decremented at order-creation time for prepaid orders that are not yet paid. No checkout step ever creates a standalone reservation: checkout_sessions.inventory_reservation_ids (column added at 146:18) is read at checkout.ts:393 but is written by ZERO code paths, so the confirm-time reservation revalidation at checkout.ts:394-403 is unreachable dead code. There is no extend/renew RPC at all.
- EXPECTED: ART-15: reserve-with-TTL at checkout entry, extend while the shopper is active, expire on abandonment, commit only on payment success, release on failure — a single lifecycle, not a pre-payment hard decrement.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:134; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:221; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:65; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:175; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:393; database/supabase/migrations/146_executable_checkout_state_machine.sql:18
- RISK: Every abandoned prepaid checkout permanently removes stock until an operator cancels the order — the sweeper cannot help because the reservation is already 'committed', not 'active'. Conversely a shopper who completes contact/delivery/payment-intent has no hold at all and can be told at confirm that the last unit is gone. Oversell and undersell both remain possible depending on which side you look at.
- RECOMMENDATION: Split the lifecycle: reserve at hosted-checkout entry (or at storefront checkout-URL issuance), persist the reservation ids into checkout_sessions.inventory_reservation_ids, commit only inside the payment-confirmation transaction, and release on failure/expiry. Delete the dead revalidation branch or make it real.
- CAPACITY: Because reserve and commit are in one transaction, the per-SKU inventory_levels row lock is held for the entire 30-40 statement order transaction rather than for a short reserve. On a hot SKU this serializes all concurrent buyers behind the slowest full order transaction (promotion evaluation, gift-card ledger, GST, outbox plus shadow mirror), not behind a millisecond decrement.

**[S1] create_checkout_order posts zero canonical ledger entries and can mark an order fully paid and captured with no ledger record**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Migration 168 fixed PAY-R2 for capture (confirm_order_payment_with_ledger, 168:238) and refund (complete_order_refund, 168:401). But create_checkout_order (170:31-597) never calls post_settlement_ledger_transaction. When a gift card covers the entire order, v_payment_due = 0 and the function sets orders.payment_status='paid', orders.status='confirmed' (170:299-300) and inserts payment_transactions with status='captured', captured_at=NOW() (170:472, 170:478) — a captured tender with no canonical ledger entry. Gift-card value moves only through gift_card_ledger_entries (170:261).
- EXPECTED: Every tender that settles value — including gift-card redemption — must post to the single canonical double-entry ledger idempotently in the same transaction as the money-state change.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:299; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:450; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:472; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:261; database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:238; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:713
- RISK: Gift-card-funded orders are invisible to the canonical ledger, so merchant_payable understates the true liability discharge and the gift-card float is tracked in a parallel ledger that reconciliation never compares. refresh_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (160:198) compares ledger against the wallet compat table only, so this gap produces no mismatch signal.
- RECOMMENDATION: Extend create_checkout_order to post a payment_capture (or a new gift_card tender) ledger transaction whenever v_payment_due = 0 or a gift-card debit occurs, keyed on the existing idempotency key. Add the gift-card liability account to the ledger_accounts account_code CHECK (139:80).

**[S1] Storefront Razorpay verification grants value on signature alone, with no provider-side capture or amount confirmation**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `POST /storefront/:sub/payments/razorpay/verify` checks the HMAC over `order_id|payment_id` (razorpay-adapter/index.ts:347-348, timing-safe at :223) and then immediately calls confirmPayment (checkout-payment-routes.ts:664). It never calls fetchRazorpayPayment. The adapter's own doc comment at razorpay-adapter/index.ts:129-134 states that the HMAC proves neither that money was taken nor how much, and instructs callers to fetch the payment before granting value. The LetBuyy SaaS billing route DOES follow that instruction (payments.ts:970-985, checking status captured and amount at least the expected value).
- EXPECTED: Symmetric treatment: the shopper money path should be at least as strict as the platform's own subscription billing path.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:639; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:664; packages/infrastructure/razorpay-adapter/index.ts:129; packages/infrastructure/razorpay-adapter/index.ts:347; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:970
- RISK: A signature valid for an authorized-but-not-captured, partially-captured, or subsequently-failed payment marks the order paid and credits merchant_payable. The compensating controls (order.payment_id must equal the server-created razorpay order at :617, plus assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed at :628) prevent cross-order replay but not amount or status divergence.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a fetchRazorpayPayment check (status captured, amount at least the order total minor, order_id binding) to the storefront verify route before confirmPayment, mirroring payments.ts:970-985. Treat the webhook as the confirming authority and the client verify as an optimistic accelerator.
- CAPACITY: Adds one outbound provider round-trip to the verify path (currently 0 network calls, ~8 DB round-trips). Acceptable at 30k orders/month; should be non-blocking-with-reconciliation if latency matters.

**[S1] The hosted checkout origin checkout.letbuyy.com has no serving implementation and 'checkout' is not a reserved subdomain**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Signed handoff URLs are built as `new URL('/checkout', checkoutOrigin(c))` producing https://checkout.<platform-domain>/checkout (cart-routes.ts:567, origin at :293-303). A Terraform CNAME for `checkout` exists (infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91). apps/storefront-runtime has no top-level /checkout route — only /store/[subdomain]/checkout. The storefront proxy resolves any non-reserved label as a tenant subdomain (proxy.ts:321 via resolveStorefrontHost, packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:119-136); 'checkout' appears in neither RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS (tenant-core:27-44) nor RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS (tenant-core:46-57), and isReservedSubdomain (tenant-core:100) is the store-creation gate.
- EXPECTED: ART-14 requires a real hosted checkout.letbuyy.com origin; the label must be reserved platform-wide and must never be claimable by a tenant.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:567; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:293; infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:27; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:46; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:100; apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:321
- RISK: Today every signed checkout URL resolves to a 404 (storeNotFound, proxy.ts:356) — the handoff is unusable end to end. Worse, if any merchant registers the subdomain 'checkout', the DNS record already points there and the storefront-runtime would serve that merchant's store at the hosted-checkout origin, letting them render the checkout page for every signed handoff on the platform.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add 'checkout' (and 'pay', 'secure') to RESERVED_STOREFRONT_LABELS and RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS immediately and audit existing sites for the label. Then either implement the hosted-checkout app at the origin or stop emitting checkout.<domain> URLs.

**[S1] The per-transaction tenant GUC and non-service_role tenant role are implemented but called from zero routes — the gateway runs entirely on blanket service_role**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `withTenantTx` performs SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant plus SET LOCAL app.site_id plus a request-id GUC inside a pooled transaction (tenant-transaction.ts:15-38) and withResolvedTenantTx wraps it (tenant-context.ts:67). Grepping the whole gateway source, the only references to either symbol are their own definition and import — no route calls them. Every gateway query goes through getSupabaseAdmin to createSupabaseServiceClient with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY (supabase.ts:13-19, supabase-adapter/index.ts:34-39), guarded only by a JavaScript predicate wrapper (tenant/tenant-scoped.ts). The tenant GUC is set only inside SECURITY DEFINER RPCs from their own parameters — for example `IF public.current_site_id() IS NULL THEN PERFORM set_config('app.site_id', p_store_id::text, true)` (166:63-65) — so the GUC is self-asserted from the argument, not derived from an authenticated caller identity.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: RLS ENABLE plus FORCE with a per-transaction tenant GUC, and the gateway OFF blanket service_role.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13; packages/infrastructure/supabase-adapter/index.ts:34; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:63; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:53
- RISK: RLS is entirely bypassed for gateway traffic; tenant isolation rests on a JS predicate guard that a single missed .eq('site_id', ...) defeats. In the money path specifically, post_ledger_transaction requires the GUC (139:250) but it is set by post_settlement_ledger_transaction (165:252) from the resolved source-site — so tenancy on the ledger is inferred from data, not from the caller.
- RECOMMENDATION: ARB decision: either route the money and inventory paths through withTenantTx on a pooled connection with app_tenant, or formally record this as an accepted V1 deviation with the JS guard as the compensating control plus a validator that fails on any unguarded tenant-table access.
- CAPACITY: Adopting withTenantTx means every money request holds a pooled Postgres connection for the transaction duration (maxConnections 3 per isolate, tenant-transaction.ts:21). At 30k orders/month that is fine, but the pool cap must be sized against Worker isolate count before the switch.

**[S1] Primary money path uses numeric(10,2) rupees rather than bigint paise (R-8)**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: orders.subtotal/shipping_cost/tax/discount/total, order_items.price/total/unit_price/tax_amount/cgst/sgst/igst, and payment_transactions.amount are all numeric(10,2) (000_baseline.sql:10131-10135, 10022-10042, 10311). Only the canonical ledger uses bigint paise (ledger_entries.amount_minor bigint CHECK > 0, 139:130). Conversion happens at the boundary via round(COALESCE(v_order.total,0) * 100)::bigint (168:172) and Math.round(Number(order.total) * 100) in TypeScript (checkout-payment-routes.ts:162).
- EXPECTED: R-8: bigint paise everywhere on the live money path; no numeric, float or int32 money.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:10131; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:10022; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:10311; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:130; database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:172; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:162
- RISK: numeric(10,2) is exact so there is no float drift in the database, but the JS side round-trips through IEEE754 Number (checkout-payment-routes.ts:162, order-execution.adapter.ts:110) and GST maths crosses the boundary twice (gateway computes minor, passes p_tax_amount numeric(12,2), RPC stores rupees, invoice re-derives minor at order-execution.adapter.ts:344). Each crossing is a rounding site; sub-paise divergence between orders.total and the ledger amount_minor is possible on multi-line GST-inclusive orders.
- RECOMMENDATION: Record an explicit ARB decision: either accept numeric(10,2) as the V1 order representation with a validator forbidding float money in TS, or schedule the bigint-paise conversion of orders/order_items/payment_transactions with a shadow-column expand-migrate-contract.

**[S2] Forbidden partitioning-now is live on five hot tables, and its shadow-mirror trigger doubles every outbox write on the checkout path**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=constitutional-amendment
- CURRENT: Migrations 079-083 create partitioned shadow tables (outbox_events_partitioned PARTITION BY RANGE (created_at), 081:36) for worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics, outbox_events, platform_queue_jobs and webhook_deliveries. Migration 131 activates a per-row mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow() trigger (131:9-55) that, on every INSERT, does a SELECT from retention_policies WHERE target_table = TG_TABLE_NAME and then a dynamic INSERT INTO shadow SELECT ($1).*. Migration 179 exists solely because a column drift in that shadow broke EVERY outbox write.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: partitioning-now is FORBIDDEN in the 100 to 10,000 merchant / ~30k orders-per-month envelope.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/081_partition_outbox_events.sql:36; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:9; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:49; database/supabase/migrations/179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql:1; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:550; services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:60
- RISK: Direct constitutional violation plus a real cost: every outbox_events insert on the checkout path (create_checkout_order at 170:550, plus every cart/order/checkout domain event via EventExecutionAdapter) triggers an extra SELECT and a second INSERT. Migration 179's own header documents that this machinery already caused a total outbox outage in production shape. The blast radius includes theme publishing.
- RECOMMENDATION: ARB decision to disable the mirror (set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false), 131:57) and drop the shadow tables, or to formally amend the Constitution. At 30k orders/month partitioning buys nothing and costs a doubled write plus an outage class.
- CAPACITY: Doubles the write amplification of every domain event. On the order-create path there are 2-3 outbox writes per order (order.created, checkout.completed, order.paid), so ~6 physical inserts plus 3 retention_policies lookups per order that a compliant design would not perform.

**[S2] Step-up token is never consumed — the same HMAC token authorizes unlimited refunds inside its 10-minute window**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Step-up IS real cryptography, not a length check: a TOTP-verified challenge is persisted with the token hash and jti (security.ts:186-215) and verifySensitiveActionStepUp recomputes HMAC-SHA256 over 'lb-stepup-v1.<payload>' and compares constant-time, binding action, actor, site and verified_at and enforcing a 10-minute window (security-controls.ts:193-215). However verification performs NO database lookup: the step_up_challenges row and its jti are never marked consumed, and neither payments.ts:497 nor payouts.ts:576 records the jti against the refund.
- EXPECTED: PAY-R7/R-10: a step-up factor should authorize one sensitive action; the stored challenge should be single-use.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/security-controls.ts:193; services/api-gateway/src/lib/security-controls.ts:215; services/api-gateway/src/routes/security.ts:186; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:497; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payouts.ts:576
- RISK: A compromised or leaked token (it travels in the X-Step-Up-Token header, and is also accepted from body.metadata.step_up_token at payments.ts:501, meaning it can land in request-body logs) authorizes every refund and payout release for that actor and site for ten minutes.
- RECOMMENDATION: On successful verification, atomically transition the step_up_challenges row keyed by challenge_token_hash or jti from 'verified' to 'consumed' and reject when the transition does not apply. Remove the body.metadata.step_up_token fallback so the token only ever travels in a header.
- CAPACITY: Adds one indexed UPDATE per sensitive action; negligible at this volume.

**[S2] Non-production checkout handoff signing falls back to a hardcoded secret literal, and production silently reuses AUTH_SECRET**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: checkoutSigningSecret (duplicated verbatim at checkout.ts:87-92 and cart-routes.ts:306-311) resolves CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET, then AUTH_SECRET, then STORE_ACCESS_SIGNING_KEY; if that value is missing or starts with SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_, it returns null only when ENVIRONMENT or APP_ENV equals 'production', otherwise it returns the literal 'letbuyy-local-sandbox-checkout-handoff-secret'. The gateway wrangler sets ENVIRONMENT='staging' for staging (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:123), and config/env/staging.env.example:26 ships CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET as a SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN placeholder.
- EXPECTED: Handoff signing keys must be per-environment, purpose-scoped, and absent-means-fail-closed in every non-local environment.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:87; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:306; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:123; config/env/staging.env.example:26; config/env/production.env.example:26; config/env/production.env.example:49
- RISK: If staging still carries the placeholder, anyone can forge a valid staging checkout handoff for any cart from a secret published in this repository. In production the fallback to AUTH_SECRET means the hosted-checkout attack surface is live even though the operator believes checkout is not cut over (SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md records SKIP-D6-LIVE-CHECKOUT-CUTOVER as pending), and it cross-uses the session-signing secret for a second purpose.
- RECOMMENDATION: Remove the hardcoded literal and the AUTH_SECRET and STORE_ACCESS_SIGNING_KEY fallbacks; fail closed in every environment except a locally detected dev runtime. De-duplicate the function into one shared module.

**[S2] Idempotency claim, the money RPC and the outbox emit are three separate transactions with no atomicity**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: On every money route the sequence is: idempotency.begin INSERT (idempotency-execution.adapter.ts:76), then the money RPC (a separate PostgREST call), then an outbox_events upsert (event-execution.adapter.ts:60), then idempotency.complete UPDATE (:162). Each is its own transaction over Supabase REST. If the process dies after the RPC commits, the idempotency row stays 'processing' with a 120-second lock (:73), and on retry the caller gets ConflictError('An identical mutation is already processing') (:130); after lock expiry it is re-claimed and the RPC is re-run — safe only because each RPC has its own internal idempotency key.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: transactional OUTBOX. The event emit must commit in the same transaction as the state change it describes.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/execution/idempotency-execution.adapter.ts:76; services/api-gateway/src/execution/idempotency-execution.adapter.ts:162; services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:60; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:267; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:411; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:433
- RISK: The gateway-level order.created and order.paid outbox emits are NOT transactional with the order or payment state change; a crash between them loses the event with no replay source (the RPCs do emit their own in-transaction outbox rows at 170:550 and 166:276, so the gateway emits are partly redundant duplicates on a different idempotency key). The idempotency record can also record 'completed' for a request whose RPC actually failed, or stay 'processing' for one that succeeded.
- RECOMMENDATION: Drop the gateway-level outbox emits that duplicate an in-RPC emit, or move idempotency bookkeeping into the RPCs so claim, state change and outbox commit atomically. At minimum add a reconciliation sweep for idempotency_keys stuck in 'processing' whose resource actually exists.
- CAPACITY: Removes 2 of the 4 write transactions per order-create and per payment-verify — roughly a 30-40% reduction in write round-trips on the two hottest money paths.

**[S2] Every order creation runs an unindexed COUNT over the store's orders for the monthly quota check**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: assertOrderQuota runs before every create_checkout_order (order-execution.adapter.ts:154) and, when the plan has a finite orders_monthly limit (free 50, starter 250, growth 2500 — packages/billing-core/src/index.ts:702/740/778), issues .from('orders').select('id', { count: 'exact', head: true }).eq('store_id', ...).gte('created_at', periodStart).is('deleted_at', null) (order-execution.adapter.ts:385-390). It is preceded by a resolve_site_entitlements RPC (:375). The available indexes on orders are idx_orders_active (store_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL, idx_orders_store (store_id), idx_orders_status (status) and idx_orders_site_id (site_id) — there is no (store_id, created_at) composite.
- EXPECTED: A per-request quota gate on the hottest write path should be O(1) against a counter or a covering index, not an exact count.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/execution/order-execution.adapter.ts:154; services/api-gateway/src/execution/order-execution.adapter.ts:374; services/api-gateway/src/execution/order-execution.adapter.ts:385; packages/billing-core/src/index.ts:702; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:17710; database/supabase/migrations/110_fk_hygiene_and_order_indexes.sql:7
- RISK: count exact forces a full index scan of every non-deleted order for the store, then a heap filter on created_at. Cost grows linearly with a merchant's lifetime order count and is paid on EVERY checkout. It also adds 2 round-trips (entitlements RPC plus count) to the synchronous order path. At 10,000 merchants this is the first thing to degrade for the highest-volume and therefore most valuable stores.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add CREATE INDEX orders_store_created_at_idx ON public.orders (store_id, created_at) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL, and better, read the monthly counter from the existing usage-metering table (record_site_usage already meters 'orders' at order-execution.adapter.ts:201) instead of counting orders.
- CAPACITY: Directly capacity-limiting: 2 of the ~15 synchronous round-trips per order create, one of which is O(orders-per-store). Removing it is the cheapest latency win on the checkout path.

**[S2] Public storefront order create performs ~15 DB round-trips and holds a per-SKU product and inventory row lock for a 30-40 statement transaction**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Measured from source for a one-line cart: loadPublishedStore 1, createPublicStorefrontTenantContext 4 (site_roles, site_role_permissions, subscriptions, resolve_site_entitlements — tenant-context.ts:115/131/76 and saas-execution.adapter.ts:66), idempotency.begin 1, resolveShippingRate 1-2, tax_registrations 1, tax_rates 1, products price per line item N (checkout-payment-routes.ts:338, inside a loop), assertOrderQuota 2, create_checkout_order 1, outbox upsert 1, idempotency.complete 1. Inside the single RPC transaction: SELECT FROM products FOR UPDATE per SKU (166:87) and SELECT FROM inventory_levels FOR UPDATE (141:121), both held to COMMIT alongside coupon evaluation, gift-card ledger writes, GST, five INSERT blocks, the outbox insert and its shadow-mirror trigger.
- EXPECTED: A checkout write should hold its narrowest lock for the shortest possible window, and should not re-read data the RPC will read again.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:338; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:265; services/api-gateway/src/execution/saas-execution.adapter.ts:66; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:87; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:121; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:419; database/supabase/migrations/170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:181
- RISK: On a hot SKU (flash sale, single popular product) all concurrent buyers serialize on the products row for the full transaction — a much wider lock than the inventory row that actually needs protecting. A shared coupon code adds a second global serialization point: UPDATE promotion_codes SET usage_count = usage_count + 1 and UPDATE coupons SET usage_count (170:419-427) are single-row updates shared by every concurrent checkout using that code. The per-item product price query at :338 duplicates a read the RPC performs anyway, adding N avoidable round-trips.
- RECOMMENDATION: Downgrade the products FOR UPDATE to a plain read (the price is immutable within the transaction and inventory is already locked separately); batch the per-item price lookup into one .in('id', ids) query or have the RPC return the GST breakdown; cache the anonymous storefront tenant context (roles, permissions, plan, entitlements are static per site) instead of 4 queries per anonymous order; make coupon usage counters an append-only redemption count rather than an in-place increment.
- CAPACITY: This is the primary capacity model input. Steady state 30k orders/month is ~0.7 orders/sec average, but Indian commerce is spiky: a 100x flash-sale peak (~70 orders/sec) against a single-SKU row lock held for a 30-40 statement transaction is where this design fails first — not on aggregate throughput, but on per-SKU lock contention. Concurrency for a single hot SKU is effectively 1 divided by the transaction duration.

**[S2] Public order create has no bot gate; COD orders commit inventory with zero payment**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: POST /v1/storefront/:subdomain/orders sits behind only the global query-cost bucket (index.ts:684, 20 points/sec refill, 200 capacity) — it is not in the shouldSkipGatewayQueryCostLimit exemption list (index.ts:356-371), so the bucket applies, but there is no Turnstile and no per-site or per-IP order-specific throttle. Turnstile is wired only for customer registration (storefront/customer-registration.ts:48) and engagement routes. For payment_method='cod' the order is created with status='confirmed' (166:199) and inventory committed (166:221) with payment_transactions.status='pending'.
- EXPECTED: An unauthenticated endpoint that permanently consumes merchant inventory needs a bot gate and a per-tenant abuse budget.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:684; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:356; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/customer-registration.ts:48; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:199; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:221; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:251
- RISK: Anonymous COD order-spam permanently removes sellable stock from every SKU in a targeted store until an operator cancels each order. There is no COD risk gate before order creation — order-risk.ts operates only on orders that already exist. Combined with the missing signed-handoff gate this is a cheap, fully anonymous denial-of-inventory against any merchant.
- RECOMMENDATION: Require Turnstile (or a customer session) on the public order-create route, add a per-(site, IP) order-rate budget, and gate COD order creation behind the existing risk engine before inventory is committed rather than after.
- CAPACITY: Absent this control there is no upper bound on the order-create rate, so no capacity model for the DB is meaningful — an adversary sets the arrival rate.

**[S2] Refund path is correctly gated and ledgered, but the gift-card restore is best-effort outside the refund transaction**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: complete_order_refund (168:311) locks the refund and order FOR UPDATE, rejects cumulative refunds exceeding the order total (168:365), and posts a balanced ledger transaction debiting merchant_payable and crediting refund_clearing (168:401). The merchant refund route requires step-up (payments.ts:497), calls the provider before writing the ledger and returns 502 without any ledger entry if the provider refund fails (payments.ts:551-558). However the gift-card restoration (restore_gift_card_on_refund, migration 174) is invoked from the order status-transition route inside a try/catch that only warns (orders.ts:1200-1211) and again in the COD cancel path (order-risk.ts:305-311) — never inside complete_order_refund, which migration 174:13 explicitly leaves untouched because it is validator-pinned.
- EXPECTED: Reversal of a gift-card-funded order should restore the redeemed balance in the same transaction that reverses the money, not as a best-effort side call on a different route.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:311; database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:365; database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:401; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:497; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:551; services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:1200; database/supabase/migrations/174_v1_gift_card_refund_restore.sql:13
- RISK: A refund executed through complete_order_refund or POST /payments/:id/refund — rather than through the order status transition — never restores the gift-card balance, silently destroying customer value. Migration 174's own header documents this exact class of bug. Partial refunds of gift-card orders are explicitly not handled (174:15-16).
- RECOMMENDATION: Call restore_gift_card_on_refund from inside complete_order_refund (proportionally for partial refunds), or make the refund route call it and fail the request when it errors rather than warning.

**[S3] Four gateway TypeScript call sites can author arbitrary canonical ledger line-sets, and writer_module is never explicitly set**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: R-6 holds structurally: exactly one SQL writer, post_ledger_transaction (139:223), reachable only through post_settlement_ledger_transaction (165:214), with append-only triggers (139:359-376), REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role, app_tenant (165:463-464), a deferred balance-enforcing constraint trigger (139:379) and writer_module text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'settlement' CHECK (writer_module = 'settlement') (139:113). But four gateway routes call the RPC directly with caller-constructed line arrays: payment-route-support.ts:185 (used by payments.ts:294 capture and :563 refund), payouts.ts:195, commission.ts:148, settlements.ts:175. No writer ever sets writer_module, so the CHECK only pins the column default and provides no attribution.
- EXPECTED: R-6: one ledger writer, with the writer identity actually recorded and enforced.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:113; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:223; database/supabase/migrations/165_d5_canonical_money_paths.sql:214; database/supabase/migrations/165_d5_canonical_money_paths.sql:463; services/api-gateway/src/lib/payment-route-support.ts:185; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payouts.ts:195; services/api-gateway/src/routes/commission.ts:148; services/api-gateway/src/routes/settlements.ts:175
- RISK: A bug or a compromised route can post any balanced pair of entries against any account code for a site it can name; the only checks are balance, currency, tenant-GUC-matches-resolved-source, and account-code membership. The writer_module column cannot be used for forensics or for a future multi-writer split because it is uniformly 'settlement'.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move each of the four TS postings behind a purpose-specific SECURITY DEFINER RPC that constructs its own lines (as the SQL callers already do), and make post_ledger_transaction take and persist an explicit writer_module argument.

**[S3] ART-14 cart binding omits an explicit subtotal term and the signed URL is a reusable bearer capability**  ·  domain=checkout  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: canonicalCheckoutCartBinding (packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:510) sorts lines by product_id, variant_id and quantity, includes unit_price_minor and line_total_minor per line, and includes de-duplicated uppercased sorted coupon codes — but no aggregate subtotal field. canonicalCheckoutHandoffPayload (:549) signs site, cart, token, channel, expires_at plus the digest. Verification (checkout.ts:263-288) recomputes the digest from the live cart and compares both digest and signature timing-safe, so cart mutation does invalidate the URL. Expiry is 10 minutes (cart-routes.ts:546) and is enforced (checkout.ts:236-239). There is no nonce or single-use marker.
- EXPECTED: ART-14 specifies the HMAC binds a hash of sorted line items plus coupons plus subtotal.
- EVIDENCE: packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:510; packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:549; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:263; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:272; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:286; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:546
- RISK: Low as a forgery risk — per-line totals are bound so the subtotal is implied and any line change breaks the digest. The residual issue is that the signed URL is a bearer credential carried in the address bar for 10 minutes; anyone who obtains it (browser history, Referer, shared screenshot) can drive the whole checkout for that cart.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add an explicit subtotal_minor term to the canonical binding to match ART-14 literally, and bind the handoff to a cart_token cookie or a one-time nonce persisted in carts.checkout_handoff so the URL cannot be replayed from a second client.

**[S3] Self-custody razorpayx rail survives only as residual CHECK-constraint enum values on frozen app-platform tables (R-7)**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: razorpayx appears in exactly three places in schema: app_billing_charges_payout_rail_check, app_billing_charges_provider_check (000_baseline.sql:8293, 8295) and partner_payout_ledger_entries_rail_check (:10263). All three tables belong to the app-marketplace revenue-share surface, which is unreachable behind the fail-closed appPlatformKillSwitch (index.ts:734-745; the middleware default-denies unless FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM is explicitly positive and the variable is declared in no wrangler environment). No TypeScript source emits the value; scripts/validators/no-self-custody-rail-validator.mjs:11 bans hardcoding it and tracks the residual under SKIP-RAZORPAYX-ENUM-PURGE.
- EXPECTED: R-7: no self-custody rail reachable; never custody.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8293; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:10263; scripts/validators/no-self-custody-rail-validator.mjs:11; scripts/validators/no-self-custody-rail-validator.mjs:31; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:734
- RISK: No live exposure at this SHA — the value cannot be written because no route reaches those tables. The residual is a latent re-enablement hazard if the app platform is ever un-frozen without first purging the enum.
- RECOMMENDATION: Ship the enum purge (drop and re-add the three CHECK constraints without razorpayx) so that un-freezing the app platform cannot silently re-open a self-custody rail. Then retire SKIP-RAZORPAYX-ENUM-PURGE.

**[INFO] Webhook ingest correctly records the event id before any mutation and short-circuits duplicates (SEC-R5/PAY-R8 satisfied)**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: POST /razorpay on the webhook service computes the HMAC twice (an explicit verifyRazorpayWebhookSignature and the adapter's webhookVerify, index.ts:721-727), both timing-safe (razorpay-adapter/index.ts:440-441, :223), and rejects on either failure. Only then does it upsert payment_webhook_events on (provider, provider_event_id) (index.ts:766, writeWebhookEvent at :64-85) and return early on duplicate (index.ts:774) BEFORE any order or ledger mutation. Age is observed and logged but deliberately not used to reject, since idempotency is the replay control (index.ts:753-761). The same pattern is repeated for the other three providers (index.ts:950, 1146, 1292).
- EXPECTED: SEC-R5/PAY-R8: signature verified server-side with timing-safe compare; idempotency key or event-id recorded before any mutation.
- EVIDENCE: services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:718; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:726; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:766; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:774; packages/infrastructure/razorpay-adapter/index.ts:440; packages/infrastructure/razorpay-adapter/index.ts:223
- RISK: None identified. The only nuance is the absence of a replay window, which is a documented and defensible trade (provider retries of old-but-valid events must still succeed).
- RECOMMENDATION: No change required. Consider asserting the x-razorpay-event-id header as the provider_event_id when present, rather than deriving it from the payload (razorpay-adapter/index.ts:452), to make dedup keys provider-authoritative.
- CAPACITY: Webhook ingest is 1 write plus 1 read plus 1 RPC per event — the cheapest money-path hop and the correct place to make payment confirmation authoritative.

**[INFO] No Wasm or extension executor is reachable from the live order path (R-12 satisfied)**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts implements a Wasm checkout-function executor with module-size, memory and instruction-budget validation (validateWasmModuleLimits at :261, evaluateCheckoutAppFunctions at :585). A repo-wide search for importers found only tests (tests/contracts/app-function-wasm-limits.test.ts:9, tests/app-platform/*.mjs) and documentation manifests — no runtime file imports it. The order-create response hardcodes extensionFunctions: [] (checkout-payment-routes.ts:430), and /storefront/:subdomain/checkout/extensions is registered behind the fail-closed appPlatformKillSwitch before the feature gate (index.ts:722-723).
- EXPECTED: R-12 / NEVER-R3: no Wasm extension runtime in the live order path in this envelope.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts:585; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:430; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:722; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/appPlatformKillSwitch.ts:25; tests/contracts/app-function-wasm-limits.test.ts:9
- RISK: None at runtime. The module is still compiled into the Worker bundle, so it contributes to bundle size and remains a one-import-away regression risk.
- RECOMMENDATION: No action required for V1. When the app platform freeze is next reviewed, either delete the module or move it behind an explicitly dynamic import so a future edit cannot accidentally wire it into checkout.

**[INFO] commission_bps is zero everywhere with a ratchet validator (R-9 satisfied)**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Migration 162 sets commission_schedules.commission_bps and commission_reconciliation_entries.commission_bps defaults to 0 (162:4, 162:7) and zeroes existing rows including commission_amount_minor and gst_amount_minor with net_payable_minor = gross_amount_minor (162:9-22). scripts/validators/commission-bps-zero-validator.mjs enforces the ratchet: it rejects any non-zero commission_bps or commissionBps literal, default or ALTER COLUMN SET DEFAULT, and positively asserts that migration 162 contains both SET DEFAULT 0 statements. The rollback file is checked to be the only place carrying SET DEFAULT 300. Runtime code reads the schedule value and multiplies (commission.ts, payouts.ts) — it does not hardcode a rate.
- EXPECTED: R-9: 0% transaction fee everywhere, enforced by a ratchet.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/162_phase_a_commission_zero.sql:4; database/supabase/migrations/162_phase_a_commission_zero.sql:9; scripts/validators/commission-bps-zero-validator.mjs:1; database/supabase/rollback/162_phase_a_commission_zero.rollback.sql:1; services/api-gateway/src/routes/commission.ts:148
- RISK: None. Note the validator is a source-text ratchet, not a database assertion — a row updated at runtime to a non-zero commission_bps would not be caught.
- RECOMMENDATION: Optionally add a database CHECK commission_bps = 0 on both tables so the invariant is enforced by Postgres rather than only by a source scan.

**[INFO] Reconciliation records mismatches without auto-plugging, but reconciles against a compatibility table rather than provider settlement, and no suspense account exists**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: refresh_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (160:198) computes variance_minor = ledger_account_balance_minor(site,'merchant_payable') minus the signed sum of wallet_ledger_entries, and upserts a row with status 'verified' or 'mismatch', leaving verified_at NULL on mismatch (160:238-243). It writes NO compensating ledger entry — there is no auto-plug. assert_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (160:260) raises on non-zero variance. It runs on the queue-workers cron (index.ts:650). The ledger_accounts.account_code CHECK admits pa_clearing, merchant_payable, platform_revenue, gst_payable, cod_receivable, reserve, vendor_payable, refund_clearing (139:80-88) — no suspense account.
- EXPECTED: Reconciliation against the external source of truth (PSP settlement), with unmatched amounts parked in a suspense account and never auto-plugged.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/160_foundation_money_ledger_authority.sql:198; database/supabase/migrations/160_foundation_money_ledger_authority.sql:238; database/supabase/migrations/160_foundation_money_ledger_authority.sql:260; database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:80; services/queue-workers/src/money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:75; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:650
- RISK: The no-auto-plug property is confirmed and good. But reconciling the canonical ledger against wallet_ledger_entries — a table that the same RPCs write in the same transaction (165:405 onward) — is close to self-reconciliation: it detects code-path divergence, not money divergence. Nothing compares the ledger to Razorpay settlement reports, so the fabricated-capture defect above would reconcile cleanly.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a suspense account code and a settlement-file reconciliation job that matches ledger payment_capture entries against PSP settlement rows, routing unmatched amounts to suspense for human clearing. Keep the existing wallet-versus-ledger check as an internal consistency probe, renamed accordingly.

**[INFO] Server-authoritative pricing holds on every shopper-facing route; no client total is trusted**  ·  domain=checkout  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Cart line prices are hydrated from the products table, never from the client (cart-routes.ts:262-276). Order totals are computed inside create_checkout_order from products.price and product_variants.price read FOR UPDATE (166:97, 166:112). Shipping is resolved server-side from shipping_rates by id (shipping-rate-resolver.ts:33, RPC 170:114-137), and the hosted checkout explicitly discards any client shipping_quote price (checkout.ts:520-555). GST is computed server-side from tax_registrations plus tax_rates (checkout-payment-routes.ts:296-369, invoice at order-execution.adapter.ts:295-335). Discounts go through evaluate_checkout_promotion (170:188). Client-supplied amount and amountMinorUnits are used ONLY as equality assertions that reject on mismatch (checkout-payment-routes.ts:484, payments.ts:1072).
- EXPECTED: CODE-R6 / ANTI-R6: no route may accept and use a client-submitted amount on the shopper path.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:262; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:97; services/api-gateway/src/lib/shipping-rate-resolver.ts:33; services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:520; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:484; services/api-gateway/src/routes/payments.ts:482; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-billing.ts:115
- RISK: No shopper-path violation found. Two non-shopper exceptions are by design: payments.ts:482 accepts a merchant-specified partial refund amount_minor (bounded by the captured amount and step-up gated), and internal-billing.ts:115/182/185 accepts operator-supplied platform-billing amounts behind the internal perimeter.
- RECOMMENDATION: No change required. Keep the assertion-only pattern and add a validator that flags any new use of a request-body amount as anything other than an equality check on the shopper path.

### Inventory notes
ORDER-CREATE CALL SITES (all reach `create_checkout_order` via OrderExecutionAdapter.createOrder, order-execution.adapter.ts:158):
1. services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:395 — PUBLIC, unauthenticated, no signed handoff. Mounted index.ts:752 before authMiddleware (index.ts:779). THE LIVE PATH (UI: apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/checkout/checkout-client.tsx:414 → /api/orders → apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/api/orders/route.ts:48).
2. services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:680 — hosted checkout confirm; gated by signed handoff (checkout.ts:221-300).
3. services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:807 — draft-order convert; merchant auth via resolveTenantContextForOwnedSite.
4. services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:1251 — admin POST /orders; merchant auth.

STOCK-DECREMENT PATHS (SQL):
- commit_inventory_reservation (141:211) — the order path (available -= qty). Reached only via create_order_with_inventory_reservations (166:221).
- release_inventory_reservation (141:277) / expire_inventory_reservations (141:327) — reserved -= qty only.
- adjust_inventory_level (000_baseline.sql:38, decrement at :122) — merchant manual adjust.
- set_inventory_level_available (000_baseline.sql:7484, :7575) — merchant absolute set.
- create_inventory_transfer_with_lines (000_baseline.sql:2214, :2289), receive_inventory_transfer_line (:4963), receive_purchase_order_line (:5175) — warehouse ops.
- release_order_inventory_with_ledger (000_baseline.sql:7120) — cancel/refund restock.
No TypeScript path writes inventory_levels (verified: only reads at inventory.ts:783, inventory.ts:1131, commerce-analytics.ts:213/415).

INVENTORY LIFECYCLE STATE: reserve (TTL 1200s, clamped 60..86400, 141:131) → commit — but both in ONE transaction (166:134 then 166:221). extend: NO extend RPC exists. expire: expire_inventory_reservations (141:304), FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, limit clamp 1..1000. Sweeper: services/queue-workers/src/inventory-reservations.ts:26, invoked services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:634 from `scheduled()` at :630; crons `17 */6 * * *` at services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:17 (top-level), :123 (staging), :237 (production). Default sweeper limit 250.

CONCURRENCY MECHANISM: pessimistic row locks, no optimistic version check on the order path (inventory_levels.version exists with an increment trigger at 141:48-51 but is never compared). Locks taken and held to COMMIT:
- products row FOR UPDATE per SKU (166:87)
- inventory_levels row FOR UPDATE per (store,product,variant,location) (141:121)
- inventory_reservations row FOR UPDATE (141:197)
- orders row FOR UPDATE (170:181; 000_baseline.sql:1462; 168:165)
- gift_card_accounts row FOR UPDATE (170:209)
- refunds plus orders FOR UPDATE (168:340, 168:352)
SINGLE-ROW SERIALIZATION HOTSPOTS: (a) products row per SKU — widest, held across the full order tx; (b) inventory_levels row per SKU/location; (c) `UPDATE promotion_codes SET usage_count = usage_count + 1` (170:419) and `UPDATE coupons SET usage_count = ...` (170:424) — one row per coupon serializes every concurrent checkout using the same code; (d) promotion_usage_counters upsert per (site,promo,code,coupon,email) (170:429); (e) gift_card_accounts row per card. NOT a hotspot: order_number is `LB-<yyyymmdd>-<8 hex>` random (166:68) with a global UNIQUE (000_baseline.sql:14587); ledger balances are derived (post_ledger_transaction 139:223 writes no running balance), ledger_accounts rows are read-mostly via ensure_ledger_account.

DB ROUND-TRIPS / WRITE TRANSACTIONS PER REQUEST (as implemented, single-line cart):
- Cart read GET /storefront/:sub/cart/:token — 2 reads (loadPublishedStore storefront.ts:742; loadCartByToken cart-routes.ts:198), 0 writes.
- Cart mutation POST/PATCH — 3 reads plus 1 write minimum (cart-routes.ts:404-424); plus 1 outbox upsert on create; plus 3 more if buyer_email (email_captured event, platform_queue_jobs upsert, carts update) = up to 4 reads / 5 writes across 5 separate transactions.
- Checkout-URL sign GET .../checkout-url — 2 reads plus 1 write (carts.checkout_handoff, cart-routes.ts:577).
- Hosted checkout step (contact/delivery/payment_intent) — verifyCheckoutHandoff 1 read, idempotency begin 1 write, loadSession 1 read, upsert 1 write, idempotency complete 1 write (plus 1 outbox on contact) = ~2 reads / 3-4 writes in 4 separate transactions.
- Public order create POST /storefront/:sub/orders — ~15 REST round-trips before the 201: loadPublishedStore 1; createPublicStorefrontTenantContext 4 (site_roles, site_role_permissions, subscriptions, rpc resolve_site_entitlements — tenant-context.ts:115/131/76 and saas-execution.adapter.ts:66); idempotency.begin 1; resolveShippingRate 1-2; tax_registrations 1; tax_rates 1; products price 1 PER LINE ITEM (checkout-payment-routes.ts:338); assertOrderQuota 2 (resolve_site_entitlements rpc plus `orders` COUNT exact head, order-execution.adapter.ts:385); create_checkout_order rpc 1; outbox upsert 1; idempotency.complete 1. FOUR separate write transactions (idempotency insert, the money RPC, outbox, idempotency update) — no atomicity between them. Deferred post-commit via waitUntil adds ~6 more (record_site_usage rpc, orders/sites/order_items/tax_rates reads, orders update, order_items update per line).
- Inside create_checkout_order (ONE transaction, ~30-40 statements): checkout_sessions replay select; shipping_rates count plus rate select; then per item — products FOR UPDATE, optional variant select, inventory_levels location select, reserve_inventory (level FOR UPDATE, level UPDATE, reservation INSERT), reservation UPDATE order_id, commit_inventory_reservation (reservation FOR UPDATE, levels UPDATE, reservation UPDATE), inventory_adjustments INSERT, order_items INSERT; plus orders INSERT, outbox INSERT, audit INSERT, orders SELECT (166), then orders FOR UPDATE, evaluate_checkout_promotion, gift-card block (4 statements), orders UPDATE, checkout_sessions INSERT, order_shipping_lines INSERT, promotion block (4 statements), payment_transactions INSERT, inventory_events INSERT..SELECT, outbox_events INSERT (plus shadow-mirror trigger = retention_policies SELECT and partitioned INSERT, migration 131:9-55), final orders SELECT.
- Payment verify POST /storefront/:sub/payments/razorpay/verify — ~8-9 round-trips: loadPublishedStore 1, loadStorefrontPayableOrder 1, idempotency.begin 1, assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed 1, confirm_order_payment_with_ledger rpc 1, outbox upsert 1, order-confirmation email enqueue 1-2, idempotency.complete 1. FOUR write transactions. HMAC is pure CPU (no provider call).
- Webhook ingest POST /razorpay — HMAC verify (0 DB) → payment_webhook_events upsert 1 write → duplicate short-circuit → order lookup plus markOrderPaid rpc plus outbox.

MONEY TYPES (R-8): orders.subtotal/shipping_cost/tax/discount/total numeric(10,2) (000_baseline.sql:10131-10135); order_items.price/total/unit_price/tax_amount numeric(10,2) (:10022-10042); payment_transactions.amount numeric(10,2) (:10311); shipping_rates.price numeric(10,2) (:11806). bigint paise ONLY in ledger_entries.amount_minor (139:130), payments.amount_minor, cod_reconciliation_entries.*_bigint, wallet_ledger_entries.amount_minor_bigint. Bridge is `round(total*100)::bigint` (168:172).

LEDGER WRITERS (R-6): one SQL writer `post_ledger_transaction` (139:223), reachable only through `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` (165:214). Its callers — SQL: confirm_order_payment_with_ledger (168:238), complete_order_refund (168:401), record_order_payment_failure_with_ledger (168:663), reconcile_cod_remittance_with_ledger (165:354), backfill_wallet_ledger_to_canonical (160:318). TS (arbitrary line-sets): payment-route-support.ts:185 (payments capture 294 / refund 563), payouts.ts:195, commission.ts:148, settlements.ts:175. `writer_module text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'settlement' CHECK (writer_module = 'settlement')` (139:113) — enforced but the column is never set by any writer, so the CHECK only pins the default. Append-only: triggers 139:359-376 plus `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON public.ledger_transactions/ledger_entries FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role, app_tenant` (165:463-464). Balanced-transaction constraint trigger 139:379-382. No `suspense` account in the ledger_accounts CHECK (139:80-88).

RECONCILIATION: refresh_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (160:198) compares ledger merchant_payable against the wallet_ledger_entries compat table, writes status verified|mismatch with variance_minor, verified_at NULL on mismatch — NO auto-plug, no compensating entry (160:238-243). assert_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (160:260) raises on non-zero variance. Scheduled at queue-workers/src/index.ts:650. It does NOT reconcile against PSP settlement files.

COD: create_checkout_order sets orders.status='confirmed' immediately for COD (166:199) with stock committed and payment_transactions.status='pending'. COD confirmation is a customer/merchant flag only (order-risk.ts:315-360), no ledger. Ledger posting happens only at remittance reconciliation (165:354, cod_receivable debit / merchant_payable credit).

R-7 razorpayx: present ONLY as residual CHECK-constraint enum values on app_billing_charges.payout_rail/provider (000_baseline.sql:8293,8295) and partner_payout_ledger_entries.rail (:10263) — all app-marketplace tables behind the fail-closed appPlatformKillSwitch (index.ts:734-745). No TS code emits it; scripts/validators/no-self-custody-rail-validator.mjs:11 bans hardcoding it.
R-9 commission_bps: defaults set to 0 (162:4,7) and existing rows zeroed (162:9-22); ratchet at scripts/validators/commission-bps-zero-validator.mjs.
R-12 Wasm: services/api-gateway/src/extensions/app-functions-runtime.ts exists (validateWasmModuleLimits:261, evaluateCheckoutAppFunctions:585) but is imported by ZERO runtime files (only tests and docs manifests). The order-create response hardcodes `extensionFunctions: []` (checkout-payment-routes.ts:430). `/storefront/:subdomain/checkout/extensions` is fail-closed at index.ts:722.

CLIENT-AMOUNT AUDIT (CODE-R6/ANTI-R6): no shopper-facing route uses a client total. `requestedAmountMinorUnits` values are used only as equality assertions against DB-derived amounts (checkout-payment-routes.ts:484, payments.ts:1072). Exceptions: payments.ts:482 `body.amount_minor` for partial refunds (bounded by captured amount, step-up gated) and internal-billing.ts:115/182/185 (behind internalPerimeter, platform billing).

### Unknowns
- Whether `CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET` is actually provisioned as a distinct secret in the deployed staging/production Workers, or whether the deployed value is still the `SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_*` placeholder so the code silently uses `AUTH_SECRET`. Needs `wrangler secret list --env production/staging` output from an ops owner.
- Whether migrations 141/166/168/170/173/174 have actually been applied to the production database, or only exist as files. Needs the applied-migration table (`supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`) from the live project.
- Whether `checkout.letbuyy.com` currently resolves and what it serves. The Terraform CNAME exists at infrastructure/dns/dns.tf:91 but no Vercel host attachment or serving app was found in repo. Needs a DNS/Vercel domain listing from the platform owner.
- Whether any merchant site already holds the subdomain `checkout`. Needs `SELECT subdomain FROM sites WHERE subdomain IN ('checkout','pay','api')` from a DBA.
- Whether `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID`/`RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` on the gateway are a single platform-level Razorpay account used for all tenants (which would put LetBuyy in the funds flow, contrary to never-custody) or per-merchant Route sub-accounts. The storefront routes read them straight from `c.env` (checkout-payment-routes.ts:464) while `requireLivePaymentCredential` (payment-route-support.ts:122) implies per-merchant credentials. Needs the deployed secret shape plus a Razorpay account-structure decision from the founder.
- Actual p95 latency and lock-wait time for `create_checkout_order` under concurrency. The transaction boundary and lock set are established from source, but hold duration is unmeasured. Needs `pg_stat_statements` / `pg_locks` sampling under load.
- Whether the `mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow` triggers are currently ENABLED in production (they can be toggled by `set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled`, 131:57). Needs `SELECT tgenabled FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname LIKE 'mirror_%'`.
- Whether `shipping_rates.site_id` is populated for rows created after migration 145 — the TS resolver keys on `site_id` (shipping-rate-resolver.ts:39) while the RPC keys on the `shipping_zones` join (170:108). Needs a row count of `shipping_rates WHERE site_id IS NULL`.
- Whether the deployed gateway Worker actually has a pooled SQL binding (HYPERDRIVE/DATABASE_URL). `withTenantTx` throws `TenantTransactionUnavailableError` without one, and `loadPublishedStoreFromPooledSql` silently falls back. Needs the deployed binding list.
- Whether an ARB/founder decision already exists accepting `numeric(10,2)` rupees on the order path as the V1 money representation, or whether R-8 conversion to bigint paise is still an open obligation.
- Whether the hosted-checkout `/v1/checkout/*` router is intended to be deployed at all in V1, or is dead scaffolding that should be unmounted. This is a scope decision, not a code fact.
