## DIMENSION: flow-storefront

### Narrative summary
Host routing lives in `apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts` (Next 16 renamed middleware; there is no `middleware.ts`). Every storefront request normalizes the Host, classifies it via `resolveStorefrontHost` (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:119) into subdomain/custom_domain/root/reserved/invalid, then calls the gateway `GET /v1/internal/store-access/resolve?host=` with `cache: 'no-store'` and a 3s abort (proxy.ts:118-122). That lookup is NOT cached anywhere — no KV, no Cache API, no in-memory memo — so every single storefront page view costs one extra gateway hop plus one `sites` SELECT, and a gateway outage fails closed to a 503 holding page for every store. The gateway resolves subdomain→site by `sites.subdomain` equality and custom domain by `sites.custom_domain + custom_domain_status='active'`, falling back to the `site_domains` table for secondary/redirect domains (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/store-context.ts:201-258). Custom-domain provisioning is a DoH TXT challenge (`_letbuyy-challenge.<domain>`, at least 2 matching answers) then CNAME match, then `attachVercelProjectDomain` against the Vercel v10 project-domains API; TLS is Vercel-issued, not Cloudflare for SaaS (services/api-gateway/src/routes/custom-domains.ts:282-321, services/api-gateway/src/lib/vercel-domains.ts:124-194). Three cache layers exist in source: (1) Next fetch Data Cache via `next:{revalidate,tags}`, (2) the gateway's Cloudflare Worker Cache API (`caches.default`) wrapper `cachedJson`, (3) KV — used only to hold a per-handle version string embedded in the Cache API key. Tier 1 is effectively dead: `apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45` stamps a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` into `X-Request-ID` on every gateway fetch, and Next 16.1.6 includes all request headers (minus traceparent/tracestate) in both the Data Cache key and the per-request dedupe key, so no fetch ever hits and no two identical fetches inside one render are deduplicated. `revalidateTag` is never called anywhere in the repo, so the roughly 20 declared `storefront:*` tags are decorative. Tier 2 is real and tenant-safe: keys are `storefront-rest:<ns>:<version>:<handle>[:<id>][:<params>]` with every part `encodeURIComponent`'d before joining on ':' (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:63-101), and `handle` is the unique `sites.subdomain`, so cross-tenant key collision is structurally impossible. There is no single-flight/lock: on a cold key every concurrent request runs the producer (storefront-cache.ts:107-130), and non-200 responses are never cached, so 404 floods hit Postgres unthrottled. The only writer of `bumpStorefrontCacheVersion` is theme publish/rollback (services/api-gateway/src/routes/themes.ts:140-158); product, collection, navigation, page and domain mutations bump nothing and rely purely on the 60s TTL. Route-segment `revalidate` values on the storefront are inert: `apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:76` awaits `resolveThemePreviewToken`, which calls `cookies()`, forcing every `/store/*` route into dynamic rendering, so no ISR/edge HTML cache exists at all — the governed "SSR/ISR + Worker cache" is SSR-only today. Renderer selection is per-store, not per-env: `resolveThemeLiquidRenderingEnabled` returns true unconditionally when `templateConfig.rendererEngine === 'liquid'`, and migration 180 wrote exactly that key onto the urban-kicks theme `default_template`, which theme install copies into `sites.template_config` — so ART-17's "Liquid default OFF" is false for every urban-kicks-derived store while migration-197 `letbuyy-core` rows stay React. The sanitizer is genuinely allowlist-based (jsdom + DOMPurify, `FORBID_TAGS:['base','embed','object','script']`, `FORBID_ATTR:['style']`, custom-element `tagNameCheck`), the Liquid engine is locked to `ownPropertyOnly:true`, `outputEscape:'escape'`, `jsTruthy:false`, `dynamicPartials:false`, and upload-time validation rejects `{% include/layout/render %}`, `<script`, `javascript:`, `data:text/html`. The one executable seam is `ADD_TAGS:['iframe']` with `src`/`sandbox`, reachable only through marketplace app blocks, and `FEATURE_APP_PLATFORM` is absent from production vars so that reader returns `[]`. Media uploads go to R2 with key `<folder>/<auth.user.id>/<ts>-<uuid>.<ext>` — user-scoped, NOT site-scoped — with a client-declared-MIME allowlist (no magic-byte sniffing), 10/50/100MB limits, and DELETE performing a real `R2_BUCKET.delete` after ownership checks. `services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:381-387` fans `/v1/mobile/dashboard` out to six uncached gateway analytics calls, and `/v1/analytics/dashboard` itself pulls two unbounded `orders` result sets into the Worker to aggregate in JS. No cache hit-rate telemetry is emitted anywhere: the only signal is the `X-Storefront-Cache` response header, and the storefront's own canary log hard-codes `cache_hit: null`.


### Findings (19)


**[S1] Random X-Request-ID header defeats the Next fetch Data Cache and per-request dedupe on every storefront gateway call**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `storefrontApiFetch` sets `X-Request-ID: crypto.randomUUID()` on every outbound gateway fetch (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45). Next 16.1.6 builds the Data Cache key from `JSON.stringify([prefix, keyPrefix, url, method, headers, ...])`, stripping only `traceparent`/`tracestate`, and the request-scoped dedupe key from `[method, filteredHeaders, mode, redirect, ...]` with the same two exclusions. Every `storefrontReadInit(60, tags)` call therefore writes a cache entry under a key that can never be read again, and two identical fetches in one render are not deduplicated.
- EXPECTED: The governed 3-tier cache expects tier 1 (Next Data Cache, 30-60s revalidate) to absorb repeat storefront reads, and Next request memoization to collapse the duplicate store/navigation fetches that layout, generateMetadata and the page component each issue.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:24-31; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:428-441; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/product-service.ts:66-82; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260; grep -n generateCacheKey node_modules/.pnpm/next@16.1.6_*/node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/incremental-cache/index.js (cacheString includes `headers`, only traceparent/tracestate deleted); node_modules/.pnpm/next@16.1.6_*/node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/dedupe-fetch.js (generateCacheKey includes filteredHeaders)
- RISK: Tier-1 cache hit rate is structurally 0%. A single product-detail render issues ~18 gateway HTTP requests (bootstrap x3, navigation x9, product x2, product-list x1, status x1, reviews x1, store-access x1) instead of ~6, and the Vercel Data Cache accumulates write-only entries. At 10,000 merchants this multiplies gateway invocations, Worker CPU and Postgres connections by roughly 3x for no benefit.
- RECOMMENDATION: Stop setting a per-call random `X-Request-ID` on cacheable GET reads (propagate the inbound request id instead, or carry it outside the fetch init headers). Add a test asserting two identical `storefrontReadInit` reads in one render produce one outbound fetch.
- CAPACITY: Directly multiplies gateway RPS and DB round-trips per storefront page view by ~3x; the single highest-leverage capacity fix in this dimension.

**[S1] Every /store/* route is dynamically rendered — no ISR or edge HTML cache exists**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:76` awaits `resolveThemePreviewToken(subdomain)`, which calls `cookies()` (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-preview-server.ts:12). Reading cookies opts the entire subtree out of static/ISR rendering, so `export const revalidate = 60` (layout.tsx:20), `= 30` (product/[productId]/page.tsx:12, collections/[slug]/page.tsx:12) and `= 60` (shop, products, blog, [...path]) never produce a cached HTML entry. `next.config.ts` declares no `experimental.ppr`, so there is no partial-prerender escape hatch.
- EXPECTED: Constitution line 260 makes `storefront-runtime` (Next.js/Vercel) 'SSR/ISR + Worker cache' canonical, with the 3-tier KV-versioned cache as the audited strong point.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:20; apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:76; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-preview-server.ts:12; apps/storefront-runtime/next.config.ts:35-141; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260
- RISK: 100% of storefront HTML is rendered per request on Vercel bom1 Node functions. There is no edge tier absorbing traffic; a viral store or a crawler burst translates 1:1 into Next SSR invocations, Liquid+jsdom renders and gateway calls. The 'ISR' half of the governed runtime does not exist at this SHA.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move the theme-preview cookie read behind a Suspense boundary or a dedicated preview route so the public storefront tree can prerender. Re-verify with `next build` output that `/store/[subdomain]/product/[productId]` reports as static/ISR rather than dynamic.
- CAPACITY: Sets the absolute ceiling on storefront throughput: every request costs a full SSR render plus the downstream fetch fan-out; nothing is servable from the CDN.

**[S1] Host-to-site resolution is uncached and is a synchronous fail-closed dependency on every storefront request**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `resolveAccess` fetches `${gateway}/v1/internal/store-access/resolve?host=` with `cache:'no-store'` and `AbortSignal.timeout(3000)` on every request (apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:112-142, invoked at :346). The gateway handler runs `resolveStorefrontSiteByHost`, which issues a `sites` SELECT by subdomain, or for custom domains a `sites` SELECT plus a `site_domains` SELECT plus a second `sites` SELECT (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/store-context.ts:209-254). No KV, Cache API, or in-process memo wraps it. `validGateCookie` (proxy.ts:245-256) documents the no-store requirement so an access-mode change is honoured immediately.
- EXPECTED: A per-request tenant resolution on the hottest path should be served from a short-TTL versioned cache (the same KV/Cache-API mechanism already built for storefront JSON), with explicit invalidation on access-mode and domain mutation.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:110-142; apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:346-358; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-store-access.ts:30-57; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/store-context.ts:201-258
- RISK: Adds one gateway round-trip and 1-3 Postgres queries to 100% of storefront traffic, uncacheable. It is also a total-outage single point of failure: any gateway error, timeout, or missing `INTERNAL_API_SECRET` turns every storefront on the platform into a 503 holding page (proxy.ts:347-354). A custom-domain host costs 3 DB queries before any content is fetched.
- RECOMMENDATION: Cache the resolve response in KV/Cache API keyed by host with a 5-30s TTL plus an explicit purge on access-mode, publish-status and domain mutations; keep the fail-closed path for a genuine miss.
- CAPACITY: One extra gateway invocation plus 1-3 DB queries per storefront request that no cache layer can currently absorb; the dominant fixed cost per page view.

**[S1] No cache stampede protection — every concurrent miss on a cold key runs the origin producer**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `cachedJson` does `cache.match(request)`; on miss it immediately calls `producer()` and only afterwards `waitUntil(cache.put(...))` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:114-129). There is no lock, no single-flight map, no Durable Object coordination, and no stale-while-revalidate background refresh in code — the SWR value is only a response header string (storefront-cache.ts:24-26).
- EXPECTED: A versioned cache in front of Postgres should coalesce concurrent cold-key requests (single-flight) or serve stale while revalidating, so a version bump or TTL expiry does not stampede the database.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:107-130; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:12-26; services/api-gateway/src/routes/themes.ts:156-157
- RISK: A theme publish bumps the version for a store and instantly invalidates every cached key for that tenant. Every in-flight request then executes the full producer against Postgres. For a busy store this is a synchronised burst of `sites` + `theme_releases` + `products` queries; the Cache API is also per-colo, so the burst is multiplied by the number of active Cloudflare colos.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add an in-isolate single-flight map keyed by cache key (promise coalescing) around `cachedJson`, and consider serving the previous version's entry while the new one is produced.
- CAPACITY: Determines the DB spike shape at TTL expiry and on every theme publish; unbounded concurrency to origin is the classic failure mode at 1k+ merchants.

**[S1] next.config declares `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` for ALL storefront paths while the Liquid path server-renders authenticated customer data**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `apps/storefront-runtime/next.config.ts:122-129` applies `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=3600` to `source: '/:path*'` with no exclusion for account, orders, cart or checkout. In the Liquid render path `renderLiquidPlan` calls `getStorefrontCustomerDrop(store.id, {includeOrders})` (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:571), which resolves the HS256 customer session cookie server-side and injects name/email/addresses/orders/wishlist into the rendered HTML; `/account`, `/account/orders`, `/account/addresses` render exactly that. Whether Next 16 / Vercel overrides this header for dynamically rendered App Router responses was NOT established at this SHA.
- EXPECTED: Any response whose body varies with the customer session must be `private, no-store` (or at minimum non-shared-cacheable). A blanket `public` directive on `/:path*` is unsafe for a multi-tenant storefront with server-rendered account pages.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/next.config.ts:122-129; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:555-575; apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/account/orders/page.tsx:10-27; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/customer-drop.ts:1-12; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/session.ts:1-16
- RISK: If any shared cache (Vercel edge, an intermediary proxy, or a merchant-fronted CDN on a custom domain) honours the declared header on a Liquid account page, one shopper's order history and address book can be served to another visitor of the same store for up to 5 minutes plus a 1-hour stale window. Even if Next currently overrides it, the config is one refactor away from the leak.
- RECOMMENDATION: Scope the `public, max-age=300` header to demonstrably anonymous paths, and add explicit `private, no-store` for `/store/:subdomain/account/:path*`, `/cart`, `/checkout`, `/orders`. Verify the real `Cache-Control` on a live authenticated `/account/orders` response before closing this.
- CAPACITY: Also caps the safe CDN-cacheable fraction: today no storefront HTML can be safely shared-cached while the Liquid customer drop renders server-side on the same host.

**[S2] Next cache tags are declared but never invalidated — `revalidateTag` is not called anywhere in the repo**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Storefront reads attach tags such as `storefront:<subdomain>`, `storefront:<subdomain>:products`, `storefront:<subdomain>:product:<id>` (store-service.ts:110,431-432,473-474; product-service.ts:29-31,44,57,72-75; review-service.ts:24-26; content-service.ts:84; composition-engine.tsx:387,463). A repo-wide search for `revalidateTag` returns zero hits in apps/storefront-runtime, services/* and packages/* — only `revalidatePath` in unrelated admin apps.
- EXPECTED: Tag-based invalidation is the only reason to pay the cost of tagging; a catalog or theme mutation should call `revalidateTag` (or an equivalent purge endpoint) so the Next Data Cache is not serving stale data for the full TTL.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:428-441; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/product-service.ts:66-82; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/review-service.ts:22-33; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:385-390; rg -n "revalidateTag" (0 hits outside next/cache imports in platform-console/theme-marketplace/platform-operations-portal)
- RISK: Merchant edits are invisible for up to the full revalidate window with no way to force a refresh; the tag metadata creates a false impression that targeted invalidation exists. Currently masked by the fact that the Data Cache never hits at all, so fixing the X-Request-ID finding without adding invalidation will surface up-to-60s staleness.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either wire a gateway-to-storefront revalidate webhook that calls `revalidateTag` on catalog/theme/navigation mutations, or delete the tags and document TTL-only semantics.
- CAPACITY: Correct invalidation is the precondition for raising the TTL, which is the cheapest way to raise the cacheable fraction.

**[S2] Only theme publish bumps the storefront cache version; catalog, navigation, page and domain mutations invalidate nothing**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `bumpStorefrontCacheVersion` / `purgeStorefrontCache` have exactly one call site: `purgeThemeStorefrontCache` in services/api-gateway/src/routes/themes.ts:140-158, which bumps the version for `sites.subdomain`, `store_name` and `custom_domain`, then deletes the single key `storefront-rest:bootstrap:0:<handle>`. Products, collections, categories, navigation, pages, policies and blog routes call neither. The per-key purge targets literal version `'0'`, so once any bump has occurred it deletes a key that no longer exists.
- EXPECTED: Every mutation that changes a cached storefront projection (products, collections, navigation, CMS pages, policies, blog, custom domain) should bump the tenant's cache version so the Cache API keys rotate.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/themes.ts:140-158; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:83-90; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:132-136; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:774; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:1041
- RISK: A merchant who edits a price, hides a product, or changes the menu sees the storefront lag by up to 60s with no way to force propagation. `cache.delete` is additionally colo-local on Cloudflare, so the per-key purge only affects the colo that served the publish request — the version bump is the only mechanism that actually works, and it is wired to one route.
- RECOMMENDATION: Call `bumpStorefrontCacheVersion(c, handles)` from every storefront-visible mutation, and drop the misleading version-'0' `purgeStorefrontCache` call or compute the live version first.
- CAPACITY: Correct invalidation is the precondition for raising the 60s TTL toward the values needed at 10k merchants.

**[S2] Zero cache hit-rate telemetry — only a response header, and the storefront render log hard-codes cache_hit: null**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `cachedJson` sets `X-Storefront-Cache: HIT|MISS|BYPASS` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:36-56) but emits no metric. The gateway's Analytics Engine binding `RUNTIME_TELEMETRY` is used only by the generic request middleware (services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:150-192), whose blobs are path/method/status-class/auth/error/country/ray — no cache state. The storefront's canary render log writes `cache_hit: null, // field present; NOT CONNECTED until per-request render cache is wired` (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:672).
- EXPECTED: Cache hit rate is the primary capacity lever for a storefront; it must be measurable per namespace and per tenant before any scale decision.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:36-56; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:150-192; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:660-675; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:340-342
- RISK: There is no way to detect from production telemetry that tier-1 is at 0% hit rate, or to observe the stampede after a theme publish. Capacity planning for 1k to 10k merchants would be done blind.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add the `X-Storefront-Cache` state as a blob and a 0/1 double to the existing `letbuyy.runtime.request` Analytics Engine data point, keyed by cache namespace and site id.
- CAPACITY: Without this, no capacity claim about the storefront cache can be evidenced.

**[S2] Liquid is the effective production renderer for urban-kicks-derived stores, contradicting the ART-17 'default OFF / canary' position**  ·  domain=theme  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `resolveThemeLiquidRenderingEnabled` returns `true` immediately when `templateConfig.rendererEngine === 'liquid'`, bypassing `THEME_LIQUID_RENDERING_DEFAULT_ENABLED = false` (packages/theme-engine/src/theme-governance.ts:14, 154-160). Migration 180 writes `default_template || jsonb_build_object('rendererEngine','liquid')` onto the urban-kicks theme row (database/supabase/migrations/180_urban_kicks_liquid_theme.sql:37). Theme install copies the theme's `default_template` into `sites.template_config` via `resolveRenderableThemeTemplateConfig` and `getThemeDefaultTemplate` (packages/theme-engine/src/index.ts:738-780; services/api-gateway/src/execution/theme-execution.adapter.ts:943,1189,1221), and `store-service.ts:684-686` passes `rendererEngine` through to the resolver. Migration 197 deliberately does NOT set the key for `letbuyy-core` and overwrites a migration-188 clone's `default_template`, so the two first-party themes render on different engines.
- EXPECTED: ART-17 and `THEME_LIQUID_PLATFORM_WIDE_FLIP_DECISION` (theme-governance.ts:15-21) record the platform-wide Liquid flip as ARB-deferred, with Liquid a per-theme canary that is default OFF pending a render-parity, SSTI/XSS and performance harness.
- EVIDENCE: packages/theme-engine/src/theme-governance.ts:14-21; packages/theme-engine/src/theme-governance.ts:144-181; database/supabase/migrations/180_urban_kicks_liquid_theme.sql:37; packages/theme-engine/src/index.ts:738-780; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:682-686; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/template-resolver.ts:241-271; database/supabase/migrations/197_letbuyy_core_first_class_theme.sql:44-52
- RISK: The governed statement 'Liquid default OFF / canary' is not what production does for any store on urban-kicks; the actual engine is decided by a DB jsonb key set by a migration, with no operator surface, no per-store toggle and no rollback path other than another migration. Two first-party themes silently diverge in render engine, chrome path and CPU profile.
- RECOMMENDATION: ARB decision: either declare Liquid the default and update ART-17, or remove `rendererEngine` from the urban-kicks `default_template` and route activation exclusively through an auditable per-store canary flag with an operator endpoint.
- CAPACITY: Liquid render costs jsdom+DOMPurify per section per request; the engine split means capacity per store is not uniform and cannot be modelled from one number.

**[S2] Liquid render runs jsdom + DOMPurify per section per request on the Vercel Node function, with a 5s wall-clock render limit and no render cache**  ·  domain=theme  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `sanitizeRenderedHtml` lazily constructs a JSDOM window plus a DOMPurify singleton and is awaited for every section (packages/theme-engine/src/theme-renderer.ts:477), every block (:926), the whole layout document (:647) and each app-block source (:1227-1228). Sections render concurrently via `Promise.all`, and the in-file comment at :706-718 records that a trivial section aborted at a 250ms `renderLimit` purely from sibling event-loop contention, that the limit was raised to 5000ms, and that the F9 benchmark shows sequential rendering is ~5x faster (42ms vs 227ms for N=25). No rendered-HTML cache exists — composition-engine.tsx:672 logs `cache_hit: null`.
- EXPECTED: A canonical Liquid renderer serving 10k merchants needs its sanitize pass off the hot render path and/or a rendered-fragment cache; a 5s wall-clock abort is a load-shedding hazard, not a safety boundary.
- EVIDENCE: packages/theme-engine/src/theme-renderer.ts:706-723; packages/theme-engine/src/theme-renderer.ts:1322-1387; packages/theme-engine/src/theme-renderer.ts:477; packages/theme-engine/src/theme-renderer.ts:647; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-engine/composition-engine.tsx:660-675; apps/storefront-runtime/package.json (jsdom ^29.1.1 is a storefront runtime dependency)
- RISK: Under load the renderLimit trips on healthy sections because of sibling contention, and the failure mode is a silent fallback to the React path with a different DOM (composition-engine.tsx:625-634). CPU per request is dominated by jsdom parsing, so storefront throughput per Vercel function is bounded by sanitizer cost, not by data fetching.
- RECOMMENDATION: Cache sanitized section HTML keyed by (release id, section id, settings hash, data hash) in the existing versioned cache, and/or sanitize once over the assembled document. Re-measure `renderLimit` afterwards and lower it back toward a real budget.
- CAPACITY: Sets CPU-per-render for every Liquid store; the 5s abort under contention is a correctness-under-load risk, not just a latency one.

**[S2] Non-200 storefront responses are never cached, so 404 and scrape traffic reaches Postgres unthrottled**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `cachedJson` only writes to the Cache API when the produced status is exactly 200 (`if ((produced.status || 200) === 200)`, services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:126-128), and `jsonResponse` sets `Cache-Control: no-store` for every non-200 (:42). The product-detail producer runs `loadPublishedStore` plus a `products` SELECT before returning 404 (public-routes.ts:834-852). The uncached `/:subdomain/status` route and `resolveAccess` also execute a `sites` SELECT for a nonexistent handle.
- EXPECTED: Negative results for a public, enumerable key space (product slugs, page slugs, unknown subdomains) should be short-TTL cached so an enumeration burst cannot be amplified into database load.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:114-129; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:40-47; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:828-852; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:607-624; packages/infrastructure/pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:36-59 (postgres max:5 per isolate)
- RISK: An unauthenticated crawler iterating `/product/<random>` on one store drives 2-3 uncached Postgres queries per request indefinitely. Combined with the uncached `resolveAccess` and `/status` calls, an unknown-host flood is a cheap way to saturate the pooled connections.
- RECOMMENDATION: Cache 404 bodies with a short TTL (5-15s) under the same versioned key, and add negative caching for unknown hosts in the store-access resolver.
- CAPACITY: Removes an unauthenticated DB-amplification path; matters most for the shared Hyperdrive pool.

**[S2] mobile-bff /v1/mobile/dashboard fans out to 6 uncached gateway calls, each repeating its own authorization query, over unbounded orders scans**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `mobileDashboard` issues `Promise.all` over six gateway requests — `/v1/analytics/dashboard`, `/analytics/orders`, `/analytics/revenue`, `/analytics/top-products`, `/analytics/traffic-sources`, `/payouts/summary` (services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:381-387). Each target independently calls `canAccessStore(db, auth.user, storeId)` (analytics.ts:554-556, 618-620). None is wrapped in any cache. `/v1/analytics/dashboard` then issues two `orders` SELECTs with `.eq('store_id',…).gte('created_at', since)` and no `.limit()`, aggregating revenue in JS (analytics.ts:568-579); the rollup projection short-circuits this only when it has data (:558-561).
- EXPECTED: A BFF composite should be a single fan-out with a shared authorization decision and a short-TTL cache, and analytics aggregation should be pushed into Postgres or the rollup projection rather than materialised in the Worker.
- EVIDENCE: services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:347-425; services/api-gateway/src/routes/analytics.ts:546-581; services/api-gateway/src/routes/analytics.ts:558-561; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:264
- RISK: One mobile dashboard open equals 6 gateway invocations plus 6 authorization queries plus at least 2 unbounded `orders` scans. At the governed ~30k orders/month a 30d dashboard pulls tens of thousands of rows into a Worker with a 128MB ceiling; the previous-period query doubles it.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add `.limit()` plus server-side aggregation (or make the rollup read mandatory) to the raw analytics fallback, hoist the store-access check into the BFF, and cache the composite per (store_id, period) for 30-60s.
- CAPACITY: Unbounded row fetch is the clearest OOM/latency cliff in the merchant surface as order volume grows.

**[S2] R2 object keys are user-scoped, not site-scoped, and MIME validation trusts the client-declared content type**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Upload key is `${folder}/${auth.user.id}/${Date.now()}-${uuid}.${ext}` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/upload.ts:168); `store_id` is recorded only in `customMetadata` (:196-203). `validateUploadMime(file)` inspects only `file.type`, i.e. the client-supplied multipart Content-Type (services/api-gateway/src/lib/upload-mime.ts:70-88) — no magic-byte sniff. Limits are 10MB standard / 50MB model / 100MB video (upload.ts:9-11,63). DELETE performs a real `R2_BUCKET.delete(key)` after `head` plus `customMetadata.store_id` ownership verification (upload.ts:236-271). Delivery is `${ASSET_PUBLIC_BASE_URL}/${key}` = `https://cdn.letbuyy.com/...` (wrangler.toml:285).
- EXPECTED: For a multi-tenant platform with DPDP erasure obligations, object keys should carry the tenant (`site_id`) so a tenant's blobs can be enumerated and erased by prefix; content type should be validated from the bytes, not the declared header.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/upload.ts:163-215; services/api-gateway/src/routes/upload.ts:236-271; services/api-gateway/src/lib/upload-mime.ts:16-88; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:285
- RISK: (1) Tenant/DPDP erasure: no prefix enumerates one site's media, so 'delete everything for store X' requires a full-bucket scan plus per-object metadata reads; a user owning several stores has all media interleaved under one prefix. (2) A file whose declared type is `image/png` but whose bytes are HTML/SVG is accepted and served from `cdn.letbuyy.com` — the SVG hard-block is bypassable by lying about Content-Type. (3) No orphan reconciliation between R2 objects and any DB media record.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add `site_id` to the key prefix for new uploads (the legacy-owner-key read path at upload.ts:97-99 already exists for back-compat), sniff magic bytes for the allowlist, and add a per-site media index enabling prefix erasure.
- CAPACITY: Prefix-based tenant deletion is the only erasure path that survives 10k merchants; per-object metadata scans do not.

**[S2] Bootstrap payload serialises the theme release twice and is fetched three times per page render**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `mapPublicStorePayload` emits both `release_config: release.template_snapshot || release.release_config` and `template_snapshot: release.template_snapshot || release.release_config` — the identical object twice — alongside `release_manifest`, `settings_snapshot`, `asset_manifest`, `cache_manifest`, `data_binding_manifest` (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:503-521). The storefront's `mapSupabaseToStore` reads only one of the two (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:548). Because request dedupe is defeated, `GET /storefront/:subdomain` is fetched three times per product-detail render (layout, generateMetadata, page).
- EXPECTED: The Liquid bundle / template snapshot should cross the wire once per render, in one field.
- EVIDENCE: packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:495-523; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:543-560; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/store-service.ts:58-70; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:580-602
- RISK: The theme bundle (24 Liquid sections for urban-kicks) is transferred 6x per page render (2 copies x 3 fetches). This is the dominant response-payload driver for the storefront-to-gateway hop and is paid on every request because the Data Cache never hits.
- RECOMMENDATION: Drop `release_config` from the public payload (or make it a reference), and collapse the layout/page store fetches to one after fixing the dedupe key.
- CAPACITY: Largest single lever on gateway egress bytes and Next SSR deserialization cost per storefront request.

**[S2] Store layout fetches up to 100 full product rows on every page render purely to seed header search**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:99-112` calls `ProductService.getProductsByStoreSubdomain(subdomain)` (limit 100, product-service.ts:41-51) on every route under `/store/[subdomain]`, commented 'Fetch products for search'. The gateway returns `PRODUCT_SELECT_FULL` — including `description`, `images`, `tags`, `variants`, `seo_description` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:353-354) — and with `PRODUCT_VARIANTS_RELATIONAL_READS = "on"` additionally hydrates `product_variants` for all 100 products in a second query (storefront.ts:493-530; wrangler.toml:266).
- EXPECTED: Header search should call a search endpoint on demand (a projection exists: `STOREFRONT_SEARCH_PROJECTION = "on"`), not preload the catalogue into every SSR render.
- EVIDENCE: apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:99-112; apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/product-service.ts:41-51; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:353-359; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:493-530; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:263
- RISK: Every storefront page — including cart, account and checkout — pays a 100-row full-column product fetch plus a variant join. This is the largest RSC payload contributor and a constant per-request DB cost that scales with catalogue width, not with what the page renders.
- RECOMMENDATION: Replace the eager fetch with a client-triggered call to the existing search projection endpoint, or restrict it to a minimal `id,name,slug,price,image` projection.
- CAPACITY: Removes roughly 2 DB queries and the bulk of the payload from 100% of storefront renders.

**[S3] A KV read is issued per cacheable gateway call (~15 per page render) even on cache hits, and the rate limiter runs on a KV write-per-request fallback**  ·  domain=cost  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: `storefrontRequestCacheKey` awaits `storefrontCacheVersion`, which does `c.env.CACHE.get(...)` before the Cache API is consulted (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:74-101, 120-122). A product-detail render triggers 15 such calls (bootstrap x3, navigation x9, products x1, product-detail x2). Separately, production sets `RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK = "on"` with an in-config comment recording that Upstash is unprovisioned, that each limited request costs one KV write, and that 'Cloudflare KV has a daily write quota … REPLACE WITH UPSTASH before scale' (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:249-260).
- EXPECTED: Version lookups should be memoized per isolate/request, and the rate limiter should be on a backend without a daily write quota before the 1k-merchant step.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:74-101; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:114-122; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:249-260
- RISK: KV read latency is added to the critical path of every cached response, and KV write volume from the rate limiter is quota-bounded — the config itself names exhaustion as the failure mode at sustained volume.
- RECOMMENDATION: Memoize `storefrontCacheVersion` per handle for the lifetime of the request/isolate, and treat Upstash provisioning as a launch blocker for the rate limiter.
- CAPACITY: The KV daily write quota is a hard external ceiling on request volume as currently configured.

**[S3] Product-view analytics are silently skipped on every cache hit**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `emitProductViewedEvent` is called inside the `cachedJson` producer for `/:subdomain/products/:slug` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:857), so it only runs on a cache MISS. With a 60s TTL and STOREFRONT_CACHE=on, at most one view event is emitted per product per 60s per colo. `STOREFRONT_VIEW_SAMPLE_RATE = "0.1"` applies a further 10% sample on top (wrangler.toml:262; storefront.ts:220-232).
- EXPECTED: View telemetry should be emitted per request, outside the cached producer, so cache effectiveness does not silently distort merchant-facing analytics.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:834-864; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:216-232; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:262
- RISK: Merchant product-view counts under-report by exactly the cache hit rate, and the distortion changes whenever the TTL or hit rate changes — making the numbers unusable for merchant decisions and for capacity inference.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move the view emission outside `cachedJson` and document the sampling rate on the merchant analytics surface.

**[INFO] Storefront cache keys are provably tenant-isolated**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `storefrontCacheKey` applies `encodeURIComponent` to every part before joining on ':' (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:63-68); `encodeURIComponent` escapes ':' to '%3A', so no part can inject a separator. Keys are `storefront-rest:<namespace>:<version>:<handle>[:<identifier>][:<sorted query params>]`, and `<handle>` is `normalizeStorefrontHandle(subdomain)` — lowercased, constrained to `/^[a-z0-9-]{3,80}$/` (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:489-493) — which maps to the unique `sites.subdomain` column. When normalization fails the raw param is used for the key but `loadPublishedStore` returns null, producing an uncached 404. The Cache API request URL is a synthetic internal host carrying the key as the path (storefront-cache.ts:103-105).
- EXPECTED: No two distinct sites may share a storefront cache key.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:63-105; packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:489-493; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:741-760; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:580-602
- RISK: None identified. Cross-tenant cache poisoning via key construction is structurally excluded at this SHA. This holds only because no cached namespace is personalized — every `cachedJson` route (bootstrap, products, products-by-ids, product-detail, collection-detail, categories, navigation, pages, policies, policy, page, blog, blog-detail) is anonymous; theme-preview and customer routes are deliberately not cached.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a regression test asserting that no personalized route is ever wrapped in `cachedJson` and that key parts remain individually encoded, so a future refactor cannot introduce a collision.
- CAPACITY: Confirms the Cache API tier can be safely made more aggressive (longer TTL) once invalidation is wired.

**[INFO] Custom-domain certificate and DNS provisioning is Vercel-managed via the project-domains API, with DoH TXT + CNAME verification**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: POST `/custom/domains` validates the domain (rejects localhost/IPv4/public-suffix/LetBuyy-owned zones/reserved labels), screens for abuse, writes `sites.custom_domain` + `custom_domain_status='pending'` + a 24-byte hex verification token, upserts a `site_domains` row, and returns TXT `_letbuyy-challenge.<domain>`, CNAME and apex-ALIAS records (services/api-gateway/src/routes/custom-domains.ts:150-258). POST `/domains/verify` requires at least 2 matching TXT answers via DoH, then a CNAME answer matching the configured target, then calls `attachVercelProjectDomain` (POST /v10/projects/{id}/domains) and `readVercelDomainStatus` (GET /v9 project domain + GET /v6 domain config), mapping to pending/verifying/active/error (custom-domains.ts:260-350; services/api-gateway/src/lib/vercel-domains.ts:124-194). TLS issuance is therefore Vercel's, gated on `VERCEL_API_TOKEN` + `VERCEL_STOREFRONT_PROJECT_ID`. `apps/storefront-runtime/vercel.json` pins `regions: ["bom1"]`.
- EXPECTED: Constitution: Cloudflare Workers + Vercel, one India region, custom domains supported.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/custom-domains.ts:150-258; services/api-gateway/src/routes/custom-domains.ts:260-350; services/api-gateway/src/lib/vercel-domains.ts:34-45; services/api-gateway/src/lib/vercel-domains.ts:124-194; apps/storefront-runtime/vercel.json
- RISK: Conformant with the governed topology. Operational note: the runtime host-to-site mapping is read from Postgres on every request, so a domain change propagates immediately but at a permanent per-request cost; certificate issuance and edge routing depend on Vercel API availability and on secrets that are deploy-time only.
- RECOMMENDATION: No architectural change required. Confirm `VERCEL_API_TOKEN` and `VERCEL_STOREFRONT_PROJECT_ID` are provisioned in the production Worker before onboarding custom-domain merchants — they are not in wrangler.toml vars and were not verifiable here.
- CAPACITY: Vercel project-domain count is an external ceiling on custom domains per project at 1k-10k merchants; not established here.

### Adversarial verdicts (3)


**OVERSTATED** — Random X-Request-ID header defeats the Next fetch Data Cache and per-request dedupe on every storefront gateway call (claimed S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45 sets a fresh `X-Request-ID: crypto.randomUUID()` on every server-side gateway fetch. In the installed Next 16.1.6, both the Data Cache key (dist/server/lib/incremental-cache/index.js:240-259) and the request-memoization key (dist/server/lib/dedupe-fetch.js:60-82, installed at patch-fetch.js:928) include the request headers and strip only traceparent/tracestate. Every one of the 19 `storefrontReadInit(30-60s, tags)` reads therefore writes a Data Cache entry under a key that can never be read again, and duplicate fetches within one render are not deduplicated. No React.cache()/unstable_cache() wrapper exists in apps/storefront-runtime/src to compensate, and the route is fully dynamic (layout.tsx:75 -> theme-preview-server.ts:11 awaits cookies(), no PPR), so no Full Route Cache absorbs the duplicates either. Measured effect: a product-detail render issues ~22 gateway fetches where ~8 distinct URLs exist, roughly 2.5-3x amplification of gateway invocations and downstream Postgres reads. The fix is free — services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:292 already synthesizes a request ID when the header is absent. Two caveats to the original claim: the "governed 3-tier cache" cited from LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260 is a Worker-KV cache that does not exist in this app (zero KV usage in src), so no constitutional invariant is breached; and `revalidateTag` is called ZERO times repo-wide, so even with the cache key fixed tier 1 would serve an unpurgeable 30-60s stale window. Impact is cost/latency only, with graceful degradation.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: MECHANISM — CONFIRMED, all three legs read directly at this SHA. (1) apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45 sets `X-Request-ID: crypto.randomUUID()` on every server-side gateway fetch (present at HEAD 5af69e0c; introduced 2026-05-24 in e24e70ee). (2) Installed Next is 16.1.6 (apps/storefront-runtime/package.json:29). IncrementalCache.generateCacheKey (node_modules/.pnpm/next@16.1.6_*/node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/incremental-cache/index.js:165) builds cacheString from [MAIN_KEY_PREFIX, fetchCacheKeyPrefix, url, method, headers, mode, redirect, credentials, referrer, referrerPolicy, integrity, cache, bodyChunks] at :246-259, and strips ONLY traceparent/tracestate at :242-243; headers are materialised via Object.fromEntries(init.headers) at :240, so x-request-id is in the key. (3) dedupe-fetch.js:60-63 defines headersToExcludeInCacheKey = {traceparent, tracestate}; generateCacheKey at :65-82 includes filteredHeaders; patch-fetch.js:928 installs createDedupeFetch as the origin fetch; patch-fetch.js:560 passes the same init into generateCacheKey. Both keys therefore vary per call.
NO MITIGATION EXISTS. 19 storefrontReadInit(30-60s, tags) call sites (store-service.ts:110,430,472; product-service.ts:29,44,57,72; review-service.ts:24; content-service.ts:82; composition-engine.tsx:387,463; collections/[slug]/page.tsx:19). grep for `React.cache(`/`unstable_cache` in apps/storefront-runtime/src returns ZERO, so no higher-level memoization compensates. The api-client passes `signal: undefined` (packages/typescript/api-client/src/index.ts:5544), which does NOT opt out of dedupe (dedupe-fetch.js checks `options.signal` truthiness), and `{...defaultInit, ...init}` (client.ts:101-104) preserves the `next` option, so client.ts:62-64 does not force no-store. Cacheability gates pass: no authorization/cookie header on these paths (patch-fetch.js:355), method GET.
STRENGTHENING FACT the auditor missed: the storefront layout awaits cookies() via resolveThemePreviewToken (src/lib/theme-preview-server.ts:11, called at layout.tsx:75) and next.config.ts sets no PPR, so the route is fully dynamic — there is no Full Route Cache above the Data Cache. Also services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:292 already synthesizes a request ID when absent, so removing the client-side random header costs no traceability.
WHY OVERSTATED — three defects in the claim as written. (a) GOVERNANCE MISATTRIBUTION: LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260 and LETBUYY_CANONICAL_SYSTEMS.md:97,250 govern a "3-tier KV-versioned" cache, i.e. Worker KV. grep for KV/caches. in apps/storefront-runtime/src returns ZERO. The Constitution does not govern the Next Data Cache, so the cited "expected state" is not law; the defect rests on the code's own intent only. (b) ARITHMETIC: counting the real graph, a product-detail render issues ~22 gateway fetches, not ~18 — layout generateMetadata 5 (status + published + 3 nav via fetchWorkerNavigationBundle at store-service.ts:484-492), layout body 5 + products 1, page generateMetadata 5, page body 5 + reviews 1. getStoreBySubdomainAnyStatus (store-service.ts:85-102) runs twice, so the claimed "status x1" is understated. Ideal with working memoization is ~8 distinct URLs, not ~6; the ~3x ratio survives, the absolute breakdown does not. (c) FRAMING: revalidateTag appears ZERO times across apps/, services/, packages/ (excluding node_modules/.next), so the `tags` argument is inert and tier 1 was never a functioning purgeable cache — fixing this line is necessary but not sufficient, which undercuts "the governed tier is defeated". (d) SEVERITY: impact is purely cost/latency amplification with graceful degradation — no correctness, tenancy, money, or security consequence. At the governed envelope (~30k orders/month, one India region) 3x amplification of a low-single-digit req/s read path is not a scale blocker, so S1 is not earned; S2 is.

**OVERSTATED** — Every /store/* route is dynamically rendered — no ISR or edge HTML cache exists (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: No /store/* route can populate the Next.js Full Route Cache: apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:76 unconditionally reads a cookie via apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-preview-server.ts:12, forcing the entire subtree to dynamic rendering, and PPR/cacheComponents is off (no `experimental` block in next.config.ts; Next 16.1.6 defaults `cacheComponents: false`). Every `export const revalidate` in that subtree is therefore inert for HTML. This is a cost/latency gap against the governed "SSR/ISR + Worker cache" model, NOT a total absence of caching: (a) next.config.ts:124-126 sets `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=3600` on all pages and Next 16 preserves user-set Cache-Control (send-payload.js:58-62), so whether an edge HTML cache exists depends on unverified Vercel CDN behavior; and (b) the API tier is edge-cached by the gateway Worker (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts, `caches.default` + KV versioning, STOREFRONT_CACHE="on"), so bursts do not reach Postgres 1:1. The claim's "1:1 gateway calls" consequence is true only because of an unrelated bug — apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45 injects a random `X-Request-ID` into every fetch, and Next 16 hashes request headers into the Data Cache key (incremental-cache/index.js:240-258), making the `next: { revalidate, tags }` Data Cache unhittable.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: PART A — the dynamic-rendering half is CONFIRMED and correctly evidenced.
- apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/store/[subdomain]/layout.tsx:76 unconditionally `await resolveThemePreviewToken(subdomain)` with no explicit token, so apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/theme-preview-server.ts:12 `await cookies()` always executes. The layout wraps every route under /store/[subdomain]/**, so the whole subtree bails to dynamic rendering and the `revalidate` exports (layout.tsx:20; product/[productId]/page.tsx:12; collections/[slug]/page.tsx:12; page.tsx:10; shop:11; products:11; blog:20; [...path]:9) cannot produce a Full Route Cache entry.
- I verified the escape hatch is genuinely absent, not just unmentioned: apps/storefront-runtime/next.config.ts has no `experimental` block at all, and Next 16.1.6 defaults PPR off — node_modules/next/dist/server/config-shared.js:101 `cacheComponents: false`. Version pinned at apps/storefront-runtime/package.json:29 and node_modules/next/package.json ("16.1.6"). Note also that `src/proxy.ts` IS the middleware (Next 16 renamed middleware.ts→proxy.ts); there is no separate middleware file.

PART B — "no ISR or edge HTML cache exists" is NOT established, and it is the load-bearing half of the title.
- apps/storefront-runtime/next.config.ts:124-126 sets `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=3600` on `source: '/:path*'` — i.e. on exactly the /store/* HTML responses the finding says are uncacheable.
- The auditor appears to have assumed Next clobbers this with its dynamic default. It does not: node_modules/next/dist/server/send-payload.js:58-62 — "If cache control is already set on the response we don't override it to allow users to customize it via next.config" (`if (cacheControl && !res.getHeader('Cache-Control'))`), so the `private, no-cache, no-store` string from server/lib/cache-control.js:15 is skipped when a next.config header is present.
- Whether Vercel's Edge Network actually stores a function response carrying `public, max-age=300` (no `s-maxage`) is provider behavior I have no authorized live access to test. So the correct state is "no Next.js full-route/ISR cache" (source-verifiable) plus "shared-cache HTTP directive configured, honoring unverified" — not the absolute "no edge HTML cache exists."
- Adjacent, and arguably a stronger finding the auditor missed: if that directive IS honored, per-request HTML that varies on the theme-preview cookie (layout.tsx:76) is marked publicly cacheable for 300s with no `Vary: Cookie`, which would let a shared cache serve a merchant's draft/unpublished theme render to the public.

PART C — the risk chain ("1:1 ... gateway calls", "no edge tier absorbing traffic") is wrong as reasoned.
- Dynamic rendering does not defeat Next's Data Cache. The service layer explicitly uses it: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:24-30 `storefrontReadInit(revalidate, tags)` → `next: { revalidate, tags }`, used at store-service.ts:110/430/472 and product-service.ts:29/44/57/72. The auditor's inference "no full-route cache ⇒ 1:1 gateway calls" does not follow.
- There IS an edge tier absorbing traffic — just at the API layer, not HTML: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:107-129 `cachedJson` uses Cloudflare `caches.default` (line 21) keyed by a KV version (lines 74-88), wired into services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts (bootstrap:600, products:792/821/863, collections:1008, navigation:1054, pages/policies/blog:1084-1313), enabled by default (`STOREFRONT_CACHE = "on"`, services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:43,134). A crawler burst therefore does not reach Postgres 1:1. (tests/performance/storefront-cache.test.mjs is a source-string grep test, not behavioral — not VERIFIED-TEST.)
- The risk statement is nonetheless accidentally true for gateway HTTP calls, for a reason the auditor did not identify: apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:45 sets `X-Request-ID: crypto.randomUUID()` on every outbound fetch, and Next 16's fetch cache key hashes all init headers except traceparent/tracestate (node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/incremental-cache/index.js:240-258, `JSON.stringify([...url, init.method, headers, ...])`). A random per-call header makes every Data Cache key unique, so `storefrontReadInit`'s revalidate/tags can never hit. That is a distinct, separately reportable defect.
- Additionally, apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:118 does an uncached (`cache: 'no-store'`) gateway access-resolve fetch on every storefront request; even a perfect ISR setup would not remove per-request gateway traffic, which further undercuts framing this as the sole cause.

SEVERITY — S1 is not earned. This is a cost/latency architectural gap (governed "SSR/ISR + Worker cache" is delivering SSR + Worker cache, not ISR), not a correctness, money, tenancy, or data-loss defect; the constitutional envelope is ~30k orders/month; the Worker cache tier absorbs DB load; and a CDN directive is configured on the HTML. S2 is the defensible level.

**OVERSTATED** — No cache stampede protection — every concurrent miss on a cold key runs the origin producer (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:114-129). Severity S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE. Risk: a theme publish version bump instantly invalidates every cached key for a tenant, producing a synchronised burst of sites + theme_releases + products queries against Postgres, multiplied by the number of active Cloudflare colos.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: cachedJson has no request coalescing and no serve-stale path: on a Cache API miss it calls producer() unconditionally (services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:121-129), so concurrent misses on the same cold key each execute the full origin producer. The 300s stale-while-revalidate value is emitted only as a Cache-Control header string (storefront-cache.ts:13, 24-26, 42) and drives no background refresh in code. This is confirmed across all 13 cached public storefront endpoints (public-routes.ts:582-1267) with STOREFRONT_CACHE="on" in all three wrangler envs. However, the practical blast radius is materially smaller than a database stampede: (a) the version bump is a KV write (wrangler.toml:184-187, 313-317; storefront-cache.ts:77,89), which propagates eventually and is edge-cached, so invalidation is staggered per colo rather than instantaneous or synchronised; (b) shopper traffic is mediated by storefront-runtime's server-only Next data cache and page ISR (apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:1,24-31; revalidate=60/30 across store pages), which serves stale while revalidating and bounds gateway-side concurrency; (c) the producer's SQL path is capped at 5 pooled connections per isolate behind Hyperdrive (packages/infrastructure/pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:52; wrangler.toml:193-194, 322-323), so excess concurrency queues in-client rather than fanning out to Postgres. No governance document requires single-flight (LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260 requires only the KV-versioned cache), and the only test (tests/performance/storefront-cache.test.mjs) is a source-string grep, not a behavioral test. This is a scale-hardening efficiency gap that becomes relevant toward the 10,000-merchant end of the envelope, not a launch blocker.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S3 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: CODE FACT — CONFIRMED. I read services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts in full. cachedJson (107-130) is exactly as described: cache.match(request) at :121, `if (matched) return cachedResponse(matched)` at :122, then unconditionally `const produced = await producer()` at :124 and `c.executionCtx.waitUntil(cache.put(...))` at :127. There is no in-flight promise map, no lock, no serve-stale branch. SWR is only a header string: STOREFRONT_CACHE_SWR_SECONDS=300 at :13, consumed solely by cacheControlHeader() at :24-26 → jsonResponse() at :42. Cited lines are accurate at this SHA. No superseding implementation exists (`ls services/api-gateway/src/lib | grep -i cache` returns only storefront-cache.ts; `git log --all -- <file>` shows only f8c1cd75 then the freeze e1d6fb18 — no later fix). The path is live and enabled: 13 call sites in services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/public-routes.ts:582,774,808,834,934,1016,1041,1071,1101,1136,1177,1241,1267; STOREFRONT_CACHE = "on" in all three envs (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:43,134,260) — VERIFIED-CONFIG, not deployment. The bump path is genuinely reachable: themes.ts:156-157 sits inside purgeThemeStorefrontCache (themes.ts:142-157), called at themes.ts:642 and themes.ts:697 (publish and rollback). So I cannot refute the defect.

RISK NARRATIVE — OVERSTATED on four independently verifiable points.
(1) "Instantly invalidates … synchronised burst" is wrong about the mechanism. bumpStorefrontCacheVersion writes via c.env.CACHE.put (storefront-cache.ts:89), and CACHE is a KV namespace, not the Cache API (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:184-187 staging, 313-317 production). storefrontCacheVersion reads it with CACHE.get and no cacheTtl (storefront-cache.ts:77). Cloudflare KV writes are eventually consistent and KV reads are edge-cached, so the new version is observed at different times in different colos. The key rotation is inherently staggered over the propagation window, not instant or synchronised. (INFERRED from documented KV semantics, not repo source.)
(2) Shopper concurrency does not reach the gateway 1:1. apps/storefront-runtime/src/lib/api/client.ts:1 is `import 'server-only'`, and storefrontReadInit (client.ts:24-31) sets Next's `next: { revalidate, tags }` on every cached read — product-service.ts:29,44,57,72; store-service.ts:110,430,472; content-service.ts:82; composition-engine.tsx:387,463 — under page-level ISR (`export const revalidate = 60`/`30` in store/[subdomain]/page.tsx:10, products/page.tsx:11, product/[productId]/page.tsx:12, layout.tsx:20, collections/[slug]/page.tsx:12). I found no browser-side direct fetch to the 13 cached gateway endpoints. Next ISR serves stale while revalidating, so the concurrency arriving at cachedJson is bounded by Next revalidations, not by shopper count.
(3) The "burst against Postgres" is bounded per isolate. The bootstrap producer's loadSiteByHandle goes through loadSiteByHandleFromPooledSql (routes/storefront.ts:800, 656-667) → getPooledSql, whose postgres.js client is created with `max: options.maxConnections ?? 5` (packages/infrastructure/pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:52), behind Hyperdrive (wrangler.toml:193-194, 322-323). Concurrency above 5 queues in the client rather than fanning out to Postgres — latency amplification, not DB overload.
(4) Minor factual slip: the bootstrap producer (public-routes.ts:582-603) queries sites + theme_releases + app blocks (loadPublicStorefrontAppBlocks), not products; products is a separate cache key.

NOT GOVERNED. GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:260 says only "The 3-tier KV-versioned cache stays canonical" — nothing mandates single-flight or serve-stale. No stampede/single-flight/coalesce requirement exists in the governance tree (rg -i over GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/ returns nothing). So "Expected state" is engineering judgment, not law.

NO TEST COVERAGE EITHER WAY. tests/performance/storefront-cache.test.mjs is a source-string grep test (assertIncludesAll over readFileSync text, lines 12-16, 18-35); it asserts 'cache.match(request)' and 'cache.put(request, response.clone())' appear in the file. It exercises no behavior, so it is not VERIFIED-TEST and it neither confirms nor refutes coalescing.

SEVERITY. TTL is only 60s (storefront-cache.ts:12), so steady state already re-runs each producer once per key per colo per minute; the missing single-flight adds a multiplier only across the producer's latency window. Combined with the Next ISR layer, the 5-connection pool cap, staggered KV propagation, and the Constitution's envelope (100 → 10,000 merchants, ~30k orders/month ≈ 1 order/minute platform-wide), this is a scale-hardening efficiency gap with no correctness, money, tenancy, or security impact. S1 is not earned; S3 is the defensible level. Evidence state VERIFIED-SOURCE holds for the narrowed code-absence claim; the risk projection is INFERRED.

### Unknowns
- Whether Vercel/Next 16.1.6 actually emits the next.config `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=3600` on a dynamically rendered `/store/<sub>/account/orders` response, or overrides it with a private/no-store directive. Settled by: `curl -sI https://<store>.letbuyy.com/account/orders -H 'Cookie: <customer session>'` against the deployed app and reading Cache-Control plus the Vercel cache headers.
- Whether Cloudflare's Cache API honours `stale-while-revalidate=300` for `caches.default` entries at this account/plan, or simply evicts at max-age. Settled by observing `X-Storefront-Cache` values across the 60-360s window against the deployed Worker.
- Actual production cache hit rate per namespace. No telemetry exists (only the `X-Storefront-Cache` header); settled only by adding the metric or sampling edge logs.
- Which theme slug new stores are provisioned with in production (`sites.theme_id` at creation). `POST /stores` takes `body.theme_id` from the caller (services/api-gateway/src/routes/stores.ts:729) with no default visible in the gateway, so the Liquid-vs-React split across the live merchant base cannot be derived from source. Settled by `select theme_id, count(*) from sites group by 1` and `select slug, default_template->>'rendererEngine' from themes`.
- Whether migrations 180/188/197 have all been applied to the production database, which determines whether `letbuyy-core` currently carries `rendererEngine='liquid'` (188 clone, un-repaired) or not (197 repaired). Settled by querying `themes` on production.
- Whether `VERCEL_API_TOKEN` and `VERCEL_STOREFRONT_PROJECT_ID` are provisioned as Worker secrets in production; they are absent from wrangler.toml vars (by design) and custom-domain activation returns 503 without them.
- Whether PostgREST `db-max-rows` caps the unbounded `orders` SELECT in `/v1/analytics/dashboard`; the supabase-js call sets no `.limit()`. Settled by reading the Supabase project's PostgREST configuration.
- Whether the `RUNTIME_TELEMETRY` Analytics Engine dataset is actually receiving data points in production (the binding is declared and `writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint` is called, but delivery is unverifiable from source).
- The real p50/p99 of the per-request `store-access/resolve` gateway hop, needed to size the proposed cache TTL and to judge how much of storefront TTFB it accounts for.
- Cloudflare KV daily write-quota headroom under `RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK=on` at current traffic; the config comment names exhaustion as the failure mode but gives no measured number.
- Whether any merchant custom domain is fronted by the merchant's own CDN, which would independently honour the `public, max-age=300` header regardless of Vercel's behaviour. Requires the live domain inventory.
- Vercel project-domain count limits for the storefront project, which bound how many custom domains can be attached before hitting a provider ceiling.
- Whether the Hyperdrive-pooled storefront read path connects as a role subject to RLS FORCE, or as a BYPASSRLS role. Only the explicit `store_id`/`subdomain` predicates were verified in source; the DB role grant was not (out of scope for this dimension but load-bearing for the pooled reads it introduces).

## DIMENSION: async

### Narrative summary
The transactional outbox has TWO write paths and only one is constitutional. Path A (SQL): every money/order RPC writes its `outbox_events` row inside the same `plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER` function body as the domain mutation — `create_checkout_order` → `checkout.completed` (000_baseline.sql:2160), `confirm_order_payment` → `payment.confirmed` (:1531), `complete_order_refund` (:1168), `create_order_with_inventory_reservations` → `order.created` (166_d6:276), `complete_refund_with_ledger` → `refund.succeeded` (168_b1_b2:519). These are genuinely same-transaction and satisfy ARCH-R8/ART-19. Path B (TypeScript): `EventExecutionAdapter.emitDomainEvent` (event-execution.adapter.ts:60-77) does a standalone PostgREST `.from('outbox_events').upsert()` AFTER the domain write commits — 35+ call sites including checkout, orders, payments, inventory, settlements — and swallows the error entirely when the table is missing from the schema cache (:79-83). Its idempotency key embeds `requestId || crypto.randomUUID()` (orders.ts:427), so a client retry with a fresh request-id writes a DUPLICATE event rather than conflicting. The purpose-built same-transaction helper `writeWithOutbox` (event-core/src/index.ts:1120) has ZERO production callers — only a unit test. Five Cloudflare Queues are declared, all produced by api-gateway and all consumed by services/queue-workers (never by a reserved service): events, analytics, search, notifications, runtime, plus two DLQs. The reserved services are confirmed manifest-only (no src, no wrangler.toml). The real relay is NOT the queues — it is the api-gateway cron `*/15 * * * *` (wrangler.toml:236) running `outbox.dispatchBatch({outboxLimit:50})` and `queue.consumeQueues(...,50)` (index.ts:947,966). That imposes a hard ceiling of 50 x 96 = 4,800 outbox events/day; at ~8 outbox events per order the platform saturates at ~600 orders/day, well under the constitutional ~1,000 orders/day (30k/month) envelope — the backlog grows without bound at the target scale. Staging has `crons = []` (wrangler.toml:120), so the relay never runs in staging at all. Idempotency is real at the DB layer (unique constraints on `outbox_events.idempotency_key`, `platform_queue_jobs(queue_name,idempotency_key)`, `webhook_deliveries(subscription_id,event_id)`, `payment_webhook_events(provider,provider_event_id)`) but ABSENT at the Cloudflare-queue email layer: the batch handler calls `dispatchEmailDeliveries` (index.ts:598) before ack, and any later failure retries the ENTIRE batch (index.ts:621), re-sending every already-sent email — up to 7 duplicate order-confirmation emails per customer on the notifications queue. There is NO tenant fairness anywhere: `claim_outbox_events` (:754), `claim_platform_queue_jobs` (:822), `claim_webhook_deliveries` (:966) and `expire_inventory_reservations` (141:323) all order globally by `created_at`/`expires_at` ASC with no per-site cap, so one hot merchant starves every other. Outbound webhook SSRF protection does not exist: `isValidWebhookTarget` (notification-core:505-512) and `isValidAppLifecycleTarget` (app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:34-41) check only `https:` + non-empty hostname, so `https://169.254.169.254/` or `https://10.0.0.5/` is accepted at registration, at fanout and at dispatch. Inbound provider ingress is correct on ordering — Razorpay verifies HMAC with `timingSafeEqual` and records `payment_webhook_events` BEFORE any mutation, short-circuiting duplicates (index.ts:718-775) — but there is deliberately NO timestamp/replay window (:752-758) and the `webhook_subscriptions.replay_window_seconds` column is declared (148:56) and never read. The DPDP consent gate for WhatsApp IS genuinely fail-closed: missing row, missing table and DB error all block the send (platform-queue-consumer.ts:853-907, 710-728). Provider mocks are NOT uniformly honest: the push dispatcher runs `PUSH_DISPATCH_MODE = "dry-run"` in production (wrangler.toml:257) and counts a dry run as `sent` (push-dispatcher.ts:381-384), a direct R-22/PAY-R10 violation. Every queue/outbox "test" in the repo is a regex over source text, not a behavioral test. A partition-shadow trigger fires on every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE of the five hottest tables including `outbox_events` and `platform_queue_jobs` (131:112-141), adding a `retention_policies` lookup plus a shadow write to each — roughly 2-3x write amplification on exactly the tables the relay hammers.


### Findings (21)


**[S0] Post-commit TypeScript outbox dual-write violates ARCH-004/PAY-007 at 35+ call sites and silently swallows loss**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Two outbox write paths coexist. The SQL path is compliant: `create_checkout_order` (000_baseline.sql:2160), `confirm_order_payment` (:1531), `complete_order_refund` (:1168), `create_order_with_inventory_reservations` (166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:276) and `complete_refund_with_ledger` (168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:519) all INSERT the outbox row inside the same plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER body as the domain mutation, so it is genuinely one transaction. The TypeScript path is not: `EventExecutionAdapter.emitDomainEvent` performs a standalone PostgREST `.from('outbox_events').upsert()` after the caller's RPC has already committed (event-execution.adapter.ts:60-77), and returns null instead of raising when the table is absent from the schema cache (:79-83). It has 35+ production call sites including checkout.ts:313, storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:184, orders.ts:417, payments/settlements/inventory. The dedicated same-transaction helper `writeWithOutbox` (packages/event-core/src/index.ts:1120-1166) has zero production callers - only packages/event-core/test/event-core.test.ts:101.
- EXPECTED: ART-19 / ARCH-R8 require the domain write and the event row to commit in the SAME transaction. ARCH-004/PAY-007 name this explicitly. Every outbox emission must be inside the RPC, and an outbox write must never be swallowed.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:60; services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:79; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:2160; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:276; packages/event-core/src/index.ts:1120; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:184; services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:417
- RISK: A gateway isolate that dies, a network blip, or a Supabase 5xx between the committed order/payment mutation and the PostgREST outbox POST loses the event permanently with no compensating record. Because `isMissingOptionalEventTable` returns null on a schema-cache miss, a mis-migrated environment loses EVERY event from these 35+ sites while the API keeps returning 200. Downstream: no order-confirmation email, no analytics row, no search projection, no merchant webhook - and no trace that anything was dropped.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move every one of the 35+ `emitDomainEvent` call sites into the RPC that performs the corresponding domain write, so the outbox INSERT rides the same transaction (the SQL path already proves the pattern). Until that lands, delete the `isMissingOptionalEventTable` swallow so a failed outbox write fails the request loudly. Retire `writeWithOutbox` or wire it - a same-transaction helper with only a test caller is misleading, since PostgREST cannot span statements in one transaction anyway.
- CAPACITY: Every silently dropped event is an order that never reaches analytics, search or notification, so measured throughput looks healthy while delivery quietly degrades - the failure is invisible in the queue depth metrics used for capacity planning.

**[S0] Outbox relay is capped at 4,800 events/day, ~1.7x under the constitutional 30k orders/month envelope**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The only outbox relay in the system is the api-gateway scheduled handler on `crons = ["*/15 * * * *"]` (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236) calling `outbox.dispatchBatch({ dispatcherId, outboxLimit: 50, webhookLimit: 50 })` (services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:947). `claim_outbox_events` hard-caps at `LIMIT LEAST(GREATEST(p_limit,1),100)` (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:756). That is 50 x 96 ticks = 4,800 outbox events/day. The Cloudflare Queues do NOT relay the outbox - `consumePlatformQueuesForCloudflareQueue` only drains `platform_queue_jobs` (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:379-392); nothing outside the cron ever calls `dispatchBatch`. Staging is worse: `crons = []` (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:120), so the relay never runs there at all. Measured fanout is ~8 outbox events per prepaid order (checkout.started, checkout.completed, order.created, payment.confirmed, payment.captured, order.paid, order.fulfilled, order.delivered).
- EXPECTED: The Constitution's envelope is 100 -> 1,000 -> 10,000 merchants at ~30,000 orders/month (~1,000 orders/day), which at ~8 events/order requires ~8,000 outbox events/day of sustained relay capacity.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:120; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:947; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:966; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:756; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:379
- RISK: At the constitutional target of ~1,000 orders/day the relay ingests 8,000 events/day against a 4,800/day ceiling. `outbox_events` grows monotonically at ~3,200 rows/day of permanent backlog, event latency rises without bound past the 15-minute tick, and order-confirmation emails, analytics and merchant webhooks fall arbitrarily far behind. Because staging has no cron at all, this ceiling can never be observed before production.
- RECOMMENDATION: Raise the cron to * * * * * (1 min) and/or raise outboxLimit toward the 100 RPC cap, and loop `dispatchBatch` within a tick until `claimed < limit`. Measure sustained events/day against a real order-rate load test before the 1,000-merchant step. Separately, enable the staging cron so the relay is exercised pre-production.
- CAPACITY: This is the single hardest throughput ceiling in the async tier: 4,800 outbox events/day = ~600 orders/day. Every other queue is downstream of it, so no amount of Cloudflare Queue concurrency raises the ceiling.

**[S0] Cloudflare batch retry re-sends transactional emails - no idempotency check before Resend**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `dispatchEmailDeliveries` iterates the whole batch and calls `sendResendEmail` per delivery with no prior lookup of an already-sent record and no Resend idempotency header (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:481-524, called at :598). It runs BEFORE the ack. Any throw anywhere later in the try block - or a Resend failure on message N - falls into `catch { for (const delivery of deliveries) delivery.message.retry(); }` (:621), which retries the ENTIRE batch including the messages whose emails already went out. The notifications queue carries the order-confirmation / cancelled / refunded / gift-card / invite / contact emails (order-confirmation-email.ts:128 sends to NOTIFICATION_QUEUE) and is configured max_batch_size = 25, max_retries = 6 (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:292-299).
- EXPECTED: A queue consumer must be idempotent per message: a redelivery of an already-processed message must not repeat an externally visible side effect. Cloudflare Queues guarantee at-least-once delivery, so this is a designed-for case, not an edge case.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:481; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:598; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:621; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:292; services/api-gateway/src/lib/order-confirmation-email.ts:128
- RISK: One transient Resend 5xx on the 20th message of a 25-message batch re-sends the 19 emails that already succeeded, and repeats on every one of the 6 retries: up to 7 duplicate order-confirmation emails per customer for the whole batch. Beyond the customer-trust damage this drives spam complaints against the sending domain, which degrades deliverability for all merchants on the shared Resend sender.
- RECOMMENDATION: Ack each message individually as it succeeds (`delivery.message.ack()` inside the loop) and `retry()` only the specific failed message, instead of the all-or-nothing batch handling at :606/:621. Additionally record a per-`idempotencyKey` sent-marker row and check it before calling `sendResendEmail`, so a redelivery after a partially-committed batch is still a no-op.
- CAPACITY: Retry amplification is multiplicative with batch size: worst case per queue is events 50x4=200, analytics 50x9=450, search 25x9=225, notifications 25x7=175, runtime 50x11=550 deliveries. Each delivery attempt also re-writes 1 distributed_queue_messages row per message + 2 telemetry rows, so a single poisoned analytics batch costs ~468 row writes before the DLQ.

**[S1] No tenant fairness in any claim path - one hot merchant starves every other**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: All four work-claiming functions order strictly by arrival time across the entire multi-tenant table with no per-site cap, no round-robin, and no partition key in the ORDER BY: `claim_outbox_events` ORDER BY created_at ASC (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:754), `claim_platform_queue_jobs` ORDER BY created_at ASC (:822), `claim_webhook_deliveries` ORDER BY created_at ASC (:966), `expire_inventory_reservations` ORDER BY expires_at ASC (141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:323). The consumer code adds nothing: `consumeQueues` simply loops queue names with a flat budget (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:396-427).
