A `partition_key` is written to `distributed_queue_messages` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:230) but that table has zero readers, so it influences nothing. The Cloudflare Queue layer has no tenant key either.
- EXPECTED: In a shared multi-tenant async tier at 1,000 -> 10,000 merchants, work claiming must bound the share any single site_id can take per claim round (per-tenant cap, weighted round-robin, or per-tenant lanes) so tail latency for a quiet tenant is independent of a noisy one.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:754; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:822; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:966; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:323; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:396; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:230
- RISK: One merchant running a flash sale, a bulk product import, or a webhook-subscription storm fills the first 50 rows of every 15-minute claim window. Every other merchant on the platform sees order-confirmation emails, analytics and outbound webhooks stall for as long as the burst lasts. With the relay already at ~1.7x under capacity, a single hot tenant converts a bounded backlog into a platform-wide outage of the async tier. The inventory sweeper has the same property: one tenant's expired reservations can consume the whole 250-row budget, leaving another tenant's stock locked for hours.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a per-site cap to each claim function - e.g. rank rows with `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY site_id ORDER BY created_at)` and take the lowest ranks first, so a claim of 50 spreads across up to 50 distinct tenants before taking a second row from any one. Apply the same to expire_inventory_reservations. This is a pure SQL change inside the existing SECURITY DEFINER functions.
- CAPACITY: This is the difference between a per-tenant capacity limit and a platform-wide one. Without it, the 4,800 events/day relay ceiling is not shared fairly - it is first-come-first-served, so the platform's effective per-merchant SLA is set by the noisiest merchant.

**[S1] No SSRF guard on outbound webhook targets - private and link-local addresses accepted (SEC-R11)**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Target URL validation is `url.protocol === 'https:' && url.hostname.length > 0` and nothing else. That single check is the whole guard, and it is applied at all three points: merchant registration (services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:470 via `isValidWebhookTarget`), outbox fanout eligibility (services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:177), and the dispatcher itself (services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:34-41, enforced at :75 by `isValidAppLifecycleTarget`). The implementation is packages/notification-core/src/index.ts:505-512. There is no DNS resolution check, no allow/deny list, and no comparison against RFC1918 / 127.0.0.0/8 / 169.254.0.0/16 / ::1 / fc00::/7 - a grep for any of those ranges across notification-core, webhook-service and the notifications route returns nothing. The dispatch `fetch` also carries no AbortSignal or timeout (app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:89-105).
- EXPECTED: SEC-R11 names webhook SSRF explicitly. A merchant-supplied outbound target must be validated against private, loopback, link-local and internal address ranges before the request is issued, with a bounded timeout and a capped redirect policy.
- EVIDENCE: packages/notification-core/src/index.ts:505; services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:470; services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:177; services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:34; services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:75; services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:89
- RISK: Any authenticated merchant can register `https://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` or `https://10.0.0.5/admin` as a webhook subscription target. The webhook-service then POSTs a signed, tenant-scoped payload there on every matching event and stores up to 2,000 characters of the response body in `webhook_deliveries.response_body_preview` (readPreview, app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:43-46) - which the merchant can read back via the deliveries API. That is a merchant-triggered blind-to-full SSRF read primitive against anything reachable from the Worker egress. The absent timeout compounds it: a deliberately slow target holds a dispatcher slot out of the 50-per-tick budget.
- RECOMMENDATION: Extend `isValidWebhookTarget` to reject hostnames that are IP literals in private/loopback/link-local/unique-local ranges and to reject `localhost`/`*.local`/`*.internal`, and re-validate after DNS resolution at dispatch time to close the rebinding window. Stop echoing the response body back to merchants, or truncate it to a status code only. Add an AbortSignal timeout (e.g. 10s) and disable redirect following on the dispatch fetch.
- CAPACITY: The missing dispatch timeout is directly capacity-relevant: the webhook dispatcher claims 50 deliveries per cron tick and processes them serially (services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:651-680), so a handful of slow targets can consume the entire tick and stall every other merchant's webhooks.

**[S1] Signed tenant envelope expires after 15 minutes, permanently dropping queue messages once a backlog forms**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `verifiedTenantSiteId` rejects any tenant-scoped message whose envelope `issuedAt` is more than 15 minutes from now: `if (!Number.isFinite(issuedAtMs) || Math.abs(Date.now() - issuedAtMs) > 15 * 60_000) throw new QueueProcessingError('Tenant envelope is expired or malformed', { deterministic: true })` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:172-175). A deterministic error is NOT retried - `normalizeDeliveries` converts it to `quarantinedDelivery` (:434-441), which is recorded as `dead-lettered` and then acked with the rest of the batch at :606. The producer side fails open by contrast: `signQueueTenantEnvelope` returns the body unsigned when no secret is configured (services/api-gateway/src/lib/queue-tenant-envelope.ts:38-40), while the consumer hard-fails on a missing envelope (:163-166).
- EXPECTED: A queue message's authenticity window must be long enough to survive the queue's own worst-case retry and backlog window. The notifications queue alone allows 6 retries at 60s (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:292-299), and a backlog can hold a message far longer than 15 minutes.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:172; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:434; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:606; services/api-gateway/src/lib/queue-tenant-envelope.ts:38; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:292
- RISK: The moment any queue backs up past 15 minutes - which the 4,800 events/day relay ceiling makes likely at the constitutional order rate - every tenant-scoped message in that backlog is quarantined as 'expired', recorded dead-lettered, and acked. That is silent permanent loss of order-confirmation emails at exactly the moment of peak load, and the failure mode is self-reinforcing: the busier the platform, the more messages are destroyed. Separately, if QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET/INTERNAL_API_SECRET are not provisioned with identical values on BOTH the api-gateway and queue-workers, the producer emits unsigned bodies and the consumer quarantines 100% of them.
- RECOMMENDATION: Do not treat envelope expiry as deterministic - a stale envelope means the system is slow, not that the message is malicious. Either widen the window to exceed the worst-case queue residence time, or re-sign on enqueue-retry, or move authenticity to a non-time-bound HMAC over (siteId, messageId, idempotencyKey) with replay protection from the existing idempotency keys. Also make `signQueueTenantEnvelope` fail closed so a missing secret is a deploy-time error rather than a silent 100% quarantine.
- CAPACITY: Couples correctness to load: message loss begins precisely when queue residence time exceeds 15 minutes, which is the same threshold the under-provisioned relay will cross first.

**[S1] Dead-letter redrive for Cloudflare queues is a no-op - nothing consumes distributed_queue_messages**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: The operator endpoint `POST /runtime/queues/:queueName/redrive-dead-letters` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:600-619) calls `redrive_distributed_dead_letters`, which only flips `distributed_queue_messages` rows from 'dead-lettered' to 'retrying' and sets available_at = NOW() (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:6937-6961). Nothing ever reads them back: a grep for `claim_distributed_queue_messages`, `complete_distributed_queue_message`, `heartbeat_distributed_queue_message` and `dead_letter_distributed_queue_message` across every .ts file in the repo returns zero callers, even though all four RPCs exist in the schema. `distributed_queue_messages` is written only by `persistQueueBatch` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:249-253) and never claimed. queue-workers also declares no `[[queues.producers]]` in its wrangler.toml, so it cannot re-enqueue to Cloudflare either. The DLQ consumers themselves record and ack (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:301-307, 318-324) and have no onward dead_letter_queue, so after 3 more failures messages are dropped by the provider.
- EXPECTED: A dead-letter queue must have a drain path that actually re-delivers messages, or its existence must be documented as audit-only. An operator action labelled 'redrive' must redrive.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:600; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:6937; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:249; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:301; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:318
- RISK: During an incident an operator calls the redrive endpoint, receives `{ redriven_count: N }`, and reasonably concludes the messages are being reprocessed. Nothing happens. The dead-lettered work - order confirmation emails, analytics events, search projections - is permanently lost while the operator believes it has been recovered. Note the outbox DLQ is different and does work: `replay_outbox_dead_letter` (:7220-7261) resets outbox_events to 'queued' and the relay genuinely re-claims it.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either implement a consumer that claims `distributed_queue_messages` with status 'retrying' and re-executes the delivery (the four supporting RPCs already exist), or add a `[[queues.producers]]` binding to queue-workers and have the redrive re-enqueue to the source Cloudflare queue. Until one of those lands, make the endpoint return an explicit NOT_IMPLEMENTED rather than a count that implies recovery.
- CAPACITY: Removes the only recovery lever after a mass dead-letter event, so a capacity-driven backlog that spills into the DLQ becomes unrecoverable data loss rather than a delay.

**[S1] Push dispatcher runs dry-run in production and counts every dry run as sent (R-22/PAY-R10)**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `PUSH_DISPATCH_MODE = "dry-run"` is set in the production var block (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:257), alongside FCM/APNS identifiers that are all `SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_*` placeholders (:258-262). `isDryRun` returns true for that value (services/queue-workers/src/push-dispatcher.ts:99) and the dispatch loop then does `if (result.dryRun) { result.sent += 1; continue; }` (:381-384) - a dry run increments the same `sent` counter a real APNS/FCM delivery would. The result is written into the notification dispatch log by the consumer (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:665-676).
- EXPECTED: R-22/PAY-R10: a provider adapter in mock/dry-run mode must never report success. Mock outcomes must be a distinct, non-success status that cannot be mistaken for delivery.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:257; services/queue-workers/src/push-dispatcher.ts:99; services/queue-workers/src/push-dispatcher.ts:381; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:665
- RISK: Production dashboards and the notification dispatch log report N pushes sent when zero devices were contacted. Nobody discovers push notifications are entirely non-functional until a merchant or customer complains, because every automated signal says success. This is the exact failure the mock-honesty rule exists to prevent.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a distinct `dryRun` counter and leave `sent` at zero when `isDryRun(env)`, so a dry run can never be summed into a delivery metric. Assert in CI that no production var block sets a `*_MODE` to dry-run/mock while the corresponding secrets are placeholders, or make the adapter refuse to construct in a production ENVIRONMENT while in dry-run.
- CAPACITY: Zero real provider calls means the measured per-message external-call cost of the notification path is currently 0, so any capacity model built on today's production telemetry understates the real cost of enabling push.

**[S2] Runtime dead-letter queue is misclassified by a substring check - DLQ messages recorded as completed and re-dispatched**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Dead-letter classification is a substring test: `const status = batch.queue.includes('dead-letters') ? 'dead-lettered' : 'completed'` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:365). The events/analytics/search/notifications DLQ is named `letbuyy-dead-letters-production` and matches. The runtime DLQ is named `letbuyy-production-dead-letter-queue` (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:316, consumer at :318-324) and does NOT contain the substring `dead-letters`, so its messages are classified 'completed'. They are then persisted to `distributed_queue_messages` with status 'completed' (:249-253), counted as zero in the `letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total` metric (:283-296), and - because `dispatchEmailDeliveries` skips only `status === 'dead-lettered'` (:483) - are eligible for email dispatch. A separate, correct singular check `queueName.includes('dead-letter')` in `platformQueueNamesForCloudflareQueue` (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:365) does exclude both DLQs from platform drain, which is why the blast radius is limited today.
- EXPECTED: Dead-letter classification must be driven by the declared DLQ names in the queue topology (config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json already lists deadLetterNames per environment), not by an ad-hoc substring of one of two naming conventions.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:365; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:483; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:283; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:316; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:365; config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json:24
- RISK: The `letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total` metric under-reports: everything that dead-letters from `letbuyy-production-queue` (max_retries 10) is silently recorded as a successful completion, so the runtime queue appears to have a zero dead-letter rate no matter how badly it fails. The audit trail in distributed_queue_messages is correspondingly wrong. If any transactional-email job type ever reaches the runtime queue, those DLQ messages would additionally trigger a duplicate send.
- RECOMMENDATION: Replace the substring test with an explicit set of DLQ names sourced from config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json (or an env var), and use the same source for both the status classification at index.ts:365 and the platform-drain exclusion at platform-queue-consumer.ts:365 so the two can never disagree again.
- CAPACITY: Corrupts the primary dead-letter signal for the runtime queue, which is the metric an operator would use to detect that retry amplification is saturating the async tier.

**[S2] WhatsApp is unreachable in production because queue-workers never sets WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: `services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml` declares no WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE, WHATSAPP_PROVIDER or TWILIO_* variables in any env block (verified by grep - only api-gateway has them, at :59-60, :150-151, :277-280). The consumer constructs the adapter from `this.env.WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE` etc. (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:730-740). With mode undefined, `normalizeMode` falls back to `isProductionRuntime(credentials) ? 'live' : 'sandbox'` (packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:146-147), so ENVIRONMENT=production resolves to 'live'; `twilioLiveConfigured` is false with no credentials, so `available = false` (:280) and the consumer records status 'failed' with WHATSAPP_UNAVAILABLE and returns (platform-queue-consumer.ts:742-758). Meanwhile api-gateway production explicitly pins WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE = "sandbox" (:277), so the two runtimes resolve to opposite modes for the same feature. In sandbox mode `sendTemplate` returns `status: 'queued'` with a synthetic `wa_sandbox_*` message id (:297-311), which the consumer maps to log status 'queued' - success-shaped, distinguishable only via the `sandbox_send` metadata flag.
- EXPECTED: The WhatsApp-first notification router is the primary India-first customer channel. Provider mode must be explicit per runtime, consistent across runtimes, and a sandbox send must not be recorded with a delivery-shaped status (R-22/PAY-R10).
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:239; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:277; packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:146; packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:280; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:730; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:742; packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:297
- RISK: Every WhatsApp order-lifecycle and COD-confirmation notification produced by the queue worker is recorded 'failed' in production and never sent - the entire WhatsApp-first channel is dark, discovered only by reading dispatch logs. The mode divergence between the two runtimes means fixing one does not fix the other. Where sandbox mode IS active (api-gateway), the 'queued' status makes a mock send look like a real one at the status level.
- RECOMMENDATION: Declare WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE and WHATSAPP_PROVIDER explicitly in every queue-workers env block so the fallback is never exercised, and add a validator that the value matches across api-gateway and queue-workers for the same environment. Change the sandbox return to a distinct status (e.g. 'sandbox') that cannot be counted as queued/sent, mirroring the push-dispatcher fix.
- CAPACITY: Sets the current per-message external provider call count for WhatsApp to 0. Any capacity or cost model derived from today's production behaviour omits the real Twilio call and its latency inside the queue handler.

**[S2] Partition shadow-mirror trigger doubles writes on the five hottest tables including outbox_events and platform_queue_jobs**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow` is a FOR EACH ROW AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE trigger installed unconditionally on `worker_request_logs`, `runtime_telemetry_metrics`, `outbox_events`, `platform_queue_jobs` and `webhook_deliveries` (database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-141). Per row it performs a `SELECT * FROM retention_policies WHERE target_table = TG_TABLE_NAME` (:18-23), then on UPDATE a DELETE from the shadow table followed by an INSERT, and on INSERT a plain INSERT (:33-52). `outbox_events` is touched at least three times per event (INSERT by the RPC, UPDATE by claim_outbox_events, UPDATE by complete_outbox_event) and `platform_queue_jobs` likewise (INSERT at fanout, UPDATE at claim, UPDATE at complete), so each fires the trigger three times.
- EXPECTED: Under a constitutional 'no partitioning now' posture, a shadow-mirror trigger on the hottest tables is a transitional cost that must be explicitly budgeted against the throughput target, or gated off until the human-approved rename-swap.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:18; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:33; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:759; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:827
- RISK: Roughly 2-3x write amplification plus one extra catalogue-style SELECT per row on exactly the tables the relay hammers hardest. At the constitutional 8,000 outbox events/day this is ~24,000 extra shadow row operations and ~24,000 extra retention_policies lookups per day for the outbox alone, before counting platform_queue_jobs and webhook_deliveries. That directly consumes the same Postgres write capacity the under-provisioned relay needs to catch up, and the per-row policy SELECT is pure overhead that could be a cached lookup.
- RECOMMENDATION: Measure the trigger's actual cost with EXPLAIN ANALYZE on a representative outbox claim/complete cycle before the 1,000-merchant step. Cache or inline the retention_policies lookup rather than re-querying per row, and consider gating the mirror behind `set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false)` (:58) until the cutover window is actually scheduled - the shadows do not need to be warm for months.
- CAPACITY: Directly multiplies the DB write rate of the async tier by ~2-3x on its hottest path, compounding the relay throughput ceiling.

**[S2] Webhook delivery dispatch capacity is 4,800/day - below one subscription per merchant at target order volume**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Outbound webhook deliveries are dispatched only from the api-gateway cron, which POSTs to webhook-service with `limit: 50` (services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:961); webhook-service then claims via `claim_webhook_deliveries` (capped at 100 by the RPC, database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:968) and processes them SERIALLY in a for-loop with an awaited fetch per delivery (services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:651-680). At 96 ticks/day that is 4,800 deliveries/day. Fanout is one `webhook_deliveries` row per active matching subscription per event (services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:183-202), and every contracted domain event lists 'webhook-service' as a consumer, so ~8 deliveries per order per subscription.
- EXPECTED: At ~1,000 orders/day with even a single webhook subscription per merchant, sustained demand is ~8,000 deliveries/day.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:961; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:651; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:968; services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:183; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:1372
- RISK: Demand (~8,000/day) exceeds capacity (4,800/day) with one subscription per merchant, so `webhook_deliveries` accumulates permanent backlog. Serial processing makes it worse: 50 sequential HTTPS calls with no timeout means one slow partner endpoint can consume the entire tick. With max_attempts defaulting to 10 (:1372) and a 120s exponential base, a partner outage multiplies claimed rows and crowds out healthy merchants - which, given the absent tenant fairness, degrades everyone.
- RECOMMENDATION: Process the claimed deliveries with bounded concurrency (e.g. Promise.all over chunks of 10) rather than serially, add a per-request AbortSignal timeout, and raise the per-tick limit and/or cron frequency so sustained capacity exceeds the expected deliveries/day at target order volume. Add the per-tenant claim cap from the fairness finding so a single failing partner cannot monopolise the batch.
- CAPACITY: 4,800 deliveries/day against ~8,000/day of demand; serial dispatch means effective throughput is bounded by partner latency, not by the configured limit.

**[S2] No behavioral test exercises the queue consumer, the outbox relay, or the webhook dispatcher**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Every test covering this dimension reads source files as strings and regex-matches them. tests/operations/queue-storm-controls.test.mjs does `readFileSync('services/queue-workers/src/index.ts')` then `assert.match(queueHandlerBlock, /for \(const delivery of deliveries\) delivery\.message\.ack\(\)/)` (:5, :30-32). tests/operations/queue-bulk-cost.test.mjs counts regex occurrences of `upsertSupabaseRows(env, 'distributed_queue_messages'` (:31-35). tests/operations/cron-noop.test.mjs compares `indexOf` positions of source fragments to assert ordering (:14-25). tests/federation/event-spine-contracts.test.mjs reads 20+ source paths and matches strings (:6-25). None constructs a MessageBatch, none invokes the `queue()` handler, none invokes `runScheduledDispatch`, none invokes `dispatchWebhookDeliveries`. A grep for behavioral entry points (`consumeQueues`, `processAnalyticsJob`, `queue(batch`) across all test files returns only these four source-regex files.
- EXPECTED: The batch retry/ack semantics, the poison-message quarantine path, the tenant-envelope verification, the consent gate and the outbox claim/complete cycle all carry S0/S1 correctness risk and require tests that execute the code and assert on observed behaviour.
- EVIDENCE: tests/operations/queue-storm-controls.test.mjs:5; tests/operations/queue-storm-controls.test.mjs:30; tests/operations/queue-bulk-cost.test.mjs:31; tests/operations/cron-noop.test.mjs:14; tests/federation/event-spine-contracts.test.mjs:6
- RISK: Source-regex assertions pass for every defect in this report. They would not catch the duplicate-email batch retry, the 15-minute envelope expiry drop, the `dead-letters` substring misclassification, or the dry-run-counts-as-sent push bug - all four are present at this SHA with a green suite. They also break on harmless refactors while proving nothing about behaviour, which is the worst of both.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add behavioral tests with a fake MessageBatch and a stubbed Supabase/Resend client that assert: (a) a mid-batch failure does not re-send an already-sent email, (b) an envelope older than the window is not silently destroyed, (c) a message from each declared DLQ name is classified dead-lettered, (d) a dry-run push reports zero sent, (e) a missing wa_optins row blocks the send. Keep the source-regex tests only where they encode a governance invariant that cannot be expressed behaviourally.
- CAPACITY: No test measures per-message DB write counts or per-batch retry cost, so every capacity figure in this report is derived from static reading rather than from an executable benchmark.

**[S2] Inbound provider webhooks have no timestamp/replay window; replay_window_seconds is declared but never read**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The Razorpay handler verifies HMAC-SHA256 with `timingSafeEqual` before anything else (services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:718-736), then explicitly declines to enforce an age limit: a comment at :752-753 states idempotency is enforced by payment_webhook_events so age is not rejected, and an event older than 300s only produces a console.warn (:754-758). `webhook_subscriptions.replay_window_seconds` exists with a 60-86400 CHECK constraint (database/supabase/migrations/148_executable_notifications_router.sql:56, 84-85) but a repo-wide grep finds it only in SELECT column lists (services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:450, 487, 524) - it is never compared against anything. The outbound signer emits `X-LetBuyy-Timestamp` and signs `timestamp.body` (services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:53-55, 85-86), but `verifyLetBuyyWebhookHmac` (:57-63) checks only that a timestamp is present, never that it is recent.
- EXPECTED: SEC-R5 requires signature verification plus a timestamp/replay window plus idempotency recording before mutation. The ordering requirement IS met - `writeWebhookEvent` upserts payment_webhook_events with `resolution=ignore-duplicates` (packages/infrastructure/supabase-adapter/index.ts default prefer) and short-circuits on duplicate at :773-775 BEFORE any order mutation. The replay window is the missing leg.
- EVIDENCE: services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:718; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:752; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:764; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:773; database/supabase/migrations/148_executable_notifications_router.sql:56; services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:450; services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:57
- RISK: Idempotency substantially mitigates replay - a re-sent captured event is deduped by (provider, provider_event_id). But dedupe depends on `razorpayProviderEventId` finding a payment/refund/order id; when none is present the fallback key is `event:created_at:signature[0..24]` (:111-116), so an old signed body could be replayed indefinitely if the dedupe row is ever pruned by retention. The declared-but-unenforced `replay_window_seconds` is worse than absent: an auditor or merchant reading the API sees a configurable replay window that does nothing.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either enforce `replay_window_seconds` in `verifyLetBuyyWebhookHmac` and in the provider ingress handlers, or drop the column so no one relies on it. Confirm the retention policy for `payment_webhook_events` never prunes rows inside the window a provider may still retry - the dedupe table is currently the only replay defence.
- CAPACITY: payment_webhook_events is the sole replay defence, so its retention window is a capacity/cost tradeoff with a direct security consequence.

**[S2] Order-confirmation email is silently dropped when Resend is unconfigured while the queue job is marked processed**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `dispatchEmailDeliveries` checks `resendConfigured(env)` - which treats any key containing 'placeholder' or 'skip_needs_human' as unset (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:476-479) - and on failure writes an error log then `continue`s past the delivery (:493-508). The batch then proceeds to ack every message at :606, so the queue message is consumed and the job is recorded 'completed' in distributed_queue_messages even though no email was sent. The producer side has the same shape: `enqueueOrderConfirmationEmail` warns and returns when neither NOTIFICATION_QUEUE nor a configured Resend key is present (services/api-gateway/src/lib/order-confirmation-email.ts:132-144).
- EXPECTED: A transactional customer email that cannot be delivered because of missing configuration should surface as a failed/pending job that an operator can drain after fixing the secret, not be consumed as complete.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:476; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:493; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:606; services/api-gateway/src/lib/order-confirmation-email.ts:132
- RISK: If RESEND_API_KEY is missing or still a placeholder in production, every customer order-confirmation email is dropped and the message is acked. The only signal is a console.error line; the queue depth is zero, the dead-letter metric is zero, and distributed_queue_messages says completed. Discovery is by customer complaint. Because the message is acked it cannot be redriven once the secret is fixed.
- RECOMMENDATION: Treat a missing provider secret as a retryable (non-deterministic) failure so the message stays in the queue and drains once the secret is provisioned, or route it to the DLQ so it is at least visible and replayable. Add a startup/health assertion that a production ENVIRONMENT with the notifications consumer bound has a non-placeholder RESEND_API_KEY.
- CAPACITY: Makes a total notification outage indistinguishable from healthy operation in every queue metric, removing the signal an operator would use to detect it.

**[S2] Post-commit event idempotency keys embed a per-request UUID, so retries create duplicate outbox events**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `emitDomainEvent` upserts on `onConflict: 'idempotency_key'` (services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:74) against a global UNIQUE constraint (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:14619). But callers build keys that vary per HTTP request: `emitOrderEvent` uses `${eventType}:${siteId}:${orderId}:${input.requestId || crypto.randomUUID()}` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:427) and `emitStorefrontOrderCreatedEvent` uses `order.created:${storeId}:${orderId}:${requestId}` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:195). By contrast the SQL path uses stable keys derived only from the aggregate - `'checkout.completed:' || v_order_id::TEXT` (000_baseline.sql:2196), `'order.created:' || v_order_id::text` (166_d6:299) - which dedupe correctly.
- EXPECTED: An outbox idempotency key must be a deterministic function of (event type, tenant, aggregate) so that a client or gateway retry of the same logical operation conflicts and is deduped, exactly as the SQL path does.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:74; services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts:427; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/checkout-payment-routes.ts:195; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:14619; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:2196
- RISK: A retried status transition or a retried checkout confirm writes a second `order.paid` / `order.created` outbox row, which fans out to duplicate `platform_queue_jobs` and duplicate `webhook_deliveries` (the latter deduped by (subscription_id, event_id), but event_id is freshly generated per row). Consequences: double-counted analytics rollups (increment_analytics_rollup is additive, not idempotent - platform-queue-consumer.ts:1569-1579), duplicate merchant webhooks, duplicate notification side effects. It also adds avoidable load to an already over-subscribed relay.
- RECOMMENDATION: Drop requestId from these idempotency keys and derive them purely from eventType + siteId + aggregateId, matching the SQL emitters. Where a genuinely repeatable event needs distinguishing, use a stable business discriminator (e.g. the status transition target), never a random UUID.
- CAPACITY: Each duplicated event costs 1 outbox row + ~1-2 platform_queue_jobs + N webhook_deliveries, all against the 4,800 events/day relay ceiling.

**[S2] Stale inventory reservation sweeper is wired as ART-15 requires, but capped at 1,000 expirations/day**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The sweeper is genuinely wired: the queue-workers `scheduled()` handler calls `runInventoryReservationSweeper` under ctx.waitUntil (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:632-646), which invokes `expire_inventory_reservations` with `sweeperLimit` (services/queue-workers/src/inventory-reservations.ts:26-29). The RPC releases `inventory_levels.reserved` and marks the reservation 'expired' with reason 'ttl_expired' inside one transaction using FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED (database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:304-348). Configuration: `INVENTORY_RESERVATION_SWEEPER_LIMIT = "250"` (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:255) on `crons = ["17 */6 * * *"]` (:237) - four runs per day, hard-capped at 1,000 by the RPC's LEAST(...,1000).
- EXPECTED: ART-15 requires the stale-reservation sweeper to be WIRED - satisfied. Sustained capacity must also exceed the reservation expiry rate at the ~1,000 orders/day target, where abandoned checkouts typically outnumber completed orders.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:632; services/queue-workers/src/inventory-reservations.ts:26; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:304; database/supabase/migrations/141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:323; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:255; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:237
- RISK: 250 x 4 = 1,000 expirations/day. At ~1,000 orders/day with a normal 2-3x abandoned-to-completed checkout ratio, expiries plausibly reach 2,000-3,000/day and the sweeper falls permanently behind - stock stays reserved against carts that will never convert, producing false out-of-stock and lost sales. The 6-hour interval also means a reservation can sit expired-but-unreleased for up to 6 hours even when capacity is adequate. And with `ORDER BY expires_at ASC` and no per-site cap (:323), one merchant's burst of abandoned carts consumes the whole 250-row budget and freezes another merchant's inventory.
- RECOMMENDATION: Raise the cron frequency (hourly or better) and/or the limit toward the 1,000 RPC cap, and loop until the RPC returns fewer rows than the limit. Instrument actual expirations/day against the budget. Add the per-site cap from the fairness finding so one tenant cannot starve the sweeper for others.
- CAPACITY: 1,000 expirations/day is a hard ceiling on reservation reclamation; exceeding it converts into permanently reserved stock, which is a direct revenue loss rather than a latency problem.

**[S2] Monthly production partition maintenance workflow executes unattended DDL with no dry-run on the scheduled path**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml runs on `cron: "15 2 1 * *"` (monthly, :5). The manual dispatch path defaults `execute` to false (:19), but the scheduled path forces execution: `EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE: ${{ github.event_name == 'schedule' || inputs.execute == true }}` (:35). It resolves to the `production-migrations` environment (:32) with `secrets.PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING` (:36) and runs `node scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs --environment=production --months-ahead=3 --execute` (:56-58), which executes `SELECT public.ensure_next_hot_table_partitions(3)` (scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:31). Blast radius is the partition shadows for worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics, outbox_events, platform_queue_jobs and webhook_deliveries.
- EXPECTED: Unattended production DDL should be preceded by a plan step whose output is compared against expectations, and should fail loudly and alert rather than only appearing as a red CI run once a month.
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:5; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:35; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:36; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:56; scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:31; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:29
- RISK: If the monthly run fails silently (GitHub Actions failure with no paging), future partitions are never created. The shadow-mirror trigger then raises `partition shadow table % is missing` (131:29-31) on EVERY insert to the five hottest tables - which, because the trigger is AFTER ROW on outbox_events, would abort the money RPCs that write outbox rows. A missed monthly cron is therefore a latent total write outage, three months out.
- RECOMMENDATION: Run the plan step first on the scheduled path and diff it before executing, and wire a failure notification (not just a red run) so a missed month is noticed within days rather than at the moment partitions run out. Add a monitoring check that asserts partitions exist at least N months ahead, independent of the workflow's own success.
- CAPACITY: Determines whether the hottest tables can accept writes at all three months from any given run - a hard availability cliff rather than a gradual degradation.

**[S3] Outbox and platform-queue idempotency uniqueness is global rather than site-scoped**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: `outbox_events` carries `UNIQUE (idempotency_key)` with no site_id in the constraint (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:14619) and `platform_queue_jobs` carries `UNIQUE (queue_name, idempotency_key)` (:14739). Both are cross-tenant. By contrast the money tables scope theirs correctly - e.g. `refund_transactions` uses `ON CONFLICT (site_id, idempotency_key)` (:3207). Today's keys are all server-constructed from event type plus a UUID aggregate id (e.g. `'checkout.completed:' || v_order_id::TEXT`, :2196), so collisions are not currently reachable.
- EXPECTED: Under forced RLS and per-transaction tenant GUC, tenant-scoped uniqueness should include site_id so that no key space is shared across tenants by construction, independent of how keys happen to be built today.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:14619; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:14739; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:3207; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:2196
- RISK: No exploitable path exists at this SHA because every key embeds a server-generated UUID. The risk is latent: any future emitter that derives an idempotency key from merchant-controlled input (an order number, an external reference, a slug) would let one tenant suppress another tenant's event via ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING - a silent cross-tenant denial of events with no error surfaced anywhere.
- RECOMMENDATION: Migrate both constraints to include site_id, matching the pattern already used by refund_transactions. Add a validator that any new ON CONFLICT on a tenant table names site_id in the conflict target.

**[INFO] DPDP WhatsApp consent gate is genuinely fail-closed (R-13/IDN-R6 satisfied)**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `hasActiveWhatsAppConsent` (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:853-907) returns optedIn=false on every non-affirmative path: no phone row and no email -> `missing_opt_in` (:882); no email row -> `missing_opt_in` (:901); a row whose consent_status is not exactly 'opted_in' or which has revoked_at set -> `withdrawn_or_not_opted_in` (:875-879, :903-906); the wa_optins table missing entirely -> `missing_ledger` (:869, :895); any other DB error -> throws, failing the job rather than proceeding (:870, :896). The caller hard-stops on !optedIn, recording status 'suppressed' with errorMessage 'WA_CONSENT_REQUIRED' and returning before the adapter is ever constructed (:710-728). The gate is also enforced independently on the merchant-facing send route (services/api-gateway/src/routes/whatsapp.ts:700). Consent is read from the `wa_optins` ledger scoped by site_id.
- EXPECTED: R-13/IDN-R6: a missing consent record must HARD-STOP the send and must never be interpreted as permission.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:853; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:869; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:882; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:710; services/api-gateway/src/routes/whatsapp.ts:700
- RISK: No DPDP exposure identified on this path. The one caveat is that this gate covers the WhatsApp channel only - the transactional email path in services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:481-524 has no consent lookup at all. That is defensible for genuinely transactional order mail, but it means the gate is channel-specific rather than router-wide, so any future marketing use of the email path would bypass it entirely.
- RECOMMENDATION: No change required for WhatsApp. Record explicitly which channels are transactional-basis (email) versus consent-basis (WhatsApp/SMS/push) so the asymmetry is a documented decision rather than an omission, and add the consent check before any non-transactional use of the email path.
- CAPACITY: The gate costs 1-2 extra wa_optins SELECTs per WhatsApp-eligible queue message.

**[INFO] Queue-to-service ownership is compliant: all five queues consumed by queue-workers, reserved services confirmed empty**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Directly addressing the seed observation: the queues named analytics, search and notifications are consumed by services/queue-workers, NOT by any reserved service. services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml declares seven [[queues.consumers]] blocks in the production env covering all five functional queues plus both DLQs (:265-324). services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml declares the five matching [[queues.producers]] (:293-311). services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml and services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml declare no queues and no crons. All six reserved roots - checkout-runtime, event-bus, notification-service, analytics-pipeline, media-service, search-service - contain exactly two files each (AGENTS.md + runtime.manifest.json), with no src/ and no wrangler.toml, verified by ls. config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json independently records producerRuntime 'services/api-gateway' and consumerRuntime 'services/queue-workers', and honestly self-labels mode 'governance-contract-not-live-evidence'.
- EXPECTED: The Constitution mandates one modular monolith plus four satellites, with six reserved services staying EMPTY. Queue naming may reference a future service so long as the consumer is queue-workers.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:265; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:292; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:293; config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json:9; services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:1; services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml:1
- RISK: No topology violation. One naming caveat worth noting: `platformQueueNamesForCloudflareQueue` routes the notifications Cloudflare queue to the analytics-events PLATFORM queue (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:369), so a notification-triggered drain also claims up to 25 analytics jobs. The two layers of 'queue' naming (Cloudflare queues vs platform_queue_jobs.queue_name) do not correspond one-to-one, which is a real source of operator confusion during an incident.
- RECOMMENDATION: No structural change required. Document the two-layer queue model explicitly - Cloudflare queue name vs platform queue name and the substring mapping between them - so an operator reading a DLQ alert can tell which layer failed.
- CAPACITY: The notifications->analytics-events mapping means every order-confirmation email batch also drains up to 25 analytics platform jobs, coupling the two workloads' capacity.

### Adversarial verdicts (5)


**OVERSTATED** — Post-commit TypeScript outbox dual-write violates ARCH-004/PAY-007 at 35+ call sites and silently swallows loss (S0, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The gateway's TypeScript outbox emission is a non-atomic post-commit dual write at 38 non-test call sites (28 files), violating ARCH-R8/ARCH-004/PAY-007. `EventExecutionAdapter.emitDomainEvent` issues a standalone PostgREST upsert to `outbox_events` after the caller's RPC has already committed (services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:60-77); the storefront checkout path is confirmed post-commit (checkout-payment-routes.ts:411 runs after `db.rpc('create_checkout_order')` at order-execution.adapter.ts:158). The SQL path is genuinely atomic at HEAD — the CURRENT `create_checkout_order` (170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:31-597) still inserts the outbox row in-body at :550 — so the ledger-bearing events (checkout.completed, payment.confirmed, refund.succeeded, order.created via 166_d6:276) are safe. The at-risk events are notification/webhook/analytics/search tier (order.created from the storefront route, order.paid, order.fulfilled/cancelled/refunded, checkout.started/failed, product/store/theme/app). Loss is real and uncompensated: no trigger writes outbox rows and no reconciliation sweeper exists. Two corrections to the original claim: (a) `writeWithOutbox` (packages/event-core/src/index.ts:1120-1166) is NOT a same-transaction helper — it performs the same two-step non-atomic write over a PostgREST-shaped interface (:1100-1109) with no BEGIN/COMMIT, differing only in that it throws rather than swallows, so it is not a valid remedy; (b) the swallow is narrow — only a PostgREST schema-cache miss returns null (:7-9, :80-82); all other errors throw, and at checkout-payment-routes.ts:441-443 that throw produces a 500 AFTER the order committed, which is loud rather than silent. Additionally (missed by the original claim), checkout.completed is emitted by both paths with different idempotency keys (SQL `'checkout.completed:'||v_order_id` vs TS `checkout.completed:${session.id}:${body.idempotency_key}` at checkout.ts:739), producing duplicate outbox rows and double-firing consumers. The pattern is locked in by source-text regex assertions at tests/federation/event-spine-contracts.test.mjs:153-235. This is a known, ARB-ACCEPTED transitional item scheduled for remediation, rated S2/S1 in the repo's own register.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The core architectural violation is REAL and CURRENT, but the severity and two load-bearing evidence elements are not earned.

CONFIRMED: (1) event-execution.adapter.ts:60-77 is a standalone PostgREST `.from('outbox_events').upsert()` with no transaction; :79-85 returns null when isMissingOptionalEventTable (:7-9) matches 'outbox_events'+'schema cache'. (2) checkout-payment-routes.ts:411 calls emitStorefrontOrderCreatedEvent AFTER service.createOrder, which is `db.rpc('create_checkout_order')` at order-execution.adapter.ts:158 — genuinely post-commit. (3) 38 non-test call sites across 28 files (`rg -n "EventExecutionAdapter\(.*\)\.emitDomainEvent|events\.emitDomainEvent" services packages apps --glob '*.ts' --glob '!node_modules' --glob '!*test*' | wc -l` -> 38); "35+" is accurate. (4) No trigger emits outbox rows — all 19 `INSERT INTO outbox_events` in database/supabase/migrations are inside RPC bodies; `CREATE TRIGGER` grep shows none writing outbox. (5) No reconciliation sweeper exists (rg for "reconcil" in services/queue-workers returns nothing outbox-related), so there is genuinely no compensating record. (6) The relay is real and wired (OutboxExecutionAdapter instantiated at services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:902; claims via claim_outbox_events at outbox-execution.adapter.ts:109), so lost rows do mean lost downstream work.

REFUTED ELEMENT 1 — `writeWithOutbox` is NOT a same-transaction helper. packages/event-core/src/index.ts:1120-1166 executes `domainWrite(tx)` and then a SEPARATE `.from('outbox_events').insert()`. Its OutboxWritableTransaction interface (:1100-1109) exposes only from().insert().select().single() — there is no BEGIN/COMMIT and no transaction primitive. It differs from emitDomainEvent only in throwing (:1160) instead of swallowing. The claim's statement that "the dedicated same-transaction helper writeWithOutbox has zero production callers" is factually wrong: it is a misnamed non-transactional helper, and adopting it would not satisfy ARCH-004.

STALE CITATION — the auditor cited create_checkout_order at 000_baseline.sql:2160, but that definition is superseded twice: 073_order_item_required_field_compatibility.sql:56 and finally 170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:31 (body ends :597). The conclusion survives — the outbox INSERT is still in-body at 170:550 emitting 'checkout.completed' at 170:568 — but the authoritative line is 170:550, not baseline:2160.

RISK OVERSTATED — "silently swallows loss" is the minority path. Every error except the narrow PGRST205 schema-cache condition THROWS (:84). At the storefront money path the throw is not caught locally: checkout-payment-routes.ts:411 sits in a try whose catch at :441-443 calls idempotency.fail(claim.id) and rethrows, so the request 500s AFTER the order committed. The dominant failure mode is loud, not silent; silence requires a mis-migrated or stale-schema-cache environment.

SEVERITY OVERSTATED — no money is lost or mis-stated. The ledger-bearing events are precisely the ones written in-transaction by SQL: checkout.completed (170:568), payment.confirmed (000_baseline.sql:1544), refund.succeeded (:1186), payment.reference.attached (:405), order.created (166_d6:276). The TS-only events (order.created from the storefront route, order.paid, order.fulfilled/cancelled/refunded, checkout.started/failed, product/store/theme/app) are notification/webhook/analytics/search tier per the contract catalog at packages/event-core/src/index.ts:471-478. The repo's own forensic register rates this same defect S2 (docs/archive/.../PAYMENTS_FORENSIC_AUDIT.md:357) and S1 (docs/archive/.../TOP_250_PLATFORM_ISSUES.md:118), and it is an ARB-ACCEPTED tracked transitional (STRATEGIC_CANONICALIZATION_2026_06_10/FINAL_ARCHITECTURE_DECISION_RECORD.md:354-356, sequenced at :450 item 7). S1 is the defensible ceiling.

UNDERSTATED ON ONE AXIS — the auditor missed that checkout.completed is emitted by BOTH paths with DIFFERENT idempotency keys: SQL `'checkout.completed:' || v_order_id` (170 region) vs TS `checkout.completed:${session.id}:${body.idempotency_key}` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:739). That yields duplicate outbox rows for one business event and double-firing consumers.

ALSO NOTED — tests/federation/event-spine-contracts.test.mjs:153,166,178,192,206,222 are source-text regex assertions that actively lock in the violating pattern; they are not behavioral tests and provide no atomicity coverage.

The governance expectation the auditor cites is correct and current: AGENTS.md:93 (ARCH-R8) requires making the outbox dual-write atomic (same-transaction), referencing ARCH-004/PAY-007.

**OVERSTATED** — Outbox relay is capped at 4,800 events/day, ~1.7x under the constitutional 30k orders/month envelope (S0, VERIFIED-CONFIG)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The production api-gateway config caps AUTOMATIC outbox relay throughput at 50 events x 96 ticks = 4,800 events/day (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236; src/index.ts:945-947; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:756 caps the RPC at 100/claim and no later migration overrides it). At the TOP of the constitutional envelope (~30,000 orders/month, LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:110) an inferred ~6-8 outbox events per order lifecycle would require ~6,000-8,000 events/day, so the currently configured cadence supports only ~40-60% of top-of-envelope volume. This is a beta free-tier tuning value, not an architectural ceiling: the same line's comment and commit ebaf61e2 record the pre-throttle `*/1` schedule, which yields 72,000 events/day (and 100/claim yields 144,000/day) — 9-18x the requirement. It has no present impact (Constitution:77 declares zero production merchants; 6-60x headroom at the 100/1,000-merchant tiers) and is already noted as a scale-time action in docs/final-v1-certification/SCALE_AUDIT.md:29. Two sub-claims are wrong: `POST /operations/outbox/dispatch` (services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:126-141) is a second, operator-triggered relay path, so the relay CAN be exercised in staging despite `crons = []` (wrangler.toml:120); and order-confirmation emails do not traverse the outbox at all — they go direct to the bound NOTIFICATION_QUEUE (src/lib/order-confirmation-email.ts:127-130; wrangler.toml:305-307), so only merchant webhook deliveries and the platform_queue_jobs analytics/search fanout are gated by this ceiling. The residual real gap is that no outbox-backlog age metric is emitted — the cron's lag metric reads replay_operation_schedules (src/index.ts:557-563), not outbox age — so a backlog at scale would accumulate unmonitored.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-CONFIG
- REASONING: THE ARITHMETIC CORE HOLDS. Every capacity citation checks out at this SHA. `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236` = `crons = ["*/15 * * * *"]` (96 ticks/day); `services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:945-947` calls `outbox.dispatchBatch({ outboxLimit: 50 })`, reached only via `runScheduledDispatch` at `index.ts:1044` (`ctx.waitUntil`); `services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:74` clamps to `Math.min(Math.max(limit,1),100)`; `database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:756` is exactly `LIMIT LEAST(GREATEST(p_limit, 1), 100)`. I checked the later-migration failure mode explicitly: `rg -l claim_outbox_events database/` returns ONLY `000_baseline.sql` across all 126 migrations, so nothing overrides the cap. 50 x 96 = 4,800/day is a correct UPPER bound (retries of `status='failed'` rows consume the same budget). `crons = []` at wrangler.toml:25 and :120 is confirmed. api-gateway declares only `queues.producers` (wrangler.toml:293-311), never a consumer, so no queue path drains the outbox; queue-workers' cron is `17 */6` (4/day) and its `scheduled()` (`services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:630+`) runs the inventory sweeper, ledger reconciliation, analytics rollup, incident reminders and compliance export — no outbox relay. `.github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:337` uses `wrangler deploy --env production`, so the production cron block is the one that would ship.

FOUR DEFECTS IN THE CLAIM.

(1) "Nothing outside the cron ever calls dispatchBatch" is FALSE. `services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:126-141` is `POST /operations/outbox/dispatch`, which constructs an `OutboxExecutionAdapter` and calls `dispatchBatch` with an operator-supplied limit (up to the RPC's 100). It is not automatic, so the sustained-capacity math survives, but the derived assertion that the ceiling "can never be observed before production" is wrong — an operator can drive the relay in staging manually despite `crons = []`.

(2) The customer-facing half of the risk is REFUTED. Order-confirmation email does NOT traverse the outbox: `services/api-gateway/src/lib/order-confirmation-email.ts:127-130` does `env.NOTIFICATION_QUEUE.send(...)` inline on the request path, and `NOTIFICATION_QUEUE` is bound in production at `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:305-307` to `letbuyy-notifications-queue-production`, which queue-workers consumes continuously via `[[queues.consumers]]`. So confirmation emails are unaffected by the 15-minute cron. Only merchant webhook deliveries (`outbox-execution.adapter.ts:createWebhookDeliveries`) and the `platform_queue_jobs` analytics/search fanout are actually gated by the relay.

(3) "Measured fanout is ~8 outbox events per prepaid order" was not measured — it is source-enumerated. The event types do exist as real emitters (`routes/checkout.ts:505` checkout.started, `routes/payments.ts:359` payment.captured, `routes/storefront.ts:313` and `lib/payment-route-support.ts:323` order.paid, `routes/orders.ts:436` order.delivered/order.paid, plus RPC inserts in 000_baseline.sql and 073), so ~6-8 per completed lifecycle is defensible, but the demand side is INFERRED, not VERIFIED.

(4) S0 IS NOT EARNED — this is a beta tuning value, not an architectural ceiling, and it has zero present impact. `GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:110` places ~30,000 orders/month at "the TOP of the envelope" (10,000 merchants); line 77 states LetBuyy has "zero production merchants," and the repo's own packet at `docs/research/2026-08-16-architecture-truth-foundation-and-scale/04_LIVE_DEPLOYMENT_TRUTH.md:111` records production traffic as HUMAN-REQUIRED/unknown. At the 100- and 1,000-merchant tiers (~10-100 orders/day, 80-800 events/day) the 4,800/day ceiling has 6-60x headroom. The throttle is deliberate and self-documenting: the comment at wrangler.toml:235 records the pre-throttle value, and `git log -S'*/15 * * * *'` shows commit ebaf61e2 changed `*/1` -> `*/15` for free-tier cost. Restoring `*/1` yields 1,440 x 50 = 72,000 events/day (9x the required 8,000), and the RPC already permits 100/claim (144,000/day). The architecture IS sized for the envelope; only the current beta config value is not, and `docs/final-v1-certification/SCALE_AUDIT.md:29` already records "Queues: throttled for free-tier (25/batch, cron drain) — raise limits + continuous consumers for volume" as a known scale-time action.

One point genuinely favors the auditor and should be preserved: there is no outbox-backlog age metric. The cron's only telemetry, `recordScheduledReplayLagMetric` (`index.ts:593`), reads `replay_operation_schedules` (`index.ts:557-563`) — event replays, not outbox age — so a growing backlog would be silent absent manual inspection via `GET /operations/outbox` (`operations.ts:192`).

**OVERSTATED** — Cloudflare batch retry re-sends transactional emails - no idempotency check before Resend. dispatchEmailDeliveries calls sendResendEmail per delivery with no already-sent lookup and no Resend idempotency header, runs before ack, and any throw falls into a catch that retries the ENTIRE batch (max_batch_size=25, max_retries=6), producing up to 7 duplicate order-confirmation emails per customer. Severity S0, VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `dispatchEmailDeliveries` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:482-526) is not idempotent per message: it calls `sendResendEmail` (:503) with no lookup of an already-sent record and no Resend idempotency key (packages/infrastructure/resend-adapter/index.ts:52-75 passes no such header), it runs at :598 before the ack at :606, and the outer catch at :620-621 retries the whole batch. A Resend failure on message N of a multi-email batch — or the deterministic invalid-payload throw at :487, which is not quarantined — therefore re-sends the emails already delivered earlier in that batch, on each of up to 6 retries (queue-workers/wrangler.toml:294-300). This is a latent defect, not a live one: `RESEND_API_KEY` is never provisioned to queue-workers (production-deploy.yml:358-362 sets only INTERNAL_API_SECRET and QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET; the RESEND put at :329 targets services/api-gateway; queue-workers/wrangler.toml has zero RESEND references), so `resendConfigured()` (:477-480) is false and the loop logs `missing-resend-secret` and skips (:490-501) — no email is sent at all today. The claim's "any throw anywhere later in the try block" is wrong: review-checks.ts:220-226, support-automation.ts:147-157, compliance.ts:195-207, executive-analytics.ts:459-473 and incident-command.ts:201-216 all catch per delivery, and risk-scoring.ts:242-246 skips non-risk job types before any I/O. The "up to 7 duplicates across a 25-message batch" figure also requires 25 notification messages within the 10s max_batch_timeout, which is far above the Constitution's ~30k orders/month envelope. Fix the idempotency before the secret is wired, since enabling the path activates the defect.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The code mechanics are real and I reproduced every cited line, but the S0 severity and the risk narrative are not earned at this SHA. Three separate checks cut it down.

CONFIRMED (code defect is genuine):
- services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:482-526 — `dispatchEmailDeliveries` loops the batch and calls `sendResendEmail` at :503 with no prior lookup of an already-sent record.
- packages/infrastructure/resend-adapter/index.ts:52-75 — `sendResendEmail` builds the message from from/to/subject/html/text/replyTo only; no `Idempotency-Key` / `idempotencyKey` request option is ever passed. Resend-side dedupe is genuinely absent.
- index.ts:598 (dispatch) precedes :606 (`delivery.message.ack()`), and the outer `catch` at :620-621 calls `retry()` on EVERY delivery in the batch. Confirmed.
- No dedupe exists anywhere else on the path: `persistQueueBatch` (index.ts:250-255) upserts `distributed_queue_messages` with `status:'completed'` at :575 — BEFORE any email is sent — and that table is never read back (the only other repo reference is services/api-gateway/src/tenant/tenant-tables.registry.ts:792). `platform-queue-consumer.ts` contains no Resend call at all (grep for `resend` returns nothing). No test in services/queue-workers/test/ covers the queue handler.
- The producer/consumer wiring is real: order-confirmation-email.ts:127-130 sends to NOTIFICATION_QUEUE and returns; api-gateway/wrangler.toml:305-307 binds the producer; queue-workers/wrangler.toml:294-300 declares the consumer with max_batch_size=25, max_retries=6, retry_delay=60.

REFUTED — "any throw anywhere later in the try block":
Every post-email processor swallows its own per-delivery errors and cannot propagate: review-checks.ts:220-226, support-automation.ts:147-157, compliance.ts:195-207, executive-analytics.ts:459-473, incident-command.ts:201-216 all wrap per delivery in try/catch and only increment `failed`. risk-scoring.ts:242-246 has no try/catch but `continue`s before any I/O when `jobType !== 'risk.score.entity'`, so it cannot throw on an email-bearing batch. The realistic trigger therefore narrows to two cases only: a Resend failure on message N>1 (index.ts:512-514), or a deterministic invalid-payload throw at :487 (which, notably, is NOT quarantined — quarantine at :407-418 only covers `normalizePayload` errors — so that one would burn all 6 retries).

OVERSTATED — the send path is gated off by an unprovisioned secret:
`RESEND_API_KEY` is never provisioned to queue-workers by the repository's own pipeline. .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:358-362 sets only INTERNAL_API_SECRET and QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET for `working-directory: services/queue-workers`; `wrangler secret put RESEND_API_KEY` appears only at :329 under `working-directory: services/api-gateway` (:331) and as a Vercel env at :449. staging-deploy.yml:133-141 is identical. `grep -c RESEND services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml` returns 0. Consequently `resendConfigured()` (index.ts:477-480) returns false, and :490-501 logs `missing-resend-secret` and `continue`s — the consumer sends zero emails. Under the deployment configuration this repository declares, the duplicate-send scenario cannot fire. (An operator could have run `wrangler secret put` out of band; that is UNKNOWN and not verifiable from here, which is why this is OVERSTATED rather than REFUTED.)

Scale caveat: the "25-message batch, 19 duplicates" figure needs 25 notification messages inside `max_batch_timeout = 10` seconds. At the Constitution's envelope (~30k orders/month, under 1 order/minute) that is not a routine batch shape; the defensible amplification is small-batch, not 19x.

Severity: even fully enabled this is duplicate transactional email — a customer-trust and sender-reputation defect, not money, tenancy, or data-integrity loss. S2, not S0. Evidence state VERIFIED-SOURCE holds for the code defect; the gating fact is VERIFIED-CONFIG.

Adjacent observation the auditor missed (separate finding, not this one): because order-confirmation-email.ts:127-130 returns immediately after `NOTIFICATION_QUEUE.send(...)` and the producer binding IS present in production (api-gateway/wrangler.toml:305-307), while the consumer has no Resend key, production order-confirmation / cancelled / refunded emails appear to be silently dropped, not duplicated. That is arguably the higher-priority defect on the same path.

**OVERSTATED** — No tenant fairness in any claim path - one hot merchant starves every other. All four work-claiming functions order strictly by arrival time across the entire multi-tenant table with no per-site cap, round-robin, or partition key; consumer code adds nothing; partition_key on distributed_queue_messages has zero readers. Severity S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: No claim path in the async tier bounds the share a single tenant can take. All four live work-claiming functions order strictly by arrival time across the whole multi-tenant table with no site_id term and no per-site cap: claim_outbox_events (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:754), claim_platform_queue_jobs (:822), claim_webhook_deliveries (:966), expire_inventory_reservations (141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:323). No later migration changes this; the partition migrations partition by RANGE(created_at), not by tenant (081_partition_outbox_events.sql:36). All four are on live paths: the api-gateway production cron "*/15 * * * *" (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236) claims 50 outbox events (services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:945), 50 webhook deliveries via the webhook-service binding (services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:641) and a flat 50-job queue budget (index.ts:966), while the queue-workers cron "17 */6 * * *" sweeps 250 reservations (services/queue-workers/src/inventory-reservations.ts:27). The consumer adds no tenant dimension (services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:400-430). A per-tenant quota model exists in the schema but is never read: tenant_execution_quotas with max_queued_jobs, priority_class and noisy_neighbor_protection (000_baseline.sql:12506) has zero TypeScript readers outside the tenant registry. Consequence, correctly bounded: one tenant's burst (e.g. a bulk product import, which does emit a per-product outbox event at services/api-gateway/src/routes/products.ts:334) inflicts head-of-line delay on every other tenant proportional to burst size divided by the fixed drain rate - order-confirmation emails, analytics and outbound webhooks queue behind it, and expired reservations of a quiet tenant can stay locked across cron cycles. This is bounded, self-clearing queueing delay rather than indefinite starvation; it degrades into an unbounded platform-wide stall only if arrival rate meets or exceeds drain capacity, which is a separate capacity finding and is not established by this evidence. Correction to the original: distributed_queue_messages is not literally reader-free - claim_distributed_queue_messages (000_baseline.sql:642) exists and orders by partition_index ASC - but it has zero TS callers and its only writer (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:226-232) inserts rows already in terminal state, so the sole partition-aware ordering in the schema is dead code.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The structural core is fully CONFIRMED and I could not refute it. Exact line checks: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:754 = "ORDER BY outbox_events.created_at ASC", :822 = "ORDER BY platform_queue_jobs.created_at ASC", :966 = "ORDER BY webhook_deliveries.created_at ASC", 141_executable_inventory_reservations.sql:323 = "ORDER BY expires_at ASC". None carries a site_id/tenant term or per-site cap.

Later-migration check (the standard failure mode) is negative: `rg "CREATE (OR REPLACE )?FUNCTION public\.claim"` over database/supabase/migrations/ outside 000_baseline returns only claim_payout_statement_release (160_foundation_money_ledger_authority.sql:357). The partition migrations do not help: 081_partition_outbox_events.sql:36 is "PARTITION BY RANGE (created_at)" (time, not tenant), and 131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:1-5 keeps them as shadow mirrors.

Call-site check (the other failure mode) is positive - all four are on live paths, not dead code: claim_outbox_events <- services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:109 <- services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:945 (outboxLimit 50) under production cron "*/15 * * * *" (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:236); claim_platform_queue_jobs <- services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:482 and services/api-gateway/src/execution/queue-execution.adapter.ts:225 (budget 50 at index.ts:966); claim_webhook_deliveries <- services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:641, invoked by the same cron with limit 50 (index.ts:950-961); expire_inventory_reservations <- services/queue-workers/src/inventory-reservations.ts:27 <- services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:634 under cron "17 */6 * * *" with INVENTORY_RESERVATION_SWEEPER_LIMIT = "250" (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml). Consumer citation verified: platform-queue-consumer.ts:400-430 loops queue names against one flat budget with no site dimension.

Corroborating evidence the auditor did not cite, which strengthens the finding: 000_baseline.sql:12506 defines tenant_execution_quotas with max_concurrent_executions, max_queued_jobs, priority_class and noisy_neighbor_protection boolean DEFAULT true, and it has zero TypeScript readers (only the registry entry services/api-gateway/src/tenant/tenant-tables.registry.ts:2783). tenant_operation_projections.fairness_score (:6808) is likewise write-model-only. Fairness was designed into the schema and never wired to any claim path. The burst source is also real: services/api-gateway/src/routes/products.ts:334 emits a per-product domain event, so a bulk import genuinely fans into outbox -> search-indexing jobs.

Two defects in the claim as written. (1) The statement that distributed_queue_messages "has zero readers" is inaccurate: claim_distributed_queue_messages exists at 000_baseline.sql:642 and even orders by "partition_index ASC, available_at ASC". It has zero TS callers, and the sole writer services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:226-232 inserts rows already in terminal status ('completed'/'dead-lettered'), so the conclusion ("influences nothing") survives - but the precise fact is that the one partition-aware ordering in the schema is dead code, not that the table is unread. (2) "Starves" and "platform-wide outage of the async tier" overstate the mechanism. FIFO with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED yields bounded head-of-line delay (burst size / drain rate), self-clearing once the burst drains; indefinite starvation requires sustained arrivals >= drain capacity, which is a capacity property. The claim imports that premise ("relay already at ~1.7x under capacity") from a separate finding and does not establish it here, so the outage consequence is INFERRED, not VERIFIED-SOURCE.

Severity: this is a latency/fairness gap with no correctness, money-integrity, or cross-tenant data-exposure impact, and a recoverable blast radius. The claim's own Expected-state text scopes the requirement to the 1,000 -> 10,000 merchant tier. Rating it S1 borrows severity from the under-capacity finding (which is the root; unfairness is the amplifier) and double-counts it. S2 is the defensible rating, escalating to S1 only if the async tier is independently shown to run at or above saturation. Evidence state stays VERIFIED-SOURCE for the current-state assertion (every cited artifact read at 5af69e0c, every call site traced); the projected consequence is INFERRED.

**OVERSTATED** — No SSRF guard on outbound webhook targets - private and link-local addresses accepted (SEC-R11). Severity S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE. Risk asserted: merchant registers https://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/ or https://10.0.0.5/admin, the dispatcher POSTs there and stores 2000 chars of response in webhook_deliveries.response_body_preview, "which the merchant can read back via the deliveries API" - a merchant-triggered blind-to-full SSRF read primitive; absent timeout holds a slot out of the 50-per-tick budget.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Outbound webhook target validation is `url.protocol === 'https:' && url.hostname.length > 0` and nothing else (packages/notification-core/src/index.ts:505-512), applied identically at merchant registration (services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:470, :512), fanout eligibility (services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:177) and dispatch (services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:34-41, enforced :75). There is no DNS resolution, no allow/deny list, no private/loopback/link-local/ULA range check, no redirect policy (default `follow`, so a merchant-controlled 302 escapes the https-only rule) and no AbortSignal/timeout (app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:89-105). The path is live: the gateway cron at services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:952-961 calls webhook-service /webhook-deliveries/dispatch (index.ts:693) with limit 50, and the delivery loop at index.ts:648-658 is sequential, so one slow merchant target stalls the whole tick. This violates SEC-R11 (AGENTS.md:237), which names the webhook SSRF as a MUST-close-before-buyer-traffic item. Correction to the original claim: the 2000-char response body IS persisted (services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:665,674 -> webhook_deliveries.response_body_preview) but is NOT merchant-readable - no route selects that column, the only delivery-list route (services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:282-292) omits it and is gated by requireRoleCategory('ops') + requireSuperAdmin (operations.ts:45), and RLS is service_role/tenant-GUC only (148_executable_notifications_router.sql:129-157). It is therefore blind SSRF, not a read/exfiltration primitive. The cloud-metadata example (169.254.169.254) is inapplicable: the dispatcher runs only as a Cloudflare Worker (services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml, package.json) with no IMDS and no RFC1918-routable egress. Residual real risk: blind SSRF probing of any Worker-reachable public/edge host, https-only bypass via redirect, unbounded per-request time on the money-event delivery path, and response bodies of arbitrary third-party targets landing in the tenant database.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The code facts are exactly as cited and the path is live, but two of the three legs of the claimed impact do not survive checking.

CONFIRMED (VERIFIED-SOURCE): packages/notification-core/src/index.ts:505-512 is literally `url.protocol === 'https:' && url.hostname.length > 0` and nothing more. It is the sole guard at merchant registration (services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts:470, and PATCH at :512; WebhookCreateSchema at notifications.ts:31-38 accepts any z.string().url()), at fanout eligibility (services/api-gateway/src/execution/outbox-execution.adapter.ts:177), and at dispatch (services/webhook-service/src/app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:34-41, enforced at :75). `rg -n "169\.254|127\.0\.0|192\.168|172\.16|RFC1918|fc00|::1|isPrivate|link.?local"` over packages/notification-core, services/webhook-service, the notifications route and services/api-gateway/src/execution returns no address-range logic. No timeout: `rg -n "AbortSignal|AbortController|signal:|timeout" services/webhook-service/src` returns nothing (fetch at app-lifecycle-dispatch.ts:89-105). The claim also MISSED a real defect: no `redirect` option is set, so the default `follow` lets a merchant-controlled 302 escape the https-only check post-redirect. Live path is real, not just mounted: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:952-961 (scheduled handler, INTERNAL_API_SECRET, limit 50) -> services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:693 -> dispatchWebhookDeliveries (index.ts:636-658). Governed requirement is real: AGENTS.md:237 SEC-R11 names the webhook SSRF explicitly (ISSUE-020) as MUST-close-before-buyer-traffic. Tests do not cover it - tests/federation/webhook-compliance.test.mjs:39-53 and services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/notifications.test.ts:84-101 are grep-style source assertions on HMAC headers only.

REFUTED leg 1 - "the merchant can read back via the deliveries API". `rg -n "response_body_preview"` outside docs/migrations yields only the two writes at services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:665 and :674. No route selects the column. The only delivery-list route, services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:282-292, omits response_body_preview AND is gated by `router.use('*', requireRoleCategory('ops'), requireSuperAdmin)` at operations.ts:45, so no merchant reaches it. services/api-gateway/src/routes/notifications.ts contains no deliveries route at all (grep "deliveries" returns nothing).
