RLS on webhook_deliveries is service_role / tenant-GUC only (148_executable_notifications_router.sql:129-130,156-157; 000_baseline.sql:28001) and merchants have no direct Supabase access. So this is BLIND SSRF from the merchant's position, not the "blind-to-full read primitive" claimed.

REFUTED leg 2 - the IMDS example. The dispatcher exists only as a Cloudflare Worker: services/webhook-service/package.json (wrangler dev/deploy, @cloudflare/workers-types) and services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml (custom domains webhooks.letbuyy.com / webhooks-staging.letbuyy.com; bindings are only API_GATEWAY_SERVICE and an Analytics Engine dataset - no tunnel, no private-network binding). A Worker has no 169.254.169.254 IMDS and its fetch egress does not route to RFC1918/loopback/link-local (INFERRED: platform property of Workers, not provable from this repo). The gateway does not fetch target_url itself - asserted by tests/federation/webhook-compliance.test.mjs:51-52 and confirmed by reading outbox-execution.adapter.ts:161-208, which only inserts rows.

UNDERSTATED leg 3 - the timeout consequence. services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:648-658 is a sequential `for...of` with `await` per delivery, so one deliberately slow target stalls the ENTIRE 50-delivery tick and can burn the cron invocation, not merely "a dispatcher slot".

Net: the guard is genuinely absent on a live, merchant-controlled, money-event delivery path and SEC-R11 names it - but the exfiltration/read-primitive and cloud-metadata framing that carried the S1 rating is not supported at this SHA.

No wrangler.jsonc / wrangler.json anywhere in the repo.

QUEUE OWNERSHIP vs RESERVED SERVICES
config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json: producerRuntime "services/api-gateway", consumerRuntime "services/queue-workers", mode "governance-contract-not-live-evidence". Reserved roots services/{analytics-pipeline,search-service,notification-service,event-bus,checkout-runtime,media-service} each contain exactly AGENTS.md + runtime.manifest.json — no src/, no wrangler.toml (verified by ls). CONCLUSION: the analytics/search/notifications queues are consumed by queue-workers, not by a reserved service. Compliant.

SCHEDULED JOBS
Cloudflare crons:
- services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:25 top-level crons=[]; :120 staging crons=[]; :236 production crons=["*/15 * * * *"] -> runScheduledDispatch (index.ts:898): preflight, replay-lag metric, outbox.dispatchBatch(limit 50), webhook-service /webhook-deliveries/dispatch (limit 50), queue.consumeQueues(['analytics-events','search-indexing','payment-events'], 50 each), then 2-5 Cloudflare queue trigger messages.
- services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:17 / :123 / :237 crons=["17 */6 * * *"] (every 6h) -> scheduled() (index.ts:625-710): custom-domain reconciler, INVENTORY RESERVATION SWEEPER (expire_inventory_reservations, limit 250), money-ledger reconciliation, executive-analytics rollup, incident postmortem reminders, compliance audit export dry-run.
GitHub scheduled workflows (only 3):
- .github/workflows/branch-lag-monitor.yml:5 cron "17 * * * *" — read-only branch lag guard, blast radius: CI only.
- .github/workflows/full-security-audit.yml:10 cron "30 4 * * 1" — weekly security audit, CI only.
- .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:5 cron "15 2 1 * *" (monthly) — env "production-migrations", EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE forced true on schedule (:35), runs scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs --months-ahead=3 -> SELECT public.ensure_next_hot_table_partitions(3) against PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING. Blast radius: production DDL on the partition shadows, unattended, monthly.

OUTBOX / QUEUE STATE MACHINE
outbox_events (000_baseline.sql:1215-1246): status queued|processing|processed|failed|dead, max_attempts default 10, UNIQUE(idempotency_key) (:14619, GLOBAL not site-scoped).
claim_outbox_events (:733-788): FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, ORDER BY created_at ASC, LIMIT<=100, lock 120s.
complete_outbox_event (:1219+), fail_outbox_event (:4040-4100): dead-letters into outbox_dead_letters at attempts>=max_attempts.
replay_outbox_dead_letter (:7220-7261): resets outbox_events to queued -> WORKS (relay re-claims).
claim_platform_queue_jobs (:796-854): ORDER BY created_at ASC, FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, LIMIT<=100.
quarantine_platform_queue_job (089_quarantine_platform_queue_job.sql:4-44): status='dead', no further processing.
claim_webhook_deliveries (:941-968): ORDER BY created_at ASC, LIMIT<=100. webhook_deliveries max_attempts default 10 (:1372), backoff webhookRetryDelaySeconds base 120s (webhook-service/index.ts:228).
distributed_queue_messages: WRITE-ONLY from queue-workers persistQueueBatch (index.ts:249-253). Zero TypeScript callers of claim_distributed_queue_messages / complete_distributed_queue_message / dead_letter_distributed_queue_message (verified by grep across all .ts).

EVENT FANOUT (packages/event-core/src/index.ts)
queueNamesForEvent (:818-838) maps contract consumers via queueNameForDomainEventConsumer (:840-852): analytics->analytics-events, search-indexing->search-indexing, payout-ledger->payment-events, notifications->NULL, webhook-service->NULL. Uncontracted fallback: payment.*/refund.* -> payment-events + analytics-events.
NOT contracted (fall through to the heuristic): payment.confirmed, refund.succeeded, refund.requested — all three are emitted by SQL RPCs.
Per-order outbox events (prepaid happy path): checkout.started, checkout.completed, order.created, payment.confirmed, payment.captured, order.paid, order.fulfilled, order.delivered ~= 8.
Per-order platform_queue_jobs: 1+1+2+2+1+2+2+1 ~= 12.
Per-order webhook_deliveries: 8 x (active matching subscriptions per site).

DB WRITES PER ANALYTICS QUEUE MESSAGE (order.created, 3 line items)
1 analytics_event_stream upsert + 4 increment_analytics_rollup RPCs (queue-projection-plans.ts:162-183) + 1 order_items SELECT + 6 item rollup RPCs (2/item, :291-318) + notification side effect (site owner SELECT, notifications INSERT, device_tokens SELECT, push dry-run) + WhatsApp path (orders SELECT, wa_optins SELECT x1-2, wa message mirror upsert, wa session upsert, wa dispatch log upsert) + complete_platform_queue_job = ~18-22 DB round trips. External provider calls: 0 (push dry-run, WhatsApp unavailable in queue-workers prod, Resend only on email job types).

RETRY AMPLIFICATION (batch-level retry, queue-workers/src/index.ts:621)
events: 50 x (1+3) = 200 deliveries; analytics: 50 x 9 = 450; search: 25 x 9 = 225; notifications: 25 x 7 = 175; runtime: 50 x 11 = 550; each DLQ: 25 x 4 = 100.
Per delivery attempt the handler writes 1 distributed_queue_messages row/message + 2 runtime_telemetry_metrics rows. A poisoned 50-msg analytics batch = 9 x 52 = 468 row writes, x2-3 with the shadow-mirror trigger ~= 1,000-1,400 row ops. Each retry also re-runs consumePlatformQueuesForCloudflareQueue, re-claiming up to 25 platform jobs and burning their attempts budget.

THROUGHPUT CEILINGS (production)
Outbox relay: 50 events/tick x 96 ticks/day = 4,800 events/day (~144k/month). At ~8 events/order => ~600 orders/day sustainable. Constitutional target ~1,000 orders/day (30k/month) => backlog grows without bound at ~1.7x over capacity. Cloudflare queues do NOT help: outbox.dispatchBatch runs ONLY in the api-gateway cron.
Platform queue drain: 3 queues x 50/tick x 96 = 14,400 jobs/day from cron, plus CF-triggered drains (drainBudget 25, PLATFORM_QUEUE_DRAIN_BUDGET_PER_BATCH=25, wrangler.toml:251). Need ~12,000/day at 1,000 orders/day — marginal.
Webhook deliveries: 50/tick x 96 = 4,800/day. At 8 events/order x 1 subscription x 1,000 orders/day = 8,000/day => unbounded backlog with a single subscription per merchant.
Inventory reservation sweeper: 250 x 4 runs/day = 1,000 expirations/day max (INVENTORY_RESERVATION_SWEEPER_LIMIT=250, wrangler.toml:255).
Cloudflare queue backlog drain (theoretical, per queue): analytics 10 concurrency x 50 batch = 500 msgs/invocation-wave; notifications 5 x 25 = 125. Not the binding constraint — the cron is.

CONFIG NOT SET / RESOLVED BY FALLBACK
PLATFORM_QUEUE_DRAIN_GENERIC_FANOUT="off" in all 3 envs (wrangler.toml:34,140,254) => letbuyy-events-production and letbuyy-production-queue map to ZERO platform queues (platform-queue-consumer.ts:363-372); they only write the audit row and dispatch emails.
No CF queue name contains "payment" and planScheduledDispatchQueueTriggers (cloudflare-queue-triggers.ts:119-133) never targets payment-events => the payment-events platform queue is drained ONLY by the api-gateway cron.
queue-workers/wrangler.toml declares no WHATSAPP_PROVIDER_MODE => normalizeMode falls back to isProductionRuntime ? 'live' : 'sandbox' (whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:146-147); with no TWILIO_* vars in queue-workers, available=false => WhatsApp order notifications are recorded 'failed'/WHATSAPP_UNAVAILABLE in production and never send.
QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET / INTERNAL_API_SECRET are Worker secrets, correctly absent from wrangler.toml. No committed secret found in any queue/webhook config.

### Unknowns
- Whether production Cloudflare Queues actually have the consumer settings declared in wrangler.toml. wrangler.toml is the desired state; a manual dashboard change or a failed deploy would diverge silently. Settled by: `wrangler queues info <name> --env production` output for all seven queues, or the Cloudflare API queue list.
- Actual production queue depth, message age, dead-letter rate, and whether the api-gateway cron is currently keeping up. The 4,800 events/day ceiling is derived from configuration; the observed steady-state backlog is not in the repo. Settled by: a `SELECT status, count(*), min(created_at) FROM outbox_events GROUP BY status` against production, plus Cloudflare Queues backlog metrics.
- Real per-order outbox event count in production. I derived ~8 from tracing the emitters, but the exact number depends on which lifecycle transitions actually fire for COD versus prepaid orders. Settled by: `SELECT event_type, count(*) FROM outbox_events WHERE created_at > now() - interval '7 days' GROUP BY 1` against production, divided by order count.
- Whether RESEND_API_KEY, QUEUE_TENANT_ENVELOPE_SECRET and INTERNAL_API_SECRET are provisioned as non-placeholder Worker secrets in production, and whether the envelope secret is IDENTICAL across api-gateway and queue-workers. If they diverge, 100% of tenant-scoped queue messages are quarantined. Settled by: `wrangler secret list` per worker plus an operator-confirmed value hash comparison (never the values).
- Whether the partition-shadow mirror trigger is currently active in production - it is gated at runtime on a `retention_policies` row with lifecycle_mode in ('partition-shadow','partition-live') (131:18-27). If no such row exists the trigger is a cheap no-op. Settled by: `SELECT target_table, lifecycle_mode, shadow_table FROM retention_policies WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`.
- The measured cost of the shadow-mirror trigger per outbox claim/complete cycle. I established the trigger's structure but not its latency. Settled by: EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on `claim_outbox_events` and `complete_outbox_event` against a production-sized table, with the trigger enabled and disabled.
- How many active webhook_subscriptions exist per site in production. The 4,800/day dispatch ceiling is breached at one subscription per merchant at target volume, but the real multiplier is unknown. Settled by: `SELECT count(*), count(DISTINCT site_id) FROM webhook_subscriptions WHERE status='active' AND deleted_at IS NULL`.
- The actual inventory reservation expiry rate versus the 1,000/day sweeper budget. Settled by: `SELECT date_trunc('day', released_at), count(*) FROM inventory_reservations WHERE release_reason='ttl_expired' GROUP BY 1` over the last 30 days, compared against the count of reservations reaching expires_at.
- Whether Cloudflare Workers egress independently blocks fetches to RFC1918 and link-local addresses. That would be a platform-level mitigation for the missing SSRF guard, but it is not a code control and I have no authorized live access to test it. Settled by: Cloudflare's documented Workers fetch egress policy plus a controlled test from a staging Worker to 169.254.169.254 and 10.0.0.1.
- Whether the monthly hot-table-partition-maintenance workflow has actually succeeded on its scheduled runs, and how far ahead partitions currently exist. Settled by: the GitHub Actions run history for that workflow, plus `SELECT relname FROM pg_class WHERE relname LIKE '%_partitioned_%' ORDER BY relname DESC LIMIT 20`.
- Whether the api-gateway production Worker is deployed with the `[env.production]` block at all. If it is deployed from the top-level config, `crons = []` (wrangler.toml:25) applies and the outbox relay never runs in production. Settled by: the deploy command used in CI (`wrangler deploy --env production`) and `wrangler deployments list` for letbuyy-api.
- Retention/pruning policy for `payment_webhook_events`. Since it is the only replay defence for inbound provider webhooks (there is no timestamp window), pruning it inside a provider's retry horizon reopens replay. Settled by: the retention_policies row for payment_webhook_events and any scheduled purge job.
- Whether any merchant has already registered a private-range or link-local webhook target. Settled by: `SELECT id, site_id, target_url FROM webhook_subscriptions WHERE status='active'` reviewed against private/loopback/link-local ranges.

## DIMENSION: tests

### Narrative summary
The 110/110 green gate is overwhelmingly text-matching, not behavioral. Of the 110 gate-lane validators declared in scripts/validate-governance.mjs, exactly 2 execute anything that performs the work being checked (mobile-token-validator.mjs spawns the token generator in --check mode; branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs runs real `git rev-list`). ZERO validators execute the runtime, issue an HTTP request, boot a Worker, or touch a database. 86 of 110 assert by substring/regex over source text; 49 of 110 use text matching exclusively with no structured parse; 48 parse JSON/TOML manifests. Two of the 110 are one-line aliases that merely `import` another gate validator (undeclared-import-validator.mjs -> dependency-manifest-validator.mjs; wrangler-env-validator.mjs -> cloudflare-environment-validator.mjs), so the true distinct-check count is 108 — and one of those aliases is listed in criticalGateValidators, meaning 2 of the 5 "critical" validators are the same code executed twice. Two validators are structurally DEAD in a real gate run: rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:36 and one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:28 both begin their main scan loop with `if (!entry.extra) continue;`, and `extra` is only true for synthetic in-memory fixtures injected via LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON (scripts/validators/phase-b-common.mjs:20), which is set nowhere except tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:147 — so during `pnpm run validate:governance` those loops inspect zero real repository files. On the test side, 582 test files exist; roughly 223 (38%) are pure source-grep (readFileSync + assert.match/includes with no import of the code under test), including all 10 files under tests/performance/, which measure nothing. The 21 files that test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs certifies as "enterprise test proof" import zero non-node modules: 19 are 12-30 line spawnSync wrappers that re-run the corresponding validator (circular — the validator requires the test, the test runs the validator), and 2 (tests/replay/replay-determinism.test.mjs, tests/queues/queue-recovery.test.mjs) reimplement the system under test inline and assert against their own local reimplementation. Against that, real behavioral coverage does exist and is non-trivial: 20 suites under tests/executable/ run `docker exec psql` against a live postgres:16-alpine container with no skip guards, and CI runs them (ci.yml:121 and ci.yml:164 both call `pnpm run test:behavioral`, which first calls scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs). tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs genuinely executes SET ROLE app_tenant/authenticated/anon/service_role with positive-controls-first and asserts zero cross-tenant rows plus fail-closed on unset GUC — but the file AGENTS.md TEST-R4 names (tests/security/cross-tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs) does not exist, and the real suite runs against a 13-table synthetic schema the test builds itself, with only migrations 163 and 164 applied. money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs is a real double-entry proof (64 postings, ledger_balance_breaks()=0, append-only triggers throwing, 12-way concurrent idempotency collapsing to one transaction) — but it exercises `post_ledger_transaction` from migration 139, and grep across services/ and apps/ shows that RPC has ZERO runtime callers; production money flows through create_checkout_order / confirm_order_payment_with_ledger, which have no equivalent behavioral test. TEST-R10 as written ("replay 000->161") is false: no workflow uses a Postgres service container and no test replays migration history; each executable test does DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE and applies one hand-picked migration onto a hand-built fixture. The reconciliation harness (scripts/money-certification/, 8 SQL invariants plus a negative-control probe) exists but is invoked by no workflow. No restore drill is executed anywhere (foundation-restore-drill.mjs is plan-only by default and refuses non-localhost targets). Playwright E2E is absent from ci.yml — it runs only in deploy workflows, and staging-deploy.yml's push trigger is disabled. There is no load-testing tool in the repository (no k6/artillery/autocannon); the only benchmark, packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-render-benchmark.test.ts, renders an in-memory fixture 25 times sequentially in one process under JSDOM at concurrency 1 with no DB or network, and its 200ms assertion (line 239) was loosened from a comparative regression guard in commit 5824b25d.


### Findings (18)


**[S1] Gate is 2/110 behavioral: no validator executes the runtime or a database**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Of 110 gate-lane validators, only mobile-token-validator.mjs (spawnSync of scripts/generators/generate-mobile-tokens.mjs --check) and branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs (git rev-list) execute work that reproduces the invariant. 86/110 assert via substring/regex over source text; 49/110 use ONLY text matching; 48/110 parse JSON/manifests. No validator makes an HTTP request, boots a Worker or Next app, or connects to Postgres.
- EXPECTED: ART-02 and R-18 require that a green gate MEANS conformance to the Constitution. A gate that cannot observe runtime behavior cannot mean that.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validate-governance.mjs:71-186 (validators array, 110 entries); scripts/validators/mobile-token-validator.mjs:3-8; scripts/validators/branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:57-90; node -e script over the 110 declared validators: text-match=86, json-parse=48, text-only=49, subprocess=2
- RISK: A conforming-looking source tree passes 110/110 while the deployed system violates every invariant the gate names. Renaming an identifier, or adding the expected token to a comment, is sufficient to satisfy most checks.
- RECOMMENDATION: Report the gate as a static-conformance gate, not a behavioral gate. Promote the invariants in section C below that are GREP-ONLY into the tests/executable/ Postgres lane or an HTTP-level integration lane before treating green as launch evidence.
- CAPACITY: The gate provides no signal about throughput, concurrency, contention, or degradation at any merchant count; nothing in it observes latency, queue depth, or connection pressure.

**[S1] Two gate validators' main scan loop is dead code during a real gate run**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:36 and one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:28 both start their offender loop with `if (!entry.extra) continue;`. phase-b-common.mjs sets extra=true only for entries parsed from process.env.LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON, which is assigned nowhere in the repository except tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:147. During `pnpm run validate:governance` the variable is unset, so both loops iterate every real file and immediately `continue` — scanning zero repository content.
- EXPECTED: A validator counted in the 110 must inspect the repository it claims to guard. 'RLS FORCE coverage' must actually check that tenant tables carry FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:34-46; scripts/validators/one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:26-35; scripts/validators/phase-b-common.mjs:19-21; tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:147; grep -rn LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON scripts tests .github -> only phase-b-common.mjs and phase-b-validators.test.mjs
- RISK: A new tenant table can be added with no FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY and the gate stays green. The residual assertions in rls-force-coverage-validator only grep two fixed migration files (155 and 164) and one test file for identifier strings.
- RECOMMENDATION: Make the loops run over real entries and treat `extra` as an ADDITIONAL fixture source, not a filter. Re-run the gate and triage whatever it now reports.

**[S1] The 21 mandated 'enterprise test proof files' are validator wrappers and self-referential tautologies**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs requires 21 named test files to exist. All 21 import zero non-node modules. 19 are 12-30 line files whose whole body is spawnSync(process.execPath, ['scripts/validators/<x>-validator.mjs']) plus assert.equal(status,0) — the validator requires the test, the test runs the validator. The remaining 2 are worse: tests/replay/replay-determinism.test.mjs defines its own replay() reducer and tests/queues/queue-recovery.test.mjs defines its own queueRetryDelayMs/planQueueRecovery, then assert against those local reimplementations. Neither imports any production module.
- EXPECTED: A test named as coverage proof must exercise the shipped implementation.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/validators/test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs:9-35; tests/typescript/typescript-governance.test.mjs:1-12; tests/migrations/migration-safety.test.mjs:1-12; tests/ci/ci-simulation.test.mjs:1-20; tests/replay/replay-determinism.test.mjs:1-30 (imports only node:test and node:assert/strict); tests/queues/queue-recovery.test.mjs:1-30 (imports only node:test and node:assert/strict)
- RISK: Queue retry/DLQ policy and event replay determinism are reported as covered while the production queue consumer in services/queue-workers/ is never executed by either test. A regression in the real retry policy cannot fail these tests.
- RECOMMENDATION: Delete the two tautological files or rewrite them to import the real queue/event modules. Reclassify the 19 wrappers as 'validator harness' rather than test coverage so the coverage number stops double-counting the gate.

**[S1] TEST-R10 is false as written: no CI job replays migration history**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: No workflow declares a `services: postgres` container. `pnpm run test:behavioral` calls scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs, which docker-runs postgres:16-alpine and creates an EMPTY database — it applies no migrations at all. Each tests/executable suite then does DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE, hand-builds a fixture schema, and applies ONE hand-picked migration file. Nothing anywhere replays 000 -> 197 (there are 126 migration files, highest numbered 197). tests/migrations/v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs is a readFileSync source grep over a hard-coded filename list, not a replay.
- EXPECTED: TEST-R10 mandates ephemeral-Postgres suites in CI that replay 000->161, SET ROLE to the production-shaped role, and assert behavioral invariants.
- EVIDENCE: scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs:55-75 (docker run, waitReady, ensureDatabase — no migration apply); grep -rn 'postgres|services:' .github/workflows/ -> only pg_dump/pg_restore --list in production-deploy.yml:250-255 and psql client install; tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:74-118 (resetDatabase + single migration 139); tests/migrations/v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs:1-30; ls database/supabase/migrations | wc -l -> 126; tail -> 197_letbuyy_core_first_class_theme.sql
- RISK: Migration ordering conflicts, cross-migration policy overwrites, and drift between the real schema and the fixture schemas are structurally undetectable. A migration that breaks when applied after its predecessors will only fail in staging or production.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add one CI job that applies every file in database/supabase/migrations in numeric order against the ephemeral container and fails on any error, then runs the RT-1..RT-9 suite against THAT schema instead of a hand-built one.
- CAPACITY: Without replaying real migrations no test observes real index/partition/trigger cost, so nothing in CI can detect a migration that will not complete or will lock at merchant scale.

**[S1] The only behavioral money proof tests a ledger RPC with zero production callers**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs is a genuine behavioral proof: 64 postings, ledger_balance_breaks() asserted 0 three times, UPDATE and DELETE on ledger_entries asserted to throw /ledger is append-only/, 12 concurrent psql writers collapsing to exactly 1 transaction and 2 entries, RLS scoping under executable_app_user, and the rollback SQL applied. It exercises public.post_ledger_transaction from migration 139. `rg post_ledger_transaction services apps packages` returns no runtime caller — only tests, governance docs, and LCIS tooling. Production money flows through create_checkout_order / confirm_order_payment_with_ledger / complete_order_refund in services/api-gateway/src/execution/*.adapter.ts, and those have no behavioral Postgres test.
- EXPECTED: TEST-R3 requires the sum-debit=sum-credit property, double-webhook/double-capture concurrency, and the reconciliation harness on the money paths that actually run.
- EVIDENCE: tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:142-282; tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:11-14 (reads migration 139 only); rg -n post_ledger_transaction services apps packages/payments packages/billing-core -> only services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/money.test.ts:133 (a source-regex assertion); services/api-gateway/src/execution/payment-execution.adapter.ts, order-execution.adapter.ts (canonical RPC call sites)
- RISK: The ledger invariants are proven on a kernel the product does not use. A double-webhook or double-capture bug in confirm_order_payment_with_ledger is not covered by any executing test.
- RECOMMENDATION: Point the money kernel suite at the canonical RPCs, or state explicitly in AGENTS.md that migration 139's kernel is a reference implementation not yet cut over, and treat TEST-R3 as unmet for the live path.
- CAPACITY: The 12-writer contention test is the only concurrency evidence in the repository, and it exercises a non-production function; nothing measures FOR UPDATE contention on the real order/payment path.

**[S2] The only benchmark measures a single-process in-memory render at concurrency 1, and its assertion was recently loosened**  ·  domain=theme  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-TEST  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-render-benchmark.test.ts renders the Urban Kicks fixture N=25 times in a loop in one Node process, against an in-memory bundle, with JSDOM/DOMPurify warmed beforehand. There is no HTTP, no database, no network, and no concurrent client — 'parallel' refers only to Promise.all across sections within one render. Commit 5824b25d replaced a comparative guard (productionSteady <= max(baselineSteady*2.5, 50)) with a flat `assert.ok(productionSteady < 200)` at line 239. The primary budget assert is total < 30,000ms for 25 renders (line 55), i.e. 1.2s per render. The recorded table shows steady ~11ms, so the 200ms bar has ~18x headroom.
- EXPECTED: A steady-state assertion presented as a performance gate should track a baseline and fail on regression.
- EVIDENCE: packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-render-benchmark.test.ts:54-55 (N=25, BUDGET_MS=30_000); packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-render-benchmark.test.ts:236-241 (200ms assertion); packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-render-benchmark.test.ts:30-40 (recorded table: production steady 11ms); git show 5824b25d (replaced 2.5x comparative guard with flat 200ms)
- RISK: A ~18x performance regression in the Liquid render path passes. Because the assertion no longer references a baseline, gradual drift is invisible.
- RECOMMENDATION: Restore a baseline-relative guard alongside the absolute ceiling, and record whether the benchmark runs in CI at all (it only runs when packages/theme-engine is in the turbo --filter=...[HEAD^1] set).
- CAPACITY: Render cost per storefront page is the main CPU driver on the Workers/Vercel tier; the current gate provides no usable regression signal for it.

**[INFO] Real behavioral Postgres coverage does exist and runs on every PR — 20 suites, no skip guards**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-TEST  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: tests/executable/ contains 21 files; 20 execute `docker exec -i <container> psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` against a live postgres:16-alpine started by scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs. None has an env-conditional skip, and `pnpm run test:behavioral` chains the container start with `&&`, so an unavailable Docker fails the CI step rather than silently skipping. Both ci.yml:121 (unit-tests) and ci.yml:164 (merge-safety-gate) invoke it. Genuinely behavioral invariants proven this way: cross-tenant denial with positive controls (tenant-redteam), append-only ledger triggers and 12-way write idempotency (money-ledger), outbox same-transaction atomicity via rollback (foundation-reference), inventory oversell prevention under Promise.allSettled contention (inventory), and session rotation/reuse rejection (foundation-wave4-session).
- EXPECTED: n/a — this is the load-bearing part of the suite and should be named as such.
- EVIDENCE: tests/executable/*.mjs (21 files; grep -c docker shows >=1 in 20 of them); scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs:55-75; package.json test:behavioral -> 'node scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs start && node --test --test-concurrency=1 tests/*/*.test.mjs'; .github/workflows/ci.yml:121,164; tests/executable/inventory-postgres.test.mjs:189,203; tests/executable/foundation-reference-postgres.test.mjs:163,257-265
- RISK: None. The risk is that this real coverage is invisible because it is averaged into a 582-file count dominated by source-greps.
- RECOMMENDATION: Report tests/executable/ as the behavioral tier explicitly, and expand it (full migration replay, canonical money RPCs, full tenant-table registry) rather than adding more structural contracts.
- CAPACITY: The inventory oversell test and the 12-writer ledger test are the only two contention proofs in the repository; both are single-container and prove correctness under contention, not throughput.

### Adversarial verdicts (5)


**OVERSTATED** — Gate is 2/110 behavioral: no validator executes the runtime or a database. Of 110 gate-lane validators, only mobile-token-validator.mjs and branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs execute work that reproduces the invariant; no validator makes an HTTP request, boots a Worker or Next app, or connects to Postgres. Risk: a conforming-looking source tree passes 110/110 while the deployed system violates every invariant the gate names. (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The 110-validator gate lane (scripts/validate-governance.mjs:71-186) is a static-analysis suite: 0 of 110 validators make an HTTP request, boot a Worker or Next app, or connect to Postgres. Exactly 2 spawn a subprocess (mobile-token-validator.mjs:3-8 re-running the token generator with --check; branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:57-90 shelling to git rev-list), and a third, manifest-lineage-validator.mjs:24-28,52, computationally reproduces its invariant by recomputing SHA-256 over 9 checked-in config files -- so 3/110 execute or recompute, and 0/110 observe runtime. Consequently, for invariants that are inherently behavioral, the gate proxies proof through source-text presence; the sharpest instance is rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:25-31, which certifies the RT-1..RT-9 tenant red-team proof by asserting substrings in the test file (including the literal message "RT-9 authenticated users must not be able to self-add super admins") rather than requiring the test to run. A green gate alone is therefore evidence of source-tree shape, not of runtime conformance. However, the gate is NOT the only barrier to production: .github/workflows/ci.yml:121,164 run `pnpm run test:behavioral`, which boots ephemeral Postgres and executes 22 unskippable executable tests in tests/executable/ (tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs:33-38 drives real psql against migrations 163/164), and .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:173-198 runs Playwright against staging as a hard `needs:` predecessor of migrate-production (:225-229) and deploy-workers-production (:285-289). The defensible defect is that gate-green is over-read as conformance evidence and that specific behavioral invariants are certified by substring, not that a conforming-looking tree can reach production with every invariant violated.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S3 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: STRUCTURAL CORE CONFIRMED. I reparsed scripts/validate-governance.mjs:71-186 programmatically: exactly 110 entries, 0 duplicates, all present on disk. Scanning all 110 for execution primitives returned 0 hits for fetch/HTTP/undici/axios, 0 for dynamic import of app code, 0 for node:vm/new Function, and 0 real Postgres clients. The 3 "postgres" regex hits are string literals, not connections: runtime-topology-validator.mjs:58 is a manifest value ("supabase-postgres-transactional-outbox"); rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:12 is a filename passed to readFile. The claim's sharpest sub-assertion -- no validator makes an HTTP request, boots a Worker/Next app, or connects to Postgres -- is exactly correct. The two cited subprocess validators check out: mobile-token-validator.mjs:3-8 spawnSync's the token generator with --check; branch-lag-guard-validator.mjs:57-90 shells to git rev-list. The auditor also correctly avoided a trap I checked: migration-safety-validator.mjs:123 contains the literal "spawnSync(" only as a FORBIDDEN TOKEN being searched for, not as an execution. Cited law is accurate verbatim: LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:419 (R-18) and :151-155 (ART-02).

MECHANISM CONFIRMED with a sharper example than the auditor gave: rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:25-31 proves a behavioral RLS red-team invariant by asserting substring presence in tests/executable/tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs -- including the literal English assertion message "RT-9 authenticated users must not be able to self-add super admins". Adding that string to the file satisfies the check without any test running.

CORRECTION 1 (count). Under the claim's own criterion ("executes work that reproduces the invariant"), the count is 3, not 2. manifest-lineage-validator.mjs:24-28 and :52 recompute SHA-256 over 9 checked-in config files and compare to declared checksums -- computational reproduction, not text matching.

CORRECTION 2 (risk framing -- the load-bearing overstatement). Behavioral verification exists in the same CI pipeline, outside the gate lane. tests/executable/ holds 22 Postgres tests that shell to real Postgres via `docker exec ... psql` (tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs:33-38) against real migrations 163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql and 164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql, asserting cross-tenant cases RT-2..RT-9; they contain NO skip guard, so they hard-fail if the container is absent rather than silently passing. .github/workflows/ci.yml:121 and :164 run `pnpm run test:behavioral` = `node scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs start && node --test tests/*/*.test.mjs`, triggered on pull_request and push to main/stepup/main (ci.yml:7-18). Separately, .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:173-198 runs `pnpm exec playwright test --project=chromium tests/e2e/` against staging, and it is a HARD predecessor of deploy: migrate-production `needs: [staging-e2e, governance-gate]` (:225-229) and deploy-workers-production `needs: [migrate-production, ...]` (:285-289). The E2E job is not decorative. Therefore "a green 110/110 gate coexists with a deployed system violating every invariant" does not follow: the static gate is one lane composed with behavioral lanes that do observe runtime and do block production deploy.

CAVEAT ON MY OWN REFUTATION: I verified workflow CONFIGURATION plus test SOURCE, not live execution -- config is not proof of deployment. But that is sufficient to refute the claim's assertion that nothing in the pipeline observes runtime behavior. Also note tests/e2e contains only 2 spec files, so E2E breadth is thin -- which supports a moderated concern, not the original S1 framing.

NET: the descriptive finding (the gate lane is a static-analysis suite; some behavioral invariants are proxied through substring presence) is real, correctly evidenced, and worth recording. The severity and the "false green reaches production" risk are not earned. S1 -> S3; evidence_state VERIFIED-SOURCE stands.

**OVERSTATED** — Two gate validators' main scan loop is dead code during a real gate run: rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:36 and one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:28 both begin their offender loop with `if (!entry.extra) continue;`, and `extra` is true only for fixtures injected via LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON, which is set nowhere except the unit test. Claimed S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The offender scan loops of two gate-lane validators are unreachable during a real gate run. `scripts/validators/rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:36` and `scripts/validators/one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:28` open with `if (!entry.extra) continue;`; `phase-b-common.mjs:52-59` marks every real workspace file `extra:false` and only fixtures from `LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON` get `extra:true`. That variable is set nowhere in the repo except `tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:147`, and `validator-orchestrator.mjs:305` spawns validators without injecting it, so both loops read every file and skip it. Every other validator using `entry.extra` uses it as an allowlist-bypass modifier, never as a bare skip-all — these two are the outliers, and `meta-no-theater-validator.mjs:31-38` cannot catch it because it only greps validator names. Consequences, precisely scoped: (a) rls-force-coverage's real enforcement reduces to four fixed-target greps (`:13-32` — the tenant registry plus migrations 155 and 164 and one test file), so a FUTURE migration adding a tenant table without FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY passes the gate; no DB event trigger substitutes for it (`grep -rln "CREATE EVENT TRIGGER" database/supabase/migrations/` is empty). (b) one-system-per-domain still performs live assertions on `config/runtime/production-topology.json` (`:9-22`), so only its regex scan is dead. There is NO current violation being masked: migration `164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:39-74` dynamically ENABLE+FORCEs every table carrying site_id/store_id at migration time, and every post-164 migration creating a tenant table (176, 184, 185, 194, 195) already includes FORCE. This is therefore a latent gate-integrity / missing-preventive-control defect that contradicts the "no false green is representable" contract at `scripts/validate-governance.mjs:12` — not an active RLS exposure. Fixing it requires baselining the ratchet to migrations after 164, since enabling the loop as written would flag roughly 35 legacy migration files.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: MECHANISM: fully reproduced, every citation is accurate. `scripts/validators/rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:36` and `scripts/validators/one-system-per-domain-validator.mjs:28` are verbatim `if (!entry.extra) continue;`. `scripts/validators/phase-b-common.mjs:52-59` builds every real workspace entry with `extra: false`; only `phaseBExtraEntries()` (`phase-b-common.mjs:19-43`) sets `extra: true`, and it returns `[]` when `LETBUYY_PHASE_B_EXTRA_FILES_JSON` is unset. My own grep across the repo (excluding node_modules/.next/dist/.turbo/.wrangler/graphify-out/worktrees) returns that variable at only four sites: `phase-b-common.mjs:20,27,31` and `tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:147`. The orchestrator spawns each validator at `scripts/governance/validator-orchestrator.mjs:305` with NO `env:` key (grep for `env\s*:` and `PHASE_B` in that file returns nothing), so it inherits an environment where the var is unset. Both validators are declared in the gate lane at `scripts/validate-governance.mjs:164` and `:166`. So during `pnpm run validate:governance` both loops iterate every real file and `continue` immediately. CONFIRMED.

CORROBORATION THE CLAIMANT DID NOT CITE: this pattern is anomalous. `grep -rn "entry.extra" scripts/` shows 13 other uses (`no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:29-33`, `identity-sole-issuer-validator.mjs:26-27`, `token-source-single-validator.mjs:36`, `canonical-ledger-only-validator.mjs:123-129`, `no-public-using-true-validator.mjs:19`, `shell-single-validator.mjs:33`, `no-reserved-service-activation-validator.mjs:22`), and in every one of them `!entry.extra` is an allowlist-bypass modifier (`if (allowed.has(path) && !entry.extra) continue;`) — never a bare skip-everything guard. Only these two use the bare form. `tests/governance/phase-b-validators.test.mjs:67-73` supplies the rls-force fixture, so the only thing that ever exercises the loop is a synthetic string — the unit test manufactures green confidence for a scan that never runs on the repo. `scripts/validators/meta-no-theater-validator.mjs:31-38` (the anti-theater validator) only greps validator NAME fragments in the gate array, so it structurally cannot detect a dead scan body. `scripts/validate-governance.mjs:12` states the execution contract "no false green is representable" — this defect contradicts that stated contract.

WHY S1 IS NOT EARNED (the claimant tested the mechanism but never tested the exposure): (1) `database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:39-74` is a DO block that enumerates every `public` BASE TABLE carrying `site_id` or `store_id` from information_schema and EXECUTEs `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + a GUC-reading `app_tenant` policy on each. That retroactively covers all ~35 pre-164 migration files that create tenant tables without a same-file FORCE (000_baseline, 076, 090, 095-106, 116-120, 124-140, 159, 160, …). (2) I enumerated every migration after 164: the only files creating tables with a tenant column are 176 (3 tables / 3 FORCE), 184 (2/2), 185 (2/2), 194 (1/1), 195 (1/1) — all carry FORCE. Migration 169 has zero FORCE but its four tables (metric_rollups, mrr_arr_rollups, cohort_snapshots, funnel_steps) contain no site_id/store_id/owner_id/developer_id/partner_id/custom_user_id, so they fall outside the loop's own trigger condition at `rls-force-coverage-validator.mjs:40`. Therefore there is NO outstanding violation the dead loop is currently masking; the exposure is latent, not present. (3) The forward-looking gap IS real and I confirmed there is no DB-side substitute: `grep -rln "CREATE EVENT TRIGGER" database/supabase/migrations/` returns nothing, so 164's DO block is one-shot and does not auto-cover tables added in future migrations; `tenant-isolation-validator.mjs` checks registry parity and service-role bypass but never FORCE; `migration-safety-validator.mjs` checks ENABLE only (lines 169-172, 188-191); `tests/executable/tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs:23,266` iterates a fixed RT_CASES list, not all tenant tables, and its runner `test:foundation:wave2` (package.json:113) appears in no workflow. So "a new tenant table can be added with no FORCE and the gate stays green" is TRUE — a missing preventive control, not a live vulnerability. (4) Fixability nuance the claim omits: removing the guard as written would immediately flag the ~35 legacy files above, so the ratchet needs baselining to migrations > 164, not a one-line deletion. (5) Scope correction: one-system-per-domain-validator is not wholly inert — lines 9-22 read `config/runtime/production-topology.json` and assert the four frozen roots are not `status:"active"`. Only its regex scan loop is dead. The claim's own wording ("main scan loop") is accurate, but "scanning zero repository content" is true of the loops, not of that validator as a whole.

EVIDENCE STATE: VERIFIED-SOURCE is correct and unchanged — the defect is established purely from source at this SHA. Nothing here is deployment-verified, and I make no live claim about the production DB's actual relforcerowsecurity state (that remains UNKNOWN; the repo itself books it as the human seam SKIP-D4-LIVE-FORCE-COVERAGE-PROBE, asserted at scripts/validators/whole-repo-end-state-validator.mjs:210).

**OVERSTATED** — The 21 mandated 'enterprise test proof files' are validator wrappers and self-referential tautologies (19 are 12-30 line spawnSync(validator)+assert.equal(status,0) wrappers; the remaining 2 — replay-determinism and queue-recovery — reimplement production logic locally and assert against their own copies; none imports a production module, so queue retry/DLQ and replay determinism are falsely reported as covered). Severity S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The 21 test files mandated by scripts/validators/test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs:9-35 are existence-checked only, and all 21 import zero production modules. Ten are validator wrappers or hybrids that spawn scripts/validators/*.mjs and assert exit 0 (typescript-governance, migration-safety, cloudflare-isolation, cloudflare-binding-governance, dependency-governance, runtime-governance, rollback-lineage-governance, ci-simulation, plus manifest-lineage-governance and workflow-trigger-coverage which add real-artifact assertions). Three are self-referential tautologies that reimplement production logic locally and assert against their own copies: replay-determinism.test.mjs:4-58/67 (own replay reducer), queue-recovery.test.mjs:4-35 (own queueRetryDelayMs/planQueueRecovery), and feature-flag-governance.test.mjs:5-18 (own stableBucket). The concrete coverage gap is queue retry delay: the shipped policy is eventRetryDelaySeconds at packages/event-core/src/index.ts:804 (base 30s, exponential, cap 3600s), called at services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:1596 and services/api-gateway/src/execution/{queue,outbox}-execution.adapter.ts:688/236, and no test in the repository imports or executes it — the test's local copy encodes a different policy (1000ms base, 10000ms cap). However, the claim's broader characterization does not hold: two of the 21 execute shipped production scripts and assert fail-closed behavior (deployment-safety.test.mjs:5-29 against infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs; execution-mode.test.mjs:5-21 against infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-live-certify.mjs), six assert against real shipped source/config text, and DLQ/quarantine behavior IS bound to the production consumer by queue-storm-controls.test.mjs:16-33/63-75/77-86 (asserting QueueProcessingError, quarantinedDelivery, DeterministicQueueJobError, quarantine_platform_queue_job, migration 089, and production max_concurrency <= 10). The validator additionally mandates a tsx contract lane whose tests do import production modules (tests/contracts/domain-event-contracts.test.ts:3-6).
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The core defect is real, but the scope, the count, and the risk statement are all materially wrong.

CONFIRMED parts.
(1) scripts/validators/test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs:9-35 does require exactly 21 named test files to exist (`exists(file)` only — no content check), and root `package.json` runs them via `node --test --test-concurrency=1 tests/*/*.test.mjs`, so all 21 are at glob depth 2 and do execute.
(2) All 21 import only node: builtins. I checked each file's import lines mechanically (imports==node_imports for all 21). Zero production modules imported.
(3) The self-referential tautologies are real. tests/replay/replay-determinism.test.mjs:4-58 defines its own `replay()` reducer and then asserts `replay(events)` deep-equals `replay(structuredClone(events))` (line 67) — a pure function compared to itself. tests/queues/queue-recovery.test.mjs:4-35 defines local `queueRetryDelayMs`/`queueRetryAvailableAt`/`planQueueRecovery` and asserts against them (lines 46-65).
(4) The retry half of the risk is CONFIRMED and is the strongest part of the claim. The shipped retry policy is `eventRetryDelaySeconds` at packages/event-core/src/index.ts:804 — `Math.min(3600, baseSeconds * 2 ** Math.min(attempt-1, 8))`, base 30s. It is called on the live path at services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:1596, services/api-gateway/src/execution/queue-execution.adapter.ts:688 and outbox-execution.adapter.ts:236. The test's local copy uses initialDelayMs 1000 / maxDelayMs 10000 / exponent cap 10 — a completely different policy. `rg -n "eventRetryDelaySeconds" tests/` returns zero hits: no test in the repo imports or executes the real function. `planQueueRecovery` exists nowhere in production source (only tests/queues/queue-recovery.test.mjs and a string literal in config/runtime/queue-lifecycle-manifest.json:163).

REFUTED / OVERSTATED parts.
(a) The "19 validator wrappers" count is wrong. Only 8 are pure spawnSync-validator wrappers: typescript-governance.test.mjs:5-12, migration-safety.test.mjs:5-12, cloudflare-isolation.test.mjs:5-12, cloudflare-binding-governance.test.mjs:5-12, dependency-governance.test.mjs:5-31, operations/runtime-governance.test.mjs:5-30, operations/rollback-lineage-governance.test.mjs:5-30, ci/ci-simulation.test.mjs:12-20. Two more are hybrids (contracts/manifest-lineage-governance.test.mjs:15-21 asserts the real config/runtime/manifest-lineage.json checksums; ci/workflow-trigger-coverage.test.mjs:25-100 asserts real .github/workflows/ci.yml trigger blocks). That is 10, not 19.
(b) Two of the 21 actually EXECUTE shipped production scripts and assert behavior, directly contradicting "a test named as coverage proof must exercise the shipped implementation" being universally violated: tests/operations/deployment-safety.test.mjs:5-29 runs infrastructure/scripts/deploy/deploy-worker-runtime.mjs and asserts non-zero exit plus /--env is required/ and /invalid --env local/; tests/security/execution-mode.test.mjs:5-21 runs infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-live-certify.mjs --mode=execute with a stripped env and asserts it fails closed. These are genuine fail-closed behavioral tests of shipped code.
(c) Six others bind to real shipped source/config text rather than being wrappers: production-deploy-workflow.test.mjs (161 lines, reads .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml + config/vercel/projects.json + config/runtime/production-topology.json), rate-limit-collapse.test.mjs (80 lines, reads services/api-gateway/src/index.ts and middleware/rateLimit.ts), queue-bulk-cost.test.mjs (67), queue-storm-controls.test.mjs (86), contracts/event-contracts.test.mjs, contracts/runtime-lifecycle-contract.test.mjs. These are grep-style assertions, not execution, but they are not tautologies.
(d) The DLQ half of the risk statement is refuted at the set level. tests/operations/queue-storm-controls.test.mjs:16-33 and :63-75 assert against the actual production queue consumer: `class QueueProcessingError` (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:70), `function quarantinedDelivery` (index.ts:374), `class DeterministicQueueJobError` (platform-queue-consumer.ts:221), `quarantine_platform_queue_job` (platform-queue-consumer.ts:1604) and the real database/supabase/migrations/089_quarantine_platform_queue_job.sql; :77-86 asserts production max_concurrency <= 10 from services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml. So DLQ/quarantine behavior IS bound to the shipped queue consumer by the mandated set — deleting or renaming it fails the gate. The claim's phrasing "the production queue consumer is never executed by either test" is literally true of the two named tests, but its stated risk ("Queue retry/DLQ policy ... reported as covered") over-generalizes to the whole set.
(e) The same validator (test-coverage-governance-validator.mjs:38-40) also mandates a `tsx --test tests/contracts/*.test.ts` lane, and that lane does import production modules — tests/contracts/domain-event-contracts.test.ts:3-6 imports `getDomainEventContract` and `parseDomainEventPayload` from packages/event-core/src/index. The auditor did not account for this counterweight.
(f) Minor citation imprecision: the claim cites replay-determinism.test.mjs:1-30 and queue-recovery.test.mjs:1-30; the files are 78 and 65 lines respectively.

UNDERSTATED on one axis: there are three local-reimplementation tautologies, not two. tests/feature-flags/feature-flag-governance.test.mjs:5-18 defines its own FNV-1a `stableBucket()` and asserts `stableBucket(x) === stableBucket(x)` — trivially true for any pure function — while the shipped `stableBucket` in packages/shared-types/src/index.ts is only checked by regex (lines 20-26).

Severity: S2, not S1. This is an assurance/governance defect producing a false coverage signal for three specific behaviors (queue retry delay, event replay determinism, feature-flag bucket determinism), not a production correctness, money, tenancy or security defect. The mandated set is not uniformly circular (2 execute shipped scripts, 6 bind to shipped source, 1 contract lane imports real modules), and the repository carries a separate executable Postgres suite (tests/executable/*-postgres.test.mjs, 20 files). VERIFIED-SOURCE is earned for the structural facts; no claim of runtime behavior is made.

**OVERSTATED** — TEST-R10 is false as written: no CI job replays migration history (S1, VERIFIED-CONFIG)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The "replay 000->197" clause of TEST-R10 (AGENTS.md:214) is unimplemented: no CI job or local script replays Supabase migration history from scratch. The rest of TEST-R10 IS satisfied — ci.yml:121 and ci.yml:164 run `pnpm run test:behavioral` (package.json:120), which docker-starts postgres:16-alpine (scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs:55-73, applying no migrations) and executes 21 real-SQL suites under 43 SET ROLE statements. What is missing is that each suite in tests/executable/ first does DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS public CASCADE, hand-builds a fixture schema, then applies 1-5 hand-picked migrations, sometimes out of numeric order (foundation-wave1-money-postgres.test.mjs:12-18 applies 139, 119, 142, 151, 160). tests/migrations/v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs:47-80 is a readFileSync/regex source grep over a hard-coded 19-file list, not a replay. Nothing exercises the 126-file 000->197 history in order. The concrete consequence, provable at this SHA: tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:94-100 builds public.sites with 5 columns while the real table (000_baseline.sql:11961-11981) requires store_name, site_type and subdomain NOT NULL — so the money-kernel behavioral suites assert against a schema the production database does not have, and fixture drift, cross-migration policy overwrites, and apply-order conflicts are structurally undetectable in CI. Partially mitigated: migration-safety-validator.mjs:17-22 statically bans the destructive replay hazards, v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs:56-71 enforces idempotency patterns on 19 files, and staging-deploy.yml:46 applies migrations to a real staging database before production-deploy.yml:281 reaches production — so apply failures surface in staging, not production.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The core technical assertion survives every refutation attempt I made, but the headline, the lead evidence, and the severity are all broader than what the evidence supports.

CONFIRMED by direct source reading:
- scripts/governance/foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs:55-73 — start() is docker run postgres:16-alpine + waitReady() + ensureDatabase(). Zero migrations applied. Exactly as cited.
- `grep -rn "services:" .github/workflows/` returns nothing across all 16 workflows; the only postgres hits are apt-get postgresql-client (production-deploy.yml:247-250, hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:44-47).
- tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:78-122 — resetDatabase() does DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS public CASCADE, hand-builds a fixture, then psql(migrationSql) for the single file 139. 20 of 21 files in tests/executable/ contain the same DROP SCHEMA line.
- tests/executable/foundation-wave1-money-postgres.test.mjs:12-18 applies 139, 119, 142, 151, 160 — numerically OUT OF ORDER, which is definitionally not a replay.
- tests/migrations/v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs:47-80 is entirely readFileSync + regex over a hard-coded 19-file list plus a grep of the validator source. No database is touched.
- `ls database/supabase/migrations | wc -l` = 126; numbering is 000 then 073->197 contiguous; 000_baseline.sql is a 28,083-line pg_dump squash.
- I searched exhaustively for a refutation and found none: no readdirSync loop over the migrations dir in scripts/ or tests/; scripts/governance/foundation-restore-drill.mjs:44-45 is pg_dump/pg_restore of an existing DB and is not wired into ANY workflow (only package.json:127 "--plan"); environment-lifecycle.yml has no migration step; tests/replay/ is event-sourcing determinism, unrelated. No from-scratch 000->197 replay exists anywhere at this SHA.

OVERSTATED on three axes:
1. Scope/title. TEST-R10 (AGENTS.md:214) is a normative MUST, not a factual assertion — it cannot be "false," only unsatisfied. Its PRIMARY clause is in fact satisfied: ci.yml:121 and ci.yml:164 run `pnpm run test:behavioral`, which per package.json:120 starts the real Postgres 16 container and then runs `node --test tests/*/*.test.mjs`, executing all 21 real-SQL executable suites; 43 SET ROLE statements appear across them (32 app_user, 6 authenticated, 5 anon, 4 executable_app_user). Only the parenthetical "replay 000->161" clause fails. Calling the whole rule false is broader than the evidence.
2. Lead evidence is non-probative and misleading. "No workflow declares a services: postgres container" is true but absence of a services: block is not absence of Postgres in CI — the script provisions it via docker run inside the job.
3. Severity. This is a missing-coverage gap, not a live defect, and three mitigations exist: scripts/validators/migration-safety-validator.mjs:17-22 statically bans DROP TABLE / DROP SCHEMA / DROP COLUMN / TRUNCATE / RENAME COLUMN / RENAME TO across all tracked migrations; tests/migrations/v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs:56-71 enforces IF NOT EXISTS / DROP POLICY IF EXISTS / DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS idempotency on a 19-file subset; and staging-deploy.yml:46 runs `supabase db push --include-all` against a real staging DB before production-deploy.yml:281 does the same for prod, so apply-order breakage surfaces pre-production — which the claim's own risk statement concedes.

UNDERSTATED in one dimension, with harder evidence than the auditor cited: the fixture schemas materially diverge from the real schema. tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs:94-100 creates public.sites with 5 columns (id, name, created_at, updated_at, deleted_at); the real table at database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:11961-11981 declares store_name text NOT NULL, site_type text NOT NULL, subdomain text NOT NULL and dozens more. The fixture could not satisfy the real NOT NULL constraints. So the money-kernel behavioral suites are evidence about a fabricated schema, not the production one — exactly the "presence-as-coverage" failure TEST-R10's second sentence warns against.

Evidence state: VERIFIED-CONFIG understates the work. The decisive evidence is executable source I read (foundation-ephemeral-postgres.mjs, the 21 tests/executable suites, v2-migration-replayability.test.mjs), corroborated by config (ci.yml, package.json, staging-deploy.yml). VERIFIED-SOURCE is the accurate label.

S2 is the defensible severity. It would only reach S1 if reframed around fixture-schema divergence invalidating the money/tenancy behavioral evidence base — a stronger and narrower claim than the one under test.

**REFUTED** — The only behavioral money proof tests a ledger RPC with zero production callers (tests/executable/money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs exercises public.post_ledger_transaction, which has no runtime caller; production money flows through create_checkout_order / confirm_order_payment_with_ledger / complete_order_refund which have no behavioral Postgres test). Severity S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The claim's premise is wrong: `post_ledger_transaction` is the transitive kernel of every production money write, not an orphan. Production reaches it via `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` (database/supabase/migrations/165_d5_canonical_money_paths.sql:253), which is called by confirm_order_payment_with_ledger (168:238), complete_order_refund (168:401), record_order_payment_failure_with_ledger (168:663), reconcile_cod_remittance_with_ledger (165:354), and directly from services/api-gateway/src/routes/settlements.ts:175, routes/payouts.ts:195, routes/commission.ts:148, lib/payment-route-support.ts:185. money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs is also not the only behavioral money proof (settlement-postgres.test.mjs and foundation-wave1-money-postgres.test.mjs both post real ledger transactions and assert idempotency and zero balance breaks). The defensible residual finding is narrower: no executable Postgres test loads migration 165, 168, or 170, so the deployed bodies of confirm_order_payment_with_ledger, complete_order_refund, record_order_payment_failure_with_ledger, create_checkout_order, and the current source-ownership-checking post_settlement_ledger_transaction are never executed — the behavioral settlement coverage runs the superseded migration-151 body, and entry-point coverage for those RPCs is source-regex assertion only. That is an S3 test-coverage gap on the entry-point layer, not an S1 unproven-ledger finding.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S3 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: Both load-bearing assertions are false. The auditor grepped only TypeScript (`rg post_ledger_transaction services apps packages`) and never traced the SQL call graph, and never read the LATER migrations (165, 168) that wire the kernel into production.

(1) "Zero production callers" / "a kernel the product does not use" is REFUTED. `public.post_ledger_transaction` (defined database/supabase/migrations/139_executable_foundation_ledger_core.sql:223) is called by `public.post_settlement_ledger_transaction` at database/supabase/migrations/165_d5_canonical_money_paths.sql:253 (the canonical body — 165 is the last definition; the only other is the superseded 151_executable_settlement_ledger.sql:6). `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` is in turn called by exactly the RPCs the auditor named as "the money paths that actually run": `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger` at database/supabase/migrations/168_b1_b2_capture_refund_ledgering.sql:238 (function defined at 168:133; I read the body at 168:133-250 and confirmed the delegation), `complete_order_refund` at 168:401 (defined 168:314), `record_order_payment_failure_with_ledger` at 168:663 (defined 168:563), and `reconcile_cod_remittance_with_ledger` at 165:354. It is also called directly from four production TypeScript call sites the auditor's grep should have surfaced: services/api-gateway/src/routes/settlements.ts:175, services/api-gateway/src/routes/payouts.ts:195, services/api-gateway/src/routes/commission.ts:148, services/api-gateway/src/lib/payment-route-support.ts:185. Chaining to the adapters the auditor cited: services/api-gateway/src/execution/payment-execution.adapter.ts:94 -> confirm_order_payment_with_ledger -> post_settlement_ledger_transaction -> post_ledger_transaction; services/api-gateway/src/execution/order-execution.adapter.ts:505 -> complete_order_refund -> same chain. So money-ledger-postgres.test.mjs proves append-only enforcement, sum-debit=sum-credit (ledger_balance_breaks() = 0 at :167, :191, :214) and idempotency under 12 concurrent psql writers (:242-255) on the exact kernel every production money write funnels through — the opposite of the claim.

(2) "The only behavioral money proof" is REFUTED. tests/executable/ holds 21 Docker-Postgres behavioral tests. At least two are money proofs on a directly-production-called RPC: tests/executable/settlement-postgres.test.mjs:264-277 asserts `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` returns the SAME transaction id for a repeated idempotency key and asserts ledger_balance_breaks() = 0 at :256; tests/executable/foundation-wave1-money-postgres.test.mjs:151-205 posts through `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` and asserts backfill variance 0, reconciliation status 'verified', and ledger_account_balance_minor = 10000. payment-, billing-, checkout-, order-, tax-postgres tests are likewise real psql-against-migration tests.

(3) What survives, at lower severity: `rg -n "migrations/[0-9]+" tests/executable/*.mjs | sort -u` shows the highest migration any executable test loads is 164. No executable test loads 165, 168, or 170. Therefore the DEPLOYED bodies of `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger` (168:133), `complete_order_refund` (168:314), `record_order_payment_failure_with_ledger` (168:563), `create_checkout_order` (170_v1_shipping_rates_gst.sql:31) and the CURRENT `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` (165:214, which added source-ownership resolution and the tenant-GUC mismatch check absent from 151) are never executed by a behavioral test — settlement-postgres.test.mjs and foundation-wave1-money-postgres.test.mjs load 151, i.e. they prove a superseded body. Coverage at that layer is source-regex only (services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/money-b1-b2-ledger.test.ts:32,49,64,87-89; money-d5-ledger.test.ts:126-154). That is a real test-coverage gap, but it is S3 not S1: the invariants at risk (append-only, balance, idempotent ON CONFLICT under concurrency) ARE behaviorally proven on the kernel these RPCs delegate to, and double-webhook races in confirm_order_payment_with_ledger are serialized by the `SELECT * FROM orders ... FOR UPDATE` at 168:167-171 before the payment_transactions UPDATE/INSERT at 168:181-231.

## DIMENSION: observability

### Narrative summary
At 5af69e0c the repository has a real but narrow first-party telemetry path and an entirely non-existent second-party (platform) telemetry path. What genuinely emits: `services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts` runs on every request and (a) writes a structured JSON line to console (which Cloudflare Workers Logs retains, since every wrangler.toml sets `[observability] enabled = true` with `head_sampling_rate = 0.1`), (b) writes one Analytics Engine data point via the `RUNTIME_TELEMETRY` binding, (c) inserts one row into Postgres `runtime_telemetry_metrics`, (d) conditionally inserts one row into `worker_request_logs`, and (e) plans a runtime alert for any 5xx. `services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:317,341` emits queue batch/dead-letter data points plus `lag_p95_ms`/`lag_max_ms`. `services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:603,624` emits `letbuyy.replay.lag.seconds` from the 15-minute production cron. Two of the four Workers — `services/webhook-service` and `services/mobile-bff` — declare the `RUNTIME_TELEMETRY` binding in wrangler but never call `writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint`; webhook-service emits 31 unstructured `console.*` lines only. There is NO live metric pipeline provable from the repository: `infrastructure/observability/otel-collector.yaml` exports to `tempo.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local` and a Loki service on a Kubernetes cluster that exists nowhere in the repo (the only k8s/helm trees are under `docs/archive/` and stale `.claude/worktrees/`), no runtime imports any OpenTelemetry SDK, and no span is ever started. `infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml` alerts on `http_server_errors_total`, `http_requests_total`, `letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total` and `letbuyy_deployment_health_gate_failed_total` — I grepped all four repo-wide and found ZERO emitters; the actual emitted names are dot-separated (`letbuyy.runtime.request`, `letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total`). The connecting env vars would be `PROMETHEUS_URL`, `GRAFANA_URL`, `OTEL_COLLECTOR_HEALTH_URL` (consumed only by `infrastructure/scripts/observability/verify-observability-rollout.mjs:60-72` in `--execute`) plus a Cloudflare API token for the Analytics Engine SQL API; `packages/lcis-adapters/src/analytics-engine/AnalyticsEngineAdapter.ts:48` itself states AE query values are NOT CONNECTED, and nothing outside that package reads the dataset — so the data points written are, at this SHA, write-only. Exactly one SLO exists (`infrastructure/monitoring/slo-catalog.yaml`: deployment-gate-success, target 100, 30d) and it is measured against `/v1/health`, which returns a hardcoded `status: 'healthy'` literal (`services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:307-315`) with no dependency probe. Alerting terminates in a hardcoded dry run: `services/queue-workers/src/incident-command.ts:94-127` sets `mode: 'dry_run'` unconditionally and only writes DB rows; no PagerDuty/Opsgenie/Slack/webhook call exists anywhere. Money-ledger reconciliation mismatches (`services/queue-workers/src/money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:102`) are counted then logged at level `info` with `status: 'completed'`, every 6 hours, with no alert. Failure handling is thin: `packages/infrastructure/provider-resilience-adapter/index.ts` implements timeouts, retries, backoff, fallback and recovery planning but has ZERO call sites; the Razorpay, Twilio-WhatsApp, Resend, Turnstile, Upstash and Supabase call sites all use bare `fetch`/SDK with no timeout and no retry. The only real timeouts in a request path are `apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:110,121,161` (3s, fail-closed to a 503 holding page). Security controls fail closed (rate limiting, Turnstile, JWKS) which is correct, but Turnstile fail-closed without a timeout means a Cloudflare siteverify outage takes signup/login fully down. Load shedding exists only as per-key rate limiting (429/503 + `Retry-After`), plus one real circuit breaker in `services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:233,309-331` and a KV backoff breaker at `services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:613`; nothing sheds on saturation signals. The single largest capacity finding is self-inflicted: every API request performs 1-2 Postgres INSERTs into `runtime_telemetry_metrics`/`worker_request_logs`, and migration `131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql` puts an AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE trigger on both that does a `SELECT` from `retention_policies` plus a dynamic INSERT into a shadow partitioned table — so observability alone costs roughly 4-6 statements per request on the one Postgres, and neither live table is ever pruned (retention is only detached on the shadow). DR is declared, not proven: `config/runtime/database-recovery-policy.json` marks PITR `human-required-paid-tier`, states RPO/RTO must be "recorded" (they are recorded nowhere), and the only backup is a `pg_dump` uploaded as a GitHub Actions artifact with `retention-days: 7` (`.github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:252-269`); the restore drill script refuses any non-localhost URL. `live-production-certification.yml` is `workflow_dispatch`-only and its execute mode is gated on Prometheus/Grafana/OTel endpoints for a stack that is not deployed, so it can only ever have produced a "plan" result; no certification report is committed anywhere. The only genuinely real provider evidence in the repo is provisioning-shaped: real Cloudflare KV namespace, Hyperdrive and D1 database IDs in `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml` — that proves resources exist in an account, not that traffic flows.


### Findings (26)


**[S0] Incident paging is hardcoded dry-run: no human is ever notified of any production failure**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The whole alert path terminates in database rows. api-gateway plans a runtime alert for every 5xx (lib/runtime-alerting.ts:108-110) with job type 'incident.page.dry_run' (:7) and enqueues it to RUNTIME_QUEUE. The queue-workers consumer sendIncidentPage() sets mode:'dry_run' as a literal (incident-command.ts:96) regardless of the configured paging_adapter, then only inserts a production_incident_events row and an audit row (:107-125). The operations API accepts paging_adapter dry_run|pagerduty|opsgenie (routes/operations/incident-command.ts:55) but always emits mode:'dry_run' (:320-321,:461-462). A repo-wide search for pagerduty|opsgenie|hooks.slack.com|SLACK_WEBHOOK|alertmanager outside docs and tests returns only the enum and the DB CHECK constraint.
- EXPECTED: A customer-impacting 5xx, dead-letter growth, or reconciliation break must reach an on-call human through a delivery channel within minutes.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/incident-command.ts:94-127; services/api-gateway/src/lib/runtime-alerting.ts:7; services/api-gateway/src/lib/runtime-alerting.ts:108-110; services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations/incident-command.ts:55; services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations/incident-command.ts:320-321; database/supabase/migrations/133_internal_os_incident_command.sql:16; rg -n -i 'pagerduty|opsgenie|hooks.slack.com|SLACK_WEBHOOK|alertmanager' --glob '!docs/**' --glob '!*.md' . -> only enum + CHECK constraint
- RISK: A total checkout or payment outage produces rows in production_incidents and nothing else. Mean time to detection equals the time until a merchant complains. At 100 merchants this is embarrassing; at 1,000 merchants and ~30k orders/month it is an unbounded revenue and trust loss with no upper bound on outage duration.
- RECOMMENDATION: Before any merchant onboarding beyond a pilot, wire one real delivery channel (a webhook to a paging provider or, minimally, an email/WhatsApp escalation via the already-integrated Resend/Twilio credentials) behind the existing paging_adapter enum, and make dry_run an explicit non-production-only mode that the production safety preflight rejects.
- CAPACITY: Not a throughput limit, but it caps safe operating scale: without detection, every other capacity ceiling in this report becomes a silent outage.

**[S0] Money-ledger reconciliation mismatches are counted, logged at info, and never alerted**  ·  domain=money  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: runMoneyLedgerReconciliation returns { mismatches: results.filter(r => r.status === 'mismatch').length } (money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:102). The scheduled handler pipes that straight into writeQueueLog('info', { route:'scheduled.money_ledger_reconciliation', status:'completed', result }) (queue-workers/src/index.ts:648-662) — the log level and status are 'info'/'completed' even when mismatches is non-zero. No metric is emitted, no incident is created, no alert is planned. The job runs on the queue-workers cron '17 */6 * * *' (wrangler.toml:17,123,237), i.e. every 6 hours. config/runtime/production-safety-preflight.json:22-26 makes MONEY_LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_ALERT_READY a hard precondition for Wave 1 shared-env cutover, and that flag is unclaimed.
- EXPECTED: Under a single canonical append-only double-entry ledger, a reconciliation break is the highest-severity signal the platform can produce and must page immediately.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:102; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:648-662; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:237; config/runtime/production-safety-preflight.json:22-26
- RISK: A ledger divergence — double-capture, missing refund posting, gift-card projection drift — is discovered up to 6 hours late and then only by someone reading 10%-sampled Cloudflare Workers Logs for a line whose level is 'info'. Financial exposure grows for the entire undetected window and India GST invoices are immutable once issued.
- RECOMMENDATION: Emit a dedicated metric on mismatch count, raise the log level to error when mismatches > 0, create a production_incidents row, and gate Wave 1 cutover on MONEY_LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_ALERT_READY actually being satisfied by a delivery channel rather than by a flag name.
- CAPACITY: Reconciliation cost scales with ledger volume; at 30k orders/month a 6-hour window covers roughly 250 orders of undetected exposure per run.

**[S1] No live metric pipeline exists: the entire OTel/Prometheus/Grafana/Loki/Tempo stack targets a Kubernetes cluster that does not exist and alerts on metrics nothing emits**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: otel-collector.yaml exports traces to tempo.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local:4317 and logs to loki.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local:3100 — Kubernetes service DNS. The only k8s/helm trees in the repo are under docs/archive/ and stale .claude/worktrees/; the constitutional topology is Cloudflare Workers + Vercel. No runtime imports any OpenTelemetry SDK (no @opentelemetry entry in any services/*/package.json or apps/*/package.json) and no span is ever started, so nothing would send OTLP even if a collector existed. prometheus-rules.yaml alerts on http_server_errors_total, http_requests_total, letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total and letbuyy_deployment_health_gate_failed_total; I grepped all four repo-wide and found ZERO emitters — the code emits dot-separated names (letbuyy.runtime.request, letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total). observability-platform.json marks four Grafana dashboards 'mustUseLiveTelemetry': true; those dashboards point at a postgres datasource uid 'supabase-ops' that no Terraform or workflow provisions.
- EXPECTED: Either a live pipeline exists with emitters matching the alert expressions, or the platform config is honestly marked as not-yet-provisioned rather than declaring mustUseLiveTelemetry.
- EVIDENCE: infrastructure/observability/otel-collector.yaml:29; infrastructure/observability/otel-collector.yaml:33; infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:5; infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:14; infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:23; config/runtime/observability-platform.json:18-39; infrastructure/observability/grafana-runtime-dashboard.json:16; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:166; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:342
- RISK: Every operational readiness claim built on 'observability' is claiming a stack that has never run. The three alert rules would evaluate to no-data forever even if Prometheus were deployed, because the metric names do not match. This is the specific pattern that turns a green gate into a false assurance.
