- RECOMMENDATION: Pick one: (a) delete the Prometheus/Grafana/Loki/Tempo artifacts and build alerting on the Analytics Engine SQL API plus Cloudflare Workers Logs, which is what the topology actually supports; or (b) provision the stack. Do not leave mustUseLiveTelemetry:true against unreachable targets. Either way, rename the alert expressions to the metric names actually emitted.
- CAPACITY: Without a metric pipeline, none of the saturation thresholds needed for the 100 -> 1,000 -> 10,000 merchant envelope can be observed, so scaling decisions are unfalsifiable.

**[S1] Observability writes are the dominant Postgres load: every API request costs roughly 4-6 statements on the single source of truth**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The gateway observability middleware runs on every request and unconditionally inserts one row into runtime_telemetry_metrics (observability.ts:265-302 -> observability-execution.adapter.ts:125-143), plus one row into worker_request_logs whenever the sampling decision records (observability.ts:338-381) — which is 100% of everything except anonymous storefront GET/HEAD (shouldRecordRequestLog:199-204). Migration 131 then puts an AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW trigger on BOTH tables (131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-121) executing mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow(), which does a SELECT from retention_policies and a dynamic INSERT into the shadow partitioned parent (:18-53). Queue batches add two more inserts each (queue-workers/src/index.ts:292-315).
- EXPECTED: Telemetry should not compete with commerce transactions for the one Postgres. Analytics Engine already receives the same data point at zero database cost.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:265-302; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:338-381; services/api-gateway/src/execution/observability-execution.adapter.ts:84-143; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:18-53; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-121; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:292-315
- RISK: Write amplification of roughly 4-6 statements per request against a pool capped at max=5 connections per isolate (pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:52). Under merchant-dashboard or checkout burst this saturates connections and lock throughput before the business workload does, and the failure mode is checkout latency, not a missing metric. This is the single most likely cause of a self-inflicted outage in the 1,000-merchant step.
- RECOMMENDATION: Make the Postgres telemetry write sampled (reuse the existing REQUEST_LOG_SAMPLE_RATE mechanism for runtime_telemetry_metrics too, which is currently unsampled), or drop it entirely in favour of the Analytics Engine data point already written on the same code path. Separately, disable the partition-shadow mirror triggers on the two hot observability tables until the swap is actually approved.
- CAPACITY: Directly sets the request-throughput ceiling: observability writes are 2x-3x the transactional write volume of a typical read request.

**[S1] The live observability tables have no retention and are never pruned**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics and operation_metrics are plain heap tables in 000_baseline.sql (:13224, :11603, :9939). Retention is defined only for the SHADOW partitioned copies: migration 079 creates retention_policies with retention_days and detach_expired_hot_table_partitions() (:265), and the monthly workflow hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml calls ensure_next_hot_table_partitions + detach_expired_hot_table_partitions. Migration 079 states explicitly that 'the live rename-swap is intentionally not performed'. I searched for any DELETE against public.worker_request_logs or public.runtime_telemetry_metrics across database/, services/ and scripts/ and found none.
- EXPECTED: High-cardinality request-log and metric tables on the single Postgres must have enforced retention.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:13224; database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:11603; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:1-4; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:265; rg -n 'DELETE FROM public.worker_request_logs|DELETE FROM public.runtime_telemetry_metrics' database/ services/ scripts/ -> no matches
- RISK: Unbounded growth of the two highest-write tables plus their shadow mirrors. Disk exhaustion, index bloat, autovacuum pressure, and a steadily worsening p95 on every query that touches the same buffer pool. At 30k orders/month with 100% request logging on all non-storefront traffic this is a matter of months, and the failure lands on the same Postgres that holds the ledger.
- RECOMMENDATION: Either complete the partitioned swap under ARB approval so the existing detach job applies to live data, or add a bounded retention job (delete/copy-out older than N days) against the live tables. Do not ship to 1,000 merchants with both the mirror trigger active and no pruning.
- CAPACITY: Storage and vacuum are the slow-moving ceiling; index bloat on worker_request_logs degrades every co-resident query.

**[S1] /v1/health is a hardcoded literal, yet it is what deployment health gates and the only SLO measure**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: healthResponse() returns { status: 'healthy', timestamp, environment, version } with no dependency probe whatsoever (api-gateway/src/index.ts:307-315), and is mounted at both /health (:670) and /v1/health (:677). /v1/health/queues only reports whether a binding object exists — Boolean(env[binding]) — and returns 503 solely for a missing binding, never for depth or drain failure (:332-348, :680-683). verify-health-gates.mjs fetches exactly these endpoints with an 8s timeout and asserts response.ok (:74-80). The single SLO in slo-catalog.yaml is deployment-gate-success measured from deployment_health_gate_results, which is populated from these probes.
- EXPECTED: A readiness probe must exercise the dependencies whose failure would make the service unable to serve: Postgres reachability, and ideally KV and the rate-limit backend.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:307-315; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:332-348; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:670-683; infrastructure/scripts/deploy/verify-health-gates.mjs:56-80; infrastructure/monitoring/slo-catalog.yaml:2-8
- RISK: A deploy in which Postgres credentials are wrong, Hyperdrive is misconfigured, or the rate-limit backend is down passes the health gate green and is promoted. The one SLO the platform tracks measures the liveness of a string literal.
- RECOMMENDATION: Split liveness (/health, keep as is) from readiness (/v1/health/ready): the latter should do a bounded SELECT 1 through the same client path production traffic uses, check the CACHE binding, and report degraded with a 503. Point verify-health-gates.mjs and deployment_health_gate_results at readiness.
- CAPACITY: A meaningless readiness probe removes the only automated stop between a bad config and full production traffic.

**[S1] No timeout on any external provider call except the storefront edge proxy**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: I inspected every outbound call site in the deployed runtimes. Razorpay (razorpay-adapter/index.ts:100,187,199), Twilio WhatsApp (whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:244), Turnstile siteverify (api-gateway middleware/security.ts:237 and identity-platform lib/turnstile.ts:85), Upstash Redis (rateLimit.ts:204,694), Supabase REST (supabase-adapter/index.ts:79,98,118,138), Supabase RPC from the webhook worker (webhook-service/src/index.ts:188) and Resend via SDK (resend-adapter/index.ts:66) all use bare fetch/SDK calls with no AbortSignal, no deadline, and no retry. The only real request-path timeout in the repository is apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:110,121,161 (AbortSignal.timeout(3000)), whose comment explicitly reasons about a hanging gateway.
- EXPECTED: Every cross-network call on a request path carries a deadline; Workers give you AbortSignal.timeout for free.
- EVIDENCE: packages/infrastructure/razorpay-adapter/index.ts:100; packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:244; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:237-247; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/turnstile.ts:85-99; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:204; packages/infrastructure/supabase-adapter/index.ts:79; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:188; apps/storefront-runtime/src/proxy.ts:110
- RISK: A slow (not failed) provider converts into held Worker invocations and held Postgres connections across the whole fleet. Because the query-cost limiter itself performs a KV or Redis round trip before admitting the request, a slow rate-limit backend stalls every /v1 route simultaneously. Partial provider degradation is precisely the failure mode this leaves undefended.
- RECOMMENDATION: Adopt the already-written provider-resilience-adapter, or at minimum add AbortSignal.timeout to every fetch listed above with per-provider budgets (Turnstile ~2s, Razorpay ~5s, Redis ~500ms, Supabase REST ~5s).
- CAPACITY: Absent deadlines, tail latency of any single dependency propagates into total concurrency consumption; this is the classic path from a degraded dependency to a full outage.

**[S1] provider-resilience-adapter — the repository's entire timeout/retry/circuit-breaker/fallback implementation — has zero call sites**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: packages/infrastructure/provider-resilience-adapter/index.ts implements executeProviderCall (retry loop with classified errors and attempt telemetry, :193-255), withProviderTimeout (Promise.race deadline, :257-269), providerBackoffMs exponential backoff (:84-86), classifyProviderHealth (:88-109), decideProviderFallback (:111-118), planProviderRecovery (:120-160) and ProviderResilienceOperationsRuntime (:162-191). A repo-wide search for executeProviderCall, withProviderTimeout, ProviderResilienceOperationsRuntime and the '@letbuyy/infrastructure/provider-resilience-adapter' subpath outside the package itself and its package.json export map returns nothing.
- EXPECTED: Either the adapter wraps the money and notification provider calls, or it should not be presented as platform resilience.
- EVIDENCE: packages/infrastructure/provider-resilience-adapter/index.ts:193-255; packages/infrastructure/provider-resilience-adapter/index.ts:257-269; packages/infrastructure/package.json:29; rg -n 'executeProviderCall|withProviderTimeout|ProviderResilienceOperationsRuntime|provider-resilience-adapter' --glob '!node_modules' . -> only the package + its export entry
- RISK: An architecture review reading the package list would conclude the platform has provider resilience. It has the types and the algorithms and applies none of them. This is defined-but-never-called, the most misleading category of readiness evidence.
- RECOMMENDATION: Wire executeProviderCall around the Razorpay, Resend, Twilio and Turnstile call sites with per-provider ProviderResiliencePolicy values, and add a validator that fails if a new outbound provider fetch is added outside the adapter.
- CAPACITY: Retry storms and unbounded waits are both unmanaged today; the adapter's rateLimit.shedOnLimit and retryAfter handling are exactly the backpressure primitives missing at the provider edge.

**[S1] Only one SLO exists and it covers deploy gates, not any user-facing behaviour; no error budget is computed anywhere**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: infrastructure/monitoring/slo-catalog.yaml contains exactly one entry: deployment-gate-success, sli successful_deployment_health_gates/total_deployment_health_gates, target 100, window 30d, errorBudgetPolicy block-production-promotion-on-failed-gate. There is no availability, latency, checkout-success, payment-success, refund-success or queue-lag SLO. No code computes an error budget or burn rate; the only burn-rate-classed alert (observability-platform.json:42-44) maps to a Prometheus rule over metrics nobody emits. infrastructure/monitoring/AGENTS.md:9 requires monitors covering auth, storefront, checkout, API gateway and webhook health 'before production readiness claims' — none exist.
- EXPECTED: For a commerce platform targeting 30k orders/month, at minimum: checkout success rate, payment capture success rate, gateway availability, gateway p95 latency, and queue lag, each with a target, a window and an error budget policy.
- EVIDENCE: infrastructure/monitoring/slo-catalog.yaml:1-8; infrastructure/monitoring/AGENTS.md:9; config/runtime/observability-platform.json:40-53; infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:4-12
- RISK: There is no definition of 'working' that can be evaluated. Deployment-rollback.md step 6 instructs the operator to 'close the rollback only when error budget burn returns below alert threshold' — an instruction that cannot be executed because no error budget exists.
- RECOMMENDATION: Define five user-journey SLOs with SLIs computable from data already collected (worker_request_logs has route, status_code and latency_ms), and delete or rewrite the single deploy-gate SLO so it is not mistaken for service health.
- CAPACITY: Without latency and success-rate SLOs there is no trigger to invest in capacity ahead of the 1,000- and 10,000-merchant steps.

**[S1] No golden signal for Postgres saturation is emitted anywhere in source**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Searching the whole repository for pg_stat_activity, pg_stat_database, pg_locks, pg_stat_statements and max_connections outside tools/ and docs/ prose returns only descriptive strings in the LCIS tooling (which itself declares them NOT CONNECTED, e.g. packages/lcis-adapters/src/postgres/PostgresAdapter.ts:185-189). No runtime, no cron, no migration and no health route reads connection count, active/idle counts, lock wait, or slow-query statistics. The pool is configured at max 5 connections per isolate with connect_timeout 5s and no statement timeout (pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:51-56).
- EXPECTED: With ONE Postgres as the constitutional source of truth, connection utilisation and lock wait are the two signals that must exist before scaling merchant count.
- EVIDENCE: packages/infrastructure/pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:51-56; packages/lcis-adapters/src/postgres/PostgresAdapter.ts:185-189; rg -n 'pg_stat_activity|pg_locks|max_connections|pg_stat_statements' services/ apps/ database/ scripts/ -> no runtime emitters
- RISK: The scale envelope is 100 -> 1,000 -> 10,000 merchants on one Postgres, and there is no way to know how close to connection exhaustion or lock contention the system is. The first symptom will be checkout timeouts. Approach-to-saturation is completely invisible.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a cron-driven probe (the queue-workers 6-hourly schedule already exists, but this warrants 1-5 minutes) that reads pg_stat_activity counts by state, the longest lock wait, and pg_stat_database xact/blk ratios, and writes them as one telemetry row + one Analytics Engine data point. This is the highest-value single instrumentation addition in this report.
- CAPACITY: This is THE capacity signal for the constitutional single-Postgres design; no other measurement substitutes for it.

**[S1] KV write-per-request is a documented saturation ceiling in production with no metric and no alarm**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:250-259 sets RATE_LIMIT_KV_FALLBACK = 'on' for production, with a founder-approved comment stating verbatim that Upstash is not provisioned, that the query-cost limiter therefore uses the CACHE KV namespace, and that 'Cloudflare KV has a daily write quota and each limited request costs one KV write, so sustained high volume can exhaust it. REPLACE WITH UPSTASH before scale.' The gateway applies queryCostRateLimit to /v1/* globally (index.ts:685-689). checkKvQueryCostLimit performs a cache.get followed by a cache.put on every admitted request (rateLimit.ts:617-623). There is no KV write-rate metric anywhere and no alert on quota approach.
- EXPECTED: A known, documented quota ceiling on the hot path must be measured and alerted before it is reached.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:250-259; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:685-689; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:603-641; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:928-931
- RISK: When the KV daily write quota is exhausted, checkKvQueryCostLimit throws, the KV path trips its backoff breaker (:613,:626) and returns queryCostUnavailableResult, which the middleware turns into 503 RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE for every /v1 data route (:990-994). That is a total, self-inflicted API outage with no advance warning, triggered purely by traffic growth.
- RECOMMENDATION: Emit a KV write counter on the same Analytics Engine data point already written per request, alert at 60% of the daily quota, and provision UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN before merchant count grows — the comment itself flags this as the pre-scale action.
- CAPACITY: This is a hard, quantified daily ceiling on total /v1 request volume and is very likely the first ceiling the platform hits as merchants grow.

**[S1] No saturation-driven load shedding, backpressure, degraded mode, or read-only mode exists**  ·  domain=topology  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: What exists: per-key fixed-window and cost-weighted leaky-bucket rate limiting returning 429 with Retry-After (rateLimit.ts:479,1005,1091,1171) and 503 RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE when the backend is down (:466-479,:990-994,:1082-1086,:1162-1166); the app-platform kill switch (middleware/appPlatformKillSwitch.ts); a per-flag kill_switch in shared-types (:4803-4804); a queue drain budget and per-isolate circuit breaker (platform-queue-consumer.ts:233,252-262,309-331); a KV backoff breaker (rateLimit.ts:613). What does NOT exist: any admission decision keyed to queue depth, DB connection count, DB latency or Worker CPU; any read-only or maintenance mode; any feature degradation under load. /v1/health/queues returns 503 only for a missing binding (index.ts:332-348), never for backlog. The gateway does count backlog rows (index.ts:435-454) but reduces the result to a boolean hasWork.
- EXPECTED: Under saturation the platform should preferentially shed non-critical reads and keep checkout and payment paths serving.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:466-479; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:332-348; services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:435-454; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:252-262; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:613
- RISK: Rate limiting is per-tenant/per-key fairness, not global protection. A legitimate aggregate load spike across many merchants passes every limiter and saturates the shared Postgres, at which point everything — including checkout — fails together rather than degrading. There is no lever to turn off analytics, exports or search and preserve the money path.
- RECOMMENDATION: Introduce a cheap global pressure signal (the backlog counts at index.ts:435-454 plus the Postgres connection probe recommended elsewhere in this report), and use it to shed the already-classified expensive routes — calculateQueryCost at rateLimit.ts:507-524 already labels /analytics, /export, /ai/ and /internal/ as high cost, so the classification work is done.
- CAPACITY: Determines whether the system degrades or collapses at the saturation point; today it collapses.

**[S2] Two of the four Workers declare the RUNTIME_TELEMETRY binding but never write a data point**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: services/webhook-service binds RUNTIME_TELEMETRY in both environments (wrangler.toml:61-63, :100-102) and never imports or calls writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint; its entire telemetry is 31 mostly-unstructured console.* lines (e.g. index.ts:735 '[Razorpay] Invalid signature', :778, :1364). services/mobile-bff declares RUNTIME_TELEMETRY in its Env type (src/index.ts:16) and binds it (wrangler.toml:79-81, :127-129) with no write anywhere. Only api-gateway (middleware/observability.ts:165, index.ts:603) and queue-workers (index.ts:317, :341) actually emit. Meanwhile tools/graph-server/production-health.mjs:27 scores 'Runtime' on '4 Workers with [observability]+RUNTIME_TELEMETRY'.
- EXPECTED: The webhook worker handles the payment provider callback path and must emit signature-failure, replay-age and processing-latency metrics.
- EVIDENCE: services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:100-102; services/webhook-service/src/index.ts:735; services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:16; services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml:127-129; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:165; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:317
- RISK: The Razorpay webhook path — the single most security- and money-critical ingress — produces no metric at all. An attacker probing signatures, or a provider misconfiguration producing mass invalid-signature rejections, is visible only in 10%-sampled unstructured logs.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add the same writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint + structured log pair to webhook-service for signature verification outcome, event age, and per-event-type processing latency; either use or remove the mobile-bff binding.
- CAPACITY: Webhook processing latency and retry volume are a queue-load input that is currently unmeasurable.

**[S2] Turnstile fails closed with no timeout, so a Cloudflare siteverify degradation takes signup and login fully down**  ·  domain=identity  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Both implementations POST to https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/siteverify with no AbortSignal and return false on any throw or non-ok response: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:237-247 and apps/identity-platform/src/lib/turnstile.ts:85-99. Missing TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY also returns false (security.ts:227-229, turnstile.ts:78-81). Fail-closed is the correct security posture; the absence of a deadline is the defect.
- EXPECTED: Fail-closed on a bot-defence control, but with a bounded deadline and an explicit, monitored decision about what happens during a provider outage.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:237-247; apps/identity-platform/src/lib/turnstile.ts:85-99; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/security.ts:227-229
- RISK: A slow siteverify holds Worker invocations on the registration and login paths until the platform request limit; a failing one blocks 100% of merchant and customer registration and login with no alert and no bypass. Turnstile becomes a hard availability dependency for all account access.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add AbortSignal.timeout(~2000) to both call sites, emit a metric on verification outcome (ok / fail / timeout / provider-error), and make the outage behaviour an explicit ARB decision (fail-closed with a status page, or fail-closed for signup and rate-limit-only for login).
- CAPACITY: Turnstile latency sits inline on the two highest-concurrency unauthenticated endpoints.

**[S2] Disaster recovery is declared policy only: no RTO, no RPO, no PITR, and the sole backup is a 7-day CI artifact**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: config/runtime/database-recovery-policy.json marks pointInTimeRecovery.status 'human-required-paid-tier' with skipId SKIP-W0-PITR-TIER and defaultEnabledByCodex false; its resumeEvidence list requires 'RPO/RTO target recorded' and 'restore timestamp tested in a non-production clone' — I searched config/, infrastructure/, scripts/, database/ and docs/runbooks/ and neither an RTO nor an RPO value is recorded anywhere. The only backup that exists is production-deploy.yml:252-260 (pg_dump custom format, pg_restore --list, gpg AES256) uploaded at :262-269 with retention-days: 7, and it runs only on a production deploy. scripts/governance/foundation-restore-drill.mjs:14-15 hard-refuses any URL that is not localhost, and :33 additionally requires LETBUYY_RESTORE_DRILL_ALLOW_EXECUTE=true — so no restore has ever been exercised against production-shaped data. There is no restore runbook in docs/runbooks/.
- EXPECTED: A commerce platform holding an immutable money ledger needs a stated RPO/RTO, PITR enabled, and at least one rehearsed restore.
- EVIDENCE: config/runtime/database-recovery-policy.json:10-18; config/runtime/database-recovery-policy.json:20-25; .github/workflows/production-deploy.yml:252-269; scripts/governance/foundation-restore-drill.mjs:14-15; scripts/governance/foundation-restore-drill.mjs:33
- RISK: If Postgres is lost or corrupted between deploys, the recovery point is the last production deploy — potentially days of orders, payments and ledger entries — and the backup itself expires 7 days after that deploy. Recovery time is unknown because it has never been measured. Declared policy is not proven capability.
- RECOMMENDATION: Founder decision to enable Supabase PITR (paid tier), record explicit RPO and RTO targets in database-recovery-policy.json, move the encrypted dump off the 7-day GitHub artifact into durable storage (R2 is already provisioned), and run one timed restore drill into a clone, recording the measured RTO.
- CAPACITY: Restore time grows with database size, which grows with the unpruned observability tables identified separately in this report.

**[S2] Live production certification cannot reach execute mode from repository state and has produced no recorded evidence**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: The workflow is workflow_dispatch-only — no schedule, no push trigger (live-production-certification.yml:3-22). Its live step runs phase4-live-certify.mjs, which evaluates six capability groups (:23-51); the 'observability' group requires PROMETHEUS_URL, GRAFANA_URL and OTEL_COLLECTOR_HEALTH_URL (:34-37) for a stack this repository does not deploy, and 'secret-rotation' requires ten ROTATE_SECRET_* values (:46-49). executeReady demands every group ready (:104); requesting execute without them throws (:106-111); auto silently collapses to plan (:114). config/runtime/live-production-certification.json:5 self-labels certificationMode 'credential-gated-plan-ready'. No phase4-live-certification-report.json is committed and no dated certification output exists anywhere in the tree.
- EXPECTED: If this is the only candidate source of live production evidence, either its preconditions must be satisfiable or it must not be counted as evidence.
- EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/live-production-certification.yml:3-22; infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-live-certify.mjs:34-37; infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-live-certify.mjs:104-114; config/runtime/live-production-certification.json:5; config/runtime/live-production-certification.json:16-22
- RISK: Any readiness narrative citing 'live production certification' is citing a plan-mode run over repository files. In execute mode the same script would deploy Workers, apply Terraform and rotate ten production secrets in one unattended step (:126-139) — a high-blast-radius operation gated only on the presence of environment variables.
- RECOMMENDATION: Drop the Prometheus/Grafana/OTel capability group (or replace it with a Cloudflare Analytics Engine + Workers Logs probe that the actual topology supports) so execute mode becomes reachable, and split secret rotation out of the certification path so certification never mutates production secrets.
- CAPACITY: No live capacity evidence of any kind can be produced today.

**[S2] validate:observability is a file-existence check, so the green gate proves only that YAML and JSON files are on disk**  ·  domain=test  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: package.json:53 maps validate:observability to scripts/validators/production-readiness-validator.mjs. That validator's assertions are exists()/shape checks: a requiredFiles array (lines 16-83) including otel-collector.yaml, prometheus-rules.yaml and the four Grafana JSONs, asserted at :89; referenced-file existence at :185, :201, :207; and JSON key presence at :210-258. infrastructure/scripts/observability/verify-observability-rollout.mjs is no stronger without credentials: :33-43 asserts YAML substrings ('endpoint: 0.0.0.0:4318', 'otlp/tempo:', 'loki:'), :46-48 asserts alert names appear in the rules text, :50-57 asserts dashboard titles/uids/panels — only :59-72, behind --execute plus three secrets, performs a live HTTP probe.
- EXPECTED: A validator named after observability should assert that emitted metric names match alert expressions and that every declared dashboard resolves to a datasource that exists.
- EVIDENCE: package.json:53; scripts/validators/production-readiness-validator.mjs:16-89; scripts/validators/production-readiness-validator.mjs:185-207; infrastructure/scripts/observability/verify-observability-rollout.mjs:33-57
- RISK: This is the mechanism by which the alert-name mismatch and the unreachable Kubernetes collector both remained green. A passing gate here is actively misleading evidence of operational readiness.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add one deterministic assertion to the validator: every metric name referenced in prometheus-rules.yaml must appear as a metricName literal in the deployed runtimes, and every dashboard datasource uid must be declared in a provisioning artifact tracked in the repo. That single check would have caught this class of defect.
- CAPACITY: Not capacity-relevant directly, but it is why capacity instrumentation gaps were not surfaced by the existing 107-validator gate.

**[S2] Cache hit ratio has no emission point; it exists only as a response header**  ·  domain=cache  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: cachedJson() classifies every storefront read as HIT, MISS or BYPASS and sets it on X-Storefront-Cache (storefront-cache.ts:37,41,51,113-128). That classification is never passed to the observability middleware: recordRequestAnalyticsEnginePoint's blobs are path, method, statusClass, authSource, errorCode, CF-IPCountry, CF-Ray (observability.ts:175-183) and the Postgres dimensions are route, method, status_code, status_class, latency_ms, error counters, auth_source, country, colo (:278-290). Neither carries cache state. The Cache API is used with an internal synthetic key host (letbuyy-storefront-cache.internal), so Cloudflare zone-level cache analytics do not cover it either.
- EXPECTED: Cache hit ratio for the storefront read path should be a first-class metric, since it directly determines Postgres read load.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:37-51; services/api-gateway/src/lib/storefront-cache.ts:113-128; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:175-183; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:278-290
- RISK: A cache-effectiveness regression (a version-bump storm from bumpStorefrontCacheVersion, a key-shape change, or STOREFRONT_CACHE toggled off) silently multiplies Postgres read load with no signal until latency degrades. Hit ratio is the leading indicator for storefront database load and it is unobservable.
- RECOMMENDATION: The classification already exists on the response — set an internal header or context value and append it to the existing Analytics Engine blob array and the runtime metric dimensions. This is a one-line-per-site change on an existing emission path.
- CAPACITY: Storefront read volume against Postgres is hit-ratio dependent; without it, read-path capacity headroom cannot be estimated.

**[S2] Cloudflare Workers Logs head sampling of 0.1 discards roughly 90% of structured error logs**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: All four Workers set [observability] head_sampling_rate = 0.1 and [observability.logs] head_sampling_rate = 0.1 with invocation_logs = true, in the default, staging and production blocks (api-gateway/wrangler.toml:13-20, 109-116, 225-232; queue-workers:7-13, 113-119, 227-233; webhook-service:7-13, 36-42, 75-81; mobile-bff:7-13, 45-51, 93-99). Head sampling selects invocations before the handler runs, so it is blind to outcome: a 500 has the same 10% chance of being retained as a 200. writeStructuredRuntimeLog escalates to console.error for 5xx (observability.ts:85-86) but that escalation cannot survive a sampling decision already made upstream.
- EXPECTED: Error and payment-path invocations should be retained at 100%; only high-volume successful reads should be sampled.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:225-232; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:227-233; services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:75-81; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:80-92
- RISK: During an incident, roughly nine out of ten error log lines simply do not exist. The webhook worker, which has no metric emission at all, loses 90% of its only telemetry. Postgres worker_request_logs becomes the sole complete error record — which is precisely the store most likely to be unavailable during a database incident.
- RECOMMENDATION: Raise head_sampling_rate to 1 for webhook-service and queue-workers (low invocation volume, high diagnostic value) and keep gateway sampling only if the Postgres/AE paths are proven to capture 100% of errors. Note that this interacts with the write-amplification finding: fix that first, then reduce reliance on Postgres for error capture.
- CAPACITY: Log volume is a cost lever; the current setting trades away nearly all diagnostic value for that saving without an explicit decision record.

**[S2] Runbooks reference a metric that does not exist and there is no restore, connection-exhaustion, or provider-outage runbook**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: The 17 files in docs/runbooks/ are short and mostly actionable — incident-sev1.md, deployment-rollback.md, queue-dlq-redrive.md and infra-recovery.md all cite real routes and scripts, which I verified exist (POST /v1/operations/runtime/incidents at routes/operations.ts:731, /readiness-gates at :878, /runtime/overview at :464, the queue redrive/recover-leases routes at :600/:578, and infrastructure/scripts/deploy/production-rollback.mjs). But queue-dlq-redrive.md:9 instructs the operator to watch 'cloudflare.queue.delivery.lag' — a metric name that appears nowhere in the repository (the emitted names are letbuyy.queue.delivery.batch and letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total). deployment-rollback.md:9 conditions closure on error budget burn, which is not computed anywhere. No runbook exists for restoring the database from the pg_dump artifact, for Postgres connection exhaustion, for KV write-quota exhaustion, or for a payment-provider outage. No on-call rotation or escalation contact appears in any runbook.
- EXPECTED: Runbooks should reference only signals that exist, and the highest-probability failure modes must each have one.
- EVIDENCE: docs/runbooks/queue-dlq-redrive.md:9; docs/runbooks/deployment-rollback.md:9; docs/runbooks/incident-sev1.md:1-13; services/api-gateway/src/routes/operations.ts:464; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:317-360
- RISK: An operator following the DLQ runbook during an incident will search for a metric that does not exist and lose time. The absence of a database-restore runbook means the one procedure with the highest blast radius is entirely undocumented.
- RECOMMENDATION: Correct the metric name in queue-dlq-redrive.md, remove or replace the error-budget condition in deployment-rollback.md, and add four runbooks: database restore from the encrypted dump, Postgres connection exhaustion, KV write-quota exhaustion, and Razorpay outage.
- CAPACITY: The missing runbooks map exactly to the three saturation ceilings identified in this report.

**[S2] Analytics Engine data points are write-only: nothing in the repository reads the RUNTIME_TELEMETRY dataset**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Four emission sites write to the dataset (observability.ts:165, index.ts:603, queue-workers/index.ts:317,341) and the binding is declared in all four Workers. The only reader-shaped code is packages/lcis-adapters/src/analytics-engine/AnalyticsEngineAdapter.ts, which at :48 declares verbatim that AE query values are NOT CONNECTED because the SQL API is POST-only and its GET-only boundary cannot reach it; nothing outside that package references the adapter. No dashboard JSON queries Analytics Engine (all four use the postgres datasource uid supabase-ops). No script, workflow or route issues an Analytics Engine SQL query.
- EXPECTED: Emitted telemetry should have at least one consumer — a dashboard, an alert evaluator, or a scheduled query.
- EVIDENCE: packages/lcis-adapters/src/analytics-engine/AnalyticsEngineAdapter.ts:48; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:165; infrastructure/observability/grafana-runtime-dashboard.json:16; rg -n 'AnalyticsEngineAdapter' --glob '!node_modules' . -> no consumers outside packages/lcis-adapters
- RISK: The Analytics Engine path is the one telemetry channel that does not load Postgres, and it is unused. During an incident, the fastest available answer source is a dataset nobody queries, so responders fall back to 10%-sampled logs or to Postgres tables that may themselves be the problem.
- RECOMMENDATION: Add a minimal Analytics Engine SQL query path (POST /accounts/{acct}/analytics_engine/sql with a scoped read token) behind the operations API, and point at least the runtime dashboard at it. This is the lowest-cost route to a real, Postgres-independent metric pipeline given the emitters already exist.
- CAPACITY: Turning the existing AE emission into a readable pipeline would deliver request rate, latency and error rate for the whole fleet without adding any database load.

**[S2] Partition-shadow triggers make partitioning active in production, which the Constitution forbids in this envelope**  ·  domain=governance  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: Migration 131 creates AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW triggers on worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics, outbox_events, platform_queue_jobs and webhook_deliveries, each executing mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow(), which reads retention_policies and writes into a PARTITION BY RANGE shadow parent (:18-53, :112-121, and the enable/disable helper at :58-108). Migrations 079 and 080 create the partitioned shadow tables. A monthly workflow maintains partitions (.github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml, cron '15 2 1 * *', execute-on-schedule). The Constitution lists partitioning-now among the constructs forbidden in this scale envelope.
- EXPECTED: Partitioning stays off until the envelope justifies it and an ARB approves the swap; migration 079 itself says the live swap is intentionally not performed.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-121; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:18-53; database/supabase/migrations/079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:1-4; database/supabase/migrations/080_partition_runtime_telemetry_metrics.sql:21; .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml:1-5
- RISK: The repository is paying the full runtime cost of partitioning (an extra SELECT plus an extra INSERT on every row of five hot tables, on the write path of every API request) while receiving none of the benefit, because the live tables are still unpartitioned heaps with no retention. It is the worst of both positions and it is a constitutional deviation that no gate flags.
- RECOMMENDATION: ARB decision: either disable the mirror triggers via the provided set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false) until the envelope justifies partitioning, or complete the swap so the cost buys real retention. Do not remain in the shadow state.
- CAPACITY: Doubles the physical write cost of the highest-volume tables while delivering zero retention benefit.

**[S3] The queue circuit breaker is per-isolate in-memory state and cannot hold across the Worker fleet**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: queueCircuitBreakers is a module-level Map<PlatformQueueName, QueueCircuitState> (platform-queue-consumer.ts:233) mutated by recordQueueDrainFailure/recordQueueDrainSuccess (:317-331) and read by isCircuitOpen (:309-315). Thresholds default to 3 failures and 60 seconds open (:262-273), configurable via PLATFORM_QUEUE_CIRCUIT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD and PLATFORM_QUEUE_CIRCUIT_OPEN_SECONDS. The same in-memory pattern is used by the KV query-cost backoff (rateLimit.ts:613 queryCostKvUnavailableUntil).
- EXPECTED: A circuit breaker protecting a shared downstream should share state, or its limits should be documented as best-effort.
- EVIDENCE: services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:233; services/queue-workers/src/platform-queue-consumer.ts:309-331; services/queue-workers/src/middleware/../../api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:613
- RISK: Cloudflare Worker isolates are created and evicted continuously, so a breaker that opens in one isolate does not protect the downstream from any other isolate, and a fresh isolate starts with a closed breaker. Under a sustained downstream failure the protection is roughly proportional to isolate lifetime rather than to the failure. This is a partial, non-deterministic mitigation being counted as a full one.
- RECOMMENDATION: Keep the in-memory breaker as a cheap first line but document its limits, and back it with shared state (the CACHE KV namespace or the RUNTIME_COORDINATOR Durable Object, both already bound and effectively unused) if the downstream it protects is one whose overload matters.
- CAPACITY: Determines whether a failing downstream is protected under fleet-wide concurrency or only within single isolates.

**[S3] Mobile crash reporting has a real transport but every shipped build carries the placeholder DSN, so it is disabled**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=provider-action
- CURRENT: Contrary to older internal audits, a working Sentry transport now exists: SentryCrashReportingSink.fromDsn parses the DSN and POSTs a Sentry envelope with an X-Sentry-Auth header (crash_reporting_service.dart:171-228), and buildMerchantCrashReportingSink composes it with the local sink when sentryEnabled (:347-362). However all four dart-defines files — local.json, device.json, staging.json and production.json — set SENTRY_DSN to the sentinel SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_MOBILE_SENTRY_DSN (each at line 9), and the release workflow builds with --dart-define-from-file=dart-defines/production.json (mobile-merchant-release.yml:53) with no secret override. So the shipped app falls back to SharedPreferencesCrashReportingSink and reports never leave the device.
- EXPECTED: A store-released app should upload crashes, with the DSN injected at build time from a CI secret rather than committed.
- EVIDENCE: apps/mobile/merchant-app/lib/services/crash_reporting_service.dart:171-228; apps/mobile/merchant-app/lib/services/crash_reporting_service.dart:347-362; apps/mobile/merchant-app/dart-defines/production.json:9; .github/workflows/mobile-merchant-release.yml:53
- RISK: Merchants on Android and iOS crash with zero visibility to the team. The engineering work is done; only the provisioning step is missing, which makes this an unusually cheap gap to leave open.
- RECOMMENDATION: Provision a Sentry project, add the DSN as a CI secret, and append an extra --dart-define at the release build step so production.json can keep the sentinel. Note that the fallback is fail-safe (crashes are retained locally), so this is a visibility gap rather than a correctness defect.
- CAPACITY: Not capacity-relevant.

**[S3] No distributed tracing exists despite the observability contract declaring traces and spans as supported signals**  ·  domain=observability  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: config/runtime/observability-platform.json:4-10 declares signals traces, metrics, logs, spans and operational-events, and infrastructure/observability/AGENTS.md:10 requires runtime telemetry to preserve tenant and request correlation. In practice a trace id is derived from X-Trace-ID, Traceparent or a fresh UUID (observability.ts:48-50), echoed as a response header (:220) and stamped onto logs, AE blobs and DB rows. It is never used to open a span: no runtime depends on any @opentelemetry package, and the traceparent field in the queue envelope (queue-workers/src/index.ts:33,222,241) is carried but never emitted to any collector (:402 sets it to null).
- EXPECTED: Either genuine spans exist with parent/child relationships, or the contract should declare correlation ids rather than traces and spans.
- EVIDENCE: config/runtime/observability-platform.json:4-10; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:48-50; services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:220; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:33; services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:402
- RISK: Cross-service latency attribution — gateway to Postgres to queue to webhook — is impossible. During a slow-checkout incident there is no way to determine which hop consumed the time; only total per-request latency exists. Correlation ids allow after-the-fact joining across three stores but not span-level attribution.
- RECOMMENDATION: Do not adopt an OpenTelemetry SDK for this envelope. Instead emit per-stage duration as additional doubles on the Analytics Engine data point already written per request (db_ms, provider_ms, queue_enqueue_ms), and amend observability-platform.json to declare correlation rather than traces/spans. That gives the attribution without the collector.
- CAPACITY: Per-stage latency attribution is what identifies which dependency is nearing saturation; today only aggregate latency exists.

**[INFO] Real Cloudflare resource identifiers are the only provider evidence in the repository, and they prove provisioning rather than traffic**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=ARB-review
- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml contains genuine Cloudflare resource identifiers: staging KV namespace id and preview_id (:186-187, 32-hex), staging Hyperdrive id (:195, 32-hex), staging D1 database_id (:200, UUID form d1e49c22-...), production KV namespace id and preview_id (:317-318, 32-hex), production Hyperdrive id (:324, 32-hex), production D1 database_id (:329, UUID form 76d197a5-...), plus real custom-domain routes api.letbuyy.com and api-staging.letbuyy.com. infrastructure/cloudflare/*.tf by contrast uses var.cloudflare_account_id throughout with no hardcoded account or zone id. Every other operational manifest self-labels as non-evidence: deployment-lineage-manifest.json:4 and environment-lineage.json:4 both mode 'governance-contract-not-live-evidence', live-production-certification.json:5 'credential-gated-plan-ready', production-safety-preflight.json:3 'build-and-prove', database-recovery-policy.json:3 'build-and-prove-not-live-action'. artifacts/ contains only artifacts/local-operational-proof/ (Lighthouse HTML/JSON and screenshots from a local run). No dated production probe output, Worker version id, deployment id, or provider API response is committed anywhere. No committed secret was found; production placeholders use the SKIP_NEEDS_HUMAN_ sentinel (wrangler.toml:276, :281-282).
- EXPECTED: An ARB should be able to distinguish 'resources exist in an account' from 'production is serving traffic'.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:186-200; services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:317-329; config/runtime/deployment-lineage-manifest.json:4; config/runtime/environment-lineage.json:4; config/runtime/live-production-certification.json:5; artifacts/local-operational-proof/
- RISK: Low, and the repository is commendably honest here — the manifests explicitly refuse to claim live evidence. The risk is only that a reader mistakes real KV/Hyperdrive/D1 identifiers for proof that production is live and healthy. They prove that someone ran a provisioning step at some point; they say nothing about traffic, error rates, or whether the current HEAD is deployed.
- RECOMMENDATION: No action required beyond preserving the existing honesty labels. When the first real deploy runs, capture wrangler whoami output, the Worker version id and a dated health probe as the first genuine live-evidence artifact — environment-lineage.json:30-39 already specifies exactly this artifact list.
- CAPACITY: No live utilisation data exists, so every capacity conclusion in this report is structural and derived from source, not measured.

### Adversarial verdicts (5)


**OVERSTATED** — Incident paging is hardcoded dry-run: no human is ever notified of any production failure. Severity S0, evidence VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Runtime incident paging has no delivery channel: alert jobs terminate in database rows, so no on-call human is pushed a notification for gateway 5xx, dead-letter growth, or reconciliation breaks. Every 5xx plans an alert (services/api-gateway/src/lib/runtime-alerting.ts:108-110) with job type 'incident.page.dry_run' (:7), enqueued from the live observability middleware (middleware/observability.ts:305) onto a configured RUNTIME_QUEUE producer/consumer pair (api-gateway/wrangler.toml:309-311, queue-workers/wrangler.toml:309-310). The consumer hardcodes mode:'dry_run' (queue-workers/src/incident-command.ts:96) irrespective of the row's paging_adapter and only inserts production_incident_events + audit_logs rows (:107-125); the operations API accepts pagerduty|opsgenie (routes/operations/incident-command.ts:55) but always emits dry_run (:320-321,:461-462), and no migration through 197 implements a provider (only 133_internal_os_incident_command.sql:16). The Prometheus rules at infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:4-30 do not close the gap: no Alertmanager receiver exists and their metric names (http_server_errors_total, letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total) match nothing the runtimes emit (letbuyy.runtime.request, letbuyy.alert.runtime.error, letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total). This is a launch blocker, not an active production loss: it is already recorded and gate-enforced as human seam SKIP-CAP-ALERTING-END-TO-END / CAP-C10 (SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:47, CAPABILITY_BACKLOG.md:16, beta-readiness-validator.mjs:236-240), no Constitutional article requires alerting, and 35 other 🔴 CRITICAL seams (including live PSP credentials) mean no live merchant money flows at this SHA. Closing it requires a provider/ops decision (PagerDuty/Opsgenie/Sentry credentials + on-call drill), so remediation is HUMAN-REQUIRED; note that a working outbound email channel already exists (services/api-gateway/src/routes/contact-public.ts:146) and is simply not used by the incident path.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: TECHNICAL CORE: CONFIRMED. I read every cited line and found no refutation.

- services/api-gateway/src/lib/runtime-alerting.ts:7 `RUNTIME_ALERT_JOB_TYPE = 'incident.page.dry_run'`; :108-110 `alertable()` returns true for every statusCode >= 500; :189 enqueues to the queue binding.
- The path is genuinely LIVE, not dead code: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:305 calls `enqueueRuntimeAlert({ runtimeQueue: c.env.RUNTIME_QUEUE ... })` inside `waitUntil` on every response. Producer binding is configured (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:309-311 RUNTIME_QUEUE -> letbuyy-production-queue) and consumed (services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:309-310). So the chain is wired end-to-end at config level.
- Consumer terminates in the DB: services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:604 calls `processIncidentCommandDeliveries`; incident-command.ts:96 `mode: 'dry_run'` is a literal while :97 only *reports* the configured adapter; :107-125 writes a `production_incident_events` row and an `audit_logs` row. There is no fetch/HTTP/email call anywhere in that file.
- No later migration fixes it: `rg -n 'paging_adapter' database/supabase/migrations/` returns exactly one hit, 133_internal_os_incident_command.sql:16 (CHECK dry_run|pagerduty|opsgenie). Migrations run to 197. Migration 133 contains only `updated_at` triggers (:80-86) — no pg_notify/pg_net.
- No provider exists: my own repo-wide search for pagerduty|opsgenie|hooks.slack.com|SLACK_WEBHOOK|alertmanager|victorops|xmatters outside docs/md finds only the zod enum (routes/operations/incident-command.ts:55) and the DB CHECK. Twilio hits are WhatsApp commerce only (packages/infrastructure/whatsapp-commerce-adapter/index.ts:234).

WHAT THE AUDITOR MISSED (strengthens, does not weaken): the grep summary "only the enum and the CHECK constraint" is incomplete — infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:4-30 declares three alert rules covering exactly two of the named expectations (`LetBuyyApiGatewayHighErrorRate`, `LetBuyyQueueDeadLetterGrowth`). They are inert paper: (a) no Alertmanager/receiver config exists anywhere; (b) the expressions reference `http_server_errors_total`/`letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total`, while the runtimes actually emit `letbuyy.runtime.request` (observability.ts:270), `letbuyy.alert.runtime.error` (runtime-alerting.ts:8) and `letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total` (queue-workers/src/index.ts:305,342) — no emitter matches; (c) no Prometheus/Grafana appears in infrastructure/cloudflare/*.tf; the only workflow hits are certification env vars (production-rollout.yml:142-144).

WHY S0 IS NOT EARNED:
1. Not governed. GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md (471 lines) returns ZERO hits for observab|alert|monitor|incident|paging|on-call. No constitutional article is violated; the "expected state" is a sound operational norm, not current law.
2. Not a silent defect — it is a recorded, gate-enforced, human-owned seam. SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:47 (SKIP-CAP-ALERTING-END-TO-END) and CAPABILITY_BACKLOG.md:16 (CAP-C10) state verbatim that "Production incidents cannot be certified as human-paged until provider DSNs, on-call routing, and delivery drills are supplied," and scripts/validators/beta-readiness-validator.mjs:236-240 asserts the gap must REMAIN recorded ("DSN presence alone is not live alert delivery"). The dry-run literal is deliberate and tracked, not an oversight.
3. No live harm at this SHA. LAUNCH_READINESS_2026_06_12/HUMAN_SEAMS_CLOSURE_PROGRAM.md carries 35 open 🔴 CRITICAL seams defined as "no merchant, no money, or no deploy without it", including :52-53 live Razorpay Route / merchant PSP credentials and :34 checkout DNS cutover. The platform cannot take live merchant money, so the claimed "unbounded revenue and trust loss at 1,000 merchants" is a post-launch projection, not present harm. S0 (active, ongoing loss) is not defensible; S1 (must close before first live merchant) is.
4. The absolute "no human is ever notified of ANY production failure" is marginally overbroad: apps/mobile/merchant-app/lib/services/crash_reporting_service.dart:171-231 is a real Sentry HTTP sink that POSTs when a DSN is present — the DSN is an unfilled placeholder (config/env/templates/mobile.dart-defines.example:13), so the channel exists but is unprovisioned, and it covers mobile crashes only, never gateway 5xx.

EVIDENCE STATE: VERIFIED-SOURCE holds for the defect statement (I read the source; the queue wiring is additionally VERIFIED-CONFIG, which is not proof of deployment). It is NOT VERIFIED-TEST: services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/observability.test.ts:167-200 exercises enqueue against a fake queue object and asserts only queued/reason/message shape; services/queue-workers/test/incident-command.test.mjs:18-28 is a source-regex test that string-matches the worker file. Neither exercises delivery.

**OVERSTATED** — Money-ledger reconciliation mismatches are counted, logged at info, and never alerted (claimed S0, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The scheduled money-ledger reconciliation emits no push signal on a break: runMoneyLedgerReconciliation returns a mismatches count (services/queue-workers/src/money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:102) that the scheduled handler logs via writeQueueLog('info', {status:'completed'}) (services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:648-662), and writeQueueLog (index.ts:426-439) only calls console.info/console.error without ever inspecting result.mismatches — so a non-zero mismatch is indistinguishable from a clean run at the log level. No metric is emitted, no incident is created, and no alert delivery channel exists in the repo. The scheduled path also deliberately uses the non-raising refresh_money_ledger_site_reconciliation rather than assert_money_ledger_site_reconciliation, which RAISEs on variance != 0 (migration 160:260-285) but is called only from backfill_wallet_ledger_to_canonical (160:348).

However, the break is NOT undetectable: (a) it is UPSERTed as a durable row in money_ledger_site_reconciliations (migration 160:198-256); (b) platform operators have a purpose-built surface — GET /v1/internal/console/money/reconciliation?status=mismatch (api-gateway index.ts:771 -> internal-console.ts:200 -> internal-console/money-reconciliation.ts:132) with an SoD-gated resolve endpoint (line 184), rendered as a critical-tone "Breaks" table at apps/platform-console/src/app/(dashboard)/money-reconciliation/page.tsx:102-129; and (c) a mismatch immediately fails the merchant wallet read closed with 409 LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED (services/api-gateway/src/routes/wallet.ts:163-176). Detection is therefore pull-based and merchant-symptomatic rather than silent.

Scope correction: the job compares only posted wallet_ledger_entries against the canonical merchant_payable account balance (migration 160:173, 198-256). It does not read payment_transactions, refunds, or gift-card tables, so double-capture posted consistently to both projections, and gift-card projection drift, are not detected by this job at any latency — a separate coverage gap, not a latency gap.

Preflight correction: MONEY_LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_ALERT_READY (config/runtime/production-safety-preflight.json:22-26) is enforced only if a human runs foundation-production-safety-preflight.mjs; evaluateProductionSafetyPreflight has no caller in the deploy pipeline (only tests and its own CLI main at line 83), and production-safety-validator.mjs:32-40 merely asserts the flag name appears as a source string. It is a runbook precondition (docs/runbooks/foundation-wave3-shared-env-apply.md:8), not a machine-enforced cutover blocker.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The code fact is confirmed exactly. money-ledger-reconciliation.ts:102 returns the mismatches count; index.ts:648-662 pipes it into writeQueueLog('info', {status:'completed'}); writeQueueLog (index.ts:426-439) is only console.info/console.error and never inspects result.mismatches, so the level stays 'info' at any mismatch count. No metric, no incident, no alert delivery exists anywhere (the only paging concept is paging_adapter defaulting to 'dry_run', migration 133:16). Cron "17 */6 * * *" confirmed at wrangler.toml:17,123,237 and queue-workers is deployed by production-deploy.yml:367-372 (VERIFIED-CONFIG, not proof of live deployment). Supporting detail the auditor missed: the raising variant assert_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (migration 160:260-285, RAISE EXCEPTION on variance != 0) exists but is called only from backfill_wallet_ledger_to_canonical (160:348) — the scheduled path uses the silent refresh_* variant.

Four pieces of contradicting evidence make the severity and risk narrative overstated. (1) The break is persisted, not ephemeral: refresh_money_ledger_site_reconciliation (migration 160:198-256) UPSERTs a durable per-site row into money_ledger_site_reconciliations with status='mismatch' and variance_minor. (2) A dedicated operator detection surface exists and is mounted on the live path: services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:771 -> routes/internal-console.ts:200 -> routes/internal-console/money-reconciliation.ts:132 exposes GET /reconciliation?status=mismatch (audit action money_ledger.breaks.read at line 170) plus an SoD-gated POST /reconciliation/:siteId/resolve (line 184), and apps/platform-console/src/app/(dashboard)/money-reconciliation/page.tsx:102-129 renders a "Breaks" metric with tone='critical' and total variance. The claim's risk statement that a break is discoverable "only by someone reading 10%-sampled Cloudflare Workers Logs for a line whose level is 'info'" is therefore factually wrong. (3) A mismatch is not silent on the merchant path: services/api-gateway/src/routes/wallet.ts:163-176 returns 409 LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED whenever the reconciliation row is absent, not 'verified', or variance != 0 — an immediate, merchant-visible, fail-closed symptom. (4) The job's scope does not cover the risk classes named: migration 160 compares only wallet_ledger_entries (posted) against the merchant_payable ledger balance via ledger_account_balance_minor (160:173); grep for gift_card|payment_transactions|refunds in migration 160 returns zero hits, so a consistently-posted double-capture yields variance 0 and gift-card projection drift is not in scope at any latency.

The claim's preflight framing is also overstated: config/runtime/production-safety-preflight.json:22-26 declares the flag and foundation-production-safety-preflight.mjs:65-73 fails closed on it, but `rg evaluateProductionSafetyPreflight` finds callers only in tests/governance/foundation-wave3-production-safety.test.ts and the script's own CLI main (line 83). production-deploy.yml:51 runs validate-governance.mjs --lane=gate, and scripts/validators/production-safety-validator.mjs:32-40 only asserts the flag NAME string appears in the preflight source. So it is a runbook gate (docs/runbooks/foundation-wave3-shared-env-apply.md:8), not a machine-enforced deploy blocker — weaker as a mitigation, but also not the "hard precondition" the claim asserts.

Finally, the cited test services/queue-workers/test/money-ledger-reconciliation.test.mjs is a readFileSync + assert.match source-regex test, so no behavioral coverage exists and nothing here reaches VERIFIED-TEST.

Net: a real operability defect (no push signal on a ledger break) but not S0 — the break is durably persisted, surfaced on a purpose-built critical-tone operator console, and produces an immediate fail-closed 409 on the affected merchant's wallet read. This is a detection-latency/paging gap, S1.

**OVERSTATED** — No live metric pipeline exists: the entire OTel/Prometheus/Grafana/Loki/Tempo stack targets a Kubernetes cluster that does not exist and alerts on metrics nothing emits (S1, VERIFIED-CONFIG)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Two observability artifacts are dead config, and the runtime manifests plus a deploy-workflow gate assert a telemetry stack that no code implements — but a real metric pipeline does exist. Dead: infrastructure/observability/otel-collector.yaml has neither a producer nor a destination (exports to tempo./loki.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local at :29,:33 — no Kubernetes topology exists outside docs/archive; and zero @opentelemetry dependency in any services/*/package.json or apps/*/package.json, no span creation, no /metrics route, so nothing ever emits OTLP). infrastructure/observability/prometheus-rules.yaml:5,14,23 alerts on http_server_errors_total, http_requests_total, letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total and letbuyy_deployment_health_gate_failed_total — all four have zero emitters repo-wide; the code emits dot-separated names (observability.ts:166, index.ts:604, queue-workers/index.ts:318,342), and no Prometheus scrape target exists in infrastructure/cloudflare/*.tf or any workflow, so these three alerts would evaluate no-data forever even if Prometheus were deployed. NOT dead: the actual pipeline is Cloudflare Analytics Engine (RUNTIME_TELEMETRY bindings for staging and production in all four Workers, e.g. api-gateway/wrangler.toml:211-213,340-342) plus Postgres runtime_telemetry_metrics rows written on the live request path (observability.ts:268-302 -> observability-execution.adapter.ts:125-143) and on every queue batch (queue-workers/index.ts:292-315), with a queue-based runtime alert enqueue at lib/runtime-alerting.ts:179-191. The four Grafana dashboards are correctly targeted at that real data (grafana-runtime-dashboard.json:19,31 query the exact emitted metric names and the server_error_count dimension written at observability.ts:284); only the Grafana instance and the 'supabase-ops' datasource are unprovisioned. The genuine false-assurance surface is (a) eight runtime.manifest.json files claiming "Emits correlated runtime telemetry to the OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, Tempo, Loki, and Grafana stack" (e.g. api-gateway/runtime.manifest.json:111) when no runtime emits OTel or Prometheus at all, and (b) verify-observability-rollout.mjs:32-57 printing "observability verification passed" from string-fragment file checks alone in the plan-mode branch wired into staging-runtime-deployment.yml:70 and production-rollout.yml:133. The --execute branch is fail-closed on missing PROMETHEUS_URL/GRAFANA_URL/OTEL_COLLECTOR_HEALTH_URL (verify-observability-rollout.mjs:63-65), and live-production-certification.json:5,17 already marks the mode "credential-gated-plan-ready", partially mitigating the "dishonest declaration" charge.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: Most of the auditor's specific facts hold, but the load-bearing headline ("no live metric pipeline exists") is refuted by source, and one third of the cited evidence (the Grafana dashboards) actually contradicts the claim.

CONFIRMED sub-facts:
- infrastructure/observability/otel-collector.yaml:29 exports traces to tempo.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local:4317 and :33 logs to loki.letbuyy-observability.svc.cluster.local:3100. `rg -l "^kind: (Deployment|Service|StatefulSet|DaemonSet)"` excluding docs/archive and .claude/worktrees returns nothing; `rg -ln "prometheus|grafana|tempo|loki" infrastructure/cloudflare/ .github/workflows/` returns nothing. No k8s/helm topology and no Prometheus/Grafana provisioning exist.
- No runtime is an OTLP producer. Looping over services/*/package.json and apps/*/package.json for "@opentelemetry" yields zero hits (the only hits are tools/central-intelligence/package.json:30-35, a dev tool). No /metrics route, no prom-client, no span creation anywhere in services/ or apps/. So otel-collector.yaml has no producer AND no destination — it is fully inert.
- prometheus-rules.yaml:5,14,23 alert on http_server_errors_total, http_requests_total, letbuyy_queue_dead_letter_total, letbuyy_deployment_health_gate_failed_total. Repo-wide grep finds those four strings ONLY in that file and in narrative audit .md files — zero emitters. Emitted names are dot-separated: services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:166 'letbuyy.runtime.request', services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:604 'letbuyy.replay.lag.seconds', services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:318,342. Confirmed name mismatch.
- config/runtime/observability-platform.json:22,27,32,37 set mustUseLiveTelemetry:true; grep shows the field has NO consumer anywhere (it is declarative only, so no gate turns green because of it — slightly weaker than implied).

REFUTING evidence the auditor missed:
1. A real metric pipeline exists in source and config. Cloudflare Analytics Engine bindings RUNTIME_TELEMETRY are declared for staging AND production in all four Workers (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:211-213 and :340-342; services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:216-218,330-332; services/webhook-service/wrangler.toml:61-63,100-102; services/mobile-bff/wrangler.toml:79-81,127-129). On the live request path the gateway writes an AE data point (observability.ts:165-192) and, in the same handler, inserts a Postgres row via ObservabilityExecutionAdapter.recordRuntimeMetric (observability.ts:268-302 -> services/api-gateway/src/execution/observability-execution.adapter.ts:125-143 inserting into runtime_telemetry_metrics). queue-workers does the same at services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:292-315. The table is a real migration (database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql, 080_partition_runtime_telemetry_metrics.sql). There is even an in-band alert path: services/api-gateway/src/lib/runtime-alerting.ts:179-191 records the metric and enqueues to a Cloudflare queue.
2. The Grafana dashboards are the ONE correctly-wired artifact, not dead config. grafana-runtime-dashboard.json:19 and :31 and grafana-queue-dashboard.json query rawSql against runtime_telemetry_metrics for exactly the metric names the code emits (letbuyy.runtime.request, letbuyy.queue.delivery.batch, letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total, letbuyy.replay.lag.seconds), and dashboard line 19's dimensions->>'server_error_count' is genuinely written at observability.ts:284. Only the Grafana instance + 'supabase-ops' datasource is unprovisioned. Lumping the dashboards into "alerts on metrics nothing emits" is inaccurate.
3. The --execute path fails closed. infrastructure/scripts/observability/verify-observability-rollout.mjs:63-65 asserts PROMETHEUS_URL / GRAFANA_URL / OTEL_COLLECTOR_HEALTH_URL and then probes /-/ready, /api/v1/rules, /api/health. config/runtime/live-production-certification.json:5 declares certificationMode "credential-gated-plan-ready" and :17 lists "Prometheus, Grafana, and OpenTelemetry health endpoints" as an executeRequires precondition — a partially honest not-yet-provisioned marker, which the claim's "expected state" says is the acceptable alternative.

STRENGTHENING evidence the auditor missed (raises the false-assurance part): verify-observability-rollout.mjs:32-57 asserts only string fragments and JSON structure, then prints "observability verification passed"; it is invoked in plan mode by infrastructure/scripts/operations/phase4-ultimate-validate.mjs:53, which runs in .github/workflows/staging-runtime-deployment.yml:70 and .github/workflows/production-rollout.yml:133 (the `if: !inputs.execute` branch). And every runtime manifest asserts a capability that does not exist: services/api-gateway/runtime.manifest.json:111 (and the identical line in queue-workers:94, webhook-service:91, mobile-bff:83, storefront-runtime:98, merchant-control-plane:95, marketing-site:94, platform-console:92) states "Emits correlated runtime telemetry to the OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, Tempo, Loki, and Grafana stack declared in infrastructure/observability" — no runtime emits OTLP or Prometheus at all.

Severity: the real defect is that two dead artifacts plus false manifest assertions plus a file-existence gate create documented-but-nonexistent alerting. It is a governance/false-assurance defect with a bounded blast radius, not a money/tenancy/security correctness failure, and real telemetry (AE + Postgres + queue alert enqueue) does flow on the request path. S2, not S1. Evidence state should be VERIFIED-SOURCE (the decisive facts — absence of any OTLP producer, dot-named emitters, DB inserts — are source facts), not VERIFIED-CONFIG.

**OVERSTATED** — Observability writes are the dominant Postgres load: every API request costs roughly 4-6 statements on the single source of truth (S1, VERIFIED-SOURCE), saturating a max=5 connection pool and causing checkout latency.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Every gateway request performs unconditional telemetry writes against the single Postgres. `observability` is mounted globally (index.ts:655) and always inserts one `runtime_telemetry_metrics` row (observability.ts:265-302 -> observability-execution.adapter.ts:125-143), plus one `worker_request_logs` row for everything except anonymous storefront GET/HEAD, which is sampled at 2% (observability.ts:338-381, :199-204; wrangler.toml:61). Migration 131's AFTER-ROW mirror trigger (131:112-122) doubles each write: a `SELECT` on `retention_policies` plus a shadow `INSERT` (131:18-53) carrying an FK check to `sites` and 3-index maintenance (079:489-503); the policies are registered (079:517-524, 080:48-50) so the early-return does not apply, and no later migration disables it. Net cost is roughly 6 DB statements for a typical recorded request and 3 for a sampled-out anonymous storefront read, for data Analytics Engine already receives at zero DB cost (observability.ts:254-263; RUNTIME_TELEMETRY binding in wrangler.toml:212,341). Two corrections to the original claim: (1) these inserts use supabase-js/PostgREST over HTTP (lib/supabase.ts:13-19 -> supabase-adapter/index.ts:34), NOT the `postgres` client whose pool is capped at max=5 (pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:41,52; used only by tenant-transaction.ts and storefront.ts), so the "max=5 pool saturation" mechanism does not apply — contention is at shared Postgres write capacity; (2) they run in `waitUntil` after the response, so they add no in-band latency to the generating request. "Dominant Postgres load" is INFERRED, not verified: authenticated requests also perform session/`users_custom`/rbac/`store-context` reads and an `audit_logs` insert per request (auth.ts:133-142, rbac.ts:250-388, store-context.ts:114-241), and no load measurement exists in-repo.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S2 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: The structural core is CONFIRMED, but the headline ("dominant load") and the failure mechanism (max=5 pool saturation) are not earned.

CONFIRMED by source:
- `services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:655` mounts `app.use('*', observability)` globally, before `basePath('/v1')` routing, so it runs on every request.
- `observability.ts:265-302` unconditionally calls `recordRuntimeMetric` (the only exclusion is the internal GraphQL REST-shim subrequest guard at :228, which prevents double counting). `observability-execution.adapter.ts:125-143` is a plain `.from('runtime_telemetry_metrics').insert(...)`.
- `observability.ts:338-381` inserts `worker_request_logs` whenever `shouldRecordRequestLog` records; `:199-204` returns `record:true` for errors/auth/payment/admin/authenticated/any non-storefront path, so only anonymous storefront GET/HEAD is sampled (default 0.02, `wrangler.toml:61,152,281`). This is exercised by a real behavioral test: `services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/observability.test.ts:29-70` asserts the record/sample decisions.
- Trigger amplification is real. `131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112-122` puts AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE FOR EACH ROW triggers on both tables; the function (`:18-53`) does a `SELECT` from `retention_policies` plus a dynamic `INSERT` into the shadow. Both policies are actually registered (`079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:517-524`, `080_partition_runtime_telemetry_metrics.sql:48-50`) with default `lifecycle_mode='partition-shadow'` (`000_baseline.sql` retention_policies check constraint), so the early-return at :25 does NOT apply. Column sets match (`000_baseline.sql:13224-13245` vs `079:465-487`), so the positional shadow INSERT succeeds. No later migration drops or disables these triggers (grep over all 197 migrations found only `179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql`, which FIXES the shadow for outbox and independently attests the trigger fires on the live path: "broke EVERY outbox write").
- Queue path: `services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:292-315` writes 2 telemetry rows per batch (one statement, two rows, two trigger firings).
- Analytics Engine genuinely receives the same point at zero DB cost: `observability.ts:254-263`, binding `RUNTIME_TELEMETRY` present in `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:212,341` (VERIFIED-CONFIG, not proof of deployment).

REFUTED — the connection-pool mechanism. The telemetry writes go through `getSupabaseAdmin` (`observability.ts:265`) -> `services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13-19` -> `createSupabaseServiceClient` -> `packages/infrastructure/supabase-adapter/index.ts:34` = supabase-js `createClient`, i.e. PostgREST over HTTP. The `max: 5` pool at `packages/infrastructure/pooled-sql-adapter/index.ts:41,52` belongs to the `postgres` client, whose only consumers are `services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:20-24` (max 3) and `services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:647-691`. Telemetry inserts never touch that pool, so "saturates connections in a pool capped at max=5 per isolate" is wrong as stated; contention is at shared Postgres write capacity / PostgREST, not that pool.

OVERSTATED — "dominant Postgres load". Not established from source. Authenticated requests already spend multiple statements on non-telemetry DB work per request: `middleware/auth.ts:133-142` (session validation + `users_custom`), `middleware/rbac.ts:250-317` (`employees`, `employee_roles`, `employee_permissions`, `break_glass_grants`), `middleware/store-context.ts:114-241` (`sites`, `subscriptions`, `site_domains`), plus `rbac.ts:388` audit_logs insert. There is no load measurement in-repo. Rate limiting is Redis/KV, not DB (`middleware/rateLimit.ts:6-9,73`), so that does not offset.

Also imprecise: the writes are dispatched via `c.executionCtx.waitUntil` (:268, :304, :349), i.e. after the response, so they add no in-band latency to the generating request; impact is indirect via shared DB capacity. Statement count is 6 for a typical recorded request and 3 for a sampled-out anonymous storefront read, not uniformly "4-6".

UNDERSTATED in one respect: each shadow INSERT also pays an FK check to `sites` (`079:489-490`) and maintains 3 indexes on the partitioned parent (`079:495-503`), and no runtime caller of `ensure_next_hot_table_partitions` exists outside migrations/scripts (grep found only `scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs`), so whether current-month partitions exist is UNKNOWN — if they do not, the doubled write buys nothing.

**CONFIRMED** — The live observability tables have no retention and are never pruned — worker_request_logs, runtime_telemetry_metrics and operation_metrics are plain heap tables with retention defined only for the shadow partitioned copies. S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE.
- CORRECTED CLAIM: No enforced retention exists for the live observability tables, and the shadow mechanism does not compensate. `worker_request_logs` (000_baseline.sql:13224), `runtime_telemetry_metrics` (:11603) and `operation_metrics` (:9939) are plain heap tables with no retention function, no prune job, and no DELETE anywhere in database/, services/ or scripts/. Retention exists only as metadata plus `detach_expired_hot_table_partitions()` (079:265) acting on the `*_partitioned` shadows, driven solely by scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:31-34 and .github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml; 079:1-2 states the live rename-swap is intentionally not performed.
