Three aggravating facts the original claim did not establish: (1) migration 131:110-141 installs AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE mirror triggers on the live tables, so every row is physically written twice (live heap + shadow partition); (2) `detach_expired_hot_table_partitions` only DETACHes and marks partitions 'cleanup-required' (079:295-302) — nothing drops them, and docs/governance/hot-table-retention-governance.md confirms physical cleanup is a manual operations action, so even shadow retention reclaims zero disk automatically; (3) `operation_metrics` is excluded from the `retention_policies_target_table_check` allowlist (079:24) and can never receive a policy, as are `runtime_telemetry_spans` and `runtime_structured_logs`. Two corrections to the original risk narrative: `worker_request_logs` IS sampled at 2% for unauthenticated storefront GET/HEAD reads (observability.ts:195-212, :41, wrangler.toml:61), but `runtime_telemetry_metrics` receives an unsampled insert on every gateway request (observability.ts:268-269 under app.use('*', observability) at index.ts:655), so total per-request row cost is undiminished; and the "matter of months" time-to-exhaustion is INFERRED, not source-established. The proven, defensible core is the total absence of any enforcement mechanism on the live tables that hold the highest write volume on the same Postgres as the canonical ledger.
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S1 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: I read the cited lines and searched for every refutation vector.

(1) Baseline schema confirmed. `database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:13224` (worker_request_logs), `:11603` (runtime_telemetry_metrics), `:9939` (operation_metrics) are plain heap tables. I enumerated EVERY baseline reference to the three names: only CREATE TABLE, pkey (`:16487`, `:15474`, `:14466`), btree indexes (`:19232`, `:19239`, `:19246`, `:18375`, `:18382`, `:17591`, `:17598`), an `update_updated_at` trigger (`:20672`, `:20350`, `:20007`), a site_id FK, and a `service_role` RLS policy (`:28039`, `:26442`, `:25198`). No retention/prune function.

(2) Retention is shadow-only, confirmed. `079_partition_worker_request_logs.sql:1-2` states the live rename-swap is intentionally not performed. `:265` `detach_expired_hot_table_partitions` iterates `partition_lifecycle_partitions` joined to `retention_policies` and issues `ALTER TABLE public.%I DETACH PARTITION` against `v_partition.shadow_table` only (`:293-303`). `:476` seeds `upsert_retention_policy('worker_request_logs','worker_request_logs_partitioned',45,...)`. `backfill_hot_table_partition_shadow` is commented "without mutating the live table" (`079` tail).

(3) The only executable retention driver is shadow-only. `scripts/migration/run-hot-table-partition-maintenance.mjs:31-34` runs exactly `ensure_next_hot_table_partitions` + `detach_expired_hot_table_partitions`; `.github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml` is its sole scheduler. `079:454` pg_cron path schedules the same two functions.

(4) Refutation attempts, all failed. No later migration (073-195) adds prune/purge/TRUNCATE/cron for these tables (`grep -rn -i "prune|purge|cleanup_old|delete_old|vacuum|pg_cron|cron.schedule" --include='*.sql' database/supabase/migrations/` returns only 079's cron block and unrelated GDPR notes). No `.delete()` against them in `services/` (`grep -rn "from('worker_request_logs')|from('runtime_telemetry_metrics')|from('operation_metrics')"` yields only 4 inserts in `services/api-gateway/src/execution/observability-execution.adapter.ts:86,98,108,127` and one read in `routes/operations.ts:389`). `services/queue-workers/wrangler.toml:17` cron `17 */6 * * *` drives five scheduled jobs (`src/index.ts:638,653,669,684,700`) — inventory sweeper, money-ledger reconciliation, executive analytics, postmortem reminder, compliance audit export — none prune telemetry. `services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:25` `crons = []`. `retentionPolicySnapshot()` (`src/lib/internal-compliance.ts:111`) is a read-only GDPR/DPDP table list (`packages/shared-types/src/index.ts:513-538`) covering customer/merchant PII, not telemetry.

(5) The write path is live and unsampled for the largest table. `services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:655` mounts `app.use('*', observability)`. `middleware/observability.ts:268-269` calls `recordRuntimeMetric` on EVERY request with no sampling gate. `:338-350` gates `recordRequestLog` behind `shouldRecordRequestLog`, which returns record:true at sampleRate 1 for errors, auth, payment, admin/operations, any authenticated request, and anything that is not a public storefront read (`:199-204`); only unauthenticated storefront GET/HEAD is sampled at 0.02 (`:41`, `wrangler.toml:61,152,281`).

(6) Three facts the auditor understated. (a) `131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:110-141` installs AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE mirror triggers on all five live tables, so every live row is physically written twice. (b) `079:295-302` only DETACHes and sets status 'cleanup-required'; nothing in the repo drops detached partitions and `docs/governance/hot-table-retention-governance.md` states physical cleanup is a manual operations action — so even the shadow retention reclaims no disk automatically. (c) `079:24` `retention_policies_target_table_check` omits `operation_metrics`, so it can never receive a policy; `runtime_telemetry_spans` and `runtime_structured_logs` are likewise uncovered by any migration.

(7) Test check. `tests/performance/partition-retention.test.mjs` is regex-over-migration-text, not behavioral — it cannot be cited as VERIFIED-TEST. At `:118-122` it asserts the combined partition migrations contain no DROP TABLE / RENAME TO / TRUNCATE, which independently corroborates that no live cutover or cleanup exists.

Corrections to the claim's risk narrative: the phrase "100% request logging on all non-storefront traffic" is right for worker_request_logs but omits that public storefront reads ARE sampled at 2%; this does not reduce total row volume because runtime_telemetry_metrics is unsampled per request. The "matter of months" time-to-failure is INFERRED (no capacity model in repo), not source-established. The structural claim — zero enforced retention on the live tables — is fully VERIFIED-SOURCE. Severity S1 holds: it is a scale/launch blocker on the same Postgres as the canonical ledger, but it is a gradual capacity failure with operational escape hatches (manual DELETE, `set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false)` at `131:59`, disk scaling) and is a knowingly deferred, human-gated item in governance — not S0 active breakage.

### Inventory notes
TELEMETRY EMISSION INVENTORY (deployed runtimes only)

A. Structured logs -> console -> Cloudflare Workers Logs (SHIPPED, sampled)
- services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:80-92 writeStructuredRuntimeLog(); level from status_code; JSON {level, service:'letbuyy-api', request_id, trace_id, site_id, route, method, status_code, latency_ms, user_id, auth_source}
- services/api-gateway/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts:214-221, 263-270, 333-341, 628-640, 755-765 structured 503 reason logs (redis_pipeline_failed, redis_unavailable, kv_unavailable)
- services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:426 writeQueueLog(level, fields)
- services/webhook-service/src/index.ts: 31 console.* calls, mostly UNSTRUCTURED string+object (e.g. :735 "[Razorpay] Invalid signature", :778, :1364); only :442 is JSON.stringify
- Gateway console.* count = 50, queue-workers = 2, webhook-service = 31
- Sink config: all 4 wrangler.toml set [observability] enabled=true head_sampling_rate=0.1 and [observability.logs] head_sampling_rate=0.1 invocation_logs=true -> 90% of invocations produce NO retained log

B. Cloudflare Analytics Engine data points (EMITTED; binding declared; nothing in repo reads them)
- packages/infrastructure/runtime-telemetry-adapter/index.ts:72-79 writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint(binding, input) -> no-op returning false when binding is undefined
- services/api-gateway/src/middleware/observability.ts:149-193 metric 'letbuyy.runtime.request' (blobs: path, method, statusClass, authSource, errorCode, CF-IPCountry, CF-Ray; doubles: 1, latencyMs, status, is5xx, is4xx, isOk)
- services/api-gateway/src/index.ts:603-621 metric 'letbuyy.replay.lag.seconds' (cron-driven)
- services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:317-339 'letbuyy.queue.delivery.batch' (doubles: count, completed, deadLettered, lagP95Ms, lagMaxMs)
- services/queue-workers/src/index.ts:341-360 'letbuyy.queue.dead_letter.total'
- Bindings: api-gateway wrangler.toml:211-213 (staging) / :340-342 (production); queue-workers :216-218 / :330-332; webhook-service :61-63 / :100-102; mobile-bff :79-81 / :127-129
- DECLARED-BUT-NEVER-CALLED: webhook-service (no writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint import at all), mobile-bff (services/mobile-bff/src/index.ts:16 declares the type only)

C. Postgres-backed telemetry (EMITTED, on the critical single Postgres)

### Unknowns
- Whether the Cloudflare account is on the Workers Paid plan. Analytics Engine `writeDataPoint` requires Workers Paid; several wrangler comments say 'Beta free-tier' (services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml:22, :235). If the account is on the free plan, every `writeRuntimeTelemetryDataPoint` call is silently dropped and the only surviving telemetry is Workers Logs plus the Postgres rows. Settling this needs the Cloudflare account plan (dashboard or `GET /accounts/{id}/subscriptions`), which I have no access to.
- Whether the `letbuyy-production-telemetry` Analytics Engine dataset actually contains data points. Requires POST /accounts/{acct}/analytics_engine/sql with an account token.
- Whether the production Supabase project has PITR enabled. `config/runtime/database-recovery-policy.json:11-12` says human-required-paid-tier; only the Supabase dashboard or Management API can confirm the current state.
- Whether `live-production-certification.yml` has ever been dispatched, and with what outcome. Requires the GitHub Actions run history for the workflow (`gh run list --workflow=live-production-certification.yml`), which is out of scope for a read-only repo audit.
- Whether the GitHub environment secrets PROMETHEUS_URL, GRAFANA_URL, GRAFANA_TOKEN and OTEL_COLLECTOR_HEALTH_URL are populated. If they are, some Prometheus/Grafana/OTel stack exists outside this repository and my conclusion that the observability capability group can never be ready would be wrong. Requires the repository's environment secret inventory.
- Whether `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` / `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` are set as Worker secrets in production. The wrangler comment at :250-259 states they are not, but Worker secrets are set out-of-band; `wrangler secret list --env production` would settle it and would change the KV write-quota risk materially.
- Actual production request volume, so the KV write-quota headroom, the Postgres write amplification, and the observability table growth rate can be quantified rather than reasoned about. Requires Cloudflare Analytics or Workers Logs.
- Whether `deployment_health_gate_results` and `operational_certification_reports` contain any rows in the production database. This determines whether the single SLO has ever had a denominator. Requires a read-only query against the production Postgres.
- Whether the partition-shadow mirror triggers are currently ENABLED in the production database. Migration 131 creates them enabled, and `set_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror_enabled(false)` may have been called out-of-band; `SELECT tgenabled FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname LIKE 'mirror_%'` would settle the write-amplification magnitude.
- Current row counts and on-disk size of `worker_request_logs`, `runtime_telemetry_metrics` and their shadow copies, needed to convert the unbounded-growth finding from a structural risk into a dated runway.
- Whether the four Grafana dashboard JSONs have ever been imported into any Grafana instance, and whether a datasource with uid `supabase-ops` exists there. Nothing in the repository provisions either.
- Whether `production-deploy.yml` has run recently enough that a usable encrypted backup artifact currently exists — the 7-day retention means the answer changes weekly. Requires the Actions artifact list.
- Whether Cloudflare Logpush is configured on the account to ship Workers Logs to durable storage. The repository configures only `[observability]`, but Logpush is an account-level setting that would materially change the log-retention finding.
- Whether a human on-call rotation exists outside the repository. `oncall_schedules` is read at services/queue-workers/src/incident-command.ts:80-92, so the schema supports it, but no schedule data or contact appears in the repo and no runbook names an escalation path.
- Whether the deployed production Worker matches this SHA. `config/runtime/deployment-lineage-manifest.json` is explicitly a governance contract, not live lineage; only Cloudflare deployment history could confirm which version is serving api.letbuyy.com.

## DIMENSION: governance-drift

### Narrative summary
The LetBuyy governance corpus (Constitution DOC 01, AGENTS_V2 DOC 02, Canonical Systems DOC 03, Legacy Elimination DOC 04, Foundation Roadmap DOC 05, Scorecard DOC 07) is internally coherent and its winner registry has been substantially executed in source — but the single most consequential invariant it declares is still unimplemented at runtime, and the workspace has accreted a new class of unadjudicated topology since the registry was written. What is genuinely done: the rejected CQRS/saga/projection/extension-host/runtime-composition package cluster is gone from `packages/`; `legacy/firebase`, k8s/Helm, the 16 mission specs and the 60+ root governance docs are archived (root .md count is 9); `production-topology.json` is honest (11 active / 6 reserved / 4 frozen, no fabricated `environment:"production"`); all six reserved services are manifest-only (2 files each); the branch-lag meta-blocker is closed (`main` == `stepup/main` == `origin/main` == 5af69e0c, lag 0); migration 162 sets `commission_bps` default 0; migration 163 re-scopes the six `USING(true)` PUBLIC policies to `service_role` plus GUC policies and REVOKEs anon/authenticated on `super_admin_allowlist`; migration 164 creates a NOBYPASSRLS `app_tenant` role with FORCE-RLS + per-tx GUC policies; migrations 139/165 give the canonical ledger append-only enforcement by trigger AND `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE`; migration 168 rewrote `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger` and `complete_order_refund` to post `post_settlement_ledger_transaction` in the same transaction; the stale-reservation sweeper and money-ledger reconciliation refresher are wired to the queue-workers `scheduled()` handler on a 6-hourly cron; the checkout handoff is cart-digest-bound and verified server-side; the design system has one DTCG token source generating the Dart bridge; React sections are frozen behind an allowlist validator; `letbuyy.store` survives only as bundle identifiers; `razorpayx` survives only as three baseline enum values. What is NOT done, and is the decisive gap: ART-08's "the gateway stops connecting as blanket `service_role`" is unimplemented — `withTenantTx`/`withResolvedTenantTx` (the only code that issues `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` + the tenant GUC) has zero route callers, while 114 non-test gateway files import `getSupabaseAdmin`, which returns `createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient(createSupabaseServiceClient(env))` — a service_role (BYPASSRLS) client wrapped by a 124-line app-layer query-shape lint. Migration 164's FORCE+GUC policies therefore never engage on the live path; L3 is DDL-real and runtime-dead, exactly the ISSUE-009 condition the Constitution declared must end, and the gate is green over it because `no-blanket-service-role-validator` only asserts the helper exists and exempts `services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts`. Second, the platform has accreted 53,254 non-test LOC across 19 `packages/lcis-*` roots plus an unregistered runtime root `services/lcis-api` (added 2026-08-15, one day before this SHA) that appears in no governance document, no runtime manifest, and none of `canonicalServiceRoots`/`reservedServiceRoots`/`forbiddenRuntimeRoots`; no validator enumerates runtime roots to enforce ARCH-R2 membership, and `package-ownership-validator.mjs` auto-admits any new `packages/*` root into the governed set. Third, the partition-shadow mirror that DOC 04 LEG-057/068 ordered disabled is still live — migration 179 (August 2026) exists only because the mirror trigger broke every `outbox_events` write — and the monthly `hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml` cron still executes DDL against `PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING` with `--execute`. Fourth, internal service-to-service auth fails OPEN: `isLegacyAcceptEnabled` defaults true and `INTERNAL_AUTH_LEGACY_ACCEPT` is unset in wrangler.toml, so the shared `INTERNAL_API_SECRET` present in ten-plus runtimes remains an accepted credential. Fifth, the evidence base is thinner than it reads: 221 of 239 files under `tests/` and 68 of 82 gateway tests use `readFileSync` against source text rather than executing behavior, so most "parity/security/contract" suites are structural lints; the genuinely behavioral proofs are `tests/executable/*` (Docker Postgres, wired into `test:behavioral` in CI) and the money I1–I8 invariant runner, which is a real instrument but requires `STAGING_DB_URL` and is in no workflow. Known drift confirmed: AGENTS.md MIG-R2 says max migration 161 / next 162 while the tree carries 197 (126 forwards, 48 rollbacks, no duplicate prefixes); 12 forwards ≥138 (138, 140, 157, 170, 175, 179, 182, 183, 184, 186, 187, 194) have no paired DOWN and no rollback-pairing validator exists despite ART-02 naming it an ADD-bucket gate; AGENTS.md Appendix B still says DOC 01/DOC 03 do not physically exist; DOC 01/03/04/05/07 still assert the 30-commit lag in the present tense. Open R-15 duplicates: the storefront compose fork (`resolveStorefrontTemplate`, 308 LOC, five live route pages) versus the engine's `composeStorefrontTheme`; `theme_editor_drafts` alongside `theme_instance_drafts`/`theme_draft_patches`; gateway `routes/auth.ts` (1,499 LOC) plus the 18-file `identity-foundation/` shadow plus six identity micro-packages; four compat ledgers with live TS writers; `users_custom`, `payment_transactions`, `sessions_v2`, `orders.store_id` (sync trigger still installed). Net: the foundation's chosen invariants are correct and unusually well-articulated; the implementation is real but not yet sufficient — tenancy, event atomicity, deployment/observability floor, and the proof layer are the four things standing between this repository and its own ratified envelope.


### Findings (26)


**[S0] ART-08's decisive change is unimplemented: RLS+GUC is DDL-real and runtime-dead; the gateway still runs every query as service_role**  ·  domain=tenancy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `withTenantTx` (the only code path that issues `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` and the per-transaction tenant GUC) is called from exactly one place, `withResolvedTenantTx` in tenant-context.ts, which itself has zero non-test callers. Every gateway data path uses `getSupabaseAdmin`, which returns `createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient(createSupabaseServiceClient(env))` — a Supabase REST client authenticated with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY (BYPASSRLS), wrapped only by a 124-line app-layer query-shape lint that requires a scope predicate but cannot detect a wrong site_id. 114 non-test gateway files import getSupabaseAdmin; ~1,300 `.from('...')` calls exist in services/api-gateway/src/routes alone. Migration 164's `app_tenant` role, FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY and per-tx GUC policies exist but never engage, because BYPASSRLS roles are exempt from FORCE.
- EXPECTED: ART-08 / R-1 / R-2: tenant data runs under a non-BYPASSRLS `app_tenant` pooled connection with `SET LOCAL app.site_id` from a validated claim on every tenant table; unset or wrong GUC yields zero rows; `service_role` is a narrow audited break-glass hatch only. DOC 05 P2 exit-gate requires RLS engaged before P3/P8.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:13; services/api-gateway/src/lib/supabase.ts:14; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:29; services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67; services/api-gateway/src/tenant/tenant-scoped.ts:7; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:11; database/supabase/migrations/164_d4_tenant_role_guc.sql:64; grep -rn 'withResolvedTenantTx' --include='*.ts' services apps packages | grep -v node_modules | grep -v __tests__  -> only the definition; grep -rln 'getSupabaseAdmin' --include='*.ts' services/api-gateway/src | grep -v __tests__ | wc -l -> 114
- RISK: The database backstop the Constitution calls load-bearing does not exist on the live path. A single application-layer miss in any of ~1,300 query sites leaks or writes cross-tenant data with no DB-level deny. This is the platform's largest single structural exposure and it blocks the DOC 05 P2 exit-gate, which gates P3 and P8.
- RECOMMENDATION: Route tenant reads/writes through `withResolvedTenantTx` on the pooled `app_tenant` connection, starting with the highest-blast tenant tables (orders, store_customers, products, sites, payments). Add a validator that fails when a file imports `getSupabaseAdmin` and touches a table present in `tenant-tables.registry.ts` without a tenant-transaction boundary, and burn that count down on a ratchet. Do not claim ART-08 satisfied on migration 164 alone.
- CAPACITY: Directly capacity-relevant in the wrong direction: `withTenantTx` opens a pooled SQL connection with maxConnections 3 per invocation and wraps every call in an explicit transaction. Migrating 1,300 call sites onto it without Hyperdrive pool sizing work will convert a stateless PostgREST workload into a connection-bound transactional one. Sizing this is a prerequisite of the 10k-merchant envelope, not an afterthought.

**[S1] The partition-shadow mirror is live in production and has already caused an outbox outage class; DOC 04 ordered it disabled**  ·  domain=data  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: Migration 131 attaches `mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow()` triggers to `worker_request_logs`, `runtime_telemetry_metrics`, `outbox_events`, `platform_queue_jobs` and `webhook_deliveries`. No later migration drops them (`grep mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow` matches only 131 and 179). Migration 179 exists solely because the shadow table lacked `published_at`, which made the positional `INSERT ... SELECT (NEW).*` raise SQLSTATE 42601 and, in its own words, 'broke EVERY outbox write — including theme release publishing via publish_theme_release()'.
- EXPECTED: DOC 04 LEG-057/LEG-068: keep the shadow tables as a seam, disable the mirror (the cron and the trigger). Constitution §10 item 4 and R-17 forbid partitioning-now within the envelope; the partition helper seam is to exist disabled.
- EVIDENCE: database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:112; database/supabase/migrations/131_hot_table_partition_shadow_mirror.sql:124; database/supabase/migrations/179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql:1; database/supabase/migrations/179_outbox_partition_shadow_published_at.sql:5; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_LEGACY_ELIMINATION_MASTER_PLAN.md:186; GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_LEGACY_ELIMINATION_MASTER_PLAN.md:202
- RISK: Every write to five hot tables is doubled, including the transactional outbox — the exact table whose reliability the events architecture depends on. Any future column added to a mirrored table silently breaks all writes to it until someone patches the shadow. This is a live availability defect and an active violation of the never-build list.
- RECOMMENDATION: Author a paired migration that drops the five mirror triggers and keeps the shadow tables empty, per LEG-057. Add a validator asserting no `mirror_hot_table_partition_shadow` trigger is attached. Treat any future need to extend the shadow as evidence the seam should be removed, not patched.
- CAPACITY: Write amplification of 2x on the five highest-volume tables (outbox, queue jobs, webhook deliveries, request logs, telemetry). At the top of the envelope (~30k orders/month plus telemetry) this is the largest avoidable write load on the single Postgres and directly consumes headroom the constitution says should be trivial.

**[S1] A monthly cron still executes partition DDL against the production database**  ·  domain=deploy  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-CONFIG  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `.github/workflows/hot-table-partition-maintenance.yml` retains `schedule: cron "15 2 1 * *"`. The job computes `EXECUTE_MAINTENANCE: ${{ github.event_name == 'schedule' || inputs.execute == true }}`, so the scheduled run always passes `--execute`, and `SUPABASE_DB_URL` resolves to `secrets.PRODUCTION_DB_CONNECTION_STRING` when no `environment` input is supplied.

- RECOMMENDATION: Delete the `schedule:` block; force `execute` to require explicit dispatch input; add the workflow to the deploy-matrix validator's set of workflows that may touch a production connection string.

**[S1] Internal service-to-service authentication fails OPEN by default on a secret shared across ten-plus runtimes**  ·  domain=security  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `isLegacyAcceptEnabled(env)` returns true unless `INTERNAL_AUTH_LEGACY_ACCEPT` is explicitly set to false/0/off/no/disabled; the comment states 'Default ON — unset or empty means legacy single-secret acceptance is active'. `INTERNAL_AUTH_LEGACY_ACCEPT` does not appear in services/api-gateway/wrangler.toml. `INTERNAL_API_SECRET` is referenced by api-gateway, webhook-service, identity-platform, storefront-runtime, merchant-control-plane, theme-marketplace, app-marketplace and platform-operations-portal. The per-caller keyring (`INTERNAL_API_KEYRING`) exists but is additive, not exclusive.
- EXPECTED: Philosophy §3.5 and the ART-05 rationale (a shared symmetric secret makes the verification key the forgery key across trust domains) applied to the internal perimeter; SKIP-W4-LIVE-SECRET-ROTATION and SKIP-W4-MTLS-CF-ACCESS name the human seams but do not authorise a fail-open default.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:41; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:43; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:47; services/api-gateway/src/lib/internal-auth.ts:150; grep -rln INTERNAL_API_SECRET --include='*.ts' apps services packages -> 10 runtimes/apps; SKIPPED_HUMAN_DECISIONS.md:29
- RISK: Any compromised runtime — including the frozen `app-marketplace`, `theme-marketplace` and `platform-operations-portal` apps that still carry the secret in their API clients — can forge internal identity assertions to the gateway's internal routes. A fail-open default contradicts the platform's stated fail-closed posture.
- RECOMMENDATION: Invert the default (`isLegacyAcceptEnabled` returns false unless explicitly enabled), set `INTERNAL_AUTH_LEGACY_ACCEPT="true"` only in the dev/staging var blocks with a dated sunset, and remove `INTERNAL_API_SECRET` from the frozen apps' clients. Validator-assert that production var blocks never enable legacy accept.

**[S1] The events backbone is still a post-commit dual write at 40 TypeScript call sites (ART-19 / R-16)**  ·  domain=queue  ·  evidence_state=VERIFIED-SOURCE  ·  authority=implementation
- CURRENT: `EventExecutionAdapter.emitDomainEvent` performs a standalone Supabase REST `.from('outbox_events').upsert(...)` after the domain write has already committed, and returns null (silently) when the outbox table is reported missing from the schema cache. 40 non-test call sites invoke it across api-gateway routes, execution adapters and lib helpers. The only same-transaction outbox write in the tree is inside the SQL RPC added by migration 166.
- EXPECTED: ART-19, Philosophy §3.4, R-16: a domain write and its event row commit in the SAME transaction; the dual-write must be made atomic (a SECURITY DEFINER RPC writing domain row plus outbox row in one tx), with a missing outbox table treated as fatal.
- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:48; services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:60; services/api-gateway/src/execution/event-execution.adapter.ts:80; database/supabase/migrations/166_d6_checkout_handoff_inventory.sql:276; grep -rn 'emitDomainEvent(' --include='*.ts' services packages apps | non-test -> 40
- RISK: Money and lifecycle events are lost on any crash between the domain commit and the outbox write, and are silently dropped when the table is unavailable. Order-paid, refund, payout and WhatsApp COD-confirm side effects all ride this path.
- RECOMMENDATION: Move outbox emission into the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs that already own each domain write (following the migration 166 pattern), or introduce a single `emit_domain_event` RPC called inside the same transaction. Remove the null-on-missing-table swallow. Add a validator banning `.from('outbox_events')` outside the RPC layer.
- CAPACITY: Each emit is an extra REST round-trip per domain mutation; folding it into the owning RPC removes one network hop per order/payment/refund and reduces the connection footprint at envelope volume.

- CURRENT: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:88 and routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:307 both resolve the checkout signing key as `CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET || AUTH_SECRET || STORE_ACCESS_SIGNING_KEY`; routes/media.ts:60 uses `MEDIA_UPLOAD_SIGNING_SECRET || AUTH_SECRET`; routes/export.ts:174 uses `INTERNAL_API_SECRET || CUSTOMER_SESSION_SECRET || AUTH_SECRET`; routes/internal-console.ts:441 uses AUTH_SECRET directly.

- EVIDENCE: services/api-gateway/src/routes/checkout.ts:88; services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/cart-routes.ts:307; services/api-gateway/src/routes/media.ts:60; services/api-gateway/src/routes/export.ts:174; services/api-gateway/src/routes/internal-console.ts:441
- RISK: If CHECKOUT_SIGNING_SECRET is not provisioned (SKIP-D6 records it as an open human seam), the checkout handoff silently falls back to AUTH_SECRET, so a leak of the auth secret becomes forgery of checkout handoffs and media uploads. Five trust domains collapse onto one key by default.
- RECOMMENDATION: Make each purpose-specific secret required (fail closed when absent) rather than falling back to AUTH_SECRET; the fallbacks are what make a missing secret invisible.

- CURRENT: `git rev-list --count main..stepup/main` and the reverse both return 0; main, stepup/main, origin/main and origin/stepup/main are all 5af69e0c. `origin/develop` is 342 commits behind main (not diverged) and ci.yml still triggers on it. Yet ART-03 states 'main was ~30 commits behind', DOC 03 §7.1 states the switch fix and 8 waves are 'working-tree-only and UNDEPLOYED', DOC 04 §1 calls the 30-commit lag 'the universal precondition', DOC 05 §5 item 1 says 'nothing is provably in the production DB until P1 clears', and DOC 07 A.1 says 'live readiness is ≈0/10 platform-wide'. - RISK: Every one of these documents is pinned to 5cf9e64e and reads as current law. An agent or reviewer applying them literally will treat resolved blockers as open and open blockers (tenancy runtime, partition mirror, lcis topology) as unnamed. The stale AGENTS.md line actively instructs agents to follow retired V1+Freeze instead of the ratified Constitution.
- RECOMMENDATION: Issue a dated amendment block per document recording what has closed since 5cf9e64e (branch lag, RLS migrations, commission zero, ledger REVOKE, dead-package deletion, archives) and what has NOT (ART-08 runtime, partition mirror, staging collapse, rollback pairing), and delete AGENTS.md:401. Doc correction for the stale facts; ARB acknowledgement for the amendment record.

### Adversarial verdicts (1)


**CONFIRMED** — ART-08's decisive change is unimplemented: RLS+GUC is DDL-real and runtime-dead; the gateway still runs every query as service_role (S0, VERIFIED-SOURCE)
- CORRECTED CLAIM: ART-08's decisive change is unimplemented: the RLS+GUC tenant backstop is DDL-real and runtime-dead, and no gateway data path runs under a non-BYPASSRLS role. `withTenantTx` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15, the only code that issues `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` at :29 and the per-tx GUC at :30) is reachable solely via `withResolvedTenantTx` (tenant-context.ts:67), which has zero non-test callers; no TS anywhere sets `app.site_id`. Instead, 114 non-test gateway files use `getSupabaseAdmin`, which returns a `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` REST client (supabase.ts:14-15; supabase-adapter/index.ts:34) wrapped only by a 124-line query-shape lint that requires a scope predicate, cannot detect a wrong site_id, and — because its proxy intercepts only `from` (tenant-scoped.ts:115) — does not cover the 48 `.rpc(` calls in routes at all; ~1,300 `.from('` sites exist in services/api-gateway/src/routes. `getSupabaseUser`, the only RLS-respecting factory (supabase.ts:24), has zero callers. Four storefront reads (routes/storefront.ts:646, 656, 671, 691) run over the Hyperdrive/DATABASE_URL pooled connection and likewise set neither the role nor the GUC (app_tenant is NOLOGIN, 164:11). Migration 164's app_tenant role, FORCE RLS and GUC policies therefore never engage, for two independent reasons: service_role/postgres carry BYPASSRLS, which overrides FORCE (documented at 196:29-35, and implied by 164:20-22 granting app_tenant membership to service_role), and — decisively and source-provably — migration 163 grants `FOR ALL TO service_role USING (true) WITH CHECK (true)` on orders (163:11), products (:27), product_variants (:39), product_options (:65), sites (:92), subscriptions (:159), billing_history (:172) and super_admin_allowlist (:186), with no later migration dropping them. The only "proofs" of engagement are string-matching: the D4 test regexes source text and the no-blanket-service-role validator uses `.includes()`; the one executable red-team test passes only because its harness performs the `SET ROLE`/`set_config` the gateway never does, and its own control at :263 shows service_role reading rows with the GUC unset. This violates Constitution R-1/R-2 (LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:392-393) on the entire live path and blocks the P2 exit gate that the roadmap makes a hard dependency of P3 and P8 (FOUNDATION_ROADMAP.md:227, :618, :620).
- CORRECTED SEVERITY/STATE: S0 / VERIFIED-SOURCE
- REASONING: Every mechanical assertion reproduces exactly at 5af69e0c, and the three standard refutation angles all fail.

CALL-SITE ABSENCE (verified): `withTenantTx` is defined at services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-transaction.ts:15 and issues `SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant` (:29) + the GUC (:30-31). Its only caller is `withResolvedTenantTx` (services/api-gateway/src/lib/tenant-context.ts:67-73). `rg -n 'withResolvedTenantTx' -g '*.ts' -g '*.mjs' .` returns exactly two hits: the definition at tenant-context.ts:67 and a regex assertion at services/api-gateway/src/__tests__/tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts:42. Zero live callers. `rg -n 'set_current_site_id|app\.site_id' -g '*.ts' services apps packages` returns only the constant (packages/tenant-core/src/index.ts:555) and the error string (tenant-transaction.ts:10) — no runtime code anywhere sets the tenant GUC. Corroborated by the repo's own roadmap: "wire `current_site_id()` to the live path (today **0 live callers** — dead code)" (GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_FOUNDATION_ROADMAP.md:314).

THE LIVE PATH (verified): supabase.ts:14-15 returns `createTenantGuardedSupabaseClient(createSupabaseServiceClient(env))`; that factory authenticates with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (packages/infrastructure/supabase-adapter/index.ts:34). The counts are exact: `rg -l getSupabaseAdmin -g '*.ts' services/api-gateway/src | grep -v __tests__ | wc -l` = 114; `rg -o "\.from\('" -g '*.ts' services/api-gateway/src/routes | wc -l` = 1300; tenant-scoped.ts is 124 lines. Stronger than claimed: `getSupabaseUser` — the only RLS-respecting client factory (supabase.ts:24) — has ZERO non-test callers repo-wide, and the guard proxy only intercepts `from` (tenant-scoped.ts:115), so all 48 `.rpc(` calls in routes bypass L1 entirely.

LATER-MIGRATION CHECK (the classic failure mode) — fails to refute, and instead strengthens the finding. Migration 196 (32 migrations after 164) states in its own header that the tables are owned by `postgres` and "both `postgres` and `service_role` carry BYPASSRLS (pg_roles.rolbypassrls = t), which takes precedence over FORCE… The gateway (service_role via getSupabaseAdmin) … [is] therefore unaffected" (196:29-35). More decisively, migration 163_d2_live_data_leak_rls.sql creates `FOR ALL TO service_role USING (true) WITH CHECK (true)` on orders (:11-14), products (:27), product_variants (:39), product_options (:65), sites (:92), subscriptions (:159), billing_history (:172) and super_admin_allowlist (:186). `rg -n 'DROP POLICY IF EXISTS service_role_' migrations 165-197` returns nothing — no later migration removes them. So the DB backstop is absent for two independent reasons, and the second is purely source-provable without relying on any managed-platform property. Note also 164:20-22 `GRANT app_tenant TO service_role`, which makes the app_tenant GUC policies applicable to service_role by role membership — they are inert only because of BYPASSRLS, exactly as the roadmap's R2.4 predicted ("the DDL is inert under `service_role` so it can land early").

CONFIG-AS-DEPLOYMENT / TEST-AS-BEHAVIOR CHECK: the auditor did not commit either error. tenant-load-bearing-d4.test.ts is pure `readFileSync` + regex over source text — it asserts the string "SET LOCAL ROLE app_tenant" exists, not that it executes. scripts/validators/no-blanket-service-role-validator.mjs:9-21 is likewise a `.includes()` string check, so the governance gate is green while the runtime path is service_role. The one genuinely behavioral test, tests/executable/tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs, proves the DDL works only when the harness itself does `SET ROLE app_tenant` + `set_config('app.site_id',…)` (:53-56, :267-269) — and its own positive control at :263 asserts that service_role with NO GUC set still returns rows. It is corroboration, not contradiction.

TWO PRECISION CORRECTIONS (neither changes severity): (1) "runs every query as service_role" is marginally imprecise — four storefront read paths run over the Hyperdrive/DATABASE_URL pooled connection instead (services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront.ts:646, 656, 671, 691), and they also neither `SET ROLE app_tenant` nor set the GUC; since app_tenant is NOLOGIN (164:11) no pooled connection can be app_tenant without an explicit SET ROLE. That is a second RLS-bypassing path, so the correction strengthens the finding. (2) The stated cause ("BYPASSRLS roles are exempt from FORCE") is correct but incomplete; the 163 `USING(true) TO service_role` policies are the more direct, source-provable cause on the core commerce tables.

SEVERITY: S0 is earned. This is a direct violation of a constitutional MUST-NOT — R-2 at LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md:393 ("No tenant query MUST run under a `BYPASSRLS` / blanket `service_role` connection") and R-1 at :392 — on the entire live data path, and ART-08 (:193-195) names it "The decisive change". The roadmap makes it a hard gate: P3 and P8 "cannot *exit* until ISSUE-009 (P2) closes" (:227, :618, :620). The risk as stated is properly conditional ("a single application-layer miss… leaks"), and migration 196 documents a real instance of exactly such an L1/L2 miss (site-private theme rows readable via a baseline `USING(true)`), so the conditional is not hypothetical.

EVIDENCE STATE: VERIFIED-SOURCE holds. The structural core (no live GUC setter, no live app_tenant assumption, service-role key client on every path) is established directly from source at this SHA, and the absence of a DB deny is established from migration 163's committed `USING(true) TO service_role` policies without needing the managed-platform BYPASSRLS fact. The BYPASSRLS link itself is documented in-repo at 196:31-35 as locally proven, which is REFERENCE-ONLY for a live cluster — but the conclusion does not depend on it.
